## Chapter One: The Beginning of Love

There are many kinds of first encounters in this world—beautiful, unforgettable, fleeting, and familiar—but none can compare to the feeling He Ran had when she first met Xiao Han. With the arrival of March, the humid, rainy season begins in Guangzhou. The weather remains unpredictable, damp, and foggy. The walls and tiles were covered with layers of water droplets, mold had grown in the dark corners, and clothes that had been hanging out to dry for several days were still not dry. All of was unbearable. He Ran hasn't been sleeping well these past few days, partly because of the torturous weather, and partly because of a man she's never met. She had dreamed of that man for several nights in a row. He had an extremely pleasant voice, and in the dead of night, he would whisper in her ear, saying all the things that lovers say that would make them blush. Perhaps this is what others call a young girl's first stirrings of love, but He Ran had never even seen the man's face, nor did she know his name. Even He Ran herself found it strange. It all started with a chance encounter a week ago. It was just an ordinary Friday afternoon. After school, He Ran and Ding Xiaoxu went home together. It had become a routine for He Ran to take a taxi and for Ding Xiaoxu to hitch a ride with her. While hailing a car at the school gate, Ding Xiaoxu suddenly scratched his head and said with a smile,

"Didn't you ask me what I wanted for my birthday? I was too embarrassed to ask for something too expensive, so how about you treat me to a haircut today?"

He Ran nodded and said,

"Okay, which one would you like to go to?"

She had many hair styling cards in her bag, all gifts from her older cousins, but she had never used them. Ding Xiaoxu's slightly round face glowed with excitement as she said,

"Let's go to that newly opened one in Zhengjia Plaza. I heard they have some really handsome hairdressers there. I really want to see them!"

He Ran was thinking about something else: since it was a newly opened store, she probably didn't have a membership card, but that didn't matter. After thinking for a moment, He Ran nodded and said,

"Okay, let's go."

The driver took them to the main entrance of the square, and then Ding Xiaoxu led the way. Two girls in blue and white school uniforms walked side by side. One was lively and cute, and the other was quiet and pure. They were a pair that attracted a lot of attention on the street. Ding Xiaoxu had made thorough preparations before arriving and knew the way well, so she quickly found her destination. He Ran can no longer recall what the hair salon, which was the talk of her female classmates and was full of handsome guys, looked like. When it comes to the memories of that afternoon, the presence of another person far outweighs everything else. Before coming, she wasn't as full of expectations as Ding Xiaoxu, so when she met the

"handsome guy"

that girls usually think of, she didn't feel much. Although the men here were handsome, they all had an undeniable effeminate air about them. Xiao Qianqin didn't like their overly avant-garde and flamboyant hairstyles, but looking at Ding Xiaoxu beside her, whose eyes were shining and who was smiling happily, He Ran thought it was fine as long as she was happy. Unable to resist the sweet talk and the praise and recommendations of a group of handsome men, Ding Xiaoxu quickly decided not only to get a haircut, but also to have it straightened and treated again. Ding Xiaoxu looked at He Ran somewhat embarrassed and said,

"I can pay for the straightening and aftercare myself."

He Ran shook her head dismissively:

"It's nothing."

For the rest of the time, Ding Xiaoxu laughed heartily amidst a crowd of people, while He Ran sat quietly to the side reading to pass the time. Later, someone started targeting her, and people kept coming up to her asking if she needed any projects. He Ran politely declined several times, but the person persisted in recommending them to her. Finally, she grew a little impatient and had no choice but to agree to get her hair washed. Led by a woman, He Ran climbed to the second floor. The lights on the second floor dimmed instantly, and the decor and furnishings exuded an elegant charm. The sound of a guzheng playing in the corridor flowed like a gentle stream. He Ran followed the woman into a secluded little room, where the lights were even dimmer and the surroundings were dark and quiet. The room contained three spacious shampoo beds, about an arm's length apart, separated by openwork folding screens painted with a scene of plum blossoms, orchids, bamboo, and chrysanthemums. The tranquil atmosphere is comparable to a high-end massage parlor, so it's no wonder that the prices are relatively high. With no one nagging in her ear, He Ran felt much more comfortable. Perhaps going upstairs to kill time was a good decision. She randomly chose a bed and sat down, and the woman told her to wait a moment. Two minutes later, she heard light footsteps behind her again. Thinking that the woman might be returning, He Ran was no longer on guard. A warm hand reached behind her head and untied her long hair, which was tied in a ponytail, and the rubber band slowly came off. The person's movements were gentle, without pulling on her scalp in the slightest. He Ran obediently took off her glasses and held them in her hand. The man then draped a white towel over her shoulders and tucked it slightly into her raised collar.

"Lie down."

Upon hearing this voice, He Ran subconsciously furrowed her brows. It was clearly a woman just now, how did it suddenly turn into a man? He Ran has never been very willing to interact with the opposite sex since childhood, but out of politeness, she did not show any dissatisfaction, nor did she immediately ask to be replaced. She obediently lay down as the man asked, slightly adjusting the position of the back of her head. She thought to herself, *Fine, this will only happen once.*

The man started running the water, adjusting the temperature as he asked her,

"Do you want a haircut later?"

"Need not."

What shampoo should I use?

"casual."

He Ran silently commented in her heart that this person's voice was quite pleasant, the kind of voice that was hard not to like. Deep, mellow, with a slightly gritty texture, like autumn leaves rustling in the wind. Having grown accustomed to Han Yu's hoarse voice during puberty, He Ran found this mature voice to be nothing short of heavenly. The man rinsed her forehead with water and asked in a low voice,

"Is this water temperature okay?"

He Ran replied,

"Okay."

The man quickly wet her hair, then squeezed out some shampoo and rubbed it into her hair.

"oh."

"No, I'm with my classmates."

The man's attempt to strike up a conversation was casual and formulaic, and He Ran's response was equally lukewarm. Neither of them wanted to say anything more, so the conversation did not continue. Unlike the people on the first floor who were enthusiastically recommending various services to her, this person simply washed her hair quietly and completed his work. At first, He Ran didn't feel anything was wrong, and even closed her eyes slightly, wanting to rest for a while, until her ear was suddenly pinched. Firstly, she feels insecure because she inevitably has to take off her glasses when getting her hair done, leaving her with a blurry vision and difficulty seeing clearly. Secondly, she feels extremely uncomfortable due to the unavoidable physical contact with many strangers. What's more, the person touching her ear now is a man. Strangely, she didn't feel the aversion she had imagined. Of course, it's definitely not enjoyable either. The man's hand lingered on her ear for an unknown amount of time before he slipped his fingers inside and scratched it. He Ran thought her body must be trembling, and trembling very obviously. itch. I want to laugh. He Ran bit her lip tightly, trying hard to suppress her emotions. She was afraid that her slight trembling would be detected by this strange man, and even more unwilling for him to discover what she was thinking at that moment. Finally, the man's hand left her ear. It was an agonizing process, and He Ran breathed a slight sigh of relief. He squeezed out some more shampoo and started scratching her scalp again.

"Is this enough pressure?"

Hearing that melodious, deep voice again now, it felt so close, as if soft feathers were enveloping my heart. He Ran couldn't quite explain the feeling of being bitten by a tiny insect, but she was certain that this emotion was very unfamiliar and something she had never experienced before. She hesitated for a moment before answering,

"Okay."

Is there anywhere else that itches?

"have."

"where?"

"..."

He Ran took a silent breath, and after a long while said,

"No more."

"Then I'll flush it."

"OK."

The whole process took about ten minutes, which is neither long nor short. After wrapping He Ran's hair with a towel, the man's job was done. He left the room, and the female manager from before came in to call He Ran to blow-dry her hair. Hearing footsteps behind her, He Ran remembered to put on her glasses and turned around, but only managed to catch a glimpse of a tall, slender black figure. More than a month has passed, and He Ran is unwilling to admit that she is obsessed with a man she has only spent ten minutes with. Ironically, it was less a man and more a voice. Standing by the window, He Ran gazed thoughtfully at the scenery in the garden below, lost in thought. After an unknown amount of time, a black sedan slowly drove in from outside the fence, the Bentley emblem on the hood indicating the owner's extraordinary status. Perhaps because she had been standing for too long, He Ran felt a little weak and reached out to gently touch her forehead. A few minutes later, her mother Yang Wenping's voice came from downstairs,

"Ranran, Xiaoyu has arrived, come down quickly to greet her..."

Her head felt even dizzier. He Ran took a deep breath, forced herself to stay awake, and turned to go downstairs. The Han and He families have been close friends for generations, their friendship continuing to this day. However, by the time of He Ran and Han Yu, they had become irreconcilable enemies. It seemed their fates were incompatible; wherever they went, they seemed to run into each other like sworn enemies. The adults, however, believed this was simply another manifestation of their close relationship, attributing it all to their immaturity. During dinner, He Ran overheard some gossip from the adults—her cousin, the second daughter of her uncle's family, had been caught having an affair with the family's hired driver a few days earlier. The driver was reprimanded and kicked out, and her cousin was severely criticized by her elders and confined to the house for a month to reflect on her actions. As Yang Wenping spoke, she turned back to give He Ran a heads-up:

"If you ever dare to do something so shameful in the future, I will disown you as my daughter."

I heard that Han Yu joined the music club last semester, met a bunch of good-for-nothing friends, and then successfully formed a rock band. At the end of a quiet, cobblestone alley, the barbershop sits quietly without any signs or markings. The two wooden doors are covered with old couplets and yellowed old photos. "No."

"Can't we wash it again after it's already been washed?"

There's no reason not to do business that comes to your door, so the man stepped aside to let her in, saying,

"Please come in."

The barbershop was a small space of about twenty square meters, with an uneven cement floor. The furnishings inside were equally old and simple, with only two barber chairs. The wooden chairs looked quite old, and the handles were worn down to the point that the patterns were no longer visible. The dressing table was cluttered with miscellaneous items, the mirror was chipped and peeling, and it was covered in dust. The man lifted a curtain and led He Ran into the inner room, where a shampoo bed was placed. Several small holes were torn in the sofa, revealing the sponge stuffed under the skin. The shampoo bed was a semi-reclining type. He Ran wasn't tall enough, so when she sat down, her legs dangled in mid-air, which wasn't very comfortable. The man found a small stool and placed it under her feet, which made her feel much better. He draped a towel over He Ran's shoulders, untied her ponytail, and helped her lie down. He Ran wasn't sure if the towel was clean, but she didn't say anything. The man's palms were calloused, and when he lightly brushed against her neck, it sent a shiver down her spine. He Ran was already starting to feel nervous as she thought about what was going to happen next. After she lay down, the man turned on the shower and asked as he rinsed her,

"Is the water temperature okay?"

He Ran said,

"Okay."

He Ran's hair was very clean. The man only squeezed out a little shampoo, and soon a large amount of foam appeared. He Ran turned her head and glanced at it a few times. The cat's fur was relatively clean, and the man didn't say anything when he saw it, so she guessed that it was probably his pet. "Can."

"It's nothing."

Does being able to cut something not necessarily mean you can cut it well? He Ran thought for a few seconds and said,

After rinsing off, her head was wrapped high with a towel. She followed the man to the outer room and casually chose a barber chair to sit down. The man took a hairdryer out of the drawer and dried her hair until it was half dry, then asked the same question again:

He Ran looked at herself in the mirror and hesitated for a while. She hasn't cut her hair short since she was discharged from the hospital two years ago. Especially her breasts, which, even without the support of a bra, were still impressive to He Ran. He Ran estimated that they were probably even bigger than her face. At this point, the man had finished cutting the back of He Ran's hair and was about to start cutting her bangs. He Ran obediently took off her glasses, as it was no longer convenient for her to scrutinize the other person. The man walked around to her side, bent down, and got close to her. Their faces were less than ten centimeters apart, and He Ran felt that he could sense if she breathed even a little heavily. She closed her eyes; her senses were sharper when she couldn't see. The cool metal brushed against her forehead, and wisps of short hair fell one by one onto her nose and cheeks, causing a slight itch. The man's fingertips inadvertently brushed against her skin, the lingering warmth burning. Even though he didn't touch her ears again, He Ran's heart inexplicably clenched because of this slight contact. Before she could even react, she had opened her eyes without warning. The man probably didn't expect her to suddenly open her eyes wide, and he paused for a moment while holding the scissors. Their eyes met, and his pupils were like a vast ocean, yet even more intense than the darkness of night. He Ran stared intently, as if determined to find something inside. My heart started racing at that moment. After a moment, the man curled his lips into a smile and said,

"You should close your eyes."

He Ran didn't say anything and closed her eyes as instructed. In those brief few seconds, He Ran noticed a defect in the man's left hand. Half of his thumb was missing, whether congenital or caused by an accident was unknown. She wanted to take a few more glances, but she was afraid the man would think she was disrespectful, so she didn't do so in the end. The man's timing was perfect. Ten minutes later, he used a sponge to wipe the stray hairs off He Ran's face and then unbuttoned her hair. He Ran opened her eyes and put on her glasses. A delicate face was reflected in the mirror, with fine features and two pale lips. He Ran's skin was very fair, almost dazzlingly so. With such features and skin tone, she should have been a beauty, but perhaps due to her personality, she never wanted to dress up too much. This simple, straight bangs and bob haircut is nothing special, and it doesn't stand out, which suits He Ran's taste perfectly. She took out her wallet with satisfaction and asked how much it cost. The man said,

"A wash, cut, and blow-dry costs 15 yuan in total."

He Ran only had one hundred yuan bills on her, and a few ten-yuan bills she had just received as change from the taxi fare. She handed the man two ten-yuan bills, and he returned one to her. He Ran looked at him with a puzzled expression. The man explained,

"You're the first customer today, so 10 yuan will do."

He Ran took the money and politely said thank you. Before leaving, she glanced back and realized that she had left her phone on the dressing table. That's fine, just leave it there. When He Ran arrived at the KTV, it was already 1 p.m. Han Yu and his band had finished their rehearsal long ago and were drinking and having fun together. When He Ran pushed open the door and walked in, Han Yu looked up but didn't immediately recognize her. If He Ran hadn't called out to him, he would have thought she was some girl who had wandered into the wrong house. Han Yu called her more than a dozen times this morning but no one answered. Later, she even turned off her phone. He Ran was obviously doing it on purpose. You can imagine how angry and frustrated Young Master Han must be right now. When Young Master Han gets angry, he usually exhibits two behaviors: either he throws a tantrum and smashes things, or he humiliates He Ran with what he thinks is a triumphant but is actually very childish. We now belong to the second category. They were playing a drinking game in the private room. Han Yu invited He Ran to sit down next to him and join in. Finger-guessing games were He Ran's weak point, but at this moment she had no right to refuse and could only obediently follow Han Yu's wishes. She lost a round of the drinking game after only a few rounds, and someone brought her a full glass of beer. He Ran didn't reach out her hand.

"I can't drink alcohol."

As she spoke, her eyes were fixed on Han Yu; clearly, whether or not she drank the beer ultimately depended on him. Han Yu stretched out his arms on the sofa and looked at her lazily:

"You have to accept the consequences of your bet."

He Ran said,

"I can use tea instead of wine."

He spoke with an air of nonchalance, saying,

"What's the point of drinking tea? You have to drink alcohol."

He Ran shook her head, still refusing. Han Yu was clearly displeased that she was late and had such a bad attitude. In the dim light, his thick eyebrows were lowered, and the high bridge of his nose cast shadows on both sides, making his handsome face appear even more rebellious and unruly. He Ran often wondered why so many girls who had been bullied by Han Yu couldn't bring themselves to hate him, and instead were happy to be ordered around by him. After thinking about it, she figured it was probably because of his good looks, which were a gift from heaven. Humans are visual creatures, and are willing to conform to foods that look good to the eye, but He Ran is unwilling to join them. So she is still trying to resist. After a brief standoff, Han Yu finally smiled, a mischievous smile. He Ran knew him so well that she knew from just one expression what new trick he was thinking of to tease her. Sure enough, a few seconds later, he said,

"I know you can't drink, so I specially prepared something else for you."

After saying that, he handed a bottle of beverage of an indescribable color to He Ran, his smile widening:

"This is a homemade drink. I added bitter melon juice, lemon juice, durian, hawthorn... It must be delicious, don't you think?"

He Ran frowned slightly, staring at the strange-looking bottle, expressing her resistance with silence.

"What, you don't want to drink?"

"May I have some tea?"

"Sure,"

Han Yu shrugged.

"If you don't want to drink, that's fine, I won't force you. But about Xu Yafei..."

He Ran interrupted him, took the cup from him, and said,

"I'll drink it."

Han Yu curled the corners of his lips and looked at her with anticipation. He Ran pinched her nose, opened her mouth, and poured the liquid from the cup into her mouth all at once. To be honest, the strange taste was not much better than a stinky ditch, and she almost vomited several times. Good heavens, what on earth did she drink? There was a fish bone stuck in her throat. He Ran forced herself to swallow, closed her eyes, and continued to pour the contents into her mouth. The other people sitting in the private room were all band members, and they knew a little about the relationship between Han Yu and He Ran. They were already used to Han Yu's malicious behavior. The cup was clearly filled with the leftovers and soup they had just finished eating, practically a swill bucket, and he actually handed it to He Ran to drink. What grudge did he have against her? The oldest drummer couldn't stand it anymore. He nudged Han Yu with his elbow and whispered,

"He's been pretty well-behaved, and he hasn't done anything to you. Why do you keep making things difficult for him?"

Han Yu snorted coldly and ignored him. She's well-behaved? She's just not obviously bad. He Ran placed the empty glass on the table and calmly wiped her mouth with a tissue.

"Is this okay?"

Han Yu's desire for revenge was finally satisfied, and a smile appeared on his face. He raised his chin to indicate that it was okay. He Ran stood up and quickly went into the bathroom to rinse her mouth. The sound of the running water made her vomiting less noticeable. The young people were full of energy and didn't break up until 10 p.m. During this time, He Ran just sat silently to the side watching them make a fuss, trying to keep her presence to herself. After that, Han Yu didn't pick any more fights with her. When it came time to pay, Han Yu generously tossed five red banknotes at the counter. The waiter, puzzled, said,

"Sir, your total bill is only three hundred yuan."

Han Yu explained,

"It's a fine."

He deliberately looked at He Ran as he spoke,

"Isn't it forbidden to bring dogs in here? I violated the rules, so it's only right that I get a fine. Is two hundred enough? If not, I can add more."

He Ran looked away, sneering inwardly,

"Childish."

After leaving the KTV, the rest of the band members took a taxi, while He Ran and Han Yu stood at the intersection, waiting for their driver to pick them up. Five minutes ago, Han Yu made a phone call to someone, and now, a girl wearing a crop top and a miniskirt appeared in front of them. Han Yu stepped forward, familiarly put his arm around the girl's shoulder, and then gestured with his chin to He Ran, instructing,

"You go back first, Xiao Ai and I are going out to play."

He Ran silently mocked,

"Xiao Ai? The Ai in AIDS?"

She nodded and was about to leave when Han Yu suddenly called out to her,

"Wait."

He Ran turned to look at him. Han Yu stretched out his right hand and said matter-of-factly,

"Give me five hundred yuan. I don't have enough cash on me."

Without hesitation, He Ran stuffed the entire wallet into his hand. She thought she could finally leave, but to her surprise, Young Master Han had a new complaint. He looked her up and down, frowned, and said disdainfully,

"He Ran, I don't like your short hair. It's ugly, like a country bumpkin."

He Ran is not a rebellious girl, unlike Han Yu who is keen on going against others. However, she had to admit that when she heard Han Yu say those words, she felt more and more that her new hairstyle was becoming more and more appealing. He Ran said goodbye to Han Yu in a cheerful mood, and the two went their separate ways. Now, she has to go and find her phone. The roads in Xiaozhou Village were already difficult to walk on, and when a light rain unexpectedly started, the bluestone paths became even more slippery. At 11 p.m., all the lights in the houses were turned off, and the alley was completely silent. The ancient trees and the river under the moonlight shone with a dim luster, and there were few people around. At first glance, the scene seemed very peaceful and relaxing, but for He Ran, who was traveling alone, it was somewhat terrifying. He Ran had never known she had the courage to walk alone at night. Feeling several men turning to look at her as they passed by, she quickened her pace involuntarily. It took her some effort to find the hair salon she had visited during the day. She knocked on, unsure if the staff were asleep at that hour. Soon, footsteps were heard inside the house, slow and steady, and then the door was opened. The man peeked out, looked at the man for a few seconds, and said,

"Oh, it's you."

He opened the door, stepped aside to let He Ran in, and asked her,

"Are you here to look for your phone?"

He Ran nodded and said,

"Yes, I'm not sure if I left it with you."

The man said,

"You left it on the table this morning and forgot to take it with you, so I put it away for you."

As he spoke, he went into the inner room and fiddled with it for a while before coming out and returning the phone to He Ran. The moment the man reached out, He Ran saw clearly that his left thumb was indeed not complete. She nodded gratefully and said,

"Thank you."

The man said in a nonchalant tone,

"It's nothing."

He Ran said,

"Then I'll go first, so as not to disturb your rest."

The man nodded.

"Okay."

After speaking, she stood still for a few seconds before continuing,

"Could I borrow your umbrella?... It's raining outside."

The man hesitated for a moment, but immediately agreed:

"Please wait a moment."

He turned and went upstairs to get it, and soon came down with an umbrella. The stairs creaked under his weight. When the man approached, He Ran asked shyly,

"Did I disturb your girlfriend's sleep?"

The man paused slightly,

"Girlfriend? Which girlfriend?"

He Ran didn't know whether he meant he didn't have a girlfriend or he had many girlfriends.

"Uh...it's that lady who was here this morning,"

He Ran said. The man understood, pursed his lips, and explained,

"Just friends."

"Oh, I misunderstood."

He Ran wisely shut her mouth and didn't ask any more questions, lest she speak too intimately with someone she didn't know well. The man handed her the umbrella and said,

"You're quite brave for a young girl like you, running out alone so late."

He Ran sighed and said,

"There's nothing I can do. I'm having a really bad day. My wallet was stolen, so I don't have money for the bus home. I don't even know where my phone is, and I can't contact my family. I had no choice but to come back and look for it."

He Ran glanced down at the umbrella in her hand; it was a large, black, long-handled umbrella, a rather dull design. She thanked her again, saying,

"Anyway, thank you."

The man paused for a moment, then said,

"Let me give you a ride; this area isn't very safe."

He Ran certainly wouldn't refuse. She nodded and said,

"Okay, thank you for your trouble."

The man locked the door and walked down the steps with He Ran. The umbrella was indeed big enough to cover two people without any problem. The two remained silent the entire way, with He Ran occasionally glancing at the man beside her out of the corner of her eye. Half of his face was obscured by the shadow of the umbrella, while the other half was exposed, bathed in the cool, white moonlight with a mysterious hue, as if telling a sad story. He Ran inexplicably thought of that song: A certain place in the white moonlight's heart So bright, yet so cold. Everyone has a period of sadness. Trying to hide it only makes it more obvious. She couldn't help but sigh softly, though she didn't know why. As you walk outside the archway, you'll find many food stalls serving late-night snacks, brightly lit and gradually becoming more crowded. He Ran stopped and said,

"This is enough."

The man opened his mouth to speak when someone suddenly jumped out from behind and patted him on the shoulder.

"Hey! Old Xiao, you're quite the ladies' man! Which girl are you out on a date with this time?"

The man turned around. The person who spoke to him was probably an acquaintance, and his tone became much more relaxed:

"You're just being cheeky."

He Ran looked in the direction of the voice and saw that it came from a fat man with a square head. The man looked familiar, but He Ran couldn't remember where she had seen him before. Perhaps it was because people with big heads and ears all looked somewhat similar.

"Hehe, aren't you going to introduce me?"

The fat man raised an eyebrow at He Ran. The man was silent for a few seconds, probably thinking about how to introduce He Ran, even though he knew nothing about her either. He Ran then took the initiative to speak,

"My name is He Ran. I'm a customer at his shop. This is the first time we've met."

"Oh, so you just met. It's alright, we'll get to know each other better!"

The fat man laughed heartily and patted his chest, saying,

"Old Xiao and I are old friends. My name isn't very sophisticated, you can just call me Fatty."

After he finished speaking, he pointed behind him and said,

"I'm the owner of this barbecue restaurant. Come and have some late-night snacks whenever you have time, it's on me!"

He Ran politely said thank you. The fat man chatted with them for a few minutes before rushing back to his shop to serve customers. He Ran turned to look at the man beside her and continued their conversation.

"That's enough to drop me off here. I can walk the rest of the way myself,"

He Ran told him. The man asked,

"You have no money on you, how are you going to get home?"

He Ran bit her lip in distress, thinking for a while before deciding how to answer. The man thought for a moment and asked,

"Where do you live?"

He Ran randomly gave an address from a street, which was not the actual address. The man said,

"It's too far. It would take at least an hour to get back by bus from here."

He took out a hundred yuan from his pocket and handed it to her:

"Call a car, hurry up."

He Ran paused for a moment, then reached out and took the money with both hands, saying apologetically,

"Thank you, I will definitely come back tomorrow to return the money and the umbrella."

The man put his hands back in his pockets and said casually,

"It's nothing, no rush."

He Ran said goodbye to him and turned to leave. She had only taken a few steps when the man suddenly called her back and said in a serious tone,

"Next time you come to a place like this, don't wear such clean shoes. They get dirty too easily."

He Ran looked at her sneakers, which were covered in mud and sand, with even the brand logo wiped off. She nodded, smiled, and said,

"Okay, thank you."

He Ran didn't get home until after midnight, and was immediately scolded by Yang Wenping. She confidently used Han Yu as a shield:

"It was Han Yu who asked me to go out and play; he wouldn't let me leave."

This tactic always worked, and sure enough, Yang Wenping's anger subsided slightly. After a long pause, she said earnestly,

"You're about to take the college entrance exam, so you need to focus. I'll talk to Han Yu's mother and tell him to stop bothering you lately."

"Hmm."

He Ran nodded indifferently.

"Then I'll go back to my room to rest."

"Go."

He Ran walked to the top of the stairs, suddenly stopped, turned around and said to Yang Wenping,

"Mom, I hate Han Yu."

Yang Wenping frowned again upon hearing this:

"What are you saying? Han Yu is just a little mischievous and playful. He will gradually mature in the future."

He Ran said,

"No matter what he becomes, I will never like him."

Yang Wenping grew impatient:

"Alright, don't say things like that again."

As He Ran turned to leave, Yang Wenping added,

"Your dad's company has been doing increasingly poorly in recent years. Be careful with your spending lately, and don't be so extravagant."

Knowing she couldn't reason with her, He Ran walked upstairs without looking back.

The next day, He Ran couldn't go to deliver the money to Xiao Han because she had to go back to school. That was fine; she didn't want to appear in front of him too frequently for the time being. The monthly exams are this week, and the atmosphere in the class has become tense without anyone noticing. He Ran, however, hasn't been affected much. Her grades have always hovered in the lower middle range, and no matter how many tutors she hires, it's all to no avail. Yang Wenping has given up on worrying about her studies, and He Ran herself is quite content with the status quo.

---

On Friday morning, after finishing the last subject, the students were dismissed immediately. They were as excited as if they had just experienced the real college entrance examination, cheering as they rushed out of the examination room. After handing in her exam paper, He Ran went straight back to her dormitory to pack her luggage, and took off her school uniform to change into ordinary clothes. Ding Xiaoxu went to her bedside, wanting to invite her to see the newly released movie later. He Ran politely declined, saying she needed to go back to her art studio later. Ding Xiaoxu was a little disappointed, but didn't press the matter. The art studio where He Ran trained was called Dongfeng Art Studio. It was run by a young painter and was not large in scale. Most of the students were college students. He Ran's aunt is a painter with some fame, and He Ran's artistic talent is probably inherited from her. Her scores in the unified examination and individual examination were excellent, with all three subjects scoring above 95, making her a sure candidate for a top-tier university. Returning to her studio after a long absence, He Ran found the entrance plastered with advertisements featuring her artwork, which left her both amused and exasperated. Stepping into the office, everything was exactly as it had been. The principal exclaimed dramatically upon seeing her,

"Oh my gosh, everyone, look who's back!"

He Ran was a star student in the art studio, and the principal had repeatedly expressed his desire to have her become a teacher. He Ran humbly declined several times, but the principal persisted in inviting her. This time, He Ran hadn't even sat down and exchanged a few words when the principal brought up the matter again. This time, He Ran softened her tone a bit, saying,

"It's not that I'm unwilling to help, but I only have two days a week, the weekend..."

The principal interrupted,

"Then come on the weekend! Anyway, you're paid by the hour, so you can come whenever you want!"

He Ran then said,

"I have no experience as a teacher. Aren't you afraid I'll mislead the students?"

The principal said boldly,

"What's there to be afraid of? Anyway, we're not doing any intensive training now, just interest classes. You can teach whatever you want!"

After thinking for a while, He Ran finally said,

"Okay, I'll give it a try."

To everyone's surprise, she agreed so easily. The principal was overjoyed and immediately began to discuss the relevant matters with her. He Ran's classes won't start until next week. After saying goodbye to the teachers she knew and the cafeteria ladies, she left the art studio first. Feeling a bit hungry, she bought a piece of bread on the way to tide her over before heading to the hair salon to find Xiao Han. Unexpectedly, they found no one there; Xiao Han was not in the store. He Ran waited in front of the shop for a long time but no one came back. Finally, she decided to go to the barbecue restaurant to ask Xiao Han's friend. The barbecue restaurant serves barbecue at night and fast food during the day. It's a small place, but business is booming. People are packed in and there's always a long queue. He Ran doesn't even have a place to stand. She waited outside the door for a while, and finally saw the fat man come out of the inner room. She quickly called out to him. The fat man turned around and saw her, greeting her familiarly,

"Hey, Xiao He!"

The fat man squeezed through the crowd and led He Ran into the store to sit down. It turned out that the people queuing outside were waiting for takeout, and there were quite a few empty seats in the dining area. The fat man, sweating profusely, asked,

"What do you want?"

He Ran said,

"Do you know where Xiao Han went? I couldn't find him at his barbershop."

"Oh—"

A sly smile appeared on the fat man's face.

"I was wondering why a young girl would come looking for me. Turns out she's looking for Lao Xiao."

He Ran explained,

"I'm here to return the money to him."

"Hehe, it's okay, I understand."

The fat man stopped and said,

"Old Xiao should still be working at this time. Why don't you take a walk around the central lake? You might run into him."

He Ran asked in confusion,

"Work? What kind of work?"

The fat man said,

"Don't you know? He has two jobs. He usually works at a barbershop, and occasionally he takes on gardening jobs."

"I see."

He Ran nodded thoughtfully. She had received the news and was about to say goodbye when the fat man said,

"Have you eaten yet? If not, why don't you eat here?"

He Ran smiled and said,

"We haven't eaten yet. Let's find Xiao Han later and invite him to come eat with us."

The fat man nodded with a smile,

"Alright."

"Oh, right."

He Ran suddenly remembered something, but she wasn't sure if she should ask since it involved privacy. She hesitated for a moment before speaking:

"Do you know what happened to Xiao Han's hand injury?"

The fat man paused for a moment, then said,

"You mean his thumb?"

He Ran:

"Mm."

The fat man put down his palm-leaf fan and slowly uttered two words:

"Debt of love."

"..."

He lowered his voice and said mysteriously,

" I heard that he used to be with a married woman. That woman's husband was rich and powerful. After the affair was exposed, he called Lao Xiao out and gave him a lesson."

He Ran fell silent after hearing this, her face solemn. The fat man suddenly patted her shoulder and said cheerfully,

"Hey, I was just kidding!"

"..."

He Ran was unsure whether to be happy or sad. The fat man added,

"This is just a rumor, probably false. When I met Lao Xiao, he had been carrying this injury for many years, and I never asked him about it. If you really want to know, you should ask him yourself."

He Ran paused for a moment, pursed her lips, and said,

"Okay, thank you."

After leaving the fast food restaurant, He Ran first went to the general store across the street and bought a pair of canvas shoes for twenty yuan. The shoes had the Converse logo on them, but they were obviously counterfeit. She took off her sneakers, which were still about 80% new, and pretended to throw them into the trash can next to her. The old woman selling shoes saw this and shouted, quickly stepping forward to snatch the shoes from her hands,

"These shoes are perfectly fine, why are you throwing them away! Give them to me if you don't want them!"

After she finished speaking, she looked at He Ran, who didn't react, so the old woman took it as her agreement. The old woman quickly wiped both shoes with a shoe-shining cloth and then put them into the shoe rack in the

"20 yuan special price"

section. Apart from the Nike logo being relatively conspicuous, there was nothing else to notice. He Ran opened her mouth as if to say something, but in the end, she decided against it. It's not too far from Xiaozhou Village to the central lake; a motorcycle ride costs only about twenty yuan. Under the blazing sun, He Ran was glad she had the foresight to bring a parasol; otherwise, she wouldn't have had the leisure to walk around the lake in the scorching sun. The central lake is known as a dating hotspot in the university town, with fragrant green grass and rippling blue water. Unfortunately, the weather is too hot, and even the most beautiful scenery cannot make people stay. He Ran was quite lucky; she found the person she was looking for in less than ten minutes. Xiao Han stood in a patch of lush green bushes, dressed in a short-sleeved T-shirt and jeans. The midday sun was so intense it was hard to look directly at him, and there was no shade around, but he was completely unaffected, taking no other sun protection measures except for a baseball cap on his head. He Ran watched him intently trimming the branches with a pair of long scissors, and suddenly realized where his clumsy hairdressing skills came from. She couldn't help but laugh. He Ran quietly walked behind him, raised the umbrella, and shielded his head. Xiao Han sensed something and turned around. He looked down at her, took a second or so to think, and then slowly called out her name:

"He Ran"

.

## Chapter Two: Testing

Although she didn't understand men, the message conveyed in Xiao Han's eyes inexplicably gave her the courage to give it a try. After Xiao Han finished work, the two went back to Xiaozhou Village for lunch. He Ran said she would treat them to lunch, and Xiao Han didn't hesitate or refuse, readily agreeing. At the fat man's fast food restaurant, he cooked a few home-style dishes. He Ran said she couldn't eat spicy food, so the fat man made the flavors milder. Several dishes were brought out of the kitchen and arranged in a circle on the table. The three sat down around the table. The fat man first picked up a piece of fish-flavored eggplant and asked He Ran to try it, asking her how it tasted. He Ran nodded, praising without reservation:

"A master chef indeed."

The fat man waved his hand and said with a smile,

"Hehe, no, no, our Brother Xiao is the real master chef. We'll have him show you his skills sometime."

As they spoke, they both looked at Xiao Han. The latter probably didn't realize that the topic had turned to him. After a couple of seconds, he nodded and said,

"Oh."

"..."

He was probably naturally quiet, and the fat guy was the one who kept the atmosphere lively throughout the meal. He first asked He Ran how old she was, and then asked her where she was from. He Ran replied without changing his expression,

"23 years old, from Anhui."

The fat man chuckled and chimed in,

"Anhui is great, Huangshan is a wonderful place."

He Ran smiled and asked,

"And you?"

The fat man said,

"I'm 27, from Hebei."

Then he pointed to Xiao Han,

"Old Xiao is 28, from Chongqing."

Xiao Han corrected,

"I'm 32, almost 33."

The fat man chuckled sheepishly and scratched his head:

"Sorry, sorry, my memory's not so good. When we met you were 28, right, right, you should be 32 now."

He Ran was somewhat surprised:

"You two look to be the same age."

The fat man was on the verge of tears:

"Girl, are you praising Lao Xiao or belittling me?"

His expression was so comical that He Ran couldn't help but cover her mouth and laugh. She was laughing happily when she noticed Xiao Han staring at her, and she immediately stopped laughing. The chat has come to an end for now; it's time to have a proper meal. The fat man took out two bottles of beer from the refrigerator, one for himself and one for Xiao Han, and then asked He Ran what she wanted to drink. He Ran thought for a moment and asked,

"Do you have yogurt?"

The fat man nodded:

"Of course."

Xiao Han ate very quickly. He had just received He Ran's yogurt when he had already finished a large bowl of rice in no time. He stood up and said to the two of them,

"I still have work to do, so I'll go first. You two continue eating."

The fat man stopped him and said,

"Hey, hey, hey, that's really outrageous of you. Xiao He hasn't even finished eating yet. He came all this way for you, and you're not even going to take care of him?"

Xiao Han seemed to glare at the fat man. He Ran wasn't sure if that look counted as a glare, but that was roughly the meaning. Xiao Han said,

"What nonsense are you spouting now?"

The fat man retorted,

"How did I make this up? Xiao He came to me this morning specifically to ask about your love life."

"..."

Xiao Han didn't react after hearing this, he just lifted his eyelids and glanced at He Ran indifferently. He Ran stared calmly at the yogurt in her hand. She should be glad that she had a poker face that never showed her emotions, otherwise she would have been blushing by now. She unconsciously bit down on the straw in her mouth, and in just a few sips, she finished the whole carton of yogurt and casually put it aside. A man's hand, roughened by years of labor, came into her view. He picked up the yogurt bottle she had just finished drinking from, smoothly tore off the paper lid, and handed it to her:

"Don't waste it."

His tone was casual, yet it sounded perfectly natural. He Ran was stunned for a moment, then obediently took the paper and silently licked off the layer of milkshake clinging to it. In the end, Xiao Han left first. The fat man tried to stop him, but He Ran didn't let him. She had come to thank him, not to disturb his work. After finishing her meal, He Ran realized she had forgotten something important: she hadn't paid Xiao Han back the money. But it doesn't matter. The fat man said he would get off work around six o'clock in the evening. Anyway, she has plenty of time, so she can just wait for him to come back here. Women in love can lose their rationality. Everyone thought my second cousin had gone mad, but He Ran thought she was very clear-headed. She always knew what she wanted and never crossed that line.

"So what do you plan to do now?"

He Ran asked. My second cousin sighed, leaned back, and sank into the comfortable mattress.

"There's nothing I can do. I'll just stay put, continue with my life, and get married when it's time."

She looked up at the ceiling, as if she were saying to He Ran, or perhaps to herself,

"I don't regret it, not at all. If I could do it all over again, I would still fall in love with him without hesitation."

"If marrying a man I don't love and completing a marriage based solely on interests is my fate, then at least before I'm at his mercy, I want to be crazy for myself once."

After my second cousin finished speaking these moving words, the two of them were lost in thought. After a long silence, the second cousin suddenly turned to look at He Ran:

"You've been talking about me, but let's talk about you too. Do you have a boy you like?"

"No."

"It's not like this happened just a day or two ago."

"None of these rich kids are good. What can they do besides partying and drinking? If you really marry him, you won't have any peace for the rest of your life."

"That engagement was just wishful thinking on the part of my mother and his mother. We don't get along, so it won't work out."

when she heard this. Soon, the headcount was completed, and the class monitor led a large group of students out of the classroom in a grand procession. "female friends,"

and since she was injured and hospitalized, he had to go and visit her. Seeing He Ran come out, Han Yu smiled maliciously and took a few steps forward to ask,

He Ran shook her head slightly. Han Yu's smile widened, and he said sarcastically,

It's actually quite simple to bump into Xiao Han; he eats lunch at Fatty's fast food restaurant almost every day. Looking out, the wind moves like waves, and the flowers sway with it, like clouds spreading across the sea. Xiao Han stood among a flower bush, trimming and pruning, his forehead and back soaked with sweat. He Ran held an umbrella behind him, trying her best to shield him from the sun. Xiao Han turned around and said to her,

"Go and rest, you don't need to follow me."

At first, He Ran said she was fine, but after persisting for several tens of minutes, she couldn't keep up with her strength anymore, so she stopped trying and obediently found a shady spot to stay. He Ran sat under the shade of a tree, while Xiao Han stood among the summer flowers, quite a distance apart, his figure appearing indistinct. When Xiao Han took a break, he came to find her. He Ran handed him a bottle of iced mineral water that she had prepared in advance. He said thank you, unscrewed the cap, and drank it down in one gulp. He Ran watched as his prominent Adam's apple bobbed up and down, and the bottle of water quickly disappeared into his mouth with this repetitive motion, and soon the bottle was empty. Xiao Han took an empty bottle to the tap next to him, filled it with tap water, and then poured the water over his head. His hair, clothes, and shoes were instantly soaked. He washed his face with the water and shook his wet hair vigorously, splashing water droplets everywhere. The scene was quite exhilarating and refreshing. He Ran was taken aback when he suddenly made such a bold move. When Xiao Han walked up to her, she asked with concern,

"Is this job very tiring?"

Xiao Han wiped the water off his face:

"You'll get used to it."

He sat down as he spoke, and He Ran handed him the bottled water in her hand. Xiao Han shook his head and said,

"No need, you drink it."

After sitting for a while, He Ran suddenly said,

"Our art studio is looking for a model, would you like to come and work as one?"

Before Xiao Han could answer, she added,

"The wages are paid daily, twenty yuan an hour. Although it's not much money, it's a very comfortable job."

Xiao Han turned his head to look at her, thought for a while, and said,

"I didn't do it."

He Ran said,

"You don't need any experience. Just sit still and let someone else draw."

Xiao Han didn't agree immediately. He asked,

"Anyone can be a model?"

He Ran nodded:

"Yes."

"Then why not just have them draw you?"

He Ran countered with a reply:

"My facial features are relatively small, making them difficult to depict. Your facial features are more three-dimensional, so the portrait you draw will have a greater visual impact."

She could say these baseless things without batting an eye, and Xiao Han wouldn't question their truthfulness anyway. After a long pause, Xiao Han finally nodded.

"Okay, when?"

He Ran said,

"I'll only be in the studio on weekends. Come next week and leave me your phone number so I can contact you."

Xiao Han said,

"Okay."

On the second weekend of May, He Ran arranged to meet Xiao Han at the entrance of the art studio at eight o'clock in the morning. Xiao Han was quite cautious and asked her on the phone what clothes she should wear. He Ran smiled and said that she could wear whatever she wanted. There were about thirty people in the art studio, and they were divided into two models. Most of the girls were sitting on Xiao Han's side, painting. He Ran moved among the easels, occasionally stopping to offer guidance or demonstrations. A girl leaned close to her ear and asked shyly,

"Teacher, can we use this model in our figure drawing classes in the future?"

He Ran looked up at Xiao Han, who was sitting upright, and said with a slight smile,

"I'll do my best."

An hour and a half later, during the break, the students all ran out to buy sweet drinks, and the art studio was empty in a matter of seconds. He Ran walked up behind Xiao Han and patted him on the shoulder,

"How are you feeling?"

Xiao Han stretched his limbs.

"It's fine."

He made it sound easy, but He Ran knew that maintaining a sitting posture for three consecutive hours was extremely difficult, especially since they were still in the practice phase. A complete portrait sketch usually takes four to five hours to draw. Xiao Han asked,

"May I smoke?"

He Ran said,

"Let's go outside in the corridor; the air conditioning is on in the classroom."

Xiao Han stood up and walked outside, taking out a pack of cigarettes from his pocket, with He Ran following behind him. He lowered his head, put a cigarette in his mouth, lit it with a lighter, and his movements looked very practiced. Exhaling a puff of smoke, Xiao Han made a conversation:

"Your art studio has a nice environment, with air conditioning."

He Ran nodded:

"Yes."

She subtly changed the subject:

"Just now, a student asked me if you were the model when I taught anatomy class."

Xiao Han asked,

"What's the difference?"

He Ran said,

"You need to take off your clothes."

Xiao Han paused slightly, then turned to look at her:

"Upper body or lower body?"

"whole body."

"..."

Xiao Han flicked his cigarette ash and said,

"Let's forget about it."

He Ran couldn't help but smile; she knew he wouldn't agree. Xiao Han said,

"What are you laughing at?"

He Ran shook her head:

"It's nothing."

The next morning, she woke up with heavy dark circles under her eyes and went downstairs. He Ran is now absolutely certain that the annoying cat that kept meowing last night was Xiao Han's pet cat. If this were her own home, she would have kicked it out by now. Standing at the top of the stairs, He Ran heard people whispering inside. She stopped and quietly lifted the curtain a crack. Inside the room, Xiao Han lay on a sofa bed that was just wide enough for one person. It was quite hot, so his upper body was exposed, and he was only covered by a thin sheet. The culprit that disturbed He Ran's sleep was lying on his lower abdomen. Xiao Han gently stroked its fur and scratched its chin from time to time. His voice was as deep and low as in a dream:

"Zaozao, you've been barking all night. Can't you shut up for a while?"

The cat nestled its head in his palm, its eyes half-closed in contentment, still whimpering softly. Xiao Han patted its head, his voice soft as if coaxing a child:

"Alright, stop barking, be quiet for a while."

After playing with the cat for a while, Xiao Han sat up, perhaps preparing to get out of bed. With this movement, the sheet slowly slid off him. After walking a short distance past the archway, you'll reach the auditorium. Streetlights are lit up one by one, and He Ran sees Xiao Han standing at the very end with a somewhat lazy expression. He Ran followed him and called out,

"A shelter for stray cats and dogs."

It sounded unfamiliar; He Ran had never heard of such a place in Xiaozhou Village before.

"Where is that place?"

she asked. Xiao Han pointed in a general direction,

"It's just around the corner. I sometimes do volunteer work there."

He Ran suddenly became curious:

"What are the requirements for volunteers? Can I go with you?"

Xiao Han paused slightly, turned his head to look at her, his gaze containing a hint of suspicion. He Ran volunteered, saying,

"I'm quite kind-hearted."

He thought for a few seconds and said,

"Go and take a look first before you make a decision."

Xiaozhou Village is small. After walking for a while and crossing a bridge, they arrived at their destination in no more than ten minutes. Standing at the foot of a small, overgrown hill in an abandoned park, Xiao Han pointed to the top of the hill and said,

"It's up there."

He Ran looked up and saw that the mountain was not high, at most the height of an eight-story building. They climbed the stone steps, each step of which was very high, equivalent to two steps on a regular staircase, making the climb quite strenuous. There were no handrails either, which made it rather dangerous. Xiao Han walked ahead, turned around and extended his right hand to her, and He Ran naturally took his hand. This was their first time holding hands, or rather, shaking hands. What He Ran felt first was the rough calluses on Xiao Han's palms, solid and with a peculiar feel. Although it was inevitably prickly, she didn't mind it at all. Halfway up the mountain, there was a four-cornered pavilion, which was also dilapidated. Several large bamboo baskets were placed on the stone steps, covered with a layer of dust. He Ran's physical condition was not good. Xiao Han noticed that her breathing had become heavier and asked her if she needed to rest in the pavilion. He Ran shook her head and said,

"Forget it, there are only a few more steps, let's give it our all."

So the two continued their ascent to the mountaintop, and only after reaching the summit did Xiao Han release her hand. He stood at the door and called out a few times, and the owner of the shelter came out to open the door for them. There was a shallow pool in front of the entrance. As requested by the owners, they walked in wearing shoes and soaked in it for a while, supposedly to disinfect and sterilize. The owner of the shelter was an elderly man, over fifty years old, but still very healthy, and his energy and spirit were no less than that of a young person. Xiao Han had been there many times and knew him well. He Ran was meeting him for the first time, but since Xiao Han brought him, the old man didn't ask any questions. After instructing He Ran on some basic things to pay attention to, he invited her to help with the work without any hesitation. The environment in this shelter was not good; in fact, it was quite appalling. As soon as you entered, you could smell a strong stench of urine. Looking around, the pets, though all kept in cages, became excited as soon as they smelled human scent approaching, and barked loudly while clinging to the bars. The dogs and cats weren't the usual lovable types either; some had pus-filled eyes, some had broken legs, and some were bald. That's why people who come here to volunteer are not here for the experience or for fun; they are here to do real hard work. The task the old man assigned them this afternoon wasn't easy either: first, to give Duoduo a bath, and then to clean its cage. Dodo is a middle-aged Old English Sheepdog, large in size, with thick and fluffy fur that hangs over his face, making it almost impossible to see his eyes. Giving him a bath is a big undertaking. He Ran doesn't like being near furry creatures, but as a

"kind-hearted"

person, she can't back down now. Xiao Han was in charge of brushing its fur, while He Ran stood a meter away, spraying it with a hose. However, the dog was very uncooperative, snatching the hose from He Ran's hand, biting it in its mouth, and spraying it at her, as if it were rebelling. He Ran was startled and couldn't dodge in time. After realizing what was happening, she hurriedly ran away, with Duo Duo biting the water pipe and chasing after her. As He Ran was frantically trying to escape, she vaguely heard Xiao Han laughing in a low voice behind her. When He Ran left the shelter at four o'clock in the afternoon, she had never been so disheveled in her life. She was dirty and smelly, and looked like she had been pulled out of a stinking ditch. Xiao Han wasn't much better off than her, but he seemed to have gotten used to it all along. He never intended to leave cleanly after coming here to work. On the way, He Ran jokingly said, seemingly unintentionally,

"You've really had a tough time. Trimming hair and cutting branches is hard enough, but I didn't expect there could be something even more tiring."

Xiao Han replied in a nonchalant tone,

"You've had it tough too, becoming a teacher at such a young age."

There seemed to be a hint of sarcasm in her words. He Ran didn't explain much, only saying,

"There's no other way, I have to earn some money to supplement the family income."

When they reached the entrance of the art studio, He Ran stopped and asked,

"Can I take a shower at your place? The dormitory only has hot water after six o'clock in the evening."

Xiao Han nodded and said,

"Okay."

He Ran told him to wait a moment while she went upstairs to get a few changes of clothes. Afterwards, the two returned to the barbershop together. Xiao Han went upstairs to boil water first and asked He Ran to sit and wait for a while. He Ran was covered in dirty water, so she couldn't really sit down and just stood there waiting. At this moment, Zaozao slowly walked in from outside the door. It seemed that even it could sense the unpleasant smell on He Ran, so it deliberately took a big detour to avoid her and went inside. Soon after, Xiao Han came downstairs. Even though he smelled the same as her, the cat didn't seem to mind at all and went over to rub against his trouser leg and act cute. These days, even cats know how to be xenophobic, He Ran thought to herself with a cold laugh.

"Was it also adopted from a shelter?"

He Ran asked. Xiao Han squatted down and rubbed Zaozao's head, replying,

"No, it's been with me for many years."

He Ran stared at the cat, which was even more affectionate than a woman, and inexplicably thought of some things. After an unknown amount of time, she suddenly turned her gaze and found that Xiao Han was also staring at her without moving.
---

She asked him,

"Why are you looking at me?"

"I'm thinking."

Xiao Han pursed his lips, his smile faint:

"I used to have a younger sister, but unfortunately she fell into a pond, otherwise she would be about your age now."

At this point, He Ran should have replied with

"Oh, what a pity,"

but she said nothing. She didn't want to be his sister at all.

---

After a while, the hot water was ready, and Xiao Han led He Ran upstairs and taught her how to turn on the water. The bathroom was right next to the kitchen, and like the kitchen, it had no door, only a curtain hanging down for privacy. The bathroom was even smaller than He Ran had imagined, probably no more than one square meter in size; if you turned around, you would bump into the wall if you weren't careful. There were two bottles of shower gel and shampoo from brands I didn't recognize in the corner, and a showerhead was fixed on the wall. Apart from that, there was nothing else in the bathroom. The environment was quite clean, and I didn't find any piles of hair or small insects crawling on the tiles. He Ran looked around and said,

"Won't it be stuffy without an exhaust fan?"

Xiao Han said nonchalantly,

"It's fine in summer, just don't wash for too long in winter."

As he spoke, he walked out, lowered the curtain, and said,

"Go ahead and wash."

He Ran looked around carefully again, but couldn't find any hooks in the bathroom, or even a place to hang clothes. Besides, the space was too cramped, and clothes would get wet if hung inside. It doesn't matter if dirty clothes are just thrown on the ground, but for clean clothes, we'll have to trouble Xiao Han to stand at the door and hand them to us. He Ran expressed her request to Xiao Han, and Xiao Han did not refuse. He Ran's clothes were folded neatly and squarely. What she held in Xiao Han's hands was a long dress, a T-shirt on top, and a small pair of underwear on top of that. Xiao Han didn't pay much attention, but for some reason, just a casual glance was enough to inexplicably etch these details into his mind— The lingerie is a simple white design, covered in mesh with a thin layer of lace embroidered along the edges, making it pretty and sweet, and indeed suitable for young girls who are in their developmental stage. He Ran usually wears overly loose clothes, which cover her whole body and make her look thin and petite. Who knew that there was such a hidden secret beneath the T-shirt? The sound of water pattering could be heard. He Ran moved slowly and deliberately, glancing towards the door every now and then. The twilight cast Xiao Han's shadow onto the crudely made curtain, and it was clear from the dark figure that he had his back to her. If you look down slightly, you can see the heels of his feet in flip-flops and the slightly rolled-up corners of his jeans. The skin on those heels was worn dry and thick from walking for a long time, with deep white lines showing through. For some reason, four words popped into He Ran's mind:

"down-to-earth"

. Ten minutes later, the sound of water stopped, and she said,

"I'm done washing."

Her voice was neither too loud nor too soft, loud enough for the person outside the door to hear. The curtain was slightly lifted at a tiny angle, and two fingers with dark skin hooked her underwear and handed it in. He Ran took it and put it on.

"alright."

The next thing handed in was her underwear.

"alright."

Next, in order, are the top and the skirt. Finally, her glasses were handed in. A moment later, He Ran finished dressing and came out, lifting the curtain. She emanated a faint warmth, and her hair was still damp with water droplets. The outside temperature was slightly lower, causing her glasses to fog up with a layer of white mist. Xiao Han remained with his back to the bathroom, ignoring everything. He Ran stood behind him, raising her hand slowly and gently. She stopped just before her fingertips touched his strong shoulder blade. She slowly lowered her arm and said,

"I'm done washing, you can wash now."

Xiao Han turned around upon hearing the sound, glanced at her, and nodded. He walked to the bedside, bent down, and randomly pulled out a few pieces of clothing from the closet. He Ran's gaze followed him as she asked,

"Do you need me to hand you your clothes?"

Xiao Han casually tossed his clothes onto the bed and said,

"No need, go downstairs and dry your hair."

He meant that after showering, he should come out naked and put on clothes. He Ran's tone rose:

"Aren't you afraid of being seen by others?"

Xiao Han said,

"There's no one else here but you."

He Ran sat down leisurely on the edge of the bed, pressing down on his clothes, her tone slightly frivolous:

"What if I want to see?"

She looked up at him, and neither of them spoke when their eyes met. After an unknown amount of time, those two insignificant words came out of his mouth again.

"Child."

He Ran wasn't sure if those two words were meant for her, but it was clear that there was no one else in the room besides her. She paused for a few seconds, then asked,

"Why are you calling me a child?"

Xiao Han said,

"You've always been a child."

He Ran asked unwillingly,

"Where is it small?"

"They're all small."

She bit her lip, then let go after a moment, and said,

"How do you know if it's small or not if you haven't looked closely?"

When she said those words, her hand was on the hem of her dress; whether or not to lift it was just a matter of a moment's thought. Her implication was obvious, and they both understood it, but Xiao Han remained silent and did not express his opinion. Looking back on that moment, He Ran regretted not speaking in a brighter place. The last rays of the setting sun were not enough to illuminate the dark little house, and Xiao Han's face was hidden behind a patchwork of shadows, so she could not see the expression on his face.
---

## Chapter Three: Dreams and Reality

In the very beginning, all the impulse stemmed from a voice that haunted my dreams. And now, the dream has finally become reality. For the next three days, He Ran went to the hair salon but couldn't find Xiao Han. Later, she asked the fat man for information and learned that Xiao Han had gone back to his hometown to take care of some things. Because of his sudden departure, He Ran felt depressed for a short while. Actually, it can't be considered leaving without saying goodbye. Xiao Han had no obligation to inform her where he was going. It seems to have become a habit; He Ran has been taking walks and sketching near the barbershop every few days these past few days, while also checking to see if Xiao Han has returned.

---

That afternoon, she strolled to the entrance of the hair salon as usual. There was still no movement inside the house, and the uncleaned stone steps were covered with fallen leaves. He Ran didn't see Xiao Han, but instead saw Zao Zao lying in the potted plant taking a nap. Seemingly sensing He Ran's arrival, it lazily raised its head, squinted at her, and then let out a soft, listless meow. Xiao Han couldn't take this cat with him, and he wondered how it had been feeding itself lately. After that, whenever He Ran had free time, she would bring some leftovers to feed Zaozao. Practice makes perfect, and occasionally the cat would affectionately rub against He Ran's legs.

---

That afternoon, Zaozao was engrossed in eating the two crucian carp that He Ran had brought when Aman arrived. He Ran heard someone call her name from behind and turned around.

"Why are you sitting by the door?"

Aman stared at her with wide eyes, then asked,

"Where's Xiao Han?"

He Ran recognized the person and replied calmly,

"He's not here."

"Not here?"

Aman was taken aback, then asked in confusion,

"Where did he go?"

"I went back to my hometown,"

He Ran said. Aman suddenly realized and clapped her hands:

"Oh, right, I had forgotten about that."

After saying that, she squatted down, turned her head to look at Zaozao who was eating and drinking heartily, and clicked her tongue and said,

"This old guy is really enjoying his food."

Aman looked at He Ran,

"Did you feed it?"

He Ran:

"Mm."

Aman said,

"Actually, you don't need to worry about it. This guy is very clever and won't starve."

He Ran said,

"It's okay, it's not a hassle to pack up some leftovers anyway."

"This old guy is quite loyal. He's stayed at Brother Xiao's house all these years and hasn't gone anywhere else. If it were me, I would have run away long ago."

Aman shook her head after saying that, and then sighed,

"Alas, humans are not as good as cats."

Aman seemed to have a hidden meaning in her words, but unfortunately He Ran wasn't very interested in what she was saying. Before long, Aman brought up a new topic, raising her eyebrows at He Ran and asking,

"How are things between you and Brother Xiao?"

He Ran feigned ignorance,

"What do you mean, 'how'?"

Aman asked bluntly,

"Have you slept with him?"

He Ran shook her head:

"No."

Aman raised her eyebrows, looking regretful and sad for her, and patted He Ran on the shoulder to encourage her. He Ran was surprised by Aman's outgoing manner and curious about her relationship with Xiao Han, so she asked,

"How long have you known Xiao Han?"

Aman thought for a few seconds and slowly said,

"We met when he first came to Guangzhou. We're from the same hometown and we looked after each other. It's been about ten years now."

He Ran quietly observed her expression as she spoke. After a long pause, she stated her opinion in a normal tone:

"You like Xiao Han."

Women often share an unspoken sixth sense. Aman was quite open-minded and readily admitted,

"I did like him for a few years, but he never liked me back, so I gave up."

He Ran asked,

"Why?"

Aman shrugged:

"He had someone else in his heart back then."

He Ran wanted to ask more questions, but then Aman received a phone call and had to leave. Seeing that Zaozao had almost finished eating, He Ran cleaned up the trash and left. On the day the college entrance examination results were released, He Ran's mood was as calm as usual. Everything was already decided the moment she submitted her answer sheet, and she had a general idea of ​​whether the result would be good or bad. The number displayed on the phone screen was 480 points, a score that exceeded He Ran's expectations. Although she was still, for an art student like her, admission to the Central Academy of Fine Arts was practically a certainty. Just as He Ran exited the results page, her phone rang. It was Yang Wenping calling, and He Ran guessed she was asking about her grades. However, after answering the phone, Yang Wenping's first question was:

"When do you have time to come home?"

He Ran asked in return,

"Is there something you need?"

"Take some time these next couple of days to go to the hospital with me to see Han Yu,"

Yang Wenping said.

"He was in a car accident."

"..."

Several years ago, during the time when He Ran was being bullied by Han Yu the most, she secretly cursed him to be hit by a car when he went out. Unfortunately, this proves the evil saying,

"Good people don't live long, but evil people live for a thousand years."

Han Yu usually acts like a bully and tyrant on campus, but he still lives a comfortable life. So when He Ran heard the news of Han Yu's car accident, she was quite surprised. Had God finally remembered to punish this bastard? In He Ran's mind, car accidents were often associated with major incidents that resulted in severe injuries. She thought she would see Han Yu lying in bed, but when she actually stood in front of Han Yu's hospital bed, she found that it was not like that at all. He only suffered minor injuries; the most serious injury was to his right leg, a slight fracture. He had a cast on, which made walking a little difficult, but the rest were just minor cuts and bruises. Yang Wenping chatted with Mrs. Han for a few minutes and then left the ward, asking He Ran to stay and talk to Han Yu.
---

He Ran was still carrying the fruit basket that Yang Wenping had asked her to buy, and it was quite heavy as she carried it. Unfortunately, her bedside table was already piled high with gifts from visitors, and she had nowhere to put it. This mischievous little devil may have a bad character, but he's quite popular. Han Yu leaned back against the pillows, and even though he was wearing a plain hospital gown, his face still held an arrogant and domineering expression. Even though He Ran was standing, he insisted on craning his neck and striking a pose as if he were looking down on her. He smirked and said,

"Do you know how I got into a car accident?"

He Ran wasn't interested in the cause of the incident, so she remained silent, but in her heart she was thinking: He deserved to go out and show off without a driver's license. Seeing that she remained silent, Han Yu suddenly steered the conversation to someone who seemed completely unrelated:

"Do you still remember Lu Jingbai?"

He Ran slightly turned her gaze away; the name sounded somewhat distant now. Han Yu thought she didn't remember, so he reminded her,

"You were our junior high classmate, right? He was your class monitor, wasn't he? I remember he was pursuing you back then?"

He Ran remained silent, her face expressionless. Han Yu continued,

"Later, you two often appeared together in the cafeteria and library, and everyone assumed you were together, so he should be considered your first love, right?"

Seeing that He Ran didn't respond, Han Yu became more and more enthusiastic:

"What happened next? Hmm, let me think... It seems like something happened to that guy's family? Then he was forced to drop out of school, so you two ultimately couldn't be together?"

Not wanting to hear him reminisce any longer, He Ran interrupted him,

"Why are you bringing him up?"

Han Yu smiled and said,

"Because I hit his car."

"..."

He Ran actually knew that Han Yu had feelings for her. But she couldn't be sure whether there was more liking or dislike in it. When they were very young, they could get along peacefully. He Ran didn't have much feeling for her younger brother, who was a year younger than her; she neither disliked nor liked him. Han Yu also kept his distance from her, and they rarely communicated.

---

Later, at some point, Han Yu became extremely rebellious. This rebellious streak was far more intense than that of any other teenager in their adolescence. Fighting, skipping classes, cheating on exams and other bad habits frequently appeared in his record book. His interactions with He Ran increasingly leaned towards pranks and tricks. Many people say that if a boy keeps pulling a girl's hair, he must be interested in her. He Ran didn't believe it at first, even though Han Yu tore her hair ties one after another. Until one time, she went home with Han Yu. The two sat in the back seat of the car, one on the left and the other on the right. When Han Yu suddenly lunged at her, He Ran was staring blankly out the window at the rows of plane trees receding into the distance. Realizing what had happened, she hurriedly pushed him away, frantically wiping the drool from her right cheek, then quickly glanced in the rearview mirror, wondering if the driver had seen what had just happened. Perhaps that action hurt Han Yu's pride, because from that day on, his revenge went beyond just playing pranks. Every time He Ran opened the classroom door, she had to be careful of the blackboard eraser falling from above, and every time she put her hand into the desk, she had to be careful of cockroaches that suddenly crawled out.

---

Later, even Han Yu's various girlfriends took it for granted that they would bully He Ran. Even though they weren't in the same class, Han Yu had plenty of ways to make her suffer. He Ran initially didn't take Han Yu's childish and petty behavior seriously at all, until Lu Jingbai got into trouble. Lu Jingbai is a very refined young man. He is not particularly handsome, but his scholarly and elegant demeanor is quite endearing. He Ran had only known him for ten days, and before she could even hold his hand, she was expelled from school. During that time, he often appeared in front of. When asked what happened, he would only say guiltily that he had fallen by accident, so He Ran naively thought that it had nothing to do with Han Yu. The news of Lu Jingbai leaving the school came very suddenly, and He Ran didn't even have time to see him one last time. It is said to be because of theft. Lu Jingbai's roommate found his missing 500 yuan in his closet. Although the amount wasn't large, theft was the most taboo thing at the school. Once you crossed that line, there was no turning back, and he was ruthlessly expelled. Afterwards, Han Yu arrogantly approached He Ran, raised his chin, and said to her,

"He Ran, none of the guys who chased after you ended up well."

It was from that time that He Ran began to find Han Yu very annoying. Her brow furrowed deeply, and she raised her voice, saying,

"Why did you go and provoke him again?"

Han Yu's smile widened, his tone filled with contempt:

"Sister, you can't blame me for this, can you? There are so many cars on the road, how am I supposed to know which one is his?"

"Besides, I'm also a victim of this accident, okay? Can't you see how swollen my legs are? Don't you even care about me?"

He Ran took a deep breath, calmed down, and asked,

" How is Lu Jingbai's injury?"

Han Yu shrugged, saying indifferently,

"Who knows? It's his fault for driving that crappy van. If you crash into it, it'll be a pile of scrap metal. He probably won't be in much better shape either."

He Ran felt that Han Yu had tested her mental resilience to a new level. She ignored him and left the ward with steady and quiet steps. She even said to Han Yu in a gentle tone,

"Get some rest."

Yang Wenping was waiting for her downstairs at the hospital. He Ran walked up to her and asked,

"How is the owner of the car that Han Yu hit now?"

Yang Wenping said,

"Mrs. Han said that she should be alright now and is out of danger."

Thinking that He Ran was worried about Han Yu's situation, she added,

"Don't worry, although Han Yu was the one who caused the accident and doesn't have a driver's license, the Han family gave the other party a lot of money to settle the matter privately, and the other party agreed."

He Ran listened and gave a faint

"oh"

. As the two walked near the parking lot, Yang Wenping stopped and said,

"Are you coming home? It's been a long time since you've been back."

He Ran shook her head:

"No, I have a class at the art studio tonight."

Yang Wenping frowned and sighed,

"Stop thinking about your art studio, that has no future!"

He Ran replied absentmindedly,

"Okay."

Yang Wenping added,

"Oh, by the way, I've made an appointment with Dean Xue to take you for a follow-up check-up on Sunday. Don't forget the time."

"Okay."

He Ran nodded, then waved to her,

"I'm leaving now, goodbye."
