He Ran saw Xiao Han again by chance. That day, she suddenly wanted to eat fruit, so she went to the market. As she finished buying fruit and was heading back to her studio, she suddenly caught a glimpse of a familiar figure standing in front of a stall not far away. Her gaze first fell on the back of the person's head, and then she noticed that he was holding the hand of a little boy. He Ran hesitated for a moment, but finally took a step and followed, walking behind the person.

"Xiao Han."

She called out to the man's back in a low voice. The man turned around, blinked after seeing her, and said,

"Hmm."

He Ran glanced at him twice, then looked down at the little boy she had just seen. Is this Xiao Han's son? She couldn't help but wonder. The little boy was timid and would hide behind Xiao Han as if facing a great enemy whenever he met a stranger, but he couldn't suppress his curiosity and would peek out to secretly look at He Ran. He Ran stared at him without changing her expression. Perhaps her expression wasn't friendly enough, because the little boy shrank back a few steps, stammering,

"Uncle... I'm scared."

uncle? He Ran paused for a moment because of the address, then looked up at Xiao Han:

"Isn't this your son?"

Xiao Han retorted,

"When did I ever say I had a son?"

He Ran said,

"Last time I asked you if you had a child, and you said yes."

Xiao Han explained,

"This is my brother's child, and I'm raising him now."

"...Oh."

He Ran's mind raced for a moment before she finally realized what was going on. Xiao Han patted the little boy's hand reassuringly and pulled him out,

"Quan Quan, say hello to your older sister."

The little boy carefully clung to Xiao Han's leg, and after a long while, he managed to utter three words:

"Hello, sister."

His voice was lower than a mosquito's buzz, and after quickly finishing speaking, he hid behind Xiao Han again. Xiao Han said,

"He's a bit timid, don't mind him."

He Ran smiled and said,

"It's nothing."

Xiao Han boarded the train yesterday at noon and arrived this morning. The adults and children hadn't had time to eat yet, and they were just about to buy some groceries to cook. He Ran then made up an excuse to follow behind. After arriving home, Xiao Han went straight into the kitchen to cook, while He Ran stayed downstairs to play with Quan Quan.

"Playing with him"

was a euphemism; she mostly just kept an eye on him to prevent him from running around. He Ran is an only child and the youngest among her relatives of the same generation. She has never interacted with children before and doesn't particularly like them. Quan Quan was shy, and He Ran didn't try to get close to her either. The two stood silently facing each other, two or three meters apart. The first floor was so quiet it was as if no one was there. The soundproofing wasn't very good, and occasionally the sound of chopping vegetables could be heard from the second floor. He Ran noticed that Quan Quan was staring intently at the bag of apples she was carrying. After thinking for a moment, she asked,

"Want some?"

Even such a soft sound could frighten him; his small body visibly stiffened for a moment, then he looked up at her in a panic. He Ran asked again, her voice slightly hesitant:

"Do you want to eat?"

The little head slowly shook, but its eyes, which bore a slight resemblance to Xiao Han's, remained fixed on the apple. He Ran smiled inwardly, stood up and walked towards him. As she passed Quan Quan, she could clearly feel him tense up. She didn't say anything, went straight upstairs to the second floor, asked Xiao Han for a fruit knife, and then went back downstairs to peel an apple. He Ran had never done this job before, so her skills were naturally quite poor. Under her knife, the apple was shaved into sharp edges, and the circle was turned into a square. Without realizing it, Quan Quan moved closer to her, staring intently at the apple in her hand. After her first attempt failed, He Ran threw the disfigured, half-sized square apple into the trash can and started peeling the second one. She was very focused, but still couldn't stick the peel back together, and couldn't help but sigh in frustration. He Ran looked up to wipe her sweat, only to find that Xiao Han had come downstairs sometime earlier. He was standing next to Quan Quan, and both the old man and the little girl were focused on her hand. When she looked up, their gazes shifted from the apple to her face. He Ran clearly read a message in Xiao Han's eyes: How come he can't even peel an apple? A little embarrassed, she coughed lightly and said,

"Why did you come down?"

Xiao Han replied,

"We're waiting for the water to boil; it'll be a while."

He Ran then handed him the apple and knife, saying,

"You peel it, I really can't manage."

Xiao Han smiled and walked towards her. His movements were very skilled, and while he was sharpening, he could also teach her with ease:

"You should hold the handle like this, following the structure of the apple. This isn't like you sharpening a pencil, so don't be so rough."

He Ran responded with a soft

"oh"

like a student. While they were slicing and chatting, Xiao Han asked,

"Have the results come out yet? How did you do?"

He actually remembered that she was a high school graduate taking the college entrance exam. It seems that this is a major event that the whole country pays attention to every year. He Ran said,

"Neither good nor bad."

Xiao Han asked again,

"Have you filled out your college application yet?"

"not yet."

Where do you plan to go to university in the future?

"It should be Beijing."

Xiao Han nodded.

"Oh, Beijing is great."

By the end of the conversation, the apple was peeled and perfectly shaped. Xiao Han picked up the stem and cut it in half, giving one half to Quan Quan and the other half to He Ran. He Ran waved her hand,

"No need, give them all to him."

Xiao Han said,

"He can't eat that much; he still needs to eat later."

He Ran then reached out and took it.

After finishing her apple, He Ran left. She had classes in the afternoon and couldn't stay there for too long. Perhaps it was something Xiao Han said to Quan Quan after He Ran left, because when she went to the hair salon the next day, she felt that Quan Quan's attitude towards her had changed. He Ran stood at the door. The little guy hesitated for a while, then suddenly mustered up his courage and walked towards her step by step. When he got close enough, he stopped and whispered,

"Thank you."

He Ran was taken aback for a moment, then asked,

"What are you thanking me for?"

Quan Quan's eyes darted around, avoiding her gaze. After a long while, she said hesitantly,

"Uncle said... the painting was a gift from you, and I really like it... Thank you."

He Ran realized what was happening, smiled, and said,

"It's okay, as long as you like it."

Xiao Han came downstairs, and He Ran noticed that he had changed his clothes. She asked,

"Where are you going?"

Xiao Han's answer was simple and clear:

"Let's get to work."

He Ran glanced at Quan Quan,

"He's going too?"

Xiao Han shook his head.

"He's staying at home."

"It's not safe for him to be alone,"

He Ran thought for a moment and said,

"How about I keep an eye on him for you?"

Xiao Han said,

"No need, don't you have class this afternoon?"

He Ran said,

"Starting today, all my classes have been moved to the evenings."

After He Ran finished speaking, she looked at Quan Quan and asked for his opinion:

"Do you want to come to the art studio with me?"

Quan Quan's lips moved slightly, but no sound came out; her eyes, however, were filled with longing. He Ran then said,

"I can teach you to draw."

Quan Quan hesitated for a moment, then looked up and tugged at Xiao Han's pants. He squeezed out a few words, his voice low and pitiful:

"I want to go."

Xiao Han gently patted his head, then glanced at He Ran and nodded in tacit agreement. After obtaining the principal's permission, He Ran took Quan Quan to the art studio. In the afternoon, while the students had their lessons in the classroom, Quan Quan sat quietly in the corner, listening attentively. He was very well-behaved, staying quietly and not disturbing anyone at all. He Ran gave him some drawing paper and a bag of watercolor pens. After drawing for a while, he looked up and called out to He Ran.

"elder sister......"

He Ran turned to look at him and asked,

"What's wrong?"

He was still very timid, and dared not look He Ran in the eye when he spoke, stammering,

"You, you said... you could teach me to draw, right?"

He Ran smiled and said,

"I can teach you, but there's one condition."

Quan Quan opened her big, bewildered eyes and asked,

"What do you mean by 'conditions'?"

"You have to promise me one thing."

"What is it?"

Quan Quan asked. He Ran said,

"From now on, call me Auntie, not Sister."

If he calls Xiao Han Uncle and she calls him Sister, wouldn't that mess up the generations? Quan Quan nodded, seemingly understanding but not quite:

"Oh, Auntie."

He Ran then smiled contentedly,

"Be good."

Xiao Han came to pick up Quan Quan around five or six in the evening, after school. The students had all gone out for dinner, and there was no one else in the art studio. He knocked on the door a few times, but after a long time without any response, he simply pushed the door open and went in. The air conditioning in the classroom had been turned off, but the cold air hadn't completely dissipated. Xiao Han was covered in sweat after finishing his work, but it felt quite cool when he went in. He glanced around and then spotted the two people in the corner. A large sheet of drawing paper was laid out on the ground, and Quan Quan lay down on it to draw. He Ran sat next to her and gave her some pointers. The two of them were so engrossed that they didn't notice his arrival. Xiao Han quietly walked up behind them. He Ran noticed something, turned around, and said,

"You're here."

Quan Quan turned around when he heard the voice. He looked very happy and said in an upbeat tone,

"Uncle."

Xiao Han nodded and asked,

"How's your drawing?"

Quan Quan picked up the painting to show him her work:

"I painted flowers and birds this afternoon."

The drawing paper was covered in patches of yellow and red, making it impossible to discern flowers and birds. Even so, Xiao Han still praised it:

"Not bad."

Quan Quan smiled shyly, revealing two dimples on her little face. Xiao Han then said,

"It's getting late, we should go back."

Quan Quan's lips immediately drooped, and she said reluctantly,

"Wait a minute, let me finish drawing this before you leave."

Xiao Han glanced at the time, and after a moment he compromised,

"Okay, we'll leave at 6:30."

He Ran found a stool for Xiao Han and sat down next to him. The faint smell of sweat emanating from Xiao Han reminded He Ran of how close they were. He Ran couldn't help but recall that afternoon, in the cramped space, the dim light casting shadows on Xiao Han 's face. They remained silent for so long that she almost forgot what she had said before. Xiao Han finally spoke up:

"Go to the first floor and wait for me. We'll talk after you take a shower."

After saying that, he turned around and went into the bathroom. He Ran was in a daze for a while before silently going downstairs. However, she didn't wait for him and left directly. Because she could sense that he wouldn't be able to say anything, and even if he could, it wouldn't be what she wanted to hear. Sometimes a man's silence is a form of rejection. Thinking of this, she subtly shifted her calf slightly to the side. When the two sat together, Xiao Han's trouser leg would always brush against her calf almost imperceptibly, a subtle sensation that was quite bothersome.
---

There are still 20 minutes until 6:30. Sitting still like this isn't a solution. He Ran casually started a conversation:

"Who does Quan Quan take after? He's so timid."

Xiao Han said,

"Let him take after his father."

He Ran had forgotten to ask before, but she noticed it when Xiao Han brought it up:

"Why are you the one raising him? Where is his father?"

Xiao Han said,

"His father passed away, and his mother went with another man. She's in Guangzhou now, but she refuses to take him with her."

He Ran fell silent after hearing this, and then turned to look at the small figure again.

"So he'll be living here with you from now on?"

"They're just here for a short visit; they'll be sent back next month."

"Why don't you consider letting him study in the city? The environment here is better."

Xiao Han pursed his lips and remained silent for a while.

"I'm trying."

At 6:30, Xiao Han left the studio with Quan Quan. Standing at the door, Quan Quan turned back reluctantly and asked He Ran,

"Can I come again tomorrow?"

He Ran nodded and smiled:

"Of course."

Xiao Han patted Quan Quan on the shoulder,

"Say goodbye to your sister."

"Sister..."

Quan Quan opened her mouth, then immediately changed her words:

"Goodbye, Auntie."

He Ran waved to them:

"See you tomorrow."

In the days that followed, when Xiao Han had to go out to work, he would leave Quan Quan with He Ran and ask her to look after her. The little guy has been having some trouble adjusting to the new environment these past few days, with frequent diarrhea. Xiao Han instructed He Ran to try to avoid feeding him unclean food from outside. But the kid is also a glutton, refusing to eat the food in the art studio cafeteria and instead eyeing the various snack shops on the street. One day at noon, He Ran took him out to eat. On the way, Quan Quan saw something and his feet suddenly froze on the ground, unable to walk any further. He Ran followed his gaze and saw a fried chicken shop, which made her frown. She tugged at his hand, but couldn't move it, and said,

"Let's go eat at that noodle shop up ahead."

Seeing that Quan Quan was standing still, He Ran said again,

"Your diarrhea hasn't gotten better yet, you can't eat these fried foods."

Quan Quan swallowed hard, but still didn't move. He Ran only knew a few tricks for dealing with children; once she reasoned with them, she was at a loss. Quan Quan's eyes sparkled, and her tone carried a hint of coquetry:

"Sister, please let me have this just once. I've never had this in my hometown."

He Ran asked,

"What did you just call me?"

Quan Quan, the mischievous little imp, immediately changed her address:

"Auntie."

"..."

Five minutes later, the adult and the child sat down by the window in Wallace. He Ran unwrapped the still-warm hamburger, but didn't hand it to Quan Quan immediately. She first made him promise:

"Just this once, and don't do it again."

Quan Quan smiled brightly and said,

"Mmm."

He Ran added,

"I can't tell your uncle."

Quan Quan nodded like a chick pecking at rice:

"I absolutely won't tell."

Once adults and children have their own little secrets, their relationship will become much closer. So on the weekend, Xiao Han took a rare day off and planned to take Quan Quan to the amusement park. Quan Quan couldn't leave He Ran out, and insisted on inviting her along as well. He Ran hadn't received any prior notice; she was still sleeping in late in her dorm on the morning of her departure. Just as she was about to get up, Xiao Han suddenly called. He briefly explained his purpose and asked if she could come out now. He Ran reacted for a few seconds, quickly washed her face, brushed her teeth, changed her clothes, and hurriedly went downstairs. Pushing open the iron gate, she found an elderly man and a young boy standing hand in hand outside, waiting for her. Quan Quan was carrying a bag of steamed buns and soy milk. She grinned at her, revealing two cute tiger teeth.

"Auntie, this is breakfast that Uncle bought for you."

He Ran was still half asleep, her head spinning. She squinted at Quan Quan, then at Xiao Han, and after a moment said groggily,

"You're welcome."

"..."

"..."

The bus traveled at a steady speed on the wide avenue, heading north into the city. The three of them sat in the last row. He Ran was by the window, Xiao Han sat next to her, and Quan Quan sat on Xiao Han's lap. After He Ran finished eating the steamed buns that the uncle and nephew had brought her, she began to feel drowsy again. Her head bobbed up and down like a fisherman, hitting the car window a few times and bumping into Xiao Han's shoulder a few times. Feeling a little embarrassed, she turned her head slightly to look at the face that was always looking straight ahead, and inadvertently caught a glimpse of the sweat on his neck and the shoulders that took up most of the seat. He Ran adjusted her posture, bending down to rest her head on the seat in front of her. A sudden urge stirred within her; she felt a sense of entitlement to lean on that broad shoulder, to stay there forever... They traveled almost all the way around Guangzhou before finally reaching their destination, by which time He Ran had already had a good sleep. Since settling down in the art studio after finishing the college entrance examination, He Ran has not traveled far from home much, so this trip can be considered a long journey for her. After getting off the car, He Ran followed Xiao Han for a while before realizing that they were going to a children's amusement park. Quan Quan was in high spirits and rushed straight to the carousel as soon as she entered. She rode it several times before finally stopping because she felt dizzy. They sat down to rest for a while, but before they had rested for long, Quan Quan suddenly saw something, stood up, pointed into the distance, and shouted,

"I want to sit on that big, round one!"

He Ran looked in the direction he was pointing, and the

"big and round"

thing that Quan Quan was talking about was actually a common Ferris wheel. Xiao Han was very accommodating to his requests. After resting for a few minutes, the three of them headed towards the Ferris wheel.

While queuing at the ticket counter, Xiao Han suddenly said,

"You guys sit down, I won't go up."

He Ran turned to look at him, puzzled:

"Why?"

Xiao Han gave a vague answer:

"It's nothing."

He Ran glanced at the price: 70 yuan per adult and half price for children, which wasn't expensive. She turned to look at Xiao Han and urged,

"You've come all this way, go up and take a look, the scenery should be nice."

Xiao Han shook his head and said,

"You guys can go up there."

Quan Quan tugged at his sleeve, trying her best to persuade him:

"Uncle, please stay with us and sit together."

In the end, it was the child's coaxing that worked. After much pleading, Xiao Han finally agreed. After queuing up at the entrance to have their tickets checked, He Ran and her group sat alone in a red sedan chair. It was a cloudy day, the gloomy sky seeming to foreshadow rain, but this didn't dampen the spirits of the tourists enjoying the panoramic view of Guangzhou. Slowly and quietly, the Ferris wheel reached its highest point. Quan Quan was extremely excited, her little face and hands pressed tightly against the window, gazing at the beautiful scenery outside and exclaiming in amazement. A slight breeze slipped in from the crack, bringing a touch of coolness. He Ran turned her head and quietly observed the man beside her. Even though Xiao Han didn't show any outward movement, He Ran still noticed something was wrong from the subtle clues on his face. His face was too tense and serious; this composure seemed deliberate. After observing for a moment, she whispered her guess:

"Are you afraid of heights?"

Xiao Han didn't move, but slightly turned his eyes to look at her, not denying her guess. Seeing how properly he sat, He Ran couldn't help but chuckle to herself. He was so cautious; if she were to do anything inappropriate to him here, he certainly wouldn't resist. As the thought crossed her mind, she decisively grasped his hand and lightly tickled his palm. Xiao Han didn't pull away, but simply turned his head to look at her silently, his eyes dark. He Ran became bolder, holding his hand the whole time, resting her head on his shoulder, and gently closing her eyes. Although taking advantage of someone's vulnerability is somewhat despicable and might cause rejection, she did it willingly. After the Ferris wheel completed a full rotation, they returned to their starting point. It was lunchtime when they got out of the sedan chair. They found a supermarket, and Xiao Han took Quan Quan inside to buy food, while He Ran sat under a row of parasols outside the store to wait. Suddenly, someone tapped her on the shoulder from behind. He Ran turned around and saw a familiar old face. She was a woman in her forties, and He Ran was deeply impressed by her family's signature large eyes. He Ran noticed that she was holding the hand of a little girl who looked about three or four years old. She hadn't seen her before, so perhaps she was born later. The woman smiled and said,

"It really is you. It's been a long time."

He Ran smiled politely and said,

"Long time no see."

The woman thought for a moment and said,

"Let me think... Your name is He Ran, right?"

He Ran nodded:

"That's right."

The woman then asked,

"How are you feeling now?"

He Ran replied,

"Very healthy."

The woman sincerely wished,

"That's good. I hope you stay healthy forever."

He Ran then asked,

"How is Yuanyuan?"

The woman's expression darkened, and after a while she said dejectedly,

"She...didn't make it."

As soon as the words

"didn't make it through"

came out, several images flashed through He Ran's mind: a cold, impersonal handcart and white sheets, and the sound of crying echoing in the corridor late at night. She was speechless for a few seconds, then whispered,

"I'm sorry."

The woman smiled optimistically:

"It's alright, so much time has passed, we've long since come to terms with it."

When Xiao Han returned with several servings of fried noodles, the woman was just leaving with her youngest daughter. He sat down next to He Ran, glanced at the woman's back, looked away, and casually asked,

"Who's that?"

He Ran answered evasively,

"She's the mother of a friend I know."

Xiao Han didn't ask any further questions. He pushed a plate of fried noodles in front of He Ran and handed her a pair of chopsticks.

"Have something to eat."

He Ran ate the meal in a sullen mood, glancing at Xiao Han every now and then. Xiao Han asked her,

"What's wrong?"

He Ran simply shook her head:

"It's nothing."

After a long while, He Ran called out to him again,

"Just now... you didn't think I was annoying, did you?"

She only said half of what she meant, but both of them understood that she was referring to the ambiguous little actions on the Ferris wheel. Xiao Han fed Quan Quan a bite of noodles, then turned to look at her and said something thought-provoking:

"I am afraid of heights, but I am not so afraid that I don't even have the ability to resist."

In the afternoon, I accompanied Quan Quan to play some other rides at the amusement park. The three of us had dinner outside before returning to Xiaozhou Village. A light drizzle unexpectedly began on the road from the bus stop to the archway. The rain wasn't heavy; it was just a fine mist drifting through the alleyways. They quickened their pace back to the barbershop, their hair and clothes inevitably covered in fine raindrops. Once inside, Xiao Han first wiped his face haphazardly with his hands, then helped Quan Quan wipe the water droplets from her face. After doing this, he turned to look at He Ran, who was standing to the side fixing her hair. The light rain wasn't a problem, but it was a real ordeal for people who wear glasses. The raindrops gathered frequently, densely packed, and were particularly uncomfortable blocking the lenses. Xiao Han walked up to He Ran and reached out to remove her glasses. He lifted a corner of his shirt, vaguely revealing his muscular waist beneath the T-shirt. He carefully wiped the lenses several times through the fabric, following their curves, then breathed on them before wiping them again.

After repeating this several times, Xiao Han returned the clean and bright glasses to her. He Ran reached out and took it, then looked up at him. His face was blurry in her severely nearsighted vision. She smiled at him, then put on her glasses. A light rain brought a slight chill, and to prevent Quan Quan from catching a cold again, Xiao Han immediately led him upstairs to take a hot bath. He Ran sat on the bed outside, separated by a curtain, waiting. Quan Quan cooperated well during her bath, without crying or fussing, and it was over in ten minutes. Xiao Han lifted the curtain, and Quan Quan came out wearing clean, warm pajamas. It was a children's bear pajama set, which looked very cute on Quan Quan's chubby face that hadn't yet lost its baby fat. He Ran couldn't help but want to pinch his cheeks. She had just stood up when she felt a momentary dizziness, lost her balance, and slumped to the side. Fortunately, the place was small, and Xiao Han reacted quickly, immediately reaching out to help her. He Ran barely managed to steady herself, her expression dazed. Xiao Han asked,

"What's wrong?"

After the dizziness subsided, He Ran said,

"It's nothing, I'm just a little dizzy."

He frowned slightly.

"Do you have a fever?"

As he spoke, he reached out and brushed aside her bangs, placing his thick palm on her smooth forehead. The different skin textures created an unusual friction. He felt it for a moment before withdrawing his hand and saying,

"Normal body temperature."

He Ran shook her head:

"No fever, just a little anemia."

Xiao Han said,

"You got caught in the rain too, be careful not to catch a cold. Go take a hot shower."

He Ran looked at his slightly damp shoulder and said,

"You got just as wet as me, aren't you afraid of catching a cold?"

"I'm in good health and rarely get sick."

"Don't say that, just in case."

Xiao Han said unhurriedly,

"You finish washing first, then I'll wash."

"I still need to wash my hair, what if I delay you?"

Xiao Han didn't reply. He Ran smiled and said,

"Why don't we shower together?"

Quan Quan stood aside, listening to the adults' endless argument, blinking her big eyes in utter confusion. Xiao Han looked down at him and said,

"Go dry your hair first. The hairdryer is in the first drawer of the table on the left on the first floor. I taught you how to use it, do you remember?"

"Okay!"

After receiving the instruction, Quan Quan nodded solemnly, and ran downstairs in his slippers, which were far too big for him. The small figure disappeared at the end of the stairs, and the second floor became quiet. With only a foot between her and Xiao Han, He Ran slightly tiptoed and brought her face close to his nose, so that they could feel each other's breath. Standing like this for so long was quite strenuous for He Ran, so she had to raise her arms and hang them behind Xiao Han's neck for support. She stared at him intently:

"Isn't it dangerous for Quan Quan to dry her hair all by herself?"

Xiao Han said in a low voice,

"It's okay, he will."

He Ran's lips curled up slightly.

"Then you can now check if I'm small or not?"

"..."

Perhaps it was his indulgence on the Ferris wheel that gave her confidence, or perhaps it was the cold rain that made her delirious. Before Xiao Han could answer, she had already pressed her lips to his. The season wasn't dry, but Xiao Han's lips were chapped, and the areas she touched felt slightly hard. She gently and slowly softened them little by little with her own warmth. Compared to her awkwardness the first time, she was much more skilled this time, calmly and deliberately moving around, their breaths mingling and continuing in this intertwined way, gradually becoming heavier. He Ran's glasses were taken off by Xiao Han, and she closed her eyes, her senses becoming even clearer. She couldn't tell whether she was encompassing him, or he was encompassing her. She changed her angles, trying to delve deeper and find the source of the voice that appeared in her dreams every night. Much later, He Ran gently pulled away from his lips, their foreheads touching, the warmth still lingering. Xiao Han stared at her intently, his expression deep and thoughtful. After a long silence, he finally said,

"He Ran, you are still too young."

Frowning slightly, He Ran said with displeasure,

"What did you say was small about me again?"

This time, Xiao Han directly told her:

"Age."

He Ran said,

"So what?"

Xiao Han lowered his head slightly,

"You're too young, I can't give you anything."

"How do you know what I want?"

He Ran stared back at him without moving.

"All I want is you."

She gently placed her hand on his face and sighed softly,

"Xiao Han, I'll be leaving here in a month. We don't have much time left."

Xiao Han remained silent.

---

After a while, he asked,

"Is this your first time?"

She knew he didn't mean anything by asking, so she honestly told him,

"Yes."

Xiao Han then asked,

"Are you sure you want to be with me? You won't regret it?"

He Ran said without hesitation,

"Why should I regret it?"

Her hands were soft and delicate, clearly indicating that she hadn't done much rough work. In the darkness, she carefully felt all his features like a blind person, and then concluded:

"Do you know what sexy is?"

Due to long-term physical labor, Xiao Han had developed some muscle, especially noticeable in his arms and shoulder blades. He Ran had carefully observed this area while drawing; it was firm and muscular, the most attractive part of a man's body in her opinion. She gently inhaled his scent: a faint smell of tobacco, a hint of sweat, and a touch of his own ruggedness. The combined aroma exuded a hormone that attracted her. In the end, Xiao Han stopped her again. He Ran frowned and said,

"Why are you, a man, so slow?"

Xiao Han gently squeezed her palm,

"Quan Quan is still downstairs, wait for me to coax him to sleep."

He Ran pursed her lips, thought for a long time, and finally agreed:

"Okay."

Like all children, Quan Quan needs to listen to fairy tales before bed to fall asleep. The storyteller is naturally Xiao Han, and He Ran also stands aside and listens for a while. Perhaps she had something to hide, but she felt that the children's books he read aloud in his deep voice were incredibly alluring. In the end, she went downstairs first and waited for him in the small room. Quan Quan fell asleep quickly, and Xiao Han was able to leave ten minutes later. He carried a stack of thin blankets with him when he went downstairs, in case He Ran caught a cold at night. On the small sofa bed in the inner room, two figures overlapped. He Ran's forehead was covered in a fine layer of sweat, and Xiao Han was also sweating a lot, the sweat sticking to his skin. In the darkness, they could see nothing but each other's eyes. They remained silent for a long time, neither of them falling asleep. She finally got what she wanted, but felt inexplicably empty inside. He Ran suddenly asked,

"Who have you slept in this bed with? "

"No."

"What about that bed upstairs?"

"Anyway, it'll be yours in the future."

"Your body is more comfortable, I can sleep on you."

He Ran woke up very early the next day and left on her own before Xiao Han could wake her up. They met at a fast food restaurant at noon. Xiao Han asked her why she left so early. He Ran said,

"The air was nice in the morning, so I wanted to go out for a walk. I saw that you and Quan Quan were sleeping soundly, so I didn't wake you up."

Xiao Han didn't ask any more questions. After getting their food, the two went to their usual spot and sat down to eat. He Ran glanced at the empty seat next to Xiao Han and asked,

"It's a pity, I haven't finished teaching him what I wanted to teach him to draw."

Xiao Han smiled faintly,

"It's alright, there will be other opportunities in the future."

Xiao Han and Quan Quan left Guangzhou by train that night. He Ran had dinner with them that evening, but since she had to go to class, she didn't go to the station to see them off. Han Yu has been discharged from the hospital. The day she received the news, Yang Wenping called He Ran and asked her to accompany her to the hospital to pick him up. He Ran was upset about Lu Jingbai's situation and was extremely reluctant to see Han Yu, so she made up an excuse that she didn't have time. Who would have thought that he still couldn't escape it, and the next day, Young Master Han came to his door in person. Even with a cane, his imposing presence was undiminished. A sketching class was in session, completely silent, when Han Yu kicked the door open, creating a loud noise that startled several students, causing them to drop their charcoal pencils. Perhaps it was the overwhelming condemnation in He Ran's eyes that made Han Yu's arrogant face gradually crumble. In the end, he said somewhat timidly,

"You go to class first, I'll wait for you outside."

He Ran raised her chin, signaling him to close the door. Strangely enough, Xiao Han had visited He Ran's studio several times, and no one had ever suspected their relationship. But Han Yu had only appeared once, and people below were already whispering and speculating: was this blond-haired, seemingly delinquent-looking boy He Ran's boyfriend? He Ran banged loudly on the easel and said,

"Draw quietly!"

He Ran was usually serious and reserved, and he was quite respected among the students. The group of people immediately dared not act recklessly again. After class, she took Han Yu to the cafeteria to talk, but there was no one there. He Ran's first words were,

"What are you doing here?"

With a nonchalant attitude, Han Yu said, shaking his leg,

"Just came to take a look."

"Visit?"

He Ran frowned almost imperceptibly.

"What are you doing here, a broadcasting student, visiting my art studio?"

Han Yu said,

"My broadcasting teacher said I might not be suited for this path, so I'm now considering whether to switch to fine arts."

Hearing his explanation, He Ran couldn't help but laugh inwardly. Aside from his glamorous appearance, which fits the standards of a broadcasting major, Han Yu's hoarse voice and lack of professionalism are simply outrageous. I wonder how much money his father will have to spend to get him into a good university this time. His consideration of

"changing careers"

was prescient, but He Ran didn't agree with it at all. If Han Yu were to study art, he would definitely have to come to the art studio for intensive training, and she didn't want to be with him every day. So He Ran said against her will,

"I think your voice is quite good, and it has a unique quality. If you keep practicing, you'll definitely be fine."

"Really?"

Hearing her say that, Young Master Han was indeed happy. He thought for a few seconds and said,

"Then I'll discuss it with my dad for a while longer."

He Ran nodded in agreement,

"Very good."

Unexpectedly, Han Yu's expression suddenly changed, and he said coldly,

"What's so good about it! Do you think I don't know what you're up to? You just want me to stay far away from you, don't you?!"

He Ran had to admit she was a little surprised; how had Han Yu's mind suddenly become so sharp? She didn't want to beat around the bush, so she laid it all out and said,

"I really don't want to see you. If you know that, why did you come?"

Han Yu was so angry that he threw his cane aside, stepped forward, grabbed her chin, and said indignantly,

"Your mother is determined to marry you into our family. Aren't you going to disappoint her too much with your performance?"

He Ran shook off his hand.

"Don't try to pressure me with those words. You don't like me anyway, so why make yourself sick?"

"I don't like you? That's not necessarily true."

He crossed his legs and said casually,

"It's strange, I've actually been missing you a little lately."

He Ran felt a chill run down her spine from his words. Just as she was about to retort, a student ran in and said to her,

"Teacher He, someone is looking for you outside."

He Ran nodded and said,

"I understand."

She couldn't be bothered to pay any more attention to Han Yu, and walked straight past him towards the studio door. Outside the iron gate, a tall, slender figure stood in the night wind. He wore a dark gray T-shirt and faded jeans, and his face was bathed in an orange glow under the streetlight. Xiao Han looked travel-worn, with a bulging bag on his back, as if he had just gotten off a train. He Ran walked up to him and stopped. Standing across the threshold, he looked at her and said,

"I've arrived. I came to let you know."

## Chapter Four: Indefinite Term

Previous page: The sunlight stretched Xiao Han's shadow infinitely long. He stood there motionless, and even without turning around, he could be seen. In the passenger seat, Xiao Han rested his chin on his hand and let out a soft yawn. Outside the window, a bicycle rang its bell and sped past, its frame barely touching the ground. A little while later, several pedestrians chased and played, chatting and laughing as they ran past. Xiao Han finally couldn't help but turn around and say to He Ran,

"You're driving too slowly."

He Ran remained focused on what was ahead, only glancing at him out of the corner of her eye, and asked,

"Is it slow? I don't think so."

Xiao Han said,

"It's really slow."

He Ran glanced down at the dashboard: 10 km/h.

"..."

She stubbornly maintained her original speed, saying,

"Isn't it better if I drive slower? If I accidentally scratch the car, you won't be able to explain it to your friend."

Xiao Han said,

"No need to be so cautious. There are few cars here, so you can drive faster."

He Ran was skeptical, but in the end she listened to him and pressed the accelerator a little harder, bringing the speed up to 20 km/h. After driving for a while, Xiao Han finally couldn't help but suggest,

"Let me drive, you can take a look."

He Ran calmly said

"Oh,"

and did as he said. The car slowly came to a stop on the small road in front of the Provincial Hospital of Traditional Chinese Medicine. He Ran opened the car door, and the two switched places and fastened their seat belts. Xiao Han gripped the steering wheel with both hands, lightly pressed the accelerator with his foot, and applied a little force, mimicking He Ran's speed of 20 km/h. He turned to her and said,

"That was your speed just now."

He Ran felt a little embarrassed. She hadn't really noticed how slow it was when she was driving, but now that she was in the passenger seat, she realized how slow it was. She was also grateful that Xiao Han had been patient enough to drive her for such a long distance. She said,

"You drive, I'll watch."

Xiao Han gradually accelerated, eventually maintaining a stable speed of around 50 km/h. He Ran rolled the window down to its lowest position, turned her head to look outside, the speed was just right, the night breeze gently caressed her face and neck, unhurried and unperturbed. She closed her eyes contentedly and shifted her neck into a comfortable position. Seeing that He Ran hadn't moved for a while, Xiao Han turned his head to look at her,

"Are you here to practice driving or to sleep?"

The warm breeze was intoxicating, and He Ran said lazily,

"You drive, I don't want to move. It's nice to just take a drive."

Xiao Han simply shut his mouth and let her be. They drove around the university town. Xiao Han drove very steadily. There weren't many cars on the road at this time, and he hardly braked except at red lights. The van's shock absorption was too low, and the vehicle bounced around slightly. Surprisingly, He Ran found it quite comfortable. She slowly turned over and quietly. If Xiao Han's features were viewed individually, they would only be considered ordinary and mediocre, but when put together, his face was quite angular and had a deep contour. Such an appearance may not be memorable at first glance, but if you stare at it for a long time and appreciate it carefully, you will have a different feeling. This pleasing feature is especially prominent when viewed from the side. He Ran's gaze finally fell on the hands gripping the steering wheel, and the abruptly severed finger. Because those hands had touched her body, and even though they were missing a part, they were still flexible and gentle, He Ran had deeply felt their warmth and strength, so she no longer found them strange or terrifying. She finally asked the question that had been on her mind for so long:

"Xiao Han, how did you hurt your thumb?"

As if he hadn't heard her question, Xiao Han looked ahead, neither saying a word nor looking at her, but He Ran noticed that he blinked. After a long while, she reached out her hand to him and waved it in front of his eyes twice. Xiao Han barely dodged,

"What's going on? I'm driving."

He Ran said,

"Stop the car."

"What are you doing?"

"I told you to stop the car."

Xiao Han glanced at her, then slowly reduced the car's speed and stopped in a secluded spot. He Ran rolled up the car window to make sure the entire space was enclosed. Then she unbuckled her seatbelt, crouched down, and climbed onto Xiao Han, settling in. The steering wheel was against her back, making it a bit cramped. Seemingly sensing her intention, Xiao Han frowned and said,

"Don't be ridiculous."

He Ran smiled and said,

"I won't do anything excessive, I'll just ask you a few questions."

Xiao Han thought for a few seconds and probably acquiesced. He moved his seat back a little, making the space slightly more spacious. He Ran wrapped her arms around his neck and kissed him before asking any questions. She felt like she was now infatuated with this feeling, like she had become addicted to something she couldn't break, and only the man in front of her could save her. He Ran's head kept hitting the roof of the car, and then Xiao Han quietly placed his hand on the back of her head. Perhaps due to the lack of air circulation, the temperature inside the car gradually rose, making it increasingly difficult for them to breathe. "How many women have you been with before?"

"Who are you helping? A man or a woman?"

Xiao Han said without looking at her,

"A woman."

He Ran simply said

"Oh"

and didn't ask any further questions. My second cousin got engaged. The man is the son of a well-known real estate tycoon, making him a good match for my second cousin. The two met through a matchmaker last month and officially got engaged this month. The engagement ceremony was held in a private mansion and was not open to the media or the public. As a collateral relative of the woman, He Ran naturally couldn't be absent. When she left Xiaozhou Village, she only told Xiao Han that she had some things to take care of at home and needed to go back for a day or two. Xiao Han didn't suspect anything and didn't ask any further questions.

---

That night, He Ran went home to sleep, and the next morning she set off with He Jin and Yang Wenping. Most of those attending the engagement are close relatives of both the man and woman, and they are already familiar with each other. Some of them had heard about the scandal of their second cousin's affair with her hired driver a few days ago, while others were still in the dark. But at this moment, everyone chose to keep quiet. After the emcee finished his remarks, the two brides-to-be and grooms-to-be came out from behind the stage. They held hands, looking like a perfect couple, but neither of them smiled. My second cousin was clearly not in good spirits; even her heavy makeup couldn't hide her pale complexion. This was He Ran's first time meeting her future brother-in-law. Like most children from wealthy families she had met, he was handsome and distinguished. Of course, what lay beneath that exterior was anyone's guess. He Ran's mother was vain by nature, and at this moment she couldn't help but secretly compare herself to others. She covered He Ran's face and kept whispering,

"Han Yu's family is better off than that man's. When you get engaged, it has to be more glamorous than your sisters'."

"How many carats is that diamond? It doesn't look that big."

"Go and chat with your future brother-in-law later. I think his brothers are all quite handsome."

He Ran remained uninterested throughout, only half-heartedly responding to Yang Wenping's words. Before the ceremony was over, she excused herself to go to the restroom and left. Outside the main gate of the villa is a flat, large lawn, and the courtyard is quiet, lush and green. The sun shines brightly, the sky is clear and blue, and it's a peaceful afternoon. He Ran took a deep breath; this was the kind of place a person should be. In the distance, two or three gardeners were slowly pushing lawnmowers across the lawn. Their diligent figures were exactly like Xiao Han's image in her mind, and He Ran couldn't help but smile. At this time yesterday, she and Xiao Han were sitting on the bus from the park back to Xiaozhou Village. She was still wearing a loose T-shirt and denim shorts with paint still on them, along with a pair of flip-flops that she had bought herself to match Xiao Han's. Now, she is wearing Lanvin's new summer collection, a simple off-white dress, and designer high heels, a very proper and conservative outfit for a high-society socialite. He Ran thought that if she appeared in front of Xiao Han now looking like this, he definitely wouldn't recognize her. Considering that she had been outside for quite a while, it was time for He Ran to go back into the lobby. As she turned around to go inside, her vision suddenly caught sight of several figures entangled and wrestling in the direction of the side door a few dozen meters away. She squinted, stared intently for a moment, then walked over there. Outside the iron gate, several security guards subdued a man on the ground. The man had several bruises on his face, indicating that he had already been beaten. The man seemed to have lost his mind; despite being beaten so badly, he was still unwilling to give up, constantly wailing,

"Let me in! I want to see her!"

He Ran had met the man once before, and after a few glances, she confirmed that he was her second cousin's driver. But this time, he seemed strangely familiar, though she couldn't quite place who he resembled. He Ran looked down at the man and said softly,

"Stop making a fuss, she won't see you."

The man ignored him and continued repeating,

These words enraged the man. He lunged forward and grabbed He Ran's leg, crying and shouting,

"You're her sister, aren't you? Please, please let them let me in! I want to see her. I have something very important to tell her!"

He Ran looked down at him, her gaze calm yet filled with pity:

" What can you do even if you see her? Can you take her away? The matter is already settled, so don't try to sway her any further."

A bucket of cold water was poured over him, and the man instantly lost all strength. He slowly released He Ran's legs, sat on the ground with a blank look in his eyes, staring into the distance. After a moment, He Ran took a step back and said softly,

"Go back now, and don't come looking for me again."

After he finished speaking, they noticed he had multiple wounds on his body; the security guards had gone too far. He Ran thought for a few seconds and said,

"Call your family to come pick you up. We'll cover your medical expenses."

The man nodded listlessly. Half an hour later, He Ran witnessed just how small the world was. The man called his cousin, whom He Ran also recognized—it was the fat man. In that instant, He Ran realized why both of them had seemed familiar to her. What concerned her even more was yet to come. The fat man hurriedly got out of a dilapidated van, and He Ran was even more familiar with the person who drove him there—it was Xiao Han. The fat man ran straight over here. He didn't see He Ran at first. He helped the limp person up and said with a pained look,

"Why are you causing trouble again, my little brother? Can't you just stay put for a moment?"

The man, who had been sitting half-dead, started making a scene again when he saw his relative. Tears streaming down his face, he pleaded,

"Brother, please help me. Let me go in and see her one last time. Please!"

He Ran stood silently to the side, observing without saying a word. Until a figure walked up to her, enveloping her in his shadow. Half an hour ago, He Ran was wondering if Xiao Han would recognize her in her current attire. Half an hour later, fate couldn't wait to create this unexpected event to confirm her guess. Xiao Han's gaze fell on her face calmly and indifferently. He just stared at her, seemingly oblivious to the chaos around him. She had clearly been recognized. He Ran didn't know what to say. Should she ask him,

"Am I pretty?"

At this moment, the fat man also looked up and saw her. His younger brother was still clinging to him and wouldn't leave. The fat man was so entangled that he couldn't get away. When he saw He Ran, he paused for a moment and asked in confusion,

"Little He? What are you doing here?"

After a second, he asked again,

"Why...why are you dressed like this?"

He Ran said casually,

"I'm here to attend the wedding."

The fat man gave a blank

"oh,"

but he couldn't care less about anything else at this point. He looked at He Ran, then at the stern-faced security guards beside her, and made a pleading gesture, saying,

"Do you know the owner of this house? Could you do me a favor and let my brother go in and take a look? He won't give up until he sees it."

He Ran shook her head, her voice carrying an almost cold distance:

"No, absolutely not."

Two nights later, He Ran accompanied Xiao Han and some of his friends to have a late-night snack at Fatty's stall. The fat man was easygoing and didn't develop any rift with He Ran after that incident. However, his other friends kept teasing He Ran after hearing about it. One person said,

"Little He, I really didn't expect that. He's usually so quiet and doesn't say a word, and he even follows Brother Xiao around being frugal. Turns out he's a real hidden talent."

Another person chimed in,

"That's right. Your family is so well-off, how could your parents bear to let you come to a place like this to suffer?"

Xiao Ding patted Xiao Han on the shoulder and teased,

"Brother Xiao, you have a good eye for talent! You found such a good wife, you won't have to worry for the rest of your life. Don't forget us when you become successful, hahaha."

Their jokes went unanswered. He Ran glanced at Xiao Han out of the corner of her eye. The latter drank his beer in silence. The dishes in front of him hadn't been touched since they were served, and even his favorite oysters were ignored. Finally, the fat guy stepped in and waved to break the awkward silence:

"Alright, alright, there's a limit to jokes. Do you think Brother Xiao is the kind of guy who lives off a woman?"

After the late-night snack, He Ran and Xiao Han left first. Tonight, the moon was obscured by dark clouds, and everything was hazy in the gray fog. When they walked through a narrow alley where they couldn't see, Xiao Han walked ahead, and He Ran followed the sound of his footsteps crunching on the sand to find their way. Xiao Han's footsteps were steady and rhythmic, pausing and tumbling, until he reached home. The two didn't see eye to eye on the way home, and when they got back, Xiao Han went straight upstairs to the kitchen. He Ran hadn't eaten much; she couldn't have beer or barbecue, and her stomach was still empty. Xiao Han made her a bowl of noodles. Xiao Han himself hadn't eaten much either, so he made enough for two. The second floor was too cramped, so they ate on the first floor. The noodles were fresh out of the pot and a little hot to the touch. Xiao Han was thick-skinned and could withstand the heat, but He Ran couldn't. Xiao Han placed an old newspaper on the table so she could put her bowl on the table to eat. While Xiao Han was doing these things, He Ran's gaze followed him the whole time. The small building was completely silent, the atmosphere as still as a scene from a depressing and obscure old movie. Finally, she spoke first:

"Are you angry?"

Xiao Han replied indifferently,

"No."

He Ran continued,

"Don't you have anything you want to ask me?"

Xiao Han replied,

"No."

He Ran took the chopsticks Xiao Han handed her and said,

"You didn't ask me about my family before, so this doesn't count as hiding anything."

Xiao Han turned to look at her. Although he didn't say anything, He Ran understood his meaning from his eyes. He Ran said,

"Don't overthink it."

Her tone carried a rare hint of seriousness:

"Regardless of what happened in the past or in the future, I'm with you now simply because I like you."

After a moment of eye contact, Xiao Han moved his dry lips and finally nodded slowly,

"I understand."

He used his chopsticks to stir the noodles in her bowl to let the steam dissipate.

"Let's not talk about this now, eat."

Having said what she needed to say, He Ran was actually quite hungry, so she picked up her chopsticks and started eating. A bowl of clear broth noodles, with no seasonings other than salt, tasted bland and tasteless, yet the two of them ate it with great relish. On a hot summer night, after a satisfying night, He Ran lay weakly in Xiao Han's arms, her eyelids drooping and not wanting to move at all. The warmth of their bodies filled the small room.

---

After a while, He Ran scratched his Adam's apple with her fingertip and softly called out,

"Xiao Han."

Xiao Han felt a little itchy and grabbed her hand.

"Hmm?"

He Ran followed behind him and whispered,

"Didn't she see you standing next to me?"

"I didn't realize you were so strong."

He Ran burst into laughter. The university town is covered by a large area of ​​green belts, and the dense forests form the most secluded and hidden natural barriers. As night fell, He Ran said,

Xiao Han was pulled into a grove of trees behind him by her. He was puzzled at first, but when they stopped deep in the woods and He Ran reached her hand toward his jeans, he realized what she was going to do. He Ran didn't stop. She raised her lips and touched his firm jaw:

"Have you tried this before?"

Xiao Han subtly lowered his brow.

"No."

"Aman said you were very experienced, is that a lie?"

Xiao Han glanced at her and said,

"You're too bold."

He Ran smiled and said,

"It's alright, no one will come here."

My back pressed against the trunk of the bald cypress, and I slid down slowly. Even through my clothes, I could feel the rough texture of the bark, and my skin felt a slight pain from the friction. Xiao Han, mindful of his surroundings, initially refused to cooperate. He Ran, however, refused to let go, striking at vital points with every move, saying,

He Ran opened her mouth as instructed, and Xiao Han leaned closer to look. A tiny white dot was sticking out from the very back of her left gum, though it wasn't very noticeable. Xiao Han said in a tone of someone who had been through it all,

"It's okay, they're just growing teeth."

Hearing him say that, He Ran didn't think much of it and went back to bed to continue sleeping. Xiao Han called her several times but couldn't wake her, so he had no choice but to go out to work by himself. He brought her lunch at noon, but He Ran's tooth still hurt a bit, so she ate a few bites slowly. Then, out of the corner of her eye, she noticed an envelope that Xiao Han had placed on the table. He Ran stood up, walked over, picked up the envelope, and looked at it. The address on the envelope was his hometown in Chongqing. It should contain the painting she had drawn for Quan Quan a while ago, which Xiao Han had been procrastinating on sending for so long. He Ran glanced at the words on the envelope again; this was the first time she had seen Xiao Han's handwriting. It was neat and square, each stroke precise and powerful, vertical lines distinct and horizontal lines clear. Xiao Han snatched the envelope from her hand, turned it over, and slammed it on the table, trying to cover up his blunder:

"My handwriting is ugly."

He Ran smiled and said,

"It is a bit ugly."

She returned to the small square table to eat, and Xiao Han sat down next to her, watching her eat. He Ran took a few bites, then stopped and asked him,

"Didn't you eat?"

Xiao Han said,

"I ate it."

"Then why are you staring at me like that?"

"..."

Xiao Han looked away and asked after a while,

"Are you coming to the park with me this afternoon?"

He Ran smiled enigmatically:

"You want me to follow you that much?"

Xiao Han said,

"No."

He Ran pouted and said,

"Then I won't go."

Xiao Han opened his mouth, but hesitated to speak. After finishing her meal, He Ran said,

"I'm going home for the next few days, so I probably won't be coming back here."

After listening, Xiao Han remained silent for a moment, then slowly nodded.

"Okay."

The consequences of turning off her phone and refusing to answer calls on her birthday were severe, especially from Han Yu. The thought of his furious outburst annoyed He Ran, so she decided to go all in and turn off her phone for several days in a row. Unable to find He Ran, Han Yu naturally contacted Yang Wenping instead. Every night, Yang Wenping would go to He Ran's bedside and patiently persuade her:

"You, so busy, so busy, what are you busy with all day long! You two haven't even gone out to play much during your summer vacation! Soon you'll be going to Beijing, and you'll have even fewer chances to see each other. How can you not seize the opportunity now?"

He Ran hugged a pillow and said indifferently,

"Let's talk about it in a few days. My second cousin just left, and I'm not in the mood."

Yang Wenping was silent for a while, then said earnestly,

"Don't end up like your second cousin. You've seen what happened to her."

One sentence made He Ran's mind even more chaotic, and she stopped responding to whatever Yang Wenping said after that. My second cousin's funeral was held at the end of August. Han Yu also attended the funeral that day. He appeared in a black suit with eight buttons neatly fastened, a rare sight for him. The eldest son of the Han family made a grand entrance, with two rows of black-clad bodyguards following behind him, clearly showing the air of someone following in his father's footsteps. Everyone treated him with utmost respect, except for He Ran, who kept her distance and wished she could stay as far away from him as possible. However, before He Ran could even leave the scene after the funeral, Han Yu angrily dragged her into his car. His leg injury hadn't fully healed, but his limp made him surprisingly fast in chasing after her. He Ran sat expressionless in the closed back seat of the car. She knew she could only hide for a moment, but she accepted it. Han Yu glared at her fiercely:

"Hide, hide, hide! Let's see where you can hide to!"

The driver dropped them off at the entrance of a high-end club, where Han Yu's bandmates had been waiting for some time in the billiards room. Among them was a new face. When Han Yu sat down on the sofa and pulled the girl into his arms, He Ran realized that she was his new girlfriend. Han Yu has had many girlfriends, from innocent to alluring, but none of them were kind-hearted and lacked discernment. They often treated He Ran like a waitress, just like Han Yu did, without knowing what their relationship was. Young people's gatherings naturally involve alcohol. Of course, they won't drink beer in such places. Young Master Han was treating everyone to a meal, and he was incredibly generous, ordering two bottles of French Cognac brandy. The band members clapped excitedly, their eyes shining:

"We're in for a treat today!"

The pool hall was dimly lit, with a faint blue hue, and the sound of the pool balls clashing was exceptionally crisp and loud. Everyone was accompanied by a beautiful woman; He Ran was the one who stood out. Han Yu sat on the sofa in the darkest spot in the room, watching the game. He Ran sat next to him and his female companion. When Han Yu reached his hand under the girl's skirt, He Ran chose to ignore it. The two people beside her made intermittent noises. He Ran yawned, but Han Yu noticed it.

"So sleepy? Have some wine to perk yourself up,"

Han Yu said, picking up his empty wine glass and handing it to her. The girl was clearly dissatisfied, complaining in her sweet voice about his lack of focus. Han Yu was clearly not the type to lower himself to coax a girl; he simply frowned and sent her away. The girl clearly didn't understand Han Yu's temper. She shoved him away, swore, and stomped off in her high heels, her face dark. With such an insensible attitude, Han Yu wouldn't be coming to her again. Han Yu naturally vented his resentment at being scolded on He Ran, while the glass of brandy with its transparent sheen remained in its original position in front of her. Han Yu said succinctly,

"Drink."

He Ran remained motionless. Han Yu raised an eyelid and said through gritted teeth,

"I've put up with you for a long time. You don't think you can get away with this so easily today, do you?"

He Ran calmly stated,

"I cannot drink alcohol."

Han Yu sneered,

"Heh, so what would you like to drink?"

He Ran said,

"It's okay to drink that weird concoction you made last time."

Han Yu maintained his forced smile:

"Sorry, I'm not in the mood for cooking today."

He Ran pursed her lips slightly and remained silent for a long while. Han Yu's gaze shifted downwards. He Ran was wearing a dignified black dress today, with her lower legs encased in dark black stockings. Her shoes were also black and plain. Her face was delicate and elegant, with no expression, and paired with such clothing, she looked like a sacred nun. The more sacred something is, the more people want to desecrate it. Han Yu slowly poured the expensive brandy onto her dress, saying,

"You are a sinner and do not deserve to wear such clothes."

He Ran looked at him, puzzled. Han Yu's eyes narrowed:

"Am I wrong? You have half a life on your shoulders."

He Ran's eyes darkened.

"Han Yu, don't talk nonsense."

This look only elicited a soft chuckle from Han Yu, his tone dripping with sarcasm:

"What are you trying to explain? I saw it all with my own eyes, at that stairwell..."

He Ran stood up, interrupting him, and said as calmly as possible,

"I'm going to change my clothes."

He Ran didn't emerge from that upscale club until four in the morning. Exhaustion was an understatement to describe her state; she wished she could just sleep with the other drunk people staggering around in the room. The alcohol made her dizzy, and her blood sugar seemed to be dropping; she felt a little short of breath. Standing on the deserted street, leaning against a lamppost to keep her balance, He Ran took out her phone from her handbag and glanced at the time. August 31st. Before she knew it, it was already the last day of August. She would be going to Beijing soon. In the other lane, a taxi turned and drove towards them. He Ran waved,

"Driver, are you going to Xiaozhou Village?"

When the car stopped at the Xiaozhou Village intersection, He Ran had already taken a nap and had finally regained some strength. After paying the driver and thanking him, she got out of the car. He Ran was wearing a tight-fitting short skirt borrowed from the club's girls; it was undeniably revealing, and the pattern on her bra was even visible through the thin fabric. Luckily, there was no one on the street at this time, otherwise she might have been dragged into an alley by some scoundrel. Reaching the barbershop, He Ran used the spare key Xiao Han had given her to open the door. She moved very quietly upstairs, and the person on the bed slept soundly, completely undisturbed. Xiao Han, a bachelor, is usually quite casual about things. In the summer, he'll shower and come out wearing only his underwear. He's even more relaxed when he's home alone, lying on the bed completely naked without a blanket, just to stay cool. It was six o'clock, early morning. He Ran walked to the bedside, bent down and leaned close to him, gently blowing a breath into his ear. The person on the bed shuddered, then slowly opened their eyes. He Ran patted his body and said,

"Move over a bit, make room for me."

The next second she was lying on the bed, pinned down by him. He Ran, with drooping eyelids and listless, said,

"Don't do it, I haven't slept all night, I'm so sleepy."

Xiao Han kissed her on the lips, but received no response. He Ran half-pushed and half-refused, her body weak and powerless. He tentatively explored her body with his hands, but He Ran was too tired to be interested.

He Ran cupped his face in her hands, her fingers tracing his lower eyelids, and breathed slowly. She murmured softly,

"Xiao Han, Xiao Han."

"Um."

She lay down beside him, burying her head in the crook of his shoulder and inhaling deeply for a moment. Besides the faint smell of sweat, there seemed to be a hint of her own scent mixed in, a scent conveyed through their prolonged skin-to-skin contact.

"Xiao Han, let's talk for a while before we go to sleep."

"Um."

---

That night, He Ran inexplicably wanted to talk to him a little longer, to talk about topics they had never touched upon before.

"Xiao Han, did you once have a woman you deeply loved?"

He Ran sensed his breathing pause for a few seconds.

"Who told you that?"

If we don't deny it, then it definitely happened.

"I'm guessing, both Oman and the fat man mentioned it."

After a long pause, Xiao Han said, avoiding the main point,

"It happened a long time ago."

Her hand slid down and finally covered his left palm.

"Is the injury on your thumb because of her?"

The

"hmm"

was answered slowly and softly, as if from another world. He Ran said,

"Tell me about your situation."

Xiao Han lit a cigarette. He thought for a long time before finally opening up. It's actually a rather cliché story, but it really happened to many people. Twenty-year-old Xiao Han was introduced to a girl from the next village by his family. The girl was very beautiful, and all the young men for miles around admired her. But she only paid attention to Xiao Han. The two gradually developed feelings, and eventually they naturally became engaged. The girl's family was slightly wealthier than Xiao Han's, able to afford to send her to university in another province, while Xiao Han continued to work in the county. Later, Xiao Han earned enough money and went to the city to find the girl. Absence makes the heart grow fonder, and their feelings for each other did not diminish. They often squeezed together on a small bed, dreaming about their future life together. Xiao Han had planned to use all his savings to marry her, and had even paid the deposit for the house. However, he suddenly received devastating news from home: his brother had been diagnosed with cancer. With a life at stake, there was no time to lose. Xiao Han had no choice but to cancel the house purchase and use the money for his brother's treatment. Later, Xiao Han returned to his hometown to care for his brother and accompany him through the last year of his life. The mutation happened that year. When Xiao Han returned to the city with less than a hundred yuan left and a newborn baby in his arms to find the girl again, everything had changed. The girl had become the mistress of a wealthy businessman. Xiao Han, unwilling to give up, went to find her. The girl, indecisive by nature, maintained a lingering connection with him. Later, the wealthy businessman discovered this and flew into a rage. Fearing repercussions, the girl quickly severed ties. The businessman had Xiao Han severely beaten, leaving him bedridden for half a month. He never went to see her again after that. Xiao Han was unwilling to recall things too deeply, and many details were only mentioned briefly. Only he knew about the hardships and setbacks he had endured. After hearing this, He Ran couldn't help but pour cold water on it:

"Are you out of your mind? She's already cheated on you, why are you still going to see her?"

Xiao Han remained silent, perhaps silently acknowledging her assessment. After a long pause, He Ran finally asked,

"Do you still love her now?"

After finishing his cigarette, Xiao Han said calmly,

"There's no such thing as love or hate, it's just an experience."

These words, however, made He Ran feel bitter. She patted his face and whispered,

"You will meet a better woman. You deserve it."

Xiao Han's lips twitched slightly, as if he wanted to say something, but in the end he closed his mouth. In the latter half of the night, when He Ran was half asleep, she felt the person next to her tossing and turning. She called his name softly a few times, with a hint of reproach, and he finally quieted down.

---

The next morning, He Ran intended to leave quietly, but Xiao Han got up even earlier than her. When she got dressed and got out of bed, Xiao Han had already prepared a large bowl of hot noodles for her. Breakfast was plentiful, with noodles and several freshly stir-fried side dishes. He Ran usually has no appetite in the morning, but today she was very considerate and finished all the noodles and stir-fried dishes, but she didn't touch the steamed oysters. Xiao Han picked up a piece for her and said,

"Try it."

He Ran wrinkled her nose and waved her hand:

"It smells fishy, ​​I don't like it. "

Xiao Han then withdrew his chopsticks and didn't insist. After the two had breakfast together, Xiao Han saw her off. They strolled side by side to the bus stop at the intersection. He Ran stopped and said,

"This is enough."

"Okay."

Xiao Han took her bag down and handed it to her. A bus slowly drove towards us and stopped in front of the bus stop. Several people lined up to board the bus. He Ran turned to him, smiled, and said,

"I'm leaving."

Xiao Han's lips moved a few times. Looking back, He Ran felt that he wanted to say something, but she spoke first:

"Goodbye."

Those two words sealed away some unspoken words from Xiao Han's mouth. Finally, he waved to her and said,

"Goodbye."

At that moment, He Ran recalled a scene from the movie they had watched together,

"The Legend of 1900"

—we said goodbye with smiles, but knew deep down that goodbye was a long way off. He will not repeat the mistake of the past, and she is no longer the girl who would have any lingering ties with him. He Ran got on the car and sat in the window seat. The sunlight stretched Xiao Han's shadow infinitely long. He stood there motionless, and even without turning around, he could be seen. As the car drove forward, amidst the swirling sand and dust, that figure gradually receded into the distance. If only time could stop at this moment, that would be the best ending.

## Chapter Five: The Youngest Son

Previous page: The gazes exchanged between lovers are never enough. In the past six months, her hair has grown quite a bit, almost reaching her chest. Her figure is still petite, but she is gradually revealing the charm of a mature woman. In her second month in Beijing, He Ran's toothache returned. At night, she lay in her bed, clutching her cheek which throbbed intermittently, unable to sleep a wink. This past month has been filled with school registration, freshman military training, and then the busy search for housing. The hectic days of September have passed, and life is gradually returning to normal. He Ran currently lives in a rented apartment near the school. She doesn't like living in a cramped dormitory because it's too noisy, and it's quieter to live alone. The room is quite nice, a one-bedroom, one-living room, one-bathroom apartment, costing four thousand yuan a month. It faces south and is warm in winter and cool in summer. The landlords are from Chongqing, a middle-aged couple. They are not young, but they have a lot of energy. They argue every night, but make up the next morning. They live right across from He Ran, separated by a wall, and the soundproofing is barely adequate. He Ran was already suffering from a toothache, and having to listen to other people arguing every night made it almost impossible for her to sleep. Several times she regretted moving here, but considering that the couple seemed nice, and that she had already paid three months' rent, she decided to stay for the time being. At the end of the month, He Ran went to the hospital for a follow-up examination. Although she was alone in another province, Yang Wenping had already helped her contact a highly qualified traditional Chinese medicine expert here. She has to have regular check-ups every month, and if there are any abnormalities in her body, she has to take a lot of medication to regulate it. Fortunately, her condition has been very stable, and apart from occasional back and leg pain at night, she is otherwise fine. October. Han Yu visited Beijing during the National Day holiday. While waiting for him at the airport, He Ran received a warning text message from Yang Wenping:

"Take Xiao Yu on a good trip this time, and don't make him lose his temper again!"

He Ran replied casually,

"Okay."

I put my phone back in my bag and looked up to see Young Master Han, dressed in a cool outfit, wearing sunglasses and dragging a large suitcase, walking towards me from the end of the hall. He Ran waved the sign with the words

"Han Yu"

on it listlessly. When he walked up to her, she gave him a forced smile:

"Beijing welcomes you."

In fact, what Yang Wenping said was not entirely unreasonable. He Ran also felt that perhaps she should ease the tension between her and Han Yu. It would not do her any good to always go against him. So this time, she adopted the attitude of a host and warmly received him. After asking several native Beijingers in her class for recommendations on fun places to visit, He Ran devised a travel itinerary. On the first day, they visited the Forbidden City and the Summer Palace.

---

The next day, they went for a stroll around the Bird's Nest and Water Cube. On the third day, they went to see a Peking Opera performance. On the last day of the holiday, He Ran took Han Yu to climb the Badaling Great Wall. There's a saying among the tour guides here:

"He who has not climbed the Great Wall is no true man; the further he climbs, the more foolish he becomes."

He Ran and Han Yu were the

"idiots"

they were talking about. Under the blazing sun, the weather was scorching hot, and the surface of the stone steps seemed to be shrouded in rising white smoke. Driven by sheer stubbornness, the two continued climbing, their backs soaked with sweat, their clothes clinging to their bodies in a semi-transparent state.

---

Later, He Ran was too exhausted to climb any further, so they took the cable car back to the ground. After exiting the station, He Ran bought an ice cream from a stall, while Han Yu followed behind her, exhausted. After paying, He Ran took a spoonful of ice cream and ate it herself, then scooped another spoonful and handed it to Han Yu, asking,

"Want some?"

Her gesture carried a hint of testing the waters. Han Yu stared at her as if he'd seen a ghost. From the very first day he arrived in Beijing, this woman had been acting strangely. No matter how much he tried to pick a fight, she always responded with a gentle and persuasive attitude. He was starting to wonder if she had become a completely different person. Seeing that Han Yu didn't react, He Ran asked again,

"Aren't you going to eat?"

"..."

Filled with suspicion, Han Yu finally opened his mouth and swallowed the nearly melted ice cream. Even though this picky young master never ate anything that other people had touched. He Ran subtly curled the corners of her lips, it seemed that his brief confession to her back in junior high school still held true. Han Yu, you really haven't improved at all. Han Yu was taking a 9 a.m. flight from Beijing the next morning. After finishing his day's activities, he suggested visiting He Ran's place. He Ran hesitated for a moment, then agreed. The two stepped out of the elevator and ran into their landlord couple, who were about to go for a walk. He Ran had never brought a boy home before, so the couple was a little surprised. They looked Han Yu up and down and asked with a smile,

"A classmate?"

He Ran smiled politely and replied,

"Friend."

The couple nodded knowingly; usually, boyfriends are also called friends. He Ran took out her keys, opened the door, led Han Yu inside, and poured him a cup of hot water. Han Yu looked around and said,

"This place is too small."

He Ran curled her lip dismissively,

"Of course it can't compare to your house."

If he went to her university dormitory, he would think this place was already quite spacious. Han Yu asked again,

"Where will I sleep tonight?"

He Ran raised her eyelids:

"Did I say I wanted you to stay overnight?"

Han Yu choked for a moment, his face turning pale. He said,

"Since you didn't refuse when I said I'd come to your house, why are you still acting coy now?"

He Ran said expressionlessly,

"The fact that I let you in only means that I don't hate you that much anymore."

Han Yu stood up, about to get angry, when He Ran said calmly,

"Han Yu, you can't rush things."

She looked up at him and said slowly,

"What you did to Lu Jingbai, what you did to me, and what your girlfriends did to me, do you think I could be so kind as to forgive and forget and start a relationship with you in the blink of an eye?... If I could, then I would definitely have ulterior motives."

"..."

After a long pause, Han Yu sat down on the sofa without any temper and said sullenly,

"I know."
---
