In truth, He Ran had long been aware that as long as Han Yu continued to pursue her relentlessly, and as long as Yang Wenping remained persistent, it was only a matter of time before she and Han Yu got together. But she also needed more time to convince herself, at least not now.

---

The next morning, He Ran saw off Han Yu, who looked terrible. This was the first time the young master Han had ever slept on a makeshift bed on the floor since birth, and he must have had a profound experience of life in the countryside. In November, the university held a painting competition, open to all formats. Participation in such activities could earn academic credits, so He Ran casually submitted one of her recently completed oil paintings. In the vibrant summer, an endless sea of ​​flowers blooms, with bees and butterflies dancing in the air. A man holding long shears stands in the painting, his head bowed, only half of his face blurred in profile. His well-defined muscles flow beneath his rolled-up sleeves, and he is one with nature. He Ran named the painting "He Stands Amidst the Splendor of Summer Flowers". The competition results were delayed, but a gallery owner somehow contacted He Ran, hoping to buy the painting for exhibition in his gallery and even collaborate long-term. The offer was very high, a rare opportunity for a fresh graduate. However, He Ran thought it over carefully and ultimately declined with humility. This painting is a secret she doesn't want to share with anyone else; she only wants to keep it to herself. The gallery owner was very disappointed, but had no choice but to give up. The painting competition has come to an end, and December has quietly arrived. He Ran rarely uses a computer to go online, and she found some recently uploaded photos in a junior high school classmate's photo album. At the junior high school reunion, everyone was present except for He Ran. After arriving in Beijing, He Ran got a new phone and a new number, while her old phone remained switched off for a long time, and many people couldn't contact her. Her gaze swept over several photos, and finally she spotted Lu Jingbai in the crowd. He Ran breathed a slight sigh of relief. January. The coldest season in Beijing has arrived, and schools are starting their winter break one after another. Early in the morning, He Ran was wrapped up like a heavy dumpling in a down jacket, wearing a mask as she set off from home to school, coughing occasionally along the way. The smog has been severe these past few days, shrouding the entire city in a gray haze. He Ran usually tries to stay home as much as possible, but today she had no choice as she had to go to school for an exam. After arriving at the examination room, He Ran found her seat and sat down. To her surprise, she found a bag of egg pancakes and soy milk on the table. She looked up in confusion and saw a boy in the row in front of her turning around and grinning at her. He Ran made no comment and silently withdrew her gaze. After the exam, He Ran booked a plane ticket back to Guangzhou for noon that day. It wasn't that she missed home, but she couldn't wait to escape this terrible weather. After a two-and-a-half-hour flight, He Ran emerged from the airport, took off her down jacket, and changed into a long trench coat. Underneath, she wore a black turtleneck sweater, of moderate thickness. Her long, slender neck made her look exceptionally elegant in this style. He Ran looked up at the azure sky and let out a long sigh. Even in winter, Guangzhou is still so hot. The day before Lunar New Year's Eve, He Ran ran into Lu Jingbai at a roadside food stall.

---

That night, Han Yu and his band were rehearsing their new song in the karaoke room, and they called He Ran over to listen in. They sang for several hours straight, screaming and wildly, still a musical style that He Ran couldn't understand. She sat in the corner, indifferent and lost in thought. Afterwards, everyone said goodbye at the entrance of the karaoke room, and Han Yu escorted He Ran home. The driver sat in the front, and the two people sat in the back, one on the left and one on the right. As the car drove into the city center, He Ran suddenly said,

"I'm hungry."

Han Yu turned to look at her.

"I'm a little hungry too. Shall we go get something to eat?"

He Ran didn't answer. She said to the driver,

"Put us down at the next intersection."

After getting out of the car, He Ran led Han Yu straight ahead and turned right at the second intersection. Once the red light turned green, they crossed the street, where a brightly lit food street stretched out on the other side. The surrounding scenery became unfamiliar, and Han Yu felt a sense of reluctance. He followed behind and asked,

"Where are we going?"

He Ran didn't say anything and continued walking forward. A short while later, they stopped in front of a barbecue stall. The area was filled with smoke and fire, and several men were vigorously fanning themselves with palm-leaf fans. Rows of fresh and plump oysters were laid out on the barbecue grill, and the smell was somewhat pungent. Han Yu frowned, his face full of disgust, and he was about to turn around and leave almost immediately. He Ran pulled him back and said,

"Let's try it first."

Han Yu glanced back at the barbecue stall, his brow furrowing in deep confusion:

"How could you like eating this kind of thing?"

He Ran didn't reply, and instead walked into the shop. It wasn't that she liked to eat it, but she was just curious why someone would like it so much. Ten minutes later, a plate of grilled oysters, costing twenty yuan, was served. Han Yu had no choice but to sit down opposite He Ran, feeling extremely uncomfortable. He Ran picked up a pair of disposable chopsticks and was about to break them apart when Han Yu stopped her again:

"Don't you say you can't eat these things? They're too harmful to your health."

He Ran gave a half-smile,

"Now you're showing concern for me, but why were you making me drink back then?"

Han Yu was speechless and reluctantly withdrew his hand. He Ran gently pushed the garlic and scallions off the oyster shell with her chopsticks, picked up a piece of oyster, and put it in her mouth. Even after being grilled and seasoned, the faint fishy taste in her mouth was still undeniable. He Ran frowned unconsciously. Like Han Yu, she didn't understand why that person would like to eat this kind of thing. After chewing the food in her mouth thoroughly and swallowing it, He Ran put down her chopsticks and rinsed her mouth with a glass of water. Sitting opposite, Han Yu suddenly smiled enigmatically after seeing something. He Ran was puzzled when he pointed in a certain direction with his chin and said,

"Look who's over there."

He Ran turned around, her gaze slightly startled. After a moment, she casually looked away, took another sip of water, and asked,

"What's wrong?"

Han Yu smiled with amusement:

"Old classmate, aren't you going up and say hello?"

He Ran said calmly,

"No need."

"Why not go? You haven't seen each other in a long time, have you?"

Han Yu thought for a moment, then pretended to realize something.

"Oh, are you afraid I'll make things difficult for him? Don't worry, I won't do anything."

He Ran stared at him with a scrutinizing gaze, unable to figure out what this man was up to. Han Yu shrugged and then reassured himself,

"I really don't mind. Go talk to him, I'll wait for you here."

After a long while, He Ran slowly stood up and walked towards the cashier. A person was standing there, head down, pressing the calculator while doing the accounting. He Ran silently observed him. Back then, he was clearly the tallest boy in the class, but now he was prematurely bent over by the pressures of life.

"Lu Jingbai."

The person whose name was called probably recognized her voice, and it took them three seconds to slowly raise their head. So much flashed through his eyes in that instant: first surprise, then avoidance, unease... He Ran pursed her lips slightly,

"Do you remember me?"

He nodded slightly.

"Yes, how could I not remember?"

He Ran asked,

"Do you work here?"

Lu Jingbai hesitated for a moment and said,

"No... This is my dad's shop. I come here occasionally to help out."

While speaking, he didn't even dare to look He Ran in the eye, and kept subconsciously glancing behind her. Later, he saw something and immediately lowered his head. He Ran suddenly realized what he was afraid of, but she didn't point it out. Lu Jingbai hurriedly tidied up the bills on the table, stuffed them into the cabinet and locked it. As he walked out, he said to He Ran,

"I'm going to deliver some takeout. If there's anything you want to eat, just tell my dad and he'll give you a discount."

Clearly, this old classmate had no intention of catching up with her. After Lu Jingbai left, He Ran returned to her seat alone. As soon as she sat down, she heard Han Yu's mocking voice:

"See? Your first love is so cowardly and timid, completely useless."

He curled his lip in disdain,

"You have really bad taste."

He Ran felt inexplicably irritable. She didn't reply, but just picked up her teacup and took a sip of water. She knew Han Yu was doing it on purpose. She didn't know what kind of psychological satisfaction her embarrassment brought him, yet he insisted on doing it. The only thing that made He Ran even more certain was that no matter how hard she tried, she would never be able to develop even the slightest affection for Han Yu. On New Year's Eve, He Ran's toothache flared up again. She could only eat a few bites of the New Year's Eve dinner before she had to leave the table and go upstairs to rest. At this moment, the family wouldn't be complete without anyone. Seeing that He Ran hadn't come downstairs for a while, Yang Wenping couldn't sit still any longer. She put down her chopsticks and said,

"I'll go upstairs and call her."

Han Yu stood up before her,

"Auntie, I'll go."

Yang Wenping and Mrs. Han exchanged a glance. The latter smiled kindly and said,

"Let Xiaoyu go."

In the bedroom on the second floor, He Ran was sitting on the floor, intently drawing a quick sketch, when Han Yu walked in without knocking. He said succinctly,

"Go down and eat."

He Ran was engrossed in her own world and ignored her. Han Yu grabbed her arm and forcibly pulled her up. He Ran frowned.

"My tooth hurts. I don't want to eat."

Han Yu said,

"If you don't want to eat, you can go sit down too."

He Ran stubbornly insisted,

"I said I wouldn't go, and I won't go."

Han Yu was unusually patient today. He Ran refused to leave, so he stood there steadily, as if he wanted to keep her waiting. After a long pause, He Ran sighed and said helplessly,

"I really have a toothache. Please, just let me have some peace and quiet tonight, okay?"

Han Yu looked at her silently, and after a few minutes he finally turned around and walked out, gently closing the door behind him.

---

After a while, He Ran vaguely heard his voice coming from downstairs:

"Auntie, Xiao Ran is feeling dizzy and not feeling well. Let her sleep for a while."

The Han family left sometime later, and Yang Wenping came upstairs to ask He Ran to see them off. At that time, He Ran was lying on the bed with her back to the door, pretending not to hear, and did not go downstairs. In the dead of night, He Ran still couldn't sleep; her toothache was unbearable. She rested her head on the velvet pillow, staring blankly out the window. The wind stirred the ethereal veil, and the silvery moonlight shone like white frost on the ground. He Ran stared wide-eyed at the distant, unreachable moon. The song involuntarily began to hum in her mind again. The white moonlight shines on both ends of the world. Some people are in your heart, but not by your side. The space beside the bed is always empty; nothing can fill it. In the darkness, she suddenly turned over and found her long-forgotten cell phone in the bedside table. The moonlight was too dim, and it took her quite a while to finally plug in the charger and then press the power button. Although she no longer uses the phone, He Ran still maintains the habit of recharging 30 yuan into it every month, at least to prevent it from being disconnected. Her inbox was overflowing with text messages; many people who didn't know she had changed her number were still sending her New Year's greetings. This includes that person. He Ran kept scrolling down until she finally found the shortest message, which contained only four words.

"Happy New Year."

It was sent at exactly 12 o'clock. The sender was Xiao Han. He Ran held her phone, frozen in that position for an unknown amount of time. My finger moved slowly across the screen, typing the words

"Tong Le"

(meaning

"Have fun together"

), then went back and deleted it after a while. She sat up angrily. He Ran, when did you become such a fussy person?

Whether to do it or not is just a matter of a single sentence. He Ran quickly deleted the text message and called Xiao Han directly. The call was answered shortly afterward.

"Where are you now?"

He Ran asked in a very calm tone, as if the two of them had just met the day before. Xiao Han said,

"At home."

"knew."

He Ran hung up the phone, quickly changed her clothes, and went out. Everything felt like it had returned to six months ago, when she impulsively decided to go find him without considering the consequences. There weren't many drivers willing to drive that far to Xiaozhou Village at this time, and it took He Ran a while to flag one down. The driver dropped He Ran off at the intersection in front of the archway, and she walked the rest of the way herself. It had only been half a month since I last saw them, and several new snack shops had opened in the square outside the auditorium. Some familiar shops in the alley had also changed their signs, and I couldn't see them clearly in the hazy night. It seems like construction is always going on in Xiaozhou Village. No matter what season you come, you can always see sand piles blocking both sides of the road. Stepping carefully on the bluestone slabs covered with mud and sand, the rough, grinding sound is particularly jarring in the quiet of the night. Following the route she remembered, He Ran finally found the barbershop, only to find it empty and the lights off. She reached out and knocked on the door.

"Xiao Han."

There was no response for a long time, and the surroundings were dark and quiet. Thinking he might have gone out to buy cigarettes, He Ran sat down at the door to wait for a while. But then she thought again and realized something was wrong; at this hour, what supermarket would still be open? Fearing something might have happened, He Ran immediately took out her phone and called him, asking,

"Didn't you say you were home? Why is no one here?"

Xiao Han said,

"I'm at my hometown."

"..."

He Ran was already waiting at Baiyun Airport before dawn. After boarding, the plane flew smoothly for two hours from Guangzhou to Chongqing, but she couldn't fall asleep at all, wasting such a comfortable sleeping environment. Xiao Han's home is in a mountainous area near Fuling, and it takes several hours to get there by bus from the airport. He Ran was feeling sleepy, but the mountain roads were winding and making her dizzy. Adding to the difficulty, the uneven, bumpy gravel roads made it impossible for her to fall asleep. When He Ran got out of the car, her face was deathly pale. Standing by the roadside, I looked around. This place was truly remote, surrounded by vast mountains. Several villages were nestled among the green hills, with each house clinging to the mountainside. The houses looked weathered, with thick earthen walls and many large cracks. Those with blue tiles on their roofs were considered to be in relatively good condition; the more impoverished families could only rely on straw and grain for shelter from the wind and rain. After standing there for a while, I saw Xiao Han walking down a hillside next to me. The sun was shining brightly, and He Ran had to squint to look up. He seemed a bit darker and had his hair cut shorter, but otherwise he hadn't changed much. He was still tall with broad shoulders, and had a cigarette between his fingers. Xiao Han didn't walk very fast, but his strides were long. He quickly reached her and stubbed out his cigarette. His first question was,

"Are you cold?"

He Ran nodded without hesitation. Of course it was cold. The temperature in Chongqing was much lower than in Guangzhou, and besides, this was a mountainous area where the wind was constantly blowing cold. He Ran had only been wearing a sweater when she left home, and now her hands and feet were so cold that she couldn't feel them. Xiao Han took off his coat and gave it to her. He was also dressed lightly, wearing only a thin thermal cotton coat under his black jacket.

"Have you eaten?"

Xiao Han asked again. He Ran shook her head and said,

"No."

Xiao Han took out a small package of red radishes from his pocket and gave them to her, saying,

"Have some first."

He Ran reached out and took it, examining it curiously for a few moments; she had never eaten it before. Xiao Han glanced behind her, probably looking for her luggage. He Ran said,

"I didn't bring anything."

Xiao Han said

"Oh,"

without asking any further questions, nodded, and said,

"Then let's go."

He Ran asked,

"Where do you live?"

Xiao Han pointed in a direction up the mountain:

"There."

He Ran looked up and followed his finger... Ah, it's really far. Their village wasn't the poorest; a road had been built before the Lunar New Year, and tourist buses occasionally passed through, making transportation relatively convenient. However, further in, the roads became much worse. Just as Mr. Lu Xun once said, roads are made by people walking on them. The dirt road is uneven and full of potholes, and when you look down you can see rows of footprints, some shallow and some deep. Occasionally, He Ran would encounter a few rural women wearing headscarves, carrying vegetable baskets, seemingly heading somewhere. Their faces, dry and rough from the wind, were flushed with a deep rosy hue, possessing a unique, rustic beauty in He Ran's eyes. Having finally reached Xiao Han's doorstep, He Ran straightened up and carefully observed her surroundings with wide eyes. This earthen house was just as simple as the ones we had seen along the way. The door was tall, and a new couplet was pasted on both sides. The six strips of red paper below the horizontal scroll were being blown wildly by the wind. The windows were grid-shaped and rusty. A pile of grains, red peppers, and a few salted fish hung by the door, and some poultry were kept in cages next to it. It seemed that someone was cooking inside, with a wisp of white smoke rising from the chimney. An old woman was sitting at the doorway, husking corn. Xiao Han walked up to her, and the old woman looked up at him, then at He Ran behind her. The old lady spoke to Xiao Han in dialect. Although He Ran couldn't understand the content, she could guess that she was asking who she was. Xiao Han replied in dialect, and then led He Ran into the house. Although the room was large, it was cluttered with a lot of things. In the corner was an old-fashioned sewing machine covered in dust, next to which were hoes and shovels used for farming leaning against the wall, and several low stools were casually placed on the ground. He Ran felt that any item she picked out from there was older than her. The center of the room was a bit tidier, with a square wooden table that was wiped clean and shiny, and four long benches arranged in a circle around it.

Xiao Han asked He Ran to sit on a bench for a while,

"The food is already being prepared, wait a little longer."

He Ran was exhausted from the journey and had climbed the mountain with Xiao Han for so long, so she didn't have much of an appetite.

"I don't want to eat, I just want to sleep, is that okay?"

Xiao Han looked down at her. She hadn't slept all night, and her face was indeed paler than the paper used to cover his windows. He nodded and said,

"Then you go to sleep first. I'll wake you up when it's time to eat."

Xiao Han led her into a room that looked like his bedroom. He Ran didn't care how tacky the pink bed sheet embroidered with dragons, phoenixes, and large flowers was; she pressed herself against it, buried her face in the pillow, and fell into a deep sleep. Xiao Han watched for a while and then said,

"Don't sleep face down, it's bad for your heart."

He Ran kept his eyes closed and ignored him. He stood there for a while, and seeing that he couldn't persuade him, he walked out first. He Ran slept until dark before getting up. Xiao Han had called her once during dinner, which she vaguely remembered, but she just couldn't open her eyes, so she didn't get to eat. After getting up, Xiao Han heated up the food he had left for her and put it on the table. He Ran only then realized she was hungry, and finished the bowl of rice in a few minutes. The day ends early in the mountains; it's only seven or eight o'clock and people are already starting to rest. While He Ran was eating, Xiao Han was making a lot of noise in the next room. She put her bowl and chopsticks on the table, stood up, and went into the room to see what was going on. This is a fairly large storage room. There's a chicken coop inside, and it smells terrible. On the right side is a broken, abandoned bed. A rope runs from left to right, with a few pieces of dried clothing hanging on it. Xiao Han was busy tidying up the messy things piled on the broken bed, probably to make room for it. He Ran asked,

"What are you doing?"

Xiao Han said,

"Make the bed."

He Ran thought for a few seconds and said,

"Who's sleeping?"

Xiao Han:

"Me."

He Ran walked behind him, sat down against the tricycle, and asked,

"Aren't you going to sleep with me?"

Xiao Han turned his head and glanced at her, but didn't say anything. He then lowered his head and continued packing his things. She walked around to stand in front of him and said casually,

"I've gone through mountains and rivers to see you, and you're not even going to show any appreciation?"

Xiao Han stopped what he was doing and remained silent for a long time. Inside the house, the hen clucked several times, still restless even after dark. He turned to look at He Ran, his gaze carrying a deep meaning.

"Didn't you say you wouldn't contact me?"

After being momentarily speechless, He Ran immediately retorted,

"You contacted me first."

As if to prove her point, she even took out her phone and waved it in front of him twice, asking,

"Do you need me to find the text message you sent?"

Xiao Han explained,

"That's a New Year's greeting."

"New Year's greetings don't count?"

He Ran raised her lip.

"Don't tell me you sent them to a group, I don't believe in that stuff."

"..."

"Besides... I only said I wouldn't contact you, I didn't say you shouldn't contact me."

She stood up, walking closer to him step by step, looking up at him:

"Why didn't you contact me?"

Xiao Han lowered his eyelids and stared at her for a long time, his eyes shining brightly in the darkness. After a period of silence, he said in a low voice,

"I want to see if you will come looking for me if I don't."

He Ran snorted lightly,

"You're really good at keeping your composure."

"I can keep my composure."

He suddenly picked her up and placed her forcefully on the bed.

"I won't come to pick you up."

He Ran frowned.

"This bed is filthy. Get me off it."

Several dust marks immediately appeared on her buttocks. Xiao Han picked her up again, supporting her buttocks with both hands. His eyes were fixed on her, his gaze filled with many indescribable emotions. Lovers can never get enough of gazing into each other's eyes. She was only eighteen, still at an age where she could develop. In the past six months, her hair had grown quite a bit, almost reaching her chest. Her figure was still petite, but she was gradually showing the charm of a mature woman. He Ran wrapped her arms around his neck and urged him with seductive eyes,

"Hurry up, let me see your hospitality."

Xiao Han's eyes flashed with a dark light, and his voice was fierce:

"You're the one who came looking for me, so don't regret it."

He Ran said firmly,

"If I regretted it, I would have taken a plane back long ago. Why would I need to suffer with you in this remote mountain forest?"

Xiao Han didn't reply, but simply hugged her tighter and carried her out of the room without saying a word. The walls of this mud house are not soundproof at all; if there is the slightest rustling or rustling in one room, the other room can hear it clearly. Before He Ran and Xiao Han could get down to business, the bed creaked a few times, and the old lady's voice came from next door. The two immediately stopped what they were doing, and Xiao Han replied to the other room. He Ran asked,

"What are you talking about?"

Xiao Han said,

"She asked me what I was doing."

"So what did you reply?"

I said,

"I'm swatting flies."

"..."

He Ran hesitated for a few seconds before asking,

"So, are we still going to do it or not?"

"Let's wait a bit,"

Xiao Han said.

"When will we wait?"

"midnight."

He Ran had no choice but to lie down obediently, feeling extremely frustrated. After a while, she suggested,

"How about we go out and find some random land?"

Xiao Han tried to scare her:

"It's dark and windy today, be careful you don't fall down the mountain."

"..."

He Ran shut her mouth and remained silent.

After that, she buried her head in Xiao Han's arms. The surroundings were very quiet, with only the sound of his heartbeat nearby and the occasional bark of a dog in the distance. This environment actually fostered a sense of tranquility, and although He Ran was not sleepy, she slowly closed her eyes. After she fell asleep, it was already past midnight. Xiao Han patted her shoulder to wake her up. The night was still long, and she couldn't help but chuckle.

"Didn't you find another woman to relieve yourself?"

Xiao Han countered,

"Who am I looking for?"

"Hmm... for example, Oman."

"I'm done with her a long time ago."

"The girls in your mountains are quite nice too, each one of them has a great figure."

Xiao Han frowned.

"Don't talk nonsense."

He Ran just made a few jokes out of boredom, but when she saw that Xiao Han was taking it so seriously, she shut her mouth. After countless rounds, He Ran lay sprawled out, breathing heavily, with half an arm and a leg draped over Xiao Han. Neither of them were dressed, and they huddled together under a thick quilt, their bodies pressed close together for warmth. He Ran's habit in winter was to cover her head with the quilt, so Xiao Han had to accommodate her. In the countryside, it was pitch black, and even more so in bed. The two slept snuggled together, the quilt uneven, and wisps of cold air would occasionally sneak in through the raised gaps. Although she occasionally felt a chill, He Ran felt more at ease this way than sleeping alone in a spacious bed with the heater on. She couldn't help but snuggle closer into Xiao Han's arms, and whispered,

"So comfortable."

Xiao Han lowered his head and gently kissed her forehead with his slightly dry lips. He Ran said,

"Why did you kiss me?"

Her sudden question caught Xiao Han off guard. He didn't answer for a while, but fortunately she didn't ask again. Rural life is monotonous and dull. After breakfast, Xiao Han carried several buckets of water back from outside and then began the long task of chopping firewood. He Ran sat listlessly in the courtyard, basking in the sun not far from Xiao Han, occasionally teasing the chickens and herding the ducks. Xiao Han glanced back at her every now and then, as if to make sure she was still there. He Ran noticed that his eyes kept wandering towards her while he was working; he had the same habit when they were doing gardening in Guangzhou. She's a grown woman, how could she possibly get lost? He Ran advised him,

"Concentrate, or you might cut your hand."

Not long after, the old lady brought out a bench from inside the house and sat down at the doorway to peel corn. He Ran actually wanted to greet her, but unfortunately, they couldn't communicate. Curious about the old lady's relationship with Xiao Han, she asked Xiao Han,

"Who is this relative of yours?"

Xiao Han said,

"My mom."

He Ran was genuinely taken aback. The old lady's hair was completely white; she must have been at least sixty or seventy years old. Logically speaking, Xiao Han was only thirty-three; his mother shouldn't be that old, right? Xiao Han explained,

"She was in her second marriage with my dad, and she's a few years older than him."

He Ran said

"Oh,"

which makes more sense. Xiao Han then gave her a general account of his family situation. After his biological mother passed away, his father remarried. His stepmother was also remarried and brought a daughter with her, who was older than both Xiao Han and his brother. Xiao Han was the third child in this newly formed family. Later, the couple had another daughter, but tragically, she drowned in a pond before she turned five. Now, Xiao Han's father and brother have also passed away, leaving only him and his eldest sister as heirs—a very small family. My older sister married and moved to the county town a few years ago and has been living there ever since. She comes back to visit during holidays. This year, she took Quan Quan to the town for a visit, and Xiao Han will have to go pick him up in a few days. It is said that Xiao Han's father died young, and his stepmother raised three children all by herself, which is truly remarkable. Upon hearing this, He Ran couldn't help but turn back and glance at the unassuming old woman peeling corn. After chopping a pile of firewood, Xiao Han carried it into the stove and started chopping another pile. Mother and son each had their own tasks, leaving He Ran with nothing to do. Unfortunately, she was lazy and didn't want to do anything. Xiao Han noticed her listlessness and comforted her, saying,

"Let's go to the market this afternoon."

Upon hearing this, He Ran finally became interested.

"There's a market here?"

Xiao Han nodded:

"Yes, but it's very far."

"How far?"

He Ran asked. Xiao Han said,

"It's about ten kilometers, and we have to cross several mountains."

He Ran then asked,

"How do we get there?"

"Take a tricycle."

He Ran smiled and nodded,

"That's alright, as long as we don't have to walk, let's go!"

---

After lunch, Xiao Han said a few words to the old lady and then took He Ran out. Xiao Han was driving an electric tricycle, and He Ran was sitting in the cargo bed in the back, with a burlap sack underneath her to keep out the dust. The mountain road was narrow and unevenly paved. The wheels rolled over mud and gravel, and He Ran sat in the back, bouncing and swaying. She wasn't used to the weather here, and to avoid her face getting dry, cracked, and red like the women here, He Ran covered her face with a thick cloth to block the wind. Along the way, Xiao Han would occasionally run into a few acquaintances from his hometown, stopping to exchange greetings. As the car drove further away, he encountered fewer and fewer people. They slowly climbed to a higher point, where their view became more expansive. The scenery here is incredibly beautiful and spectacular. In the distance, the mountains stretch endlessly, shrouded in mist, with ancient trees and clear waters reflecting the azure sky, creating a breathtaking and refreshing atmosphere. Xiao Han, who had grown up here, wasn't distracted by the scenery and focused on riding his tricycle. He Ran, after sighing, also looked away at the endless yellow dust and flying sand below the road, seemingly already getting used to the rhythm of the tricycle's swaying. Everything and everyone here was unfamiliar to her. Their way of life was ordinary, without any great joys or sorrows or ups and downs. Far from the city and the hustle and bustle, it inexplicably gave her a sense of belonging. Perhaps this is what is meant by

"home is where the heart is."

After crossing several mountains, they finally arrived at the market. The market was larger than He Ran had imagined; the street stretched as far as the eye could see, and it sold all sorts of things. Xiao Han asked He Ran if there was anything she needed to buy. He Ran thought for a while and asked,

"Is there a pharmacy? My tooth hurts, and I want to buy some anti-inflammatory medicine."

Xiao Han nodded:

"Yes."

Then he led He Ran forward. They had walked for a while, and when they were almost at the pharmacy, Xiao Han suddenly stopped. He stood there stunned for a moment, then pulled He Ran and quickly walked in the opposite direction, as if they had encountered a ghost. He Ran asked, puzzled,

"What are you doing?"

Xiao Han didn't reply, but quickened his pace until he pulled He Ran into a deserted corner before stopping and saying,

"We ran into an acquaintance."

He Ran was even more puzzled:

"So what?"

Xiao Han looked into her eyes, hesitated for a long time, and finally told her,

"I was supposed to go on a blind date today. The person I just met was the matchmaker."

"..."

He Ran took a deep breath, her tone becoming somewhat strange:

"You even go on blind dates."

"..."

Xiao Han remained silent.

"So, are you going to blame me for suddenly showing up and disturbing your blind date?"

He Ran said, reaching out to pinch his thigh hard. Xiao Han dodged to the side without explaining anything. He's thirty-three this year. In the village, everyone his age is already married and has a family. He's still single, so it's no wonder his family is worried. Xiao Han honestly said,

"Don't be angry, I won't go now that you're here."

He Ran understood the reasoning, but she also knew that if she hadn't come today, Xiao Han might have gone on a blind date with that girl. A strange tightness gripped their hearts, and they stood there, staring at each other without saying a word.

---

After a while, He Ran grew impatient and urged,

"Have you checked if your matchmaker has left yet?"

Xiao Han glanced in that direction and said,

"They didn't leave."

He Ran scoffed,

"How can it take so long to buy medicine?"

Xiao Han said,

"It seems like I started chatting with the pharmacy owner."

"..."

He Ran was getting impatient, so she simply said,

"If you won't go, I will. Anyway, she doesn't know me."

Xiao Han let out a soft sigh, and as if nothing had happened, took her hand and said,

"Let's go together."

They walked to the pharmacy entrance, arguing, and went inside. He Ran glanced at the middle-aged woman standing in front of the cashier. She looked to be around forty or fifty years old, with a round face and figure, and seemed to be a kind-hearted person. As soon as the aunt saw Xiao Han, she waved and greeted him:

"Oh, Sanzi, what are you doing here? Didn't you say you were going to pick up Quan Quan?"

Although she also spoke in dialect, it wasn't as heavily accented as the old lady's, and He Ran could barely understand her. Xiao Han replied,

"We were originally going to go, but Quan Quan said he wanted to stay there for a few more days, so we didn't go."

After exchanging a few pleasantries, the woman noticed He Ran, who had come in with him. A hint of confusion appeared on her face.

"...Who is this?"

Xiao Han pulled He Ran closer to him and said,

"My youngest child."

He Ran frowned almost imperceptibly, trying hard to pull her hand out of his palm, but Xiao Han held it too tightly and she couldn't move. After the woman left, He Ran asked, puzzled,

"Why did you tell her I was your daughter?"

Xiao Han said,

"The 'youngest child' here doesn't mean that."

He Ran asked,

"What does that mean?"

Xiao Han pulled her into the pharmacy, refusing to tell her anything. After leaving the pharmacy, they bought some miscellaneous local specialties elsewhere and finally returned to the village before dark.

---

After dinner, He Ran turned on her phone to call home, something she hadn't done in a long time, only to find that there was no signal at all. She walked around the house for a while, but still couldn't find a signal, so she had to run to the kitchen to ask Xiao Han. Xiao Han said,

"There's only a signal at the top of the mountain. I'll take you up there after I finish washing the dishes."

When Xiao Han finished his work, He Ran was already waiting at the door. She was still lazy and wanted to ask Xiao Han to give her a ride on his bike when she went out, but Xiao Han refused. He said,

"It's not far, you can walk there. You need to exercise."

Xiao Han was right on this point, so He Ran had no choice but to listen to him. They climbed to the top of the mountain in twenty minutes, found a large rock at random, and sat down back to back. He Ran's phone finally received some signal, but it was still intermittent. She quickly called Yang Wenping and told her that she was traveling with her classmates and wouldn't be back for a few days. Yang Wenping hadn't received any prior notification, so she naturally gave her a good scolding. He Ran didn't want to listen to her rambling on and on, so she hung up the phone, citing poor signal. Xiao Han listened quietly to her phone call the whole time, smoking without saying a word. The weather is nice tonight, and you can see stars, densely covering the entire night sky. This kind of sight is rare in the city. He Ran looked up at the stars and became lost in thought. The person behind her shifted slightly, a movement that brought her back to reality. Although she and Xiao Han were back to back supporting each other, Xiao Han certainly didn't dare to exert much force, otherwise he would have crushed He Ran's small frame. He Ran suddenly noticed something suspicious. She sat up straight, turned to look at Xiao Han, and asked,

"Isn't there no signal in the mountains? I called you on New Year's Eve, how come you answered so quickly?"

Xiao Han looked to the side, avoiding her gaze, and said casually,

"I'm going for a walk on the mountaintop."

He Ran scoffed,

"You're really free, aren't you? Running to the mountaintop for a stroll all day. The mosquitoes' whole family should thank you."

Xiao Han said seriously,

"There are no mosquitoes in winter."

"...Don't try to change the subject!"

He Ran stared at him intently, and under that persistent scrutiny, Xiao Han's gaze began to dart away. He Ran turned around and suddenly brought her face close to his, almost bumping into his chin. She chuckled smugly, then asked,

"Were you waiting for my call?"

Xiao Han did not reply. She stared intently at her and asked,

"Is that right?"

After a long while, he finally uttered a soft

"Mmm"

.
---

He Ran smiled again and asked,

"How did you know I would call you?"

Xiao Han pursed his lips and said,

"It's just... I have a feeling it might."

"Because you texted me?"

"Um."

He Ran shrugged and said,

"See, so you contacted me first, not me who took the initiative to contact you."

Xiao Han:

"..."

"But in the end, I still came all this way to find you, and you achieved your goal."

He Ran smiled half-heartedly and casually took his arm:

"I didn't realize you were so cunning."

"..."

Sitting on the stone for too long was uncomfortable, so He Ran made the most of the space around her and sat on Xiao Han's lap. Facing each other, she gently stroked his firm jaw with her soft fingertips and said,

"You should shave."

Xiao Han didn't reply. He stared at her intently and called out to himself,

"Little one."

He Ran frowned, puzzled.

"What exactly do you mean?"

Xiao Han kept her mouth shut and didn't say anything. If he wouldn't tell her, so be it; she could just go back and ask someone else. Their eyes met, and they remained silent. In his dark eyes, He Ran saw countless stars, and a figure that was most deeply etched in her memory. She placed her hands on his shoulders, tilted her head back, and met his eyes.

"Want to kiss me?"

Xiao Han said in a deep voice,

"Hmm."

He Ran was too weak to go down the mountain, so Xiao Han carried her back. When they got home, it was already late at night. Afraid of disturbing the old lady's rest, they tiptoed into the house, locked the door, and went to bed. The bed was completely cold, and He Ran shivered from the cold. She hurriedly stuffed her two icy feet into Xiao Han's thighs to rub them. Xiao Han warmed them up while massaging the sore muscles in her calves. He Ran finally fell asleep as the bed gradually warmed up.

---

The next day, Xiao Han was going to the county town to pick up Quan Quan and visit some relatives. He asked He Ran if she wanted to come with him. He Ran asked,

"How long will it take?"

Xiao Han thought for a moment and said,

"I might have to stay overnight."

He Ran was immediately displeased. This trip would inevitably involve meeting all sorts of distant relatives, which was He Ran's biggest headache. Besides, if those relatives asked who she was, she wouldn't know how to introduce herself. After thinking it over, He Ran finally said,

"I'll just stay at home. You should go and come back soon."

Judging from Xiao Han's expression, he probably wanted He Ran to go with him, but he didn't force her. After Xiao Han left, only He Ran and the old lady remained in the mud house. After breakfast, the two of them each took a bench and sat in the middle of the courtyard to warm themselves by the fire. Because they couldn't speak the same language, they still didn't communicate. The old lady was busy peeling dried corn, while He Ran remained idle. Around noon, He Ran lazily yawned and glanced to the side. The old lady was still peeling her corn. Half an hour later, it was almost noon. He Ran felt hungry. When she turned her head again, the old lady was still peeling corn and showed no intention of making lunch. He Ran couldn't help but wonder, did she not eat lunch when Xiao Han wasn't home? When it was past one o'clock, the old lady finally put down what she was saying, turned around and said something to He Ran. He Ran couldn't understand, so she could only stare blankly at her. The old lady spoke another phrase in her dialect, pointing at He Ran and then towards the kitchen. This time, He Ran understood what she meant; she was being asked to cook... He Ran didn't know how to cook, and what's more, the kitchen in this mountain village didn't have natural gas, so she had to start a fire herself, which really gave her a headache. She sat behind the stove, fiddling with it for ages but couldn't get the fire going. Completely at a loss, she finally decided to just make a few cold dishes to get by. That noon, their side dishes were cucumber salad with vinegar and cold preserved egg and kelp salad. The taste wasn't very good, but fortunately, the old lady wasn't picky and ate it all up. However, He Ran's smugness didn't last long. While washing dishes, she accidentally broke someone's plate. This upset the old lady, who then nagged her incessantly for a long time. He Ran didn't understand, but she wasn't annoyed. Later, she took a broom and cleaned up the broken pieces on the ground, and the little incident passed. Xiao Han originally planned to stay overnight in the county town, but he couldn't bear to leave the two people at home. After picking up Quan Quan, he had lunch in the county town and then rushed back. He Ran watched him get off the tricycle and gave him a genuine smile. At least with him around, she wouldn't go hungry that night; even she herself couldn't compliment her cooking skills. When Quan Quan saw He Ran, she was so happy that she cried out and rushed over to grab He Ran's hand and wouldn't let go. He pulled He Ran into the house and eagerly showed her his latest paintings. The child was indeed very passionate about painting; there were at least a hundred paintings in He Ran's hands. Even He Ran couldn't help but exclaim in surprise,

"Where do you find the time to draw so many?"

Quan Quan said somewhat shyly,

"I just draw when I'm bored."

"Bored?"

He Ran smiled.

"The New Year is the most lively time of the year, how could you be bored?"

His expression fell,

"Nobody plays with me..."

"No one's with you?"

He Ran said.

"Aren't there many children in the village?"

Quan Quan pouted, looking rather pitiful:

"But they don't play with me."

He Ran asked, puzzled,

"Why?"

"They say I have no father or mother."

"..."

This sentence made He Ran fall silent.

She never expected that even in the most simple and honest mountains there would be some children with bad intentions. With mixed feelings, she touched Quan Quan's head and didn't know what to say for a moment. Quan Quan turned around and patted He Ran on the back, saying optimistically,

"Auntie, don't be sad. I'm happy as long as I can draw."

He Ran was overwhelmed with emotion. She pursed her lips and said,

"Keep it up, and become a little painter in the future."

Xiao Han cooked dinner that night. His culinary skills were beyond question. The vegetables grown by the mountain people were pesticide-free and had an indescribable sweetness. The fish they raised were also plump and delicious. He Ran ate with great relish. The people here are raised with the idea that

"every grain is hard-earned"

from childhood. They cook only what they can eat and there can be no leftovers at every meal. Following local customs, He Ran, though already full, noticed that everyone else's bowls were clean, so she used her chopsticks to pick up the last few grains of rice from the bottom of their bowls and ate them. After the meal, Xiao Han was the one who cleared the dishes as usual. He Ran handed him the empty bowl, which was completely devoid of rice, and he smiled at her with satisfaction. The old lady, who hadn't spoken until now, suddenly pointed at He Ran and said something. He Ran didn't understand and sat there blankly. Xiao Han, on the other hand, stopped what he was doing, looked at the old lady, and then looked at He Ran thoughtfully. Quan Quan whispered a reminder from the side,

"My grandma wants you to wash the dishes."

He Ran paused for a few seconds, then stood up and held out her hands to Xiao Han,

"Give them to me."

Xiao Han shook his head and said,

"No need."

He Ran's hand was still outstretched:

"Let me try."

Xiao Han insisted,

"I'll wash it."

The old lady, who had been watching, was clearly displeased and called Xiao Han into her room for a lecture. Although they were talking in another room, the earthen wall was not soundproof at all, and they could still hear each other. He Ran turned to Quan Quan and asked,

"What are they saying? Translate it for me."

Quan Quan looked down at her nose, then at her heart, and said,

"You really want to know?"

He Ran nodded:

"Mm."

Quan Quan hesitated for a moment before saying,

"My grandma said that a woman should be able to work, and you can't marry a wife like this, who's lazy and good-for-nothing..."

As he finished speaking, his voice trailed off, and he subconsciously glanced at He Ran's expression. She, however, remained indifferent, seemingly unconcerned. When He Ran returned home that evening, the old lady and Quan Quan had already gone to bed early. He Ran dozed off for a while, but was woken up by Xiao Han's noise. After playing dead and letting for a while, He Ran opened her eyes and asked him,

"What did you just say to your mother?"

Xiao Han said,

"I didn't say anything."

He Ran asked,

"Then why did it take so long to talk?"

Xiao Han remained silent. He Ran moved his head away, grimaced, and said,

"I know your mom must have said something bad about me. Even though I don't understand what she's saying, I can tell from her eyes."

Xiao Han remained silent. He Ran asked him,

"Is that right?"

Xiao Han said,

"No."

He Ran gave a soft hum, seemingly casually,

"It's okay if you don't like me, anyway, I'm not going to be your daughter-in-law."

These words made Xiao Han's eyes darken. He pressed down on her arms with both hands and suddenly increased the pressure. He Ran frowned and punched him lightly,

"What are you doing? Does that hurt?"

He ignored her, lowered his body, and hugged her even tighter. He Ran placed her hand on his chest, trying to push him away,

"What are you doing? Be gentle!"

Xiao Han ignored her, and He Ran kept patting his back, calling out,

"Xiao Han!"

Her voice was too loud, and fearing it would wake the two people in the other room, Xiao Han simply covered her mouth with his tongue. After pushing and shoving for a while, He Ran realized that Xiao Han had always been giving in to her. When he got serious, she couldn't budge him at all. After it was over, she was in the same state as last night, with a blank expression and not wanting to move at all. Xiao Han reached for the tissue box on the bedside table, only to find it empty. He got out of bed and opened a new pack. He Ran pointed to the purplish-blue marks on her body and couldn't help but curse,

"You really think I came all this way just to sleep with you? You've used up all your energy on me!"

Xiao Han didn't look at her. After a while, he lowered his eyes and said in a faint voice,

"I'm sorry."

He Ran turned away, not wanting to pay him any attention. A few minutes later, Xiao Han turned off the light and climbed into bed. He Ran felt him press against her from behind. She was still angry and wanted to get away from him, but perhaps because it was too cold, her body unconsciously craved that warmth. Xiao Han reached out and pulled her into his arms, his hand resting lightly on her lower abdomen. The warmth emanating from his palm gradually warmed He Ran's body, and her heart softened unconsciously. The night was quiet and warm. On a red-skinned train that has restarted its journey to Guangzhou. The nearly 22-hour journey was extremely difficult for He Ran. Xiao Han bought her a hard sleeper ticket, while he bought a standing ticket. They didn't have much luggage; all they brought on the train were local specialties that He Ran had bought. He Ran's phone had long since run out of battery, and she only managed to charge it after buying a charger on the train. To kill time, she randomly picked a movie to watch with Xiao Han, but the mobile network was poor, and the video buffered every few minutes, making it quite boring. He Ran didn't eat anything at noon, just drank a few sips of water. She didn't dare drink too much water, trying to minimize the number of times she needed to use the restroom. During the Spring Festival travel rush, the train carriages were too crowded, the restrooms were clogged, and the smell was unpleasant; she didn't want to go a second time after her first visit. After 10 p.m., the lights in the carriage were turned off, and everyone around her gradually went to bed. He Ran then took out her toothbrush and toothpaste, brushed her teeth, tied her hair up in a high bun, and washed her face. She returned to her bed and let Xiao Han wash up.

Xiao Han stood up, looked at her, and smiled. He Ran asked,

"What are you laughing at?"

Xiao Han said,

"I haven't seen you tie your hair up in a long time."

He Ran took off the rubber band, shook her hair, and said,

"It's still inconvenient to let it grow long. You can cut it short for me when we get back."

Xiao Han nodded and said,

"Okay."

He returned shortly after washing up, and He Ran was already lying down on the bed. Xiao Han glanced at her, then lowered the seat by the window, intending to sleep sitting up tonight. He Ran squeezed herself into the bed to make half a space for him,

"You can sleep here."

Xiao Han glanced over there again. There wasn't even enough space for him to put half his leg up. He shook his head and said,

"No need, I'll just sit here."

He Ran didn't insist. She lay there for a while, closed her eyes, and gradually became sleepy. Just as she was about to fall asleep, the person on the upper bunk started snoring. She shared a cubicle with a family of three, who must have also returned home after the New Year. The husband was a middle-aged man, and He Ran kept staring at her as she took off her clothes after entering the carriage, so he was probably not a well-behaved fellow. The man's snoring grew louder and louder, penetrating every nook and cranny of He Ran's ears. He Ran, awakened by the noise, couldn't fall back asleep. She endured it for a while, then finally couldn't resist sitting up and saying to Xiao Han, who was sitting at the other end,

"Come and sleep with me."

Xiao Han wasn't asleep either. When she called him, he stood up and walked over, asking in a low voice,

"What's wrong?"

He Ran frowned and said,

"The person on the top bunk snores louder than your mother, how can they sleep?"

Xiao Han smiled helplessly, sat down on the edge of the bed, and took off his shoes. He Ran lay on her side, making room for him. Even though He Ran was very thin, it was still too cramped for two people to share a bed. Xiao Han had half of his body sticking out of the bed, and he could fall off if he wasn't careful. They were pressed very close together, and Xiao Han reached out and covered her ears with his hands. His palms were broad and thick, completely enveloping He Ran's ears and isolating them from the outside world. Although I can't hear the noise anymore, my ears are itchy. He Ran said,

"What should I do? I don't want to sleep anymore."

"... "

Xiao Han asked,

"Then what do you want to do?"

He Ran didn't reply immediately, but thought about it carefully first. Xiao Han said,

"There are so many people on the bus, don't do anything reckless."

He Ran rolled her eyes:

"I haven't said anything yet, what are you thinking?"

"..."

"Let's sing a song,"

she suddenly said.

"Um?"

I want to hear you sing. Xiao Han laughed,

"You still say you're not a child? You need someone to sing to you to sleep."

He Ran scolded playfully,

"So, are you going to sing or not?"

What do you want to hear?

"It will be all right."

Xiao Han patted her back and began to hum softly, still the same song,

"Life is Like a Summer Flower"

. His voice was simple and unadorned, deep and resonant. His singing, devoid of any technical skill, sounded incredibly genuine and comforting to He Ran. He Ran asked,

"Why do you always sing this?"

Xiao Han's answer was simple:

"I can only sing this."

He Ran laughed; the fat man had guessed correctly. She said,

"I want to hear you sing something else."

What song? He Ran thought for a moment and asked,

"Can you sing Cantonese songs?"

Xiao Han said,

"I know a little."

"Then just pick any song."

Xiao Han pondered for a moment, and when he spoke again, the melody had changed to another old song. Holding you, the warmth of those days resurfaces. The innocent dreams of childhood remain untainted in my heart. Today, you and I tried to stand side by side again. The feelings of yesteryear are now fresh and new. He Ran hadn't expected him to sing a song by Leslie Cheung, a song with a tender and affectionate melody. She had once loved this song,

"The Way We Were,"

but listening to it now, nestled in Xiao Han's arms, it felt completely different. The same melody repeated endlessly, soothing and lingering. He Ran's eyelids gradually grew heavy, her thoughts drifted away, and she sank into his voice. He Ran didn't sleep well in the unfamiliar environment, waking up several times in the middle of the night. Each time she opened her eyes, the face of the person lying on the opposite bed was different, which was a bit scary in itself. He Ran had never ridden a train before, so this was quite a rare experience for her.

At 10 a.m. the next day, the train arrived at its final destination on time. Although they had just had a nap, He Ran and Xiao Han still looked tired from the long journey. Thinking about what they would face when they got home, He Ran suddenly wanted to stay on the train and not move. They were the last to get out of the carriage after everyone had left. Their homes were in opposite directions. Next, Xiao Han would take the subway back to Xiaozhou Village, while He Ran would take a taxi in the other direction. They parted ways at the exit, only saying to each other,

"Be careful on your way."

As if the scene from six months ago were repeating itself, Xiao Han stood motionless in place, watching He Ran leave. He is a fixed point, while she is a straight line with infinite possibilities; once she lets go, she will be lost forever. Time seemed to have passed for a long time, but He Ran had only taken a few steps forward. At that moment, she suddenly stopped the car and turned back to him. She moved so fast she almost jogged, a gust of wind even rising between her trouser legs, before finally stopping in front of him. Before Xiao Han could reach out and hug her, she had already tiptoed and kissed him on the cheek, then whispered,

"I'll contact you later."

Xiao Han smiled and nodded slightly:

"Okay."

## Chapter Six: First Clues

Chapter Preface:

"If you don't like her, just say so, and I won't see her."

Xiao Han's focused gaze was like the first ray of light at the juncture of night and dawn.

"But don't do that kind of thing again."

He Ran originally thought that she would be scolded by Yang Wenping when she got home, but that was not the case. In the past two days, a wealthy businessman in the province has been investigated, implicating a large number of people under his command. He Ran's father, who had close ties with the businessman, was also affected, and his company's profits plummeted. Yang Wenping was already overwhelmed with this matter and had no extra energy to discipline He Ran. Two nights later, He Ran was invited out by Han Yu. Han Yu had just finished the entrance exam for the Communication Department of China University of Communication before the Lunar New Year, and the results came out not long ago. With his father backing him up, he should have had a smooth experience. He was in high spirits at the time, but He Ran didn't give him a friendly look. Instead of being interrogated by Yang Wenping, the questioning started with him first. In the billiard room, Han Yu walked over and sat down next to He Ran. The leather sofa was slightly dented, and his face looked unpredictable in the dim light.

"Where have you been traveling all these days?"

"Chongqing"

.

"I can't get through on the phone either."

There is no signal in the mountains.

"Who did you go with?"

"classmate."

Which student?

"From Beijing, you don't know him."

Han Yu sneered,

"Show me the photos."

He Ran handed him the phone without making a sound. The recent photos in the album are indeed of mountains and rivers, and He Ran herself occasionally appears in a few of them. However, her way of taking landscape photos is different from others; she doesn't pose or smile, which makes these photos look like candid shots. Han Yu flipped through the photo album, his gaze probing as he asked,

"Where are your classmates?"

He Ran said,

"She took the photos for me."

"No group photo?"

"She's ugly and refused to take the photo."

"..."

Han Yu finally returned the phone to her and asked,

"Why didn't you call me?"

He Ran said,

"I had made an appointment with someone else."

"Then ask me out first next time."

"The place I went to was very harsh. Could you, a young master, handle it?"

Han Yu said,

"That's because you haven't found the right place. As long as you're willing to spend money, you can eat and live well even in the most difficult environments."

He Ran casually changed the subject:

"Let's talk about it another time."

After sitting for a while, Han Yu started to get suspicious again:

"What's your classmate's name?"

Even if I told you, you wouldn't know him.

"In the same class as you?"

"..."

He Ran finally lost her patience, frowned and said,

"Are you done yet? Han Yu, you're not anyone to me now. I don't need to report everything I've done to you, do I?"

Perhaps it was because Han Yu's attitude towards her had gradually improved recently that He Ran became more emboldened. In the past, she would not have dared to refute Han Yu in this tone. Just as she finished speaking, He Ran's phone vibrated twice. She glanced at the caller ID, stood up, and said,

"I'm going out to answer this call."

Before Han Yu could react, she stood up and left. Outside the pool hall, her phone continued to vibrate in her hand. He Ran calmly pressed the answer button. Xiao Han's voice came through the phone, low and muffled,

"When are you coming over?"

He Ran smiled silently.

"What, you missed me?"

Xiao Han did not respond. He Ran had figured out his habits; if he didn't answer this question, it meant he agreed. After a moment of silence, he said,

"I'm near your house now, come out and collect some local specialties."

He Ran was slightly taken aback for a moment,

"How did you know where I live?"

Xiao Han said,

" You said that last time."

He Ran thought back for a moment. It was when they first met that she left her phone at his barbershop, and when she went back to look for it, he did ask for her home address. He Ran said,

"That address was just something I gave randomly. I don't live there, and I'm not home right now either."

After saying that, she didn't receive a response from Xiao Han, but she could feel that his breathing on the other end seemed to have become heavier. He Ran continued,

"But I'm also in Baiyun District now, which is quite close to you. Come over when you have time."

After a long pause, Xiao Han gave a low

"hmm"

and then hung up the phone. When the phone rang for the second time, He Ran came out of the side door of the club and walked a little further. Xiao Han was standing by the roadside, with a backpack on his back filled with local specialties that He Ran had bought. He Ran walked up and patted his shoulder. Xiao Han turned around and looked at her. The club was well-heated, and He Ran was only wearing a thin shirt when she came out, looking out of place on the cold street. Xiao Han took off his backpack and gave it to her, then took off his own coat and draped it over her shoulders. The bag was stuffed full, and He Ran, being short, was dressed up like a primary school student in the blink of an eye, especially with Xiao Han's long coat. She pursed her lips, waved to him, and said,

"I'm leaving now; my friend is waiting."

Xiao Han nodded and watched her leave without saying a word. He seemed lost in thought, not uttering a single word from the moment He Ran arrived until she left. Back at the club, He Ran looked into the deepest part of the billiard room. Han Yu was still sitting on the sofa. She casually walked over and sat down next to him. The peace lasted only a few seconds. After a long while, Han Yu suddenly asked,

"Who is that man?"

He Ran didn't even lift her eyelids, and asked,

"Which man?"

Han Yu raised an eyebrow:

"Do I need to tell you? Whose clothes are you wearing?"

He Ran said slowly,

"Since you've already seen it, why are you asking me?"

Han Yu paused for a moment, then gritted his teeth and said,

"He Ran!"

He Ran said casually,

"I met you in the art studio before, and you've met me before too."

Han Yu said,

"I'm asking him what he wants from you!"

"Is this what I'm giving you?"

He Ran patted the bag on her lap.

"He's also from Chongqing. We met at the train station and came back together."

As she spoke, she unzipped her bag and took out a pack of red radishes, handing the rest to Han Yu.

"These are local specialties I brought for you, but they probably won't suit your taste."

After doing all that, He Ran stopped caring about anything else. She crossed her legs and focused her gaze on the situation on the other side of the pool table. Han Yu's gaze was fixed on that unmoved face, and after a long while he said quietly,

"You'd better not try anything funny."

He Ran laughed,

"What? Are you going to use the same trick you used on Lu Jingbai again?"

She said nonchalantly,

"If that makes you feel at ease, then go ahead and do it. He's just an employee, so it won't take much effort on you."

Han Yu remained calm and composed; who knew how much of her words were true and how much were false?

"Actually, I've never even held hands with Lu Jingbai."

He Ran turned to look at him.

"Han Yu, you either need to change your bad temper, or you'd better get rid of all the women around me."

Every time she finished arguing with Han Yu, He Ran felt incredibly exhausted. She didn't know what was wrong with her lately; she just couldn't control her emotions. The wisest choice when dealing with Han Yu was to remain calm and observe; perhaps she shouldn't be so aggressive. In the second half of the game, Han Yu left with a glamorous girl in his arms, leaving only He Ran and his friends in the billiard room. With Han Yu gone, He Ran saw no need to stay any longer. She put on her trench coat, emerged from the room, and breathed a sigh of relief. Walking along the corridor, He Ran made a phone call to Xiao Han.

"Did you go back?"

"No."

Where?

"Downstairs from you."

He Ran came out of the side door of the club and saw Xiao Han standing under the lamppost by the roadside. She walked quickly towards him and handed him his coat, saying,

"Put this on quickly."

The temperature dropped at night, and He Ran's breath steamed with every word she spoke. Watching Xiao Han get dressed, she reached out and touched the back of his hand. Strangely, even after standing outside for so long, his hand was still warm.

"Shall we go to your place tonight?"

He Ran asked. Xiao Han nodded.

"Okay."

"Let's wait a bit before we go. I'm a little tired right now."

He Ran sighed, leaned forward, and gently nestled into his arms.

"Let me lean on you for a bit."

Xiao Han stood there without moving, and He Ran said again,

"Lower yourself a little."

Xiao Han bent down as instructed, and the height was just right. He Ran rested her chin on his shoulder and squinted for a while. He didn't ask her what she was doing there, and she didn't ask him why he had been waiting there. Time seemed to slow down as Xiao Han's tall figure enveloped He Ran. From the far end of the street, the two figures appeared to overlap completely, forming a harmonious whole. He Ran cherished this moment of peace, both physically and mentally. In the cramped second-floor room, two bodies were wrapped in thick blankets. He Ran abandoned the pillow and nestled her face against Xiao Han's chest. The outdoor temperature was only five or six degrees Celsius, yet a thin layer of sweat had formed on her body from the rising temperature inside the blankets. Staring blankly at the condensation on the window for a while, He Ran suddenly propped her chin up and asked,

"Xiao Han, how much money do you have?"

"What?"

"I'm asking you how much savings you have."

Xiao Han paused for a moment, then asked,

"Do you need money?"

No. Xiao Han looked at her, puzzled:

"Then why ask that?"

"I'm just asking, what, can't I say?"

"No."

Xiao Han estimated for a long time before saying,

"About tens of thousands of yuan."

"Only tens of thousands?"

He Ran asked.

"Um."

"Is this much money enough for you to support yourself?"

"enough."

"He's still too young and has a weak constitution. Every time he comes here, he catches a cold or fever because he can't adapt to the environment,"

Xiao Han paused and said,

"We'll bring him over after he finishes elementary school."

He Ran then asked,

"So you're saving up for his junior high school tuition?"

"Junior high is no problem, now we're working on high school."

"Do you have enough money?"

"That's enough."

After a pause, He Ran suddenly said,

"What if you still want to support me?"

Xiao Han was stunned for a moment,

"What?"

"I'm asking you,"

He Ran repeated,

"would your money be enough to support me?"

Xiao Han reacted a little slowly, staring at her for a few seconds before nodding,

"That's enough."

He Ran couldn't help but laugh, disagreeing:

"You only have tens of thousands of yuan. You have to support yourself, pay Quan Quan's tuition, and support me. How can that be enough?"

Xiao Han hesitated for a moment, then said,

"I have some money with Fatty."

He Ran asked, puzzled,

"What do you mean?"

Xiao Han said,

"When Fatty opened his fast food restaurant, he borrowed money from me, saying he would give me a share of the profits every year."

"Oh, I see."

He Ran nodded thoughtfully. Although the fast food restaurant was small, it was extremely popular, and the profits he received from it were definitely more than what Xiao Han earned from gardening and hairdressing.

"Then why didn't you take it?"

He Ran asked. Xiao Han said,

"I have enough money to live on on my own. I don't know what to do with the money I take, so I'll just leave it with him for now."

"You mean..."

He Ran smiled,

"If you want to support me, you'll have to use your hidden wealth beforehand?"

After thinking carefully for a while, Xiao Han said,

"I can ask Fatty to return the money to me. I want to open a flower shop and then we can live a good life together."

The last half-sentence left He Ran in a daze for a moment. When she came to her senses, she smiled and said,

"It's good to have a flower shop."

She continued,

"But I don't need you to support me, so you should save your money for now."

In the darkness, it was impossible to see the expression on Xiao Han's face; all that could be seen was that he turned his head to the side and remained silent. He Ran leaned closer and gently stroked his cheek.

"What's wrong? Are you unhappy?"

Xiao Han said in a muffled voice,

"No."

He Ran placed her hand on the back of his hand, pressed her whole body against it, and said with a smile,

"If you have it, you have it, why pretend?"

Xiao Han didn't answer. He asked in a low voice,

"Do you talk to other men like this?"

"What attitude?"

Xiao Han glanced at her up and down, his meaning unspoken. He Ran pursed her lips and said after a few seconds,

"I don't usually take the initiative to talk to other men."

"Why?"

"There's nothing to talk about,"

she answered simply, then winked at him.

"I only like you."

"..."

Xiao Han suddenly recalled the scene when he first met He Ran. The way she looked at him from below the steps was beyond her age, calm and reserved, yet it also carried a subtle sense of purpose. Thinking about it now, she was indeed prepared; she had already washed her hair but specifically asked him to wash it again.

"You're a bit precocious,"

Xiao Han said, expressing his thoughts. He Ran smiled gently,

"You just realized that?"

The night was deep, and all was quiet in the cold season. He Ran yawned unconsciously and began to feel sleepy. She got off Xiao Han, lay back in her seat, and murmured,

"If you really open a flower shop, remember to send me flowers after I graduate."

Xiao Han turned his head to look at her. After she graduates... How many years later is that?

"good."

---

The next morning, Xiao Chen was preparing breakfast in the kitchen when He Ran was woken up by the vibration of her phone on the bedside table. Still sleepy, she picked up her phone, got out of bed, and walked towards the kitchen.

"It's your call."

He Ran went back to the bedside, found her glasses and put them on. Then she leaned against the wall, brushing her teeth while listening to Xiao Han on the phone. She could only vaguely understand the dialect Xiao Han was speaking. Five minutes later, Xiao Han hung up the phone. He Ran asked casually,

"Is it a phone call from home?"

Xiao Han seemed to still be pondering the contents of the phone call, his expression serious. Seeing his expression, He Ran asked,

"What's wrong?"

Xiao Han collected his thoughts and said,

"It was my sister-in-law who called."

He Ran was taken aback.

"Quan Quan's mother?"

"Um."

Why did she suddenly call you? Xiao Han said,

"She regretted it and said she wanted to take back custody of Quan Quan."

He Ran frowned briefly.

"Didn't you refuse to take Quan Quan with you before? Why did you suddenly change your mind?"

Xiao Han slowly told her the details:

"Before, it was her husband's daughter who firmly refused. Now that we heard that the daughter had an accident, they plan to adopt another boy and thought of Quan Quan."

He Ran asked,

"So what do you mean?"

Xiao Han pondered for a moment and said,

"She asked me to meet her this afternoon to talk, but I need to think about it for a while longer."

He Ran said,

"Quan Quan is still young, you need to keep a close eye on him."

Xiao Han nodded.

"Mm."

In the afternoon, Xiao Han asked He Ran if she wanted to go with him. He Ran was usually the least willing to deal with this kind of thing, but thinking that she would be too bored staying at home alone, and that it concerned Quan Quan's future so she couldn't be careless, she finally decided to go with Xiao Han. The meeting place was arranged at Xiao Han's sister-in-law's house, located in a decent residential area in Liwan District. It had four bedrooms, two living rooms, and a terrace, making it a fairly well-off family. As soon as Xiao Han and He Ran entered the house, his sister-in-law led them into the living room and warmly served them tea. His sister-in-law stared at He Ran curiously for a while, then said uncertainly,

"...Who is this?"

Xiao Han introduced her succinctly:

"Girlfriend."

His sister-in-law belatedly realized what was going on, let out a long

"Oh,"

and then laughed,

"Finally, you have a girlfriend! That's a good thing."

She then turned to He Ran and said,

"Your younger sister looks so young. How old are you this year?"

He Ran smiled politely at her and said,

"I'm twenty-three."

His sister-in-law nodded, and Xiao Han gave He Ran a strange look but said nothing.

"Are you home alone?"

Xiao Han asked. His sister-in-law said,

"Yes, Feifei said she wanted to get some fresh air, so her dad pushed her out for a walk."

After chatting about trivial matters, the two finally started discussing Quan Quan. They were speaking Chongqing dialect, and He Ran became a mere decoration, quietly observing her surroundings. Opposite the sofa was a beige background wall, with a moderately sized LCD screen hanging in the center. A corner of a family photo was faintly reflected on the black screen. He Ran turned around and looked up at the family photo framed on the wall. The photo frame is large, at least a meter in length and width, and very eye-catching. In the photo, three people sit side by side, smiling. The male head of the household was fairly good-looking, wearing gold-rimmed glasses. His daughter resembled her father, with kind-looking features. The family photo must have been taken before the girl's accident; she looked perfectly fine in the picture. He Ran's gaze lingered on the girl's face for a long time, her brows twitching imperceptibly. Xiao Han's sister-in-law noticed He Ran's gaze and introduced with a smile,

"This is my husband, and this is his daughter Feifei. Sigh..."

She paused, her tone filled with regret,

"Feifei accidentally broke her leg a few years ago, and later her eyes became infected. Otherwise, she would be in college now."

No sooner had he finished speaking than the sound of the door lock turning was heard, and the two people he had mentioned returned. The girl named Feifei was blind, and the injury to her leg had left her with a chronic condition, so she had to be carefully helped in by her father. Xiao Han followed his sister-in-law to greet them, while He Ran remained seated. The girl was clearly very averse to the fact that she was about to have a younger brother. She didn't give Xiao Han a friendly look and angrily locked the door when she returned to her room as a form of protest. He Ran watched Xiao Han's expression of being turned away from afar and couldn't help but smile. Since there was still some time before dinner, they only had afternoon tea at Xiao Han's sister-in-law's house before leaving. On the way back, Xiao Han asked He Ran,

"What do you think of that family?"

He Ran thought for a while and said,

"Your sister-in-law seems alright, but I didn't say much to that man, so I don't know what kind of person he is. And his daughter..."

At this point, He Ran paused for a long time before speaking again and concluding directly:

"It's better not to let Quan Quan go. That girl has a bad temper, and it won't be easy for you to get along with her in the future."

Xiao Han said,

"Girls from only-child families tend to be a bit willful."

"It's not a matter of being a little willful."

He Ran's tone unconsciously became a little heavier.

"She's so much older than Quan Quan, if she bullies him, Quan Quan will have nowhere to cry."

Xiao Han didn't reply, but stared at her quietly for a while.

"You're acting a bit strange today."

He Ran remained silent, turning her head to face the window; she had noticed it herself. The transparent car window reflected a face devoid of expression. Her gaze was unfocused, and after a long pause, she slowly said,

"I know that girl; she was my high school classmate."

"Xiao Han."

She took a soft breath, then continued,

"I pushed her down the stairs."

She turned to meet Xiao Han's eyes and repeated what she had just said, her voice flat:

"I pushed Xu Yafei down the stairs."

Despite receiving such harsh words, the pair of eyes opposite her simply looked at her quietly, without any irritation or reproach. Xiao Han seemed to be waiting for her to give a reasonable explanation. But there was no explanation. At that moment, a fleeting thought of revenge seized He Ran's mind. She wanted to see Xu Yafei tumble down the stairs, so she reached out and pushed her forcefully. There was no excuse; it was purely to satisfy her desire for revenge. Of Han Yu's many girlfriends, Xu Yafei was the only one He Ran had ever slept with, and she also suffered the worst fate. He Ran still remembers that night when she was locked in the equipment room of the gymnasium and kept there all night. The surroundings were cold, damp, and pitch black. Squeaks came from the corners, and cockroaches occasionally scurried across her feet; everything was repulsive. He Ran felt disgusted by these pests, but not afraid of them. She had experienced similar encounters many times before and was used to it.

But that day was unusual. He Ran's aunt was critically ill, and she had planned to visit her in the hospital after school. This visit might be the last time she saw her aunt.
