After struggling to carry the basket full of basketballs into the equipment room, she had just straightened up when the iron door behind her slammed shut without warning, accompanied by Xu Yafei's smug laughter from outside. At that moment, He Ran was truly anxious, and for the first time, she begged Xu Yafei to let her out. The latter, however, laughed heartily and said, "You actually begged me? That's hilarious! He Ran, say what you just said again, I want to record it and play it for Han Yu!" He Ran did not speak again.

---

The next morning, the school's physical education teacher found her and let her out. She ran to the hospital without stopping, panting heavily as she pushed open the door to the familiar ward, only to find a stranger on the bed. He Ran did not get to see her aunt one last time. Xu Yafei is no good, and neither is He Ran. Although He Ran knew that all the trouble stemmed from Han Yu, she couldn't do anything about him, so she vented her anger on. She disliked those petty pranks, so she directly sent Xu Yafei to meet the local earth god. She just hadn't expected things to escalate to the point of causing her to lose her sight. He Ran didn't tell Xiao Han the details; she only told him the result. Time seemed to stand still as the wheels rolled over a large pothole, causing a violent jolt. He Ran twisted her slightly sore neck and said,

"Do you think I'm a bad person?"

Xiao Han continued to stare at her, his gaze calm. After waiting for a while, his answer was intriguing:

"You've always been a little bad."

"..."

He Ran did not comment, nor did she want to delve into the matter with him. After a while, she said,

"Anyway, I don't agree with sending Quan Quan to their home. Xu Yafei will bully him."

Xiao Han nodded and said,

"I understand."

When the car arrived at the entrance of Xiaozhou Village, they stood up and got off one after another. Xiao Han walked in front; he was a little thirsty, and after getting off the car, he went straight into a supermarket across the street. He gave He Ran a bottle of mineral water and two bottles of yogurt. After paying the bill, He Ran only took one bottle and said,

"One glass is enough for me, you can drink the other one."

As she spoke, she slowly tore off the lid of the cup, licked the top layer of milkshake clean, and then drank the yogurt in the cup. Xiao Han watched her from the side, wanting to reach out and touch her head, but in the end he withdrew his hand. Sometimes she can be a little naughty, but most of the time, she gives off a quiet and well-behaved vibe, making people want to gently hug her and protect her. Half of the holiday had already passed in the blink of an eye, and He Ran hadn't even started her winter break homework. She couldn't stay cooped up with Xiao Han all day anymore. She had planned to go home after having dinner at his place that evening, but just as she reached the barbershop, she received a call from Han Yu—

"Come out for dinner tonight."

After hanging up the phone, He Ran looked up at Xiao Han standing in front of the steps and sighed softly,

"I won't be having dinner with you tonight."

Xiao Han asked in a low voice,

"What's wrong?"

He Ran said,

"Nothing much, just a friend asked me to come."

Xiao Han thought for a moment, then nodded slightly and said,

"Then go ahead."

He Ran smiled and said,

"Okay, see you later."

He Ran walked back along the road she had come from, and reached the intersection in about ten minutes. While waiting for a taxi, a woman with a child came up to her and asked for directions.

"Hello, could you tell me how to get to Xiaozhou Village?"

The person who asked the question was a rather pretty woman with a Sichuan accent. She was probably in her early thirties and dressed very fashionably, which made her look quite out of place standing in this desolate place overgrown with weeds. He Ran didn't like women who were pretty but not very bright. She pointed to the ground and said,

"This is it."

"Oh!"

The woman suddenly realized, and smiled at her somewhat embarrassedly,

"Thank you."

He Ran pursed her lips meaningfully,

"No need."

After thanking the woman, she led the child toward Xiaozhou Village. As she was leaving, He Ran inexplicably turned back to take another look at her profile. Was it just her imagination? She felt like she'd seen him somewhere before. This problem troubled He Ran the entire way, and she was still frequently distracted even after seeing Han Yu. Distracted, He Ran followed Han Yu to the door of the private dining room, only then realizing that tonight's dinner was actually a reunion of Han Yu's junior high school classmates. He Ran didn't know why Han Yu had invited her to his class reunion, as she wasn't familiar with the people in his class. Han Yu was indeed the school bully back in the day. The moment he pushed open the door and walked into the private room, he immediately caused a small stir. Actually, Han Yu was quite popular. He was more loyal than anyone else when dealing with his group of brothers. However, he was unpredictable and unreliable when dealing with He Ran, and he was like a piece of chewing gum that you couldn't shake off. At first, no one recognized He Ran, and Han Yu didn't seem inclined to introduce her to everyone. He Ran sat quietly for a while, then quietly left the room to get some buffet food from the lobby. While waiting in line for sashimi, someone walked up behind her, tapped her on the shoulder, and asked in a somewhat uncertain tone,

"Are you... He Ran?"

He Ran was still immersed in the chance encounter that afternoon. When she heard someone call her name, she paused for two seconds before turning around. The person speaking to her was one of Han Yu's junior high school classmates, a girl with slightly curly hair. He Ran vaguely remembered her and nodded, saying,

"Yes, that's me."

When the girl saw that she had admitted it, she smiled quite excitedly and introduced herself, saying,

"My name is Zhou Tian, ​​do you remember me?"

He Ran's gaze lingered on her face for a few more seconds before she shook her head and said,

"Sorry, I don't remember."

"Hehe, it's normal that you don't remember me, I've changed quite a bit."

The girl named Zhou Tian was quite talkative and didn't seem to mind He Ran's coldness at all. She quickly started a new topic:

"Are you better now?"

Seemingly realizing her wording wasn't precise enough, she pointed to He Ran's hair again and said,

"What I mean is, your hair has grown so long, has chemotherapy ended?"

He Ran didn't answer the question. Instead, she asked in surprise,

"Did you see me back then?"

"Yes."

Zhou Tiantian nodded and said,

"That time I accompanied Han Yu to the hospital to see you, but he didn't let me into the ward. I just peeked at you from outside the door."

. Qin Zao then tentatively said,

I left the coffee shop; it was still early. The weather was nice today—the sun was shining warmly, and the wind was gentle and the clouds were clear. "When did you arrive?"

"noon."

Have you eaten yet? He Ran shook her head:

"Not yet."

The two of sat at opposite ends, each with a large bowl of noodles. Xiao Han made plain noodles in a simple broth, without adding anything, and the taste was neither too salty nor too bland. He Ran finished it quickly. The weather has been gradually warming up these past few days. He Ran reached out and touched a thin layer of water droplets on the table, and said casually,

"It's starting to get damp."

"Hmm."

Xiao Han nodded and said,

"It's already February, and the humid season is almost here."

Upon hearing his words, He Ran seemed to remember something, and smiled softly as she looked down,

"It's been almost a year."

Xiao Han asked, puzzled,

"What do you mean, 'a year'?"

Women always have their own little secrets, He Ran stuck out her tongue, unwilling to tell her:

"It's nothing."

After finishing their noodles, Xiao Han was about to clear the table when He Ran grabbed his hand and eagerly pulled him upstairs. She sat down on the edge of the bed and began unbuttoning her coat. Xiao Han also took off his clothes, but instead of getting straight to the point, he said,

"I'm going to take a shower first; I sweated a lot today."

He Ran followed him closely, smiling,

"Then let's shower together."

Upon hearing this, Xiao Han turned around and glanced at her, neither refusing nor agreeing. The bathroom was too crowded, so they stood facing each other and could feel the heat emanating from each other. The shower gel was finished, leaving only a bar of soap. Xiao Han handed it to He Ran, but He Ran didn't reach out to take it. Instead, she squinted and said,

"You help me wash."

Xiao Han stared at her, motionless. He Ran smiled, her eyes gleaming mischievously.

Xiao Han thought for a moment, then nodded and said,

"Okay."

He made her turn around, took the soap and started lathering her from behind, while holding a bath towel in his other hand. He was truly focused on giving her a bath, and naturally used a lot of force, almost scrubbing off a layer of He Ran's skin. He Ran winced in pain and yelled,

"Why are you using so much force? Are you rolling dough?"

Xiao Han was amused by her,

"How can you get it clean without putting in some effort?"

He Ran hummed softly,

"Then be gentle."

The noise in the bathroom finally subsided, and Xiao Han carried her out of the bathroom and laid her flat on the bed. He had dried the water droplets off her body. The night breeze was a bit chilly, and He Ran couldn't help but shiver when she first came into contact with the cold air. Xiao Han wrapped her in the blanket. He Ran stared at him intently, her eyes moist, still warm from the lingering kiss. Xiao Han changed his angle and deepened the kiss.

"What are you doing here?"

"I..."

"Where's Xiao Han?"

"oh"

and came down the stairs. Only when she got closer did she notice Qin Zao's injured face, a cut near her cheekbone. Turning her head to look at her daughter, she saw that her hair was also disheveled, and she looked like she had suffered. He Ran frowned and asked,

"What's wrong with you?"

Qin Zao said in a low voice,

"Someone is causing me trouble."

"Who?"

He Ran asked. Qin Zao avoided eye contact, refusing to continue.

He Ran waited calmly, then tried a different approach to get information out of her:

"..."

"It was his wife who came to cause me trouble."

He Ran had heard Xiao Han recount Qin Zao's past once, and she remembered most of it. She sat down opposite Qin Zao and said calmly,

"You've been with him for so many years, and you can't even handle this little thing?"

"He used to always protect me, but now that he's in jail, he can't care about anything else, and his wife..."

He Ran blinked, understanding dawning on her. People like them were like concubines in the imperial harem; while the emperor was by their side, they enjoyed boundless glory. But when the emperor left the palace, their power was gone, and they didn't even know how they died. With a sigh, He Ran asked,

"I have no money."

He Ran couldn't believe it.

"He used to have them, but his wife took them all back."

"early"

? Xiao Han invited them over for breakfast. His arrival finally allowed Qin Zao to escape He Ran's interrogation. She breathed a sigh of relief and took her daughter upstairs to wash her hands first. Xiao Han glanced at He Ran, but before he could explain, He Ran spoke first:

After they finished eating, a few customers came into the shop, mostly elderly people who lived nearby, asking Xiao Han to cut their hair. Xiao Han thought for a moment and said,

"Then let's grow flowers. Roses and gerberas are good too... but roses are not easy to grow."

He looked at He Ran, seemingly deep in thought, and finally made the decision for her:

"Let's grow gerberas."

He Ran asked,

"Why?"

"Chrysanthemums suit you quite well."

He Ran's face darkened, thinking he was joking with her, and she glared at him. Xiao Han said seriously,

"I heard that student recite a poem in the park before."

. For the next two days, He Ran stayed home quietly and did her homework. When people are busy, they don't have time to think about anything else, but her sleep was terrible for the past two days, and she had many dreams. In the end, He Ran still decided to make the call to Han Yu:

He Ran insisted on her opinion, her tone firm:

"Xiao Han, I know my own body. There's no need for a blood test. Just take some fever reducers and anti-inflammatory drugs."

He could hear the stubbornness in her voice, sighed helplessly, and finally turned to the doctor and said,

"Then please prescribe some medicine first."

On the way back, He Ran sat in the back seat, swaying unsteadily. Xiao Han held her hand, his brow furrowed with worry.

"Why didn't you listen to the doctor? Wouldn't a blood test and an injection have helped you recover faster?"

He Ran coughed weakly a few times, then remained silent.

"41°C."

Xiao Han said with lingering fear,

"Luckily I found out early, otherwise your brain would have been fried."

He Ran grinned, reached out and hugged his waist, saying nonchalantly,

"It's good if your brain gets damaged, then you won't remember anyone else and will only follow you around."

Xiao Han stared at her for a few moments, then hugged her tightly.

"Little girl, don't scare me."

At the end of February, the wind was gentle and the clouds were light, and the weather was quietly warming up. He Ran had been standing downstairs at the apartment building for a while, glancing down at her watch for what seemed like the umpteenth time. When the clock struck 9:30, she finally met the person she was looking for. Qin Zao walked in from the side gate of the community carrying two bags of groceries, her head down, seemingly lost in thought. He Ran walked up to her, tapped her high heels on the ground, and said,

"Hi."

Qin Zao looked up and saw her, then froze. He Ran pouted and said,

"Going to buy groceries so early?"

"Hmm..."

Qin Zao hadn't come to her senses yet, and nodded blankly. He Ran smiled slightly,

"Aren't you going to invite me up for a seat?"

Sitting down on the sofa, He Ran carefully examined the duplex apartment. The house is spacious, with a terrace, and is quite bright. The decoration is luxurious, and the furniture is clearly from designer brands. But these are just appearances. The LCD TV on the wall was cracked, the sofa was scraped, the potted plants outside the balcony were overturned and soil was scattered everywhere, and the corner was piled with broken glass. It seems Qin Zao wasn't lying about this; she really has gotten herself into trouble. After looking around, He Ran looked away. At this moment, Qin Zao slowly came out of the kitchen, carrying two glasses of water, and placed them on the coffee table.

"Please have some."

He Ran didn't stand on ceremony. She picked up her cup and slowly blew on it before asking,

"Where's your daughter?"

Qin Zao replied,

"She's still sleeping."

He Ran took a sip of water and said casually,

"Did someone come and smash things again last night? The kids must have been scared."

Qin Zao's expression changed upon hearing this, and she bit her lower lip tightly without saying a word. Noticing her expression, He Ran didn't say anything more. She took a card out of her handbag and slowly placed it on the table.

"Can you explain the reason to me?"

Qin Zao fixed her gaze for a moment, then lowered her head even further and remained silent.

"If you don't want to accept this money, just give it back to me."

He Ran's tone remained calm, but there was a distance between them.

"Why did you have to bring this to Xiao Han's attention?"

"..."

She hit the nail on the head:

"You still can't forget him?"

Qin Zao remained silent. After a brief silence, He Ran said slowly,

"Then I'll ask you one last time, are you willing to leave?"

Qin Zao opened her mouth stiffly, but couldn't utter a single sound. Her expression said it all, and He Ran understood. She put the card back in her bag, stood up and left, telling her,

"I won't force you to leave if you don't want to. You'd better think twice before you go."
---
title: 'Chapter Six: First Clues'
chapter: 68
wordCount: 1022

He Ran walked briskly to the entrance, turned around as if to say something more, but suddenly felt weak in her limbs. Her body tilted, and the world before her eyes was instantly swallowed by darkness. Before she closed her eyes, she saw Qin Zao scream and rush towards her. When she opened her eyes again, she was faced with a stark white ceiling. The smell of disinfectant in the air reminded He Ran where she was. Her eyes slowly moved to the other side and she saw Han Yu standing beside the hospital bed. Han Yu's eyebrows were thick, and they were especially noticeable when he furrowed them. He asked anxiously,

"Are you feeling any better?"

He Ran didn't answer, and squinted to adjust to the bright light in front of her. She asked him in return,

"What are you doing here?"

Han Yu explained,

"Someone called, and then your mom contacted me and asked me to come pick you up."

He Ran softly said

"Oh,"

gradually regaining her lost memories. Yes, she had indeed fainted at Qin Zao's house. He Ran looked around and asked,

"Where's the woman who called you?"

Han Yu said,

"I didn't let her in from outside the ward."

He Ran pursed her lips, lost in thought. Han Yu, still uneasy, asked with a sense of urgency,

"What happened? You were fine before, why did it suddenly flare up again?"

He Ran glanced at him, but replied calmly,

"Don't make a fuss, I just have a fever."

Han Yu still didn't dare to take any chances and said,

"I'll have Dr. Xue examine you again later."

He Ran remained noncommittal. She got up, got out of bed, put on the slippers provided for patients, and walked to the door. Han Yu followed worriedly,

"Where are you going?"

"The restroom."

He Ran turned around and said,

"Don't follow me."

He Ran opened the ward door, and Qin was already standing outside, chatting with several nurses. He stopped when he saw He Ran come out. After the nurse left, Qin Zao secretly glanced at He Ran, her eyes hesitant. He Ran remained expressionless, her eyes fixed straight ahead:

"Ask whatever you want."

Qin Zao hesitated for a moment before speaking:

"Sister, I heard them say...you, you have...leukemia?"

He Ran lowered her eyelids and gave a nonchalant

"hmm"

. Qin Zao opened his mouth wide, not knowing what to say. He Ran sat down on a stool against the wall and said casually,

"I had it before, but it's cured."

Upon hearing this, Qin Zao seemed to breathe a sigh of relief, but two seconds later He Ran continued,

"However, the possibility of a relapse cannot be ruled out."

Qin Zao immediately fished up again, her eyes wide open. He Ran couldn't help but laugh,

"What kind of reaction is that?"

Qin Zao lowered her eyes, her expression intriguing:

"I...I still hope that you can be alright."

He Ran glanced at it again, then smiled and said,

"Thank you."

Qin Zao remained silent for a long time before asking,

"Does Xiao Han... know about this?"

He Ran said,

"I don't know."

"Aren't you going to tell him?"

Qin Zao looked at her in surprise.

"He doesn't understand this disease, and knowing it will only increase his worries."

He Ran rubbed his temples, looking tired.

"It's not too late to tell him if it's really diagnosed as a relapse."

Qin Zao probably wanted to say something, but after glancing at He Ran several times, he swallowed his words back. He Ran was allowed to be discharged from the hospital the next day after her fever subsided. In the days that followed, she was quite weak and stayed home to recover. Han Yu hired a nanny to stay by her bedside day and night. Rather than taking care of her, it was more like surveillance, and He Ran couldn't even leave the house. Yang Wenping and He Jin returned from out of town over the weekend. That evening, He Ran had little appetite and went straight upstairs to rest without eating dinner. Before going to sleep, Yang Wenping pushed open her bedroom door and came in to check on her. He Ran felt her sit down on the edge of her bed, hold her breath slightly, and turn her back to her. Yang Wenping said,

"I know you're not asleep, you don't need to pretend."

Old ginger is spicier; after all, she is He Ran's mother. He Ran simply opened her eyes and asked,

"What is it?"

Yang Wenping sat down by her bedside, softened her tone, and inexplicably started talking to He Ran about some seemingly unimportant things:

"Someone from higher up has been arrested, and your father has also been implicated. We've run into a lot of trouble lately and haven't had a good night's sleep for many days. We haven't had any extra time to care about you either."

"Fortunately, with the help of Han Yu's father, many thorny issues have been resolved, and we should be able to get through this difficult time soon."

At this point, Yang Wenping's voice suddenly turned cold and sharp:

"On the condition that you don't do anything stupid."

"Men can have a wild time outside, but women absolutely cannot cheat on men, understand?"

These words were full of double meaning, and Yang Wenping's eyes held an even deeper meaning. He Ran simply replied calmly,

"Oh."

"Don't always be so perfunctory."

Yang Wenping couldn't help but frown.

"I'm not unaware of your little tricks, I just choose not to call you out on them."

He Ran said without changing her expression,

"I didn't try anything clever."

Yang Wenping snorted and asked,

"Who are you going to see every few days when you run off?"

He Ran replied,

"Friend."

Seeing that she was lying and wouldn't admit it, Yang Wenping simply brought it up directly:

"Who is that man in the surveillance video? Should I go and check?"

He Ran took a deep breath, and her voice finally became more even,

"That's enough, stop talking."
---

"Okay, I won't say more, but you need to know what's important."

Yang Wenping tucked her in, stood up, and said softly,

"School is about to start, so you should rest well at home these few days and not go anywhere."

She took a few steps outside, then turned back to look at He Ran, and said the last sentence:

"When you can't control yourself, just think about what happened to your second cousin."

The door closed gently, and the room fell silent again. Without turning on the lights, He Ran turned to look at the white moonlight outside the window and let out a long sigh. She had never been so tired before; she wished she could just close her eyes and never wake up again. He Ran turned over, and it seemed that there was still a faint scent of Xiao Han between the blankets and sheets. She buried her nose and hair in the scent, inhaling deeply, reluctant to leave. Xiao Han... Xiao Han. In her second month in Beijing, the gerbera daisy He Ran had bought finally bloomed. It had only been a bud at first, but when she checked on it a few days later, it had somehow blossomed into a cluster of flowers. As a result, it finally gained He Ran's attention. He Ran moved it to her desk and kept it by the window so that she could see it whenever she looked up while painting. In the afternoon, a gentle breeze blew, and the shadows of the petals swayed and danced on the drawing paper, seemingly close yet distant, looking like a pair of lovers entwined. For some reason, He Ran recalled someone's inadequate words,

"When my flowers bloom, all others wither."

He paused slightly, then couldn't help but smile. After that, she kept getting distracted and couldn't calm down anymore. Unable to create a satisfactory work, He Ran angrily tore off the drafts one by one, crumpled them all into balls, and threw them into the trash can. With two hours to go before her doctor's appointment, He Ran decided to leave early. She grabbed a coat and headed out the door, but in her frustration, she accidentally kicked over a trash can, spilling out scraps of paper and fruit peels. He Ran ignored them, too lazy to clean them up. While waiting for the elevator, He Ran happened to run into the landlord couple. The man must have done something wrong to offend the woman, because she had a sour face and refused to talk to him. The man kept pleading,

"Oh, my dear, please don't be angry!"

The woman snapped,

"Hmph, which one is your youngest son! Get out, get out of here!"

He Ran stood to the side watching them flirt and banter as if no one else was around, wanting to laugh but unable to. At this moment, she finally understood what Xiao Han meant by

"youngest child,"

but unfortunately, it was a little too late. After waiting at the hospital for half an hour, He Ran's blood test results came back. Her white blood cell count had risen slightly, which was not a good sign. Fortunately, all other indicators were normal. The doctor advised He Ran to continue taking traditional Chinese medicine and observe her for a few more days. After leaving the hospital, He Ran drove straight home. Beijing is notorious for its traffic jams, especially during rush hour. He Ran drove slowly on the inner ring road, his right foot numb from pressing the brake pedal so hard. She deeply regretted driving herself; if she had taken a taxi, she could have taken a nap in the back seat. As they approached a traffic light, the phone on the passenger seat rang. He Ran glanced at it; it was her Guangzhou number, which she hadn't used in a long time. After arriving in Beijing, Xiao Han would call her about once a week. He Ran never answered, but she wouldn't hang up either. She would just listen quietly for the phone to ring, forty seconds to finish, and then let it hang up automatically. The red light was unusually long today. He Ran slumped over the steering wheel and let out a soft sigh. When she got home, she was completely exhausted. When she opened the door and saw the house full of trash, she had no desire to clean up at all. So she simply let herself go and collapsed onto the sofa to take a nap. When He Ran woke up, it was already evening. She decided to find a temporary worker to clean her room. She placed an order on a housekeeping website, and the worker arrived in about ten minutes. The cleaning lady was a woman in her fifties who seemed quite honest. He Ran felt comfortable leaving the housework to her, and then took her clothes to the bathroom to take a shower. Halfway through her shower, there was a sudden knock on the door. He Ran turned the water down and raised her voice, asking,

"What is it?"

The older woman outside the door said,

"Miss He, your phone is ringing."

He Ran said,

"It's okay, just leave it there, I'll pick it up later."

The kind-hearted woman said,

"I've already answered it for you."

"..."

He Ran was inevitably upset, but considering the other person's age, she couldn't blame them for making decisions on their own. She wrapped a bath towel around herself, opened the door a crack, and the older woman handed her a phone from the outside. He Ran said thank you and took it. He Ran paused slightly when her gaze fell on the contact's name. Except for the first week when she lost contact, Xiao Han would call her constantly, but afterwards he never called her twice in one day. Unless, he's really desperate. The call duration displayed on the phone screen was slowly ticking away, and He Ran unconsciously coughed lightly. Before her brain could process what to say, the person on the other end suddenly spoke,

"Taking a shower?"

The deep voice and the speed of his speech were familiar to He Ran. For a moment, a mix of inexplicable emotions welled up within her. Rough yet tender fingers, the lingering song in my ears, and the passionate embrace every night. After what seemed like an eternity, she finally responded softly,

"Mmm."

"Why didn't you answer the phone?"

Xiao Han's question was direct and abrupt. Just like when they stood under the streetlights in front of the Xiaozhou Village Hall, he asked her,

"Why did you hide it?"

He Ran could only respond with silence. A long silence. In the silence, Xiao Han's voice turned cold and hard:

"He Ran, are you going to back out again?"

He Ran didn't speak. She looked up at her face in the mirror—pale, empty, and devoid of emotion. Recently, her eyesight had started to deteriorate again, and she couldn't see herself clearly through a thick fog. Xiao Han's questions became increasingly pressing:

"You never intended to take responsibility for this relationship from the very beginning, did you?"

"So your age is fake, and your address is fake too, isn't it?"

he asked urgently, stubbornly demanding an answer.

"Isn't that right?"

He Ran still didn't respond. Her hearing gradually deteriorated, and she could only vaguely hear what the person on the phone was saying. She didn't know how long she was in a daze before she came to her senses. Just as she was about to hang up, she realized that Xiao Han had already done it. He Ran put her phone aside and continued taking a shower. The only answer he could receive was a sentence he could no longer hear:

"It used to be, it wasn't then, and now it has to be..."

From that day on, Xiao Han never called the phone again. He Ran also stopped using it, neither charging it nor topping up its credit.

---

A few days later, the phone shut down automatically. One month later, the number expired. That's fine too. It all started as a dream, and after indulging for too long, it's time to return to reality.
---

## Chapter Seven: Moths to a Flame

Previous page: He and she were like moths drawn to a flame, embracing each other with the determination to perish, yet trying to find a glimmer of hope on the road to ruin. In July, universities across the country begin their summer break. The Central Academy of Fine Arts (CAFA) bids farewell to a group of graduates and is about to welcome a new batch of faces. He Ran didn't return to Guangzhou this summer; instead, she stayed comfortably in Beijing. Ding Xiaoxu had come to visit her at the beginning of the month, supposedly to attend her boyfriend's graduation ceremony and drop by to see her, but due to some unforeseen circumstances at the university, the ceremony had to be postponed until the second half of the year. Ding Xiaoxu's trip was in vain; he spent most of Beijing time there before returning home. He Ran had just seen Ding Xiaoxu off when Han Yu arrived at the end of the month. He had already received his admission letter from the Communication University of China and came to the school early to check it out. He Ran then took him on a tour around Beijing. This summer, He Ran let Han Yu kiss her on the lips. Han Yu has had many girlfriends, but he still doesn't know how to kiss; he just rushes in headfirst. His kisses have no flavor, at least He Ran didn't notice it. She was numb and stiff throughout, and was thoroughly disliked by Young Master Han. Han Yu couldn't shake his playboy nature; he was fickle and always looking for new women. He Ran frequently saw the ambiguous text messages on his phone, each one from a different person. He has his freedom, and He Ran chooses to turn a blind eye. The day before school started, Han Yu asked He Ran to accompany him to the hair salon for a new hairstyle. Anyone who's good-looking and has a bit of money is bound to be arrogant and vain, and Han Yu is the most typical example. He and the stylist discussed it for ages but couldn't come to a conclusion, while He Ran, listening nearby, was getting drowsy. Eventually, she got impatient and decided to go upstairs to get her hair washed. The second floor was less crowded and much quieter, with dimmer lighting. A tall celadon vase was placed at the top of the stairs, and the sound of a guqin (a traditional Chinese stringed instrument) filled the hall. All the utensils were made of dark brown Chinese toon wood, exuding a strong sense of antiquity. He Ran recalled the name of the shop and belatedly realized that it was a chain of the hair salon where she first met Xiao Han. No wonder the decoration style was the same. She was led deep into the corridor by the guide. Han Yu had a VIP card, so she entered a VIP room. There were only two shampoo beds in the room, right next to each other; it was supposedly a couples' room. Several plates of fresh fruit were on the table. He Ran casually forked a piece of dragon fruit and put it in her mouth, then chose the nearest shampoo bed and lay down. The person washing her hair arrived two minutes later, by which time He Ran was already feeling lightly sleepy. She squinted and sensed someone deliberately walking in quietly. She was slightly puzzled, but remained lying quietly. The man entered the room without saying a word, walked straight to He Ran, and sat down behind her. He tucked a towel into He Ran's collar, gathered her hair with one hand, and turned on the shower with the other. The water was a little cold at first, and he adjusted it slowly. Is this temperature okay? Upon hearing this voice, He Ran suddenly opened her eyes and met a pair of clear black and white eyes above her. She opened her mouth slightly, as if about to say something, when Han Yu walked in. He Ran closed her eyes and mouth as if nothing had happened. Han Yu sat beside her, excitedly talking about how avant-garde the new hairstyle the stylist had designed for him was. Even though He Ran pretended to be asleep, it did not affect his high spirits at all. A few minutes later, He Ran suddenly said,

"I need to use the restroom."

Han Yu turned his head and asked,

"Do you have a restroom here?"

The girl who washed Han Yu's hair was very young. She nodded shyly and said,

"Yes."

The man who had been silent until now spoke up:

"I'll take you there."

He Ran said politely,

"Okay, thank you for your trouble."

The man rinsed the foam off her hair, wrapped it simply with a towel, and helped her sit up. He Ran put on her glasses and followed him out of the room. The restroom is at the end of this corridor, in the other direction. The man walked quickly, his legs seeming to fly, a speed imbued with certain emotions. He Ran followed at a leisurely pace, and the man had to stop and wait for her halfway there. He looked around and, seeing no one, simply grabbed her hand and started walking forward, forcing He Ran to quicken her pace. The two reached the restroom door, but the man didn't stop there. Instead, he pulled He Ran into a stall and locked the door. Then he pulled down one side of his mask, revealing his entire face. He didn't actually need to do that; He Ran knew it was him. He Ran will never forget those eyes that were darker than anyone else's. She remembered not only his eyes, but also the sound of his footsteps and the speed of his speech. He Ran leaned against the door, Xiao Han pressed close to her, staring down at her. Their bodies were so close that He Ran could feel the cold, hard buckle of his belt digging into her. The required attire for this job is a white shirt and black trousers. He Ran had considered what Xiao Han would look like in formal attire; at the time, she found it hard to imagine, but now it seemed perfectly appropriate. While she was thinking about these random things, the pair of dark eyes opposite her just kept staring at her without blinking, just like an eagle hiding in the shadows. Xiao Han suddenly leaned down, and sensing his intention, He Ran raised her foot, the tip of her high heel grinding against his toes. Xiao Han frowned, but remained silent. He Ran spoke up,

"Explain."

Xiao Han followed her words:

"Explain what?"

He Ran asked,

"Why are you here?"

Xiao Han opened his mouth and said something similar to what he said half a year ago, but completely different:

"You didn't look for me, so I came to look for you."

Six months ago, he said,

"I want to see if you will come looking for me if I don't."

Only he himself knows the changes in his mindset during that time. He Ran remained silent, but Xiao Han repeated himself, his tone resolute:

"I said I would come to Beijing to find you, so here I am."

He Ran remained silent, while Xiao Han's face slowly lowered again. They got closer, their breaths mingled, and his warm breath brushed against her skin and pores, carrying a hint of probing.
---

Just as their four lips were about to meet, He Ran whispered,

"I have a boyfriend."

Xiao Han paused slightly and stopped. He asked,

"The boy who came with you?"

"Um."

Xiao Han said,

"He's been here before, with other girls."

"I know."

"Knowing you still...!"

He Ran lowered her eyelids and said softly,

"Xiao Han, he is different from you."

He pulled back and stared at her intently, a heavy mist welling up in his eyes.

"What's different?"

He Ran did not answer. Xiao Han tried to discern a signal from her eyes, but the long wait yielded no response.

"You told me you wouldn't regret it, you clearly said that."

Xiao Han took a step back, his words filled with too many complex emotions. He Ran still didn't look at him, her expression indifferent:

"I don't regret being with you, but that doesn't mean we'll be together in the future."

Xiao Han gripped her wrist tightly, almost gritting his teeth as he said,

"Then you shouldn't have provoked me in the first place!"

Xiao Han left, and as he left, he punched the door panel behind He Ran's head. This was the first time she had ever seen him so obviously express his emotions. He Ran sat alone on the toilet, lost in thought for a long time before getting up and leaving. For the next few months, Xiao Han did not appear in front of He Ran. To be precise, He Ran had never inquired about Xiao Han's whereabouts, so she didn't know what he was doing or whether he had returned to Guangzhou. In October, Ding Xiaoxu came to Beijing again during the National Day holiday, this time to attend her boyfriend's graduation ceremony. In order to create a beautiful memory on the day of graduation photos, Ding Xiaoxu repeatedly asked He Ran to go shopping with her so she could buy some new clothes. Unfortunately, He Ran has been overwhelmed with club matters lately, and in order to save more time, she has moved back to the dormitory every day. She doesn't have any free time to go out shopping. Despite repeated rejections, Ding Xiaoxu persisted, and He Ran finally had no choice but to agree to spend an afternoon with her. They spent several hours shopping near the China World Trade Center, and Ding Xiaoxu finally bought a dress that suited her perfectly. He Ran, as the host, paid for the dress for her. Just as Ding Xiaoxu was about to head home, she suddenly remembered something else:

"Oh dear, I almost forgot to buy flowers! We can't forget the flowers!"

She turned to He Ran and asked,

"Is there a flower and bird market nearby?"

He Ran said yes, and she took her to the shop where she bought the gerberas, in Shilihe. The flower and bird market here is actually a large pedestrian street, covering a wide area, selling all sorts of miscellaneous items. From large wholesale stalls to small roadside vendors, it has everything. Although it was a flower and bird market, there were also many shops selling antiques and calligraphy and paintings. He Ran and Ding Xiaoxu were dazzled by the sights as they walked along. Even after the morning market hours, the streets were still crowded, and it was inevitable to bump into each other. Someone was handing out flyers on the roadside, and He Ran took one and casually threw it into a nearby trash can. Ding Xiaoxu looked through it carefully, then excitedly patted He Ran on the shoulder,

"Hey! Let's go to this place, they have a grand opening promotion, buy flowers and get a free pet!"

He Ran remained noncommittal.

"How are you going to bring the pet back to Guangzhou?"

Ding Xiaoxu was speechless for a moment, then said,

"Can I give it to my boyfriend to raise?"

He Ran thought for a moment and said,

"Then let's go take a look."

They found the flower shop by following the small map on the flyer. From a distance, it looked no different from other flower shops, but upon closer inspection, they noticed the difference. The flower shop entrance is surrounded by potted plants, each one glistening with dewdrops, a testament to the owner's meticulous care. The sign, however, is ridiculously simple, bearing only two square characters:

"Flower Shop."

What caught He Ran's attention were the several iron cages outside the door, containing a few kittens and puppies that were meowing happily. In front of the cages hung a row of brightly colored fish wrapped in transparent bags. In addition, there were several styrofoam boxes on the ground, some containing turtles, some containing hamsters, and some containing newborn chicks and ducklings. Is this a flower shop or a pet shop? He Ran felt a surge of curiosity and prepared to go inside the shop. Just as her hand touched the doorknob, she noticed a job posting on the glass door. The handwriting was clumsy; the character

"启"

(qǐ) was written as

"起"

(qǐ), crossed out, and rewritten. Ding Xiaoxu noticed it too and couldn't help but laugh out loud. He Ran pushed open the door and walked in,

"Let's go in and take a look."

However, she had only taken half a step inside when she was caught off guard and saw the person standing behind the counter— Xiao Han was focused on shaving a middle-aged man's head. At that moment, an untimely thought popped into her head:

"A gardener who doesn't sell ducks isn't a good hairdresser."

"Ouch!"

Chen Liang cried out in pain, clutching the back of his neck and glaring at Xiao Han.

"Old Xiao, take it easy!"

Xiao Han stared blankly ahead for several seconds before snapping out of it, slowly removing the razor.

"Oh."

Ding Xiaoxu stepped forward, looked at Chen Liang, then at Xiao Han, and asked,

"Which one of you is the boss?"

Xiao Han said,

"I am."

Ding Xiaoxu politely asked,

"Do you have any flowers suitable for graduates?"

"Yes, there is."

Xiao Han nodded and succinctly introduced it to her:

"Lily."

Ding Xiaoxu hesitated and said,

"Uh... I'm giving it to my boyfriend, so lilies aren't really appropriate."

Xiao Han changed his mind and said,

"Then let's send Fu Lang away."

Ding Xiaoxu then asked,

"What kind of flower is Fulang?"

Xiao Han walked a few steps to the flower stand, pointed to a pot of big red flowers and said,

"This is it, its scientific name is Gerbera, also known as sunflower."

Upon hearing this, He Ran, who was standing to the side, couldn't help but look at it a few more times. It was indeed exactly the same as the gerbera daisy she kept at home. Ding Xiaoxu asked curiously,

"Does this flower have any special meaning?"

Xiao Han said,

"Perseverance and fearlessness in the face of difficulties are why many graduates came to buy this flower recently."

Ding Xiaoxu was quite satisfied upon hearing this and immediately made a decision:

"Then I'll take this one. Can you make it into a bouquet for me? Decorate it nicely, and I'll come to pick it up tomorrow afternoon."

Xiao Han nodded and said,

"No problem."

Ding Xiaoxu went to the counter and paid the deposit. Xiao Han handed over a business card, saying,

"Contact me if you have any questions."

As he spoke, the business card was facing He Ran. Ding Xiaoxu joked with a smile,

"Boss, didn't I buy your flowers? You should give me this business card, right?"

Xiao Han said

"Oh,"

and subtly turned the business card toward Ding Xiaoxu. Ding Xiaoxu reached out and took it, then laughed and said,

"Thank you, boss."

"It's nothing."

As dusk falls and the forests are ablaze with color, the overlapping emeralds and flowing gold create the most beautiful scenery of the season. Although the items that were lost were not particularly valuable, the fact that such incidents occurred one after another was inevitably frightening. Several girls reported the case to the dormitory manager, but there has been no result, and the matter has to be put on hold for the time being. This was a suggestion from a traditional Chinese medicine doctor, and it has been very helpful in improving her condition. Under the blue sky and white clouds, she practiced her boxing while recalling what had happened last night. As the sound of rain disturbed her sleep, she suddenly heard a noise. "He Ran"

. The two walked to the stands around the playground and sat down to rest. He Ran took out her thermos and took a sip of water. After her lips were moistened, she asked,

They sat there for quite a while, gazing at the tall buildings in the distance, each lost in their own thoughts. Xiao Han was emitting a sour, foul odor, as if something had been splashed on his clothes. He Ran asked,

"Aren't you planning to go back to Guangzhou?"

Xiao Han said,

He Ran said,

"A flower shop can be opened anywhere."

"I'll be here from now on."

He Ran sighed almost imperceptibly,

"You shouldn't have come."

Xiao Han did not respond. He Ran continued,

"You'll realize later that this decision was wrong."

Xiao Han remained silent for a while, then his phone rang.

"love at first sight,"

only this man's kiss could ignite the deeper desire within her. Two figures embraced tightly under the streetlight. Time seemed to pass too slowly, as if one could reach the end of time with just a blink of an eye. He gently swirled the wine in his glass and said with a faint smile,

"He Ran, guess who I ran into today?"

He Ran raised her eyes and replied,

"Who?"

Han Yu gestured towards the bouquet of flowers placed next to her seat and said,

"You know the owner of this florist too."

He Ran's eyelids twitched, vaguely sensing that he was about to say something, but she remained still. Han Yu continued,

"When the boss came to deliver flowers today, I thought he looked familiar at first, but after taking a closer look, I realized that he was the same man I saw in Xiaozhou Village. You said he was your friend."

His words startled He Ran. Han Yu suffers from mild prosopagnosia, which isn't really a disease; arrogant people often have this problem. But the fact that he can still remember Xiao Han after so long, even his appearance, is clearly not a good thing. He Ran remained expressionless, simply replying with an

"oh,"

"You mean Xiao Han, right?"

"Has he come to Beijing? I haven't heard anything about it."

She cut off a piece of lobster and casually popped it into her mouth.

"I'll go see him next time I have the chance."

Han Yu smiled enigmatically,

"Sure, I'll go with you."

Halfway through dinner, He Ran's phone suddenly rang. She turned her head to look and saw a familiar-looking unknown number. She rarely adds people to her contacts, except for those she's very close to. The caller was Xiaobai, and He Ran answered the call in front of Han Yu. She had always been rather indifferent to her suitors, but this time she surprisingly chatted with them for quite a while. Ten minutes later, He Ran ended the call. Han Yu's mood was ruined, and he stared at her with an uncertain expression. The expected questioning arrived as scheduled. Who made the call?

"classmate."

"Male or female?"

"Male."

"Is he pursuing you?"

"yes."

As winter deepened, after days of persistent smog, the sky finally cleared up. Taking advantage of the fresh morning air, He Ran jogged slowly on the playground. The wind was gentle today, lacking its usual biting force, and swept lightly across the ground. A few dry leaves were carried by the wind and spread across the track, making a crisp sound as Xiao Han's shoes rolled over them. He quickened his pace slightly and caught up with He Ran, who was running slowly not far ahead. This is his fourth lap, and her second lap. Xiao Han stopped beside He Ran and realized that he could keep up with her simply by maintaining a brisk walking pace. He turned his head and tried to strike up a conversation with her again:

"Why did you suddenly start exercising?"

Unable to get a response, he had no choice but to chime in:

"This is a good habit, you should keep it up."

He Ran remained silent for a while, while Xiao Han continued to mutter to himself,

"Your shoelaces are loose, stop and tie them first."

He Ran continued running forward at a steady pace, completely ignoring him. Xiao Han knew she was determined not to talk to him. He sighed, looked away, quickened his pace, and started running his fifth lap. He Ran couldn't run anymore after finishing her second lap. She walked to the side of the stands, panting, and sat down. Seeing this, Xiao Han quickly followed her, handed her a bottle of mineral water, and said,

"Have some water."

He Ran avoided the question, saying,

"I have my own."

Xiao Han was taken aback.

"You've finally decided to talk to me."

He Ran unscrewed her thermos and took a few small sips. She slowly wiped her mouth and said coldly,

"I remember clearly saying that only I can contact you, and you can't come to me."

"Why can't I contact you?"

"What's the difference between you contacting me and me contacting you?"

"I came to bring you these gloves."

"That's the same."

"But you haven't contacted me for the past two weeks."

"So what if it's half a month? Can't you just bear with it?"

Xiao Han was speechless, his mouth half-open, then slowly closed. The two sat in silence for a few minutes. It was 8:30, and He Ran had to go back to take a shower and change her clothes. She stood up and said slowly,

"I'll come see you this weekend."

Xiao Han didn't react much, just gave a neutral

"hmm"

. He Ran took a few steps, then turned back to him and said,

"The gloves are very nice, thank you."

With a slight smile, she said,

"If you're not too busy, could you knit me another scarf?"

At this point, a slight expression finally appeared on Xiao Han's face, and he nodded in agreement:

"Okay."

After school, He Ran packed up her easel and prepared to leave the department when she was suddenly stopped at the door by someone. She looked up at the person who had come in and asked,

"Is there something you need?"

Xiao Bai stood in front of her, scratching his head shyly, and said,

"Um... He Ran, I'd like to invite you to a movie, is that alright?"

He Ran thought for a moment and said,

"I don't really want to watch a movie."

Unexpectedly rejected so easily, Xiaobai pinched her nose dejectedly,

"Fine then... never mind."

He Ran then added,

"But we can go see an art exhibition."

The person opposite paused for a moment, then smiled warmly.

"An art exhibition? You mean Master Fu's exhibition? I'm planning to go see it in the next few days!"

He Ran nodded:

"Yes."

"That's great!"

Xiaobai was overjoyed, but she suppressed her excitement and asked cautiously,

"When are you going? Let's go together."

He Ran said,

"I'm easy, you decide the time."

"Okay, okay."

Xiaobai nodded several times, then asked,

"Are you free tomorrow? Let's go tomorrow."

He Ran smiled and said,

"Okay, then see you tomorrow afternoon."

---

The next day after school, He Ran and Xiao Bai set off together, attracting a crowd of onlookers. He Ran greeted them confidently, while Xiao Bai, whether nervous or shy, couldn't utter a single word. Upon arriving at the art museum, He Ran put away her joking attitude and began to seriously appreciate each painting. Fu Jue is a recognized genius, young and promising, but unfortunately, he has been plagued by negative news, and not long ago, news of his extramarital affair surfaced. Perhaps influenced by public opinion, this exhibition was much quieter than usual, with only a handful of people attending. He Ran wasn't interested in the rumors; she focused solely on appreciating the paintings. Rumors may be false, but talent can be truly revealed through the artwork. Master Fu's painting skills truly deserve his reputation as the foremost oil painter in China. After viewing the art exhibition, it was nearly six o'clock. Xiaobai naturally invited He Ran to have dinner together, and He Ran did not refuse.

---

After dinner, Xiaobai took a longer route to take He Ran home. He hesitated several times along the way, but He Ran knew what he was thinking and didn't point it out.

When they arrived at her apartment building and were about to say goodbye, Xiaobai finally mustered the courage to speak:

"He Ran! I, I... I like you."

He became more and more nervous as he spoke, and by the end, his words were slurred:

"Would you... go out with me?"

He Ran was silent for a few seconds, then solemnly replied,

"I'm sorry."

She only uttered three words, but they were enough to make the face opposite her pale. Xiao Bai chuckled and scratched his head, forcing a smile,

"Hehe, it's okay, I know you probably don't like me."

He Ran kept her mouth shut and didn't reply. Xiao Bai tentatively asked again,

"Can we still be friends in the future?"

He Ran nodded thoughtfully and said softly,

"As long as you're willing."

Xiao Bai left. Watching his departing figure, He Ran silently said

"I'm sorry"

again in her heart. When she said

"I'm sorry,"

she was referring to another

"I'm sorry."

---

On Friday night, a heavy snowfall blanketed the entire city of Beijing. He Ran stood by the window, looking at the snow-covered world outside, and made a phone call to Xiao Han.

"The snow is too heavy, so it's fine if you can't come."

Xiao Han's voice was muffled by the howling north wind,

"It's okay, I'm almost at your neighborhood."

A few minutes after hanging up the phone, the doorbell rang, and He Ran quickly went to open the door. Xiao Han stood outside the door, his head, eyelashes, and shoulders covered in snowflakes, a chill seeping in. He Ran quickly pulled him inside and closed the door. He had just gone to the supermarket and was carrying several bags of groceries, all for dinner. He Ran led him into the kitchen and put the bags down. Xiao Han's hands were rough, cracked and hard from the wind. He Ran took his hands, warmed them against her face, and frowned, saying,

"Don't do so much work in winter, or you'll get frostbite."

Xiao Han smiled and said,

"I wear gloves when I work."

"And yet you're still so frozen?"

"I got cold while I was knitting you a scarf."

He Ran couldn't help but glare at him:

"Don't you have heating in your house?"

"I didn't pretend,"

Xiao Han said truthfully. He Ran scoffed,

"It's amazing you've survived."

Xiao Han withdrew his hands and took out the ingredients one by one from the shopping bag to start cooking dinner. He Ran turned the heating up even higher, and Xiao Han soon felt hot. He took off his clothes and was left with only a long-sleeved shirt, and sweat was still dripping from his head. He Ran's kitchen was just for show; she had never turned on the gas stove, and the pots and pans were still exactly as they were when she first moved in. Xiao Han washed all the kitchen utensils before starting to chop vegetables and cook. He Ran looked through the ingredients and asked,

"What are we having for dinner tonight?"

Xiao Han replied,

"Radish stew with mutton, steamed oysters, and a stir-fried plate of greens."

He Ran listened with little interest, saying,

"I don't really like any of these."

Xiao Han said,

"It's good to eat something warm and nourishing in winter."

He Ran scoffed,

"Oysters are really nutritious?"

Xiao Han glanced at her and said,

"Don't underestimate it. It's good to eat more of it in winter."

He Ran smiled.

"How is it good?"

Xiao Han listed them one by one:

"Strengthen bones, relieve insomnia, nourish the kidneys and replenish essence."

He Ran patted his bottom,

"Then eat more."

"..."

Dinner was ready quickly. He Ran didn't touch the oysters, but she ate several pieces of lamb.

"Is it because I haven't had your cooking in so long? It tastes so good!"

she praised as she filled a second bowl with rice. Xiao Han smiled and said,

" Just call me if you want to eat it, and I'll make it for you."

He Ran laughed, but didn't reply after she finished laughing.

---

After dinner, Xiao Han washed the pots and dishes in the kitchen, while He Ran took her pajamas and went into the bathroom to take a shower. She washed for twenty minutes, then invited Xiao Han to come in and wash as well. Xiao Han initially refused, saying,

"It's so cold, there's no need to wash. "

He Ran threatened,

"Don't get into my bed if you don't wash up."

Upon hearing this, Xiao Han had no choice but to turn around and walk into the bathroom. Men aren't particular about showering; he came out in just five minutes. He Ran had changed into a sheer nightgown and was sitting on the bed adjusting the shoulder straps. Seeing Xiao Han come out, she let go of his hand and smiled at him. He Ran was wearing Victoria's Secret's latest lingerie, black transparent lace wrapping around her beautiful body, concealing her private parts while revealing her lovely curves. She got off the bed and walked gracefully to him, and Xiao Han stared at her intently. His child has indeed grown into a young woman. He Ran placed her hands on his arms and wrists, looked up and asked,

"Do you like it?"

Xiao Han gave a low, nasal sound:

"Mmm."

He Ran twirled in front of him.

"Does it look good?"

Xiao Han, oblivious to any romantic gestures, asked,

"Aren't you cold?"

He Ran's face fell, and she said unhappily,

"I specially bought this for you to wear, and you're asking me if I'm cold?"

Xiao Han quickly corrected himself:

"You look good in anything."

Girls love to hear this kind of talk, and He Ran was no exception. She squatted down and deftly unbuckled his belt and buttons, a charming smile playing on her lips:

"Here's a little reward for you."

When He Ran woke up, it was already bright daylight. She felt a slight breath on her face, which tickled her, so she opened her eyes. She touched her damp lips and chuckled softly,

"Steal a kiss from me."

Xiao Han readily admitted it. He stared intently at He Ran and suddenly said,

"You look like a cat."

He Ran blinked and asked,

"Why?"

"Small body, small face, small nose, small mouth, big eyes."

She gave a soft snort and reached out to pluck the newly sprouted stubble from his chin.

"Then you're a tomcat."

Xiao Han took her hand in his, completely enveloping it in his palm, and asked,

"What would you like to eat for breakfast?"

He Ran hesitated for a moment, about to speak, when Xiao Han's pupils suddenly dilated and he stared at her blankly,

"You have a nosebleed."

Before He Ran could react, Xiao Han had already jumped off the bed and helped her sit up.

"Sit up straight, don't tilt your head back."

This time, He Ran also saw it; bright red liquid hung from the tip of her nose, about to fall, and finally dripped into her palm. Xiao Han quickly grabbed a few tissues and stuffed them into her right nostril. She was bleeding heavily, and the tissues were soon stained red, so Xiao Han simply grabbed the whole roll of toilet paper. He Ran remained calm, pressing her nose with her hand while giving Xiao Han a reassuring look. More than ten minutes later, the bleeding finally stopped. Countless bloodstained crumpled pieces of paper lay on the bedside table, a horrifying sight. Xiao Han frowned and asked,

"When did you start doing this?"

"It's an old problem."

He Ran remained calm as ever. After a while, she spoke:

"Xiao Han, there's something I've never told you."

Xiao Han asked,

"What is it?"

He Ran said casually,

"I am a leukemia patient."

The power suddenly went out in the apartment complex, forcing Xiao Han and He Ran to walk down the stairs in the dark. He Ran tripped and fell halfway down, and fearing she might have another accident, Xiao Han insisted on carrying her for the rest of the way. He Ran thought he was making a fuss over nothing. She said helplessly,

"I'm just sick, I'm not so sick that I can't walk. Put me down."

Xiao Han ignored her and carried her down the stairs step by step. He Ran told him several times but he ignored her, so she simply shut up. Let him carry her if he wants; he's got the stamina anyway. After finally reaching the first floor, He Ran's feet were finally back on flat ground. She tossed the car keys to Xiao Han and said,

"My car is parked on the side of the road. You can drive it later."

Xiao Han said,

"Okay."

The roads were slippery due to the snow, and all vehicles were moving very slowly. At a traffic light, Xiao Han stepped on the brakes. As the countdown began, his gaze drifted aimlessly to the window, where he noticed the driver of the car opposite was staring at him strangely. It's quite a rare sight to see an old man driving a small, pink Porsche. Xiao Han was completely unaware. When the red light turned green, he stepped on the gas and drove forward toward the hospital. Half an hour ago, He Ran and Xiao Han argued for a long time about whether or not to go to the hospital. He Ran knew her own body best, but Xiao Han insisted on seeing the test results before he would believe it. In the end, He Ran gave in. Upon arriving at the hospital, they went through the entire blood test procedure step by step. When the results came out, they weren't too surprising. He Ran's white blood cell count rose to 15.9. If this level remains unchanged, it can only mean that the condition has relapsed. Listening to the doctor's detailed explanation, Xiao Han fell silent. On the way home, the atmosphere in the carriage was very heavy. Icy roads caused numerous traffic accidents, bringing the entire inner ring road to a near standstill. He Ran and Xiao Han were trapped in the middle of the road, having traveled less than ten kilometers in an hour. She tapped on the car window listlessly, sighed, and complained,

"I told you not to come out. It would have been so much better to stay at home."

Xiao Han turned around and said seriously,

"Kid, you should be hospitalized."

He Ran rejected the idea without hesitation,

"I won't stay."

"Why don't you stay in the hospital?"

Xiao Han frowned.

"The doctors all recommended that you be hospitalized for observation."

He Ran said calmly,

"It's not that bad yet. Doctors always like to exaggerate the severity of a condition. If they cure it, they seem incredibly capable; if they fail, they have a reason to excuse themselves."

Xiao Han patiently advised,

"At least the doctor won't harm you. Don't always act like it's nothing."

"I know my own illness well, and chemotherapy won't do me any good at this time."

He Ran stopped, starting to get a little impatient.

"You don't understand this disease, and it's a waste of breath to tell you anything."

"How could I not know!"

Xiao Han suddenly shouted urgently,

"My brother is suffering from this disease..."

He was halfway through his sentence when he suddenly turned his head to the side, swallowing the rest of his words. He Ran paused for a moment, then turned to look out the window, feigning composure, but felt a tightness in her chest. They had never been so confrontational before, with neither willing to back down first. After an unknown amount of time, Xiao Han finally turned around and looked at He Ran,

"So what do you plan to do now?"

He Ran said,

"I'm taking traditional Chinese medicine and practicing Qigong."

Xiao Han asked,

"Will it work?"

"What do you think?"

She raised an eyebrow at him with a half-smile. Her body broke free of the seatbelt's restraint, and she leaned close to his face, her breath sweet as orchids:

"I did alright last night, didn't I?"

Xiao Han remained unsmiling and pressed her back into her seat, saying, "You're such a worry." It's hard to believe that they were stuck in traffic for the entire afternoon and didn't get home until after sunset.

---

That evening, Xiao Han made fried rice for He Ran, but he was still in a bad mood and didn't say a word at the dinner table. After He Ran finished eating, Xiao Han washed the dishes, cleaned the kitchen, and then prepared to leave. He Ran escorted him to the entrance and watched him bend down to tie his shoelaces.

"Aren't you staying tonight?"

she asked in a low voice.

"No, thank you."

Xiao Han put on his shoes, stood up and said,

"I have to get up at four o'clock tomorrow to go to the early market."

He Ran smiled and said,

"You're working very hard."

"It's not enough."

He looked down at her, his attitude solemn:

"I want to live a good life with you, and I need to work even harder."

"You're saying that again."

He Ran frowned almost imperceptibly.

"I've already told you I don't need you to support me."

Xiao Han insisted,

"Whether you use it or not is one thing, whether I do it or not is another."

"You've already broken our agreement."

He Ran's face fell, and she turned her back to him.

"Either we don't meet anymore."

"Don't try to provoke me with those kinds of words."

Xiao Han walked around to her and said,

"I know you can't live without me either."

He Ran chuckled and said coldly,

"What made you think that?"

Xiao Han placed his hands on either side of her waist, his eyes fixed on her:

"From your eyes."

He said slowly,

"From the first time I cut your bangs, when you opened your eyes."

Xiao Han said firmly,

"He Ran, you need me."

He straightened her slightly disheveled clothes and stated,

"If it weren't for that, you wouldn't have come looking for me."

Their eyes met for a long while, and a strong emotion welled up from the depths of Xiao Han's dark eyes. His sincere gaze was heartfelt, and he longed for her response. As if her life force had been seized, all the strength in her body suddenly drained away. She gave in, lowering her eyelids as she said,

"Xiao Han, you're a madman."

"You made the same mistake ten years ago, and you haven't improved at all."

"It's not a mistake,"

Xiao Han said. What is that?

"You are different from Qin Zao."

"What could be different?"

He Ran smiled faintly, her voice weak:

"Different paths, same destination."

Xiao Han frowned, speaking of the past with considerable composure,

"You are more conflicted than her. You clearly can't live without me, yet you push me away time and time again."

He Ran said softly,

"Do you feel like I've played you?"

Xiao Han said calmly,

"No, I know you've always wanted to be with me, but you just won't tell the truth."

He Ran remained silent for a long time before saying,

"Alright, let's not talk about the past."

Now that things have come to this, she really needs to have an honest and open conversation with Xiao Han. He Ran sighed silently,

"You're right, I really can't live without you, not before, and not in the future..."

"There are many factors that prevent us from being together, but what bothers me the most is not the outside world, but you."

He Ran reached out and smoothed the deep furrows between his brows, and whispered,

"You always say that I'm going to back out, but what I'm most worried about is you going to back out."

Xiao Han frowned even more and grabbed her hand:

"What do I regret?"

He Ran adjusted her tone, speaking sternly:

"Let's not talk about anything else for now. I'll just ask you one question: are you afraid?"

Xiao Han asked,

"What are you afraid of?"

He Ran said,

"Everything I'll have to face from now on."

Xiao Han replied decisively,

"I'm not afraid."

He Ran said seriously,

"Don't make it sound so easy. Think about it carefully."

Xiao Han replied without hesitation,

"I'm not afraid."

Even if the future is unpredictable?

"We'll cherish every day we can be together."

"Even if you might lose another finger?"

He Ran picked up his left hand and gently massaged his missing thumb.

"I've experienced Han Yu's methods of revenge firsthand."

Xiao Han said firmly,

"If I were afraid, I wouldn't be standing here."

He Ran remained silent for a long time before speaking again:

"I don't like people making promises easily, but now I want to hear you swear an oath."

She stared intently at him.

"You swore you would never give up on me."

Xiao Han didn't speak for a moment. He looked back at her and asked,

"And what about you?"

He Ran smiled and said,

"You don't need to worry about me. Since I've made this request of you, I will only be more determined than you."

"Fine."

Xiao Han said in a deep voice, each word filled with resolute determination:

"I swear, this will last until death."

He Ran smiled faintly, and with her other hand stroked upwards along the contour of his jaw,

"This time you used the idiom correctly."

She leaned her head close to his chest, took a deep breath as if making a decision, and said,

"Xiao Han, I've remembered what you said. Don't go back on your word."

Xiao Han said,

"I have never broken my promise."

He Ran gripped his hand tightly and closed her eyes:

"Okay, I'll go crazy with you."

Her first stirrings of affection were because of him, and her wavering was also because of him. Only his unwavering gaze could give her the courage to take a gamble. Now she is ready to give her all to love him. On this thorny path, whoever backs down first will perish together.

## Chapter Eight: Love is Selfish

Chapter Preface: What's wrong with that? Love is inherently selfish. The moment she decided to be with Xiao Han, the collapse of the heavens and the earth became irrelevant to her; all she needed was for him to be by her side. The story of He Ran going to an art exhibition with a male classmate somehow got embellished and reached Han Yu's ears. True to his usual hot temper, this young master stormed into He Ran's house that very afternoon to confront her. He Ran calmly and carefully brewed coffee to Han Yu's liking before sitting down opposite him. When Han Yu questioned her, she readily admitted,

"I did go out with someone else, so what?"

"So what?!"

Han Yu laughed angrily.

"He Ran, don't forget who your boyfriend is now! Don't you understand the importance of self-respect and chastity?"

He Ran smiled faintly, her expression full of disdain.

"What's wrong with going to an art exhibition? Don't you hug people all the time?"

Seemingly not expecting her to say something so contrary to him, Han Yu's chest heaved violently with anger. Young Master Han has always thought about everything from a self-centered perspective. He can flirt and live a carefree life, but he will never allow anyone to cheat on him. The air was thick with tension, but He Ran had no intention of stopping there.

"Since you're here today, I'll make things clear to you."

"Han Yu, let's break up."

When she uttered those two words so casually and without warning, Han Yu's eyes widened and veins bulged on his forehead. He stared at her in disbelief:

"Break up?!"

"Yes."

"Say that again if you dare!"

He Ran repeated without changing her expression:

"Han Yu, I don't want to play with you anymore."

Han Yu gritted his teeth, took several deep breaths, and said with a fierce look in his eyes,

"Is it because of that brat who went to the art exhibition with you!"

He Ran lowered her head and touched her fingernails.

"Interpret it however you like."

Han Yu shouted,

"Do you think you can be together in peace just because you dumped me?!"

He Ran didn't take it seriously,

"This doesn't seem to be your business, does it?"

These words were like adding fuel to the fire, and Han Yu was enraged beyond measure.

"He Ran, you...!"

The last two words were not uttered, but instead, an angry slap was delivered. When Han Yu swung his hand over, He Ran didn't have time to dodge. That slap was really powerful; He Ran could even feel the wind from the slap on her cheek. Her body was shaken by the fierce force and she fell heavily to one side, landing on the sofa. He Ran sat up after a while, covering her face with her left hand, expressionless. A moment later, her mouth was filled with a hot, metallic taste. She calmly pulled out a few tissues, opened her mouth, and spat out a mouthful of blood. Han Yu was stunned for a moment, and his anger from just now completely disappeared. When He Ran spat out his second mouthful of blood, a hint of panic crept into his voice,

"Are you... alright?"

He Ran didn't even lift her eyelids, pointing to the door and issuing an order to leave:

"Leave here immediately, and I'll be fine."

Han Yu hesitated, standing there for a long time before finally slamming the door shut in anger and leaving. He Ran's gum bleeding had never been this severe before; Han Yu's slap had been quite forceful. When Xiao Han came to find her that evening, half of her face was swollen like a peach. Xiao Han was startled and asked her what happened. He Ran only explained that her tooth was inflamed again. This answer did not convince Xiao Han. He stood there for a while and said in a muffled voice,

"I just saw a cigarette butt at your door."

The implication is,

"Who came today?"

He Ran smiled and said,

"So you're not so stupid after all."

She sat down next to him and decided not to hide it anymore, telling him,

"Today I broke up with Han Yu, and he slapped me."

Upon hearing this, Xiao Han frowned deeply. He Ran said calmly,

"It's nothing serious, don't worry."

Xiao Han thought for a moment, then said seriously,

"Next time he causes you trouble, just call me."

He Ran looked at him and nodded in agreement,

"Okay."

---

After dinner, He Ran went to take a shower. When she came out of the bathroom, Xiao Han was sitting on the sofa, engrossed in calculating today's income. He Ran walked quietly to his side and glanced down at him a few more times. Xiao Han had his own way of doing accounts; others might find it messy, but he himself kept it clear and organized. He was scribbling on the draft paper with a broken pen in his hand. He Ran couldn't stand it anymore and found him a calculator.

"Use this to speed things up."

Xiao Han refused, shaking his head and saying,

"I'm used to calculating on paper."

He has many quirks, so He Ran didn't force him,

"Okay."

After settling the accounts, Xiao Han put away his crumpled little booklet. He Ran pouted and asked,

"How did the calculation go?"

Xiao Han's tone was fairly cheerful:

"There have been many holidays these past few months, so business has been pretty good. We've managed to break even and even make a little extra."

He Ran smiled and said,

"Then congratulations!"

Xiao Han picked her up and carried her into the bedroom. He Ran clung to him, continuing their previous conversation:

"There are too many bad people now. We must walk together when we walk at night from now on."

"Especially since you live off-campus, you need to be careful."

He Ran listened quietly without commenting. Another person present, equally distracted, was Zhang Amin. She was sullen because of Xiao Han; for the past few days, she wondered if she had done something wrong, as Xiao Han had been avoiding her. Zhang Amin had finally managed to become somewhat familiar with him, but now she didn't know where to begin. Xiao Bai returned to school in the afternoon. Rumors are always exaggerated. His injuries were not serious, but he had a few bruises on his face. During the break, He Ran went to the infirmary and bought a bottle of ointment. At this time, most people had gone to the canteen to buy drinks, and there were only two or three people in the classroom. Xiao Bai was lost in thought when a hand holding a medicine bottle suddenly appeared in front of him. He slowly raised his head following the beautiful, slender hand, and instinctively opened his mouth when he saw He Ran.

"Take this to avoid infection of the wound,"

He Ran said softly. Xiao Bai said thank you softly and took the medicine bottle. He looked at He Ran, hesitating several times as if he wanted to say something. He Ran said,

"Ask whatever you want."

"He Ran, have you offended someone?"

"Why do you say that?"

Xiao Bai:

"Those people yesterday... warned me to stay away from you."

He Ran nodded and told him,

"Those people were probably called by my boyfriend."

"You have a boyfriend?"

He Ran said casually,

"We've already broken up."

Xiao Bai lowered her head and slowly said,

"...Oh."

"I'm sorry, I didn't expect to drag you into this."

He Ran apologized sincerely. Xiao Bai smiled kindly and waved her hand, saying,

"I'm fine."

After class started, Zhang Amin sat behind He Ran. She leaned closer, poked He Ran's back with her pen cap, and whispered,

"Did you break up with your boyfriend?"

It seemed she had overheard their conversation. He Ran nodded and said,

"Yes."

Zhang Amin pondered for a few seconds and then agreed,

"Your boyfriend treated you so badly, it's good that you broke up. Women should be cherished."

He Ran shrugged and remained silent. Zhang Amin's attention immediately shifted to the scarf around her neck. She reached out and touched it a few times, asking curiously,

"This scarf is so pretty! Where did you buy it?"

He Ran subtly avoided her hand and said,

"It wasn't bought, a friend knitted it."

"Oh."

Zhang Amin said with some disappointment,

"What a pity, I wanted to buy the same one."

The conversation ended, and the teacher returned to the classroom. Everyone stopped talking and focused on their own drawings. Recently, He Ran has been waking up at night due to swelling and pain in her legs, so she has started trying to gradually increase the duration of her Qigong practice in the hope of suppressing the pain. She gets up at six o'clock every day, practices for two hours, then goes home to shower before going to school. "I really can't remember."
