After class, the three of them went straight to the cafeteria for lunch. Liu Rui and Huang Xiaoli walked ahead with great enthusiasm, while Zhang Amin brought up the rear. He Ran walked closer to her and said in a low voice, "Petty theft is a disease. You'd better see a psychologist." Zhang Amin's face froze instantly, and she turned her head to stare at her incredulously. He Ran quickened her pace and walked ahead of her.

---

That night, He Ran nestled in Xiao Han's arms and told him about it before going to sleep.

"Xiao Han, my scarf was stolen. Could you knit me another one?"

Xiao Han looked puzzled:

"How could someone steal a scarf?"

He Ran resisted rolling her eyes.

"How should I know? He's probably sick."

Xiao Han was both amused and exasperated, and asked again,

"What color do you want?"

"Just make it the same as last time,"

He Ran added.

"Make it a little longer, because I'm afraid of getting cold."

He nodded:

"Okay, I'll do it as soon as possible."

"By the way,"

He Ran rolled over, asking casually,

"Has Zhang Amin been coming to your shop to see you lately?"

Xiao Han didn't look at her, and after a while he answered,

"Yes, I didn't pay much attention to her."

He Ran said,

"She's the one who took my scarf."

"..."

She couldn't help but sigh resentfully:

"I really don't understand. How come so many young girls like an old man like you, with no house and no car?"

Xiao Han kept his mouth shut, staring at her without saying a word. He Ran continued,

"It's a good thing I've tamed you. Don't go around harming other little girls anymore."

Xiao Han smiled, still not saying anything, but lowered his head and kissed her.

---

A few days later, while Xiao Han was cleaning the flower shop, he ran into Zhang Amin, who was still determined to find him. Xiao Han put the broom aside, went into the inner room, took out a basket of colorful yarn balls, handed it to Zhang Amin, and said,

"Choose your favorite color."

Zhang Amin looked at him, puzzled:

"What are you doing?"

Xiao Han said,

"Knitting scarves."

Zhang Amin was stunned for a moment, then her eyes lit up with barely suppressed joy:

"Are you going to knit me a scarf?"

"Um."

"You, I...why?"

Zhang Amin was so surprised that she couldn't finish her sentence.

"Why did you suddenly knit me a scarf?"

Xiao Han said in a flat tone,

"I'll knit one for you, and you can stop taking He Ran's from now on."

Zhang Amin froze on the spot, her eyes wide open, feeling a burning pain on her face. Xiao Han's expression was calm and indifferent, showing no intention of criticizing her; in fact, he was quite tolerant. But the more indifferent he seemed, the stronger the sense of shame she felt was compared to being directly exposed. Embarrassment, embarrassment, and shame overwhelmed her from all sides. Zhang Amin stammered,

"No need, thank you."

"Brother Xiao, I... I suddenly remembered I have something to do, I have to go now."

She could no longer stand there, she just wanted to escape as soon as possible, and after saying this, she turned and left in a hurry. He Ran originally thought that with Xiao Bai covering their tracks for a while, Han Yu wouldn't discover anything so quickly. But that wasn't the case; a week later, he came back to find her again. As He Ran was packing her luggage for the winter break trip back to Guangzhou, the doorbell rang. Looking through the peephole, the face she least wanted to see appeared. There was no escaping it; He Ran slowly opened the door. Han Yu stood in front of her, exuding a fierce aura, and roared angrily,

"He Ran, you're getting bolder and bolder, you've completely fooled me!"

He Ran didn't hold back either:

"It's not that I'm bold, it's that you're too stupid."

"Yes, I was too stupid!"

Han Yu sneered, his face twisting even more.

"I should have seen through. I must have been out of my mind to believe your lies time and time again!"

He Ran looked at him calmly,

"So how did you find out this time? Did someone tell you?"

"Never mind whether anyone told me or not!"

Han Yu grabbed a vase from the doorway and smashed it on the ground. His eyes were bloodshot as he said,

"I'm asking you, did you give that million yuan you borrowed from me to that man?!"

"I'll pay you back,"

He Ran answered evasively,

"but don't ask me how you'll use it."

Han Yu slammed his foot on the door, yelling,

"I fucking want to kill someone!"

He Ran turned around and walked leisurely into the house, but Han Yu grabbed her wrist with a strong force. He Ran turned around, looked at his tightly clenched other hand, and raised an eyebrow.

"What, are you going to slap me again?"

His anger flared, and Han Yu released her hand.

"I won't touch you,"

he said fiercely.

"But that man, I won't let him get away with it!"

"I've already investigated him. He's over thirty, penniless and powerless. I really don't know what you see in him!"

Han Yu mocked mercilessly.

"He Ran, I think you're either crazy or stupid, or do you naively believe your mother would approve of your relationship?"

He Ran stood there, frowning.

"I told you, my affairs are none of your business."

Han Yu chuckled coldly for a moment, then walked up behind her. He lowered his head, his face close to her ear,

"Want to make a bet and see how long he can last?"

Han Yu deliberately lowered his voice and said in a gloomy tone,

"Believe it or not, he's even more of a coward than Lu Jingbai. I only need to scare him a little and he'll run away in a panic."

"Go ahead and go."

He Ran was unconcerned about the danger he might pose.

"He's not Lu Jingbai."

"Does it matter? I'll let you know that poor people are all the same at heart!"

Han Yu paused slightly, looking at her coldly:

"Your family is the same. I heard your father has recently incurred a lot of debt. Maybe in a few years your family will be ruined. Don't come crying to me then."

"Have you said enough?"

He Ran was not swayed by his provocation. She slowly walked into her room.

"If you've said enough, please leave quickly. I still have a lot to do."

Han Yu stopped chasing after them and snorted heavily,

"He Ran, we'll see!"

He uttered a harsh remark and turned to leave. The weather in Guangzhou is warm, so He Ran didn't need to bring too many clothes back; a small suitcase would suffice. After packing, it was almost nine o'clock. She was a little hungry, so she cooked herself a bowl of egg porridge, following the method Xiao Han had taught her a few days ago. The taste was passable, and after eating, she went to bed early. Recently, He Ran has been tossing and turning in bed when she's alone, and has had to resort to sleeping pills. She falls asleep quickly after taking them, but inexplicably wakes up around 3 or 4 a.m. After that, she can't fall back asleep, and He Ran feels constantly uneasy, as if something is about to happen. Wanting to do something to dispel this unease, she called Xiao Han. No one answered the first time, but a few minutes later, Xiao Han called her back. While He Ran was suffering from insomnia, Xiao Han was sleeping soundly. Startled awake, he was still confused and asked her,

"Are you at the movie theater yet? Yes, get up right now."

He Ran chuckled,

"No, it's only three o'clock now, I'm still at home."

Xiao Han breathed a sigh of relief, slowly came to his senses, and asked,

"Why are you calling me in the middle of the night?"

He Ran said,

"It's nothing, I just wanted to hear your voice."

"Insomnia?"

Xiao Han asked in a low voice.

"Um."

Do you want me to tell you a story? He Ran couldn't help but chuckle:

"No need, I'm not a child anymore."

Knowing he was alright, she said softly,

"Go to sleep, see you tomorrow."

Putting her phone aside, He Ran closed her eyes and cleared her mind. She hadn't slept all night, and finally, at seven in the morning, the sky began to lighten. She got up, washed, and rushed out the door without lingering for a moment. This morning, He Ran skipped her boxing practice and went straight to the flower and bird market to find Xiao Han. It was the off-season, and it was too cold for anyone to go out so early, so business at the market was naturally slow. There weren't many people on the street, but He Ran spotted a group of people walking out from the intersection at a glance. Han Yu was in the lead, followed by two or three other men. They met unexpectedly, and Han Yu saw He Ran. He led a group of people and walked towards her menacingly. He Ran had never seen Han Yu look so disheveled. His face was bruised and swollen, his hair was covered in mud and stray weeds. It turned out that beneath Han Yu's glamorous exterior, he was nothing more than a coarse commoner. Someone must have angered Han Yu, because he took out all his anger on He Ran:

"Your man is fucking tough! I've never been hit by anyone before in my life!"

"I won't swallow this!"

Han Yu glared at her, baring his teeth, and cursed,

"He Ran, you just wait! I won't let this go!"

He Ran quickened her pace and arrived at Xiao Han's shop. As soon as she reached the entrance, she saw flowerpots shattered into pieces on the ground. Inside, the shop was in complete disarray, as if a typhoon had swept through. Iron cages were leaning to one side, and cats and dogs had run out and were running wild. Several chicks had been trampled to death on the ground, their bodies covered in blood. Clearly, with his business disrupted, Xiao Han is out of luck for today. The person in question was standing with his back to the door, diligently cleaning the floor with a broom and rag. Hearing the sound of high heels, he turned around and happened to see He Ran walking in from the doorway. Xiao Han glanced at the time. They had agreed to meet at 7 p.m., and it was only 7 a.m. now.

"Why are you here so early?"

He Ran walked straight up to him, looked him over from head to toe, and breathed a sigh of relief when she saw that he was alright.

"I just ran into Han Yu at the intersection,"

He Ran said.

"Hmm,"

Xiao Han replied softly. He Ran didn't know whether to laugh or cry.

"You're really something. Do you even know whose son you hit?"

"I don't know."

Xiao Han's explanation was only one simple sentence:

"Anyway, he hit you."

"Yes, you helped me vent my anger."

He Ran smiled slightly and said,

"But you shouldn't have been so impulsive. Someone like Han Yu would hit you ten times back if you hit him once."

Xiao Han pursed his lips tightly.

"It's okay, I'm not afraid."

After he finished speaking, he took a broom and walked around He Ran, sweeping up a pile of broken tiles at her feet. He Ran watched him walk a few steps forward before realizing that his movements were not very nimble. She followed him and asked,

"What happened to your leg?"

Xiao Han answered evasively,

"It's a little bruised."

He Ran frowned.

"What hit you?"

Xiao Han said,

"A stick."

She snatched the broom from his hand, frowning slightly, and said,

"Stop doing this, let's go to the hospital."

Xiao Han stood still and said,

"It's just a little bruising, it'll be fine in a few days."

He Ran pulled him forcefully to leave, insisting,

"No, what if we leave behind any problems later?"

Xiao Han hesitated for a moment and said,

"Okay, I'll go with you."

He then added a condition:

"But you also have to get a check-up done for me."

He Ran looked at him, puzzled

"I'm perfectly fine, why would I need a checkup?"

"Let's check your blood count,"

Xiao Han said. He Ran reluctantly lowered her eyebrows.

"I'm going back to Guangzhou tomorrow. Why bother with blood tests now? It'll ruin my mood."

Xiao Han said,

"It's precisely because you're about to leave that you're having this checkup done for me, otherwise I wouldn't feel confident."

After thinking for a moment, she finally conceded,

"Okay, okay, let's go."

He Ran's car was parked not far away. She helped Xiao Han into the passenger seat and fastened his seatbelt. After arriving at the hospital, He Ran accompanied him to have his leg examined and medication applied before the two went to the second floor to have his blood drawn. The process of waiting for the result is always very long. Xiao Han sat in the chair, staring at her without blinking, seemingly lost in thought.

As always, the doctor followed He Ran around like a mother hen, constantly nagging:

"Your condition requires chemotherapy; it's too dangerous if you don't."

He Ran had heard this phrase at least a hundred times when, and its terror was no less than that of the golden headband. "Could you cooperate with the doctor more and stop making me worry?"

"It's not that I'm uncooperative."

"Even if it's just for me."

After finishing work in the afternoon, they went to a restaurant that He Ran liked for dinner as planned, and then went to see a movie in the evening. While queuing to buy tickets, an elderly couple with white hair were ahead of us. The elderly couple spoke Chongqing dialect and had poor hearing, so they tried to communicate with the ticket seller for a long time without any success. The people behind them were getting impatient and began to complain. Seeing this, Xiao Han stepped forward to help translate, and the elderly couple thanked him profusely after buying their tickets. Watching the two elderly people support each other as they walked away, Xiao Han was lost in thought, and it took him a long time to look away. He Ran knew what he was thinking, smiled at him knowingly, and said,

"It's not easy to be so romantic at this age."

Xiao Han had a late-night snack and nodded in agreement. After they finished watching the movie, their day's itinerary was over, and they didn't get home until 9:30 p.m. He Ran took a shower first, turned on the heater, and lay on the bed reading for a while. Xiao Han then came out of the bathroom and sat down next to her. He snatched the book away and said sternly,

"Don't read while lying down, it'll hurt your eyes."

He Ran was somewhat dissatisfied,

"I was waiting for you, wasn't I?"

She wrapped her arms around his waist, her voice softening:

"Why did you take so long to shower today? Did we switch roles?"

"Have you packed your luggage?"

He Ran nodded:

"Mm."

Xiao Han lifted the blanket, picked her up, and laid her horizontally on his lap. Before we started, he glanced at the time and said,

"You have to catch a flight early tomorrow morning, so let's finish at 10:30."

He Ran smiled gently,

"I'll listen to you."

He Ran reached out twice within half an hour, her hands clinging to Xiao Han's back, her nails digging deep into his flesh. The suffocating feeling of being submerged gradually subsided, and He Ran opened her eyes, staring blankly at the ceiling above her head. Xiao Han was right, she needed him. "Your hair is beautiful."

Xiao Han's tone was more like talking to himself. He Ran gave a long

"hmm"

sound, turned her face to the side and gently placed her hand on his, making his movements more fluid. Xiao Han brought his nose close and sniffed for a moment,

"It smells very good."

He Ran asked,

"So, do you prefer my short hair or long hair?"

Xiao Han said,

"That's fine."

He Ran smiled silently. After resting for a while, Xiao Han suddenly spoke:

"Kid."

"Um?"

"I've made up my mind."

He Ran turned her head to look at him,

"Have you decided what you want to do?"

Xiao Han said,

"The long-term plan we talked about before."

He Ran nodded:

"Tell me about it."

a word representing everything distant and unknown, is what is truly the most extravagant hope. He Ran smiled faintly, her lips curving upwards.

Mrs. Han didn't know what he was doing there. He Ran was still worried, so after everyone had left that evening, she called Xiao Han to ask how he had been these past few days. "Han Yu didn't cause you any trouble?"

"Let's celebrate the New Year."

"My mom is back, let's talk about it tomorrow."

He Ran remained silent. After all, they were mother and daughter, and He Ran's every subtle expression could not escape her eyes. Yang Wenping spoke up:

"I'd be crazy to be with Han Yu."

"Going to the restroom."

"Let me tell you, if you don't break up with that man, I won't acknowledge you as my daughter!"

Her voice echoed throughout the corridor, seriously disturbing the rest of other patients. The on-duty nurse rushed out in time to stop her. He Ran seized the opportunity to escape and went into the bathroom around the corner. She closed the door, locked herself in, put down the toilet seat, and sat down for a long time, lost in thought. After two days of hospitalization and expert consultation, He Ran's condition was finally diagnosed. She was found to have relapsed, with acute lymphoblastic leukemia mixed with neurological infiltration. Dr. Xue solemnly announced:

The only people with absolute authority here, besides the doctors, were Yang Wenping; He Ran's opinions were disregarded. On the day she signed the chemotherapy consent form, He Ran requested to move from a single room to a double room. People who usually prefer quiet find themselves inexplicably afraid of loneliness when they arrive at a hospital, the quietest place they can be. The side effects of chemotherapy soon became apparent in He Ran. The day after the injection, she began to feel nauseous, dizzy, and had a poor appetite. She forced herself to drink a bowl of porridge in the morning, but vomited it all up by noon. The girl in the same ward as her was two years younger and was more seriously ill. After a long period of chemotherapy, all her hair had fallen out, and she had to wear a woolen hat. He Ran sat on the edge of the bed, hugging a trash can, vomiting uncontrollably. The girl came over and comforted He Ran:

"It's normal to feel like this the first time you get a shot. You'll get used to it."

He Ran stopped, wiping her mouth as she said,

"This isn't my first time."

The girl paused for a moment, then said,

"I thought you had just gotten this disease because you had just moved in."

He Ran said,

"I had a transplant before, but now it has relapsed."

Yu Zhen's shy expression held a hint of disappointment as she lowered her head and said in a very soft voice,

"I've never been in a relationship before."

He Ran remained silent for a moment. From an outsider's perspective, it's truly pitiful that someone would contract this disease at such a young age, before even having the chance to try many things. On second thought, I realized that I was only two years older than him. But He Ran never felt sorry for herself, perhaps because she met Xiao Han. Many things are a matter of chance; encountering them is fate.

"You'll meet him in the future,"

He Ran comforted her.

"Maybe he's waiting for you not far away, so you need to get better quickly before you can go find him."

Yu Zhen nodded, smiling sweetly,

"Mm."

The days in the hospital were dull and boring; the only pleasure I had each day was taking a walk in the garden. He Ran was glad that the hospital she was staying at was very large, with a large garden area, many tree-lined paths, and a wide lawn in the center where she could fly kites. Every afternoon, He Ran would find a tree here to sit down and relax. She would take out her sketchbook and charcoal pencils and casually sketch some flowers and plants, and the afternoon would pass by leisurely like that. Yu Zhen's legs have recently become increasingly swollen and painful, making it impossible for her to get out of bed and walk. She can only follow He Ran in a wheelchair. While He Ran was painting, she would read books and recite poems beside her. Yu Zhen is a devoted fan of Ken Wilber, and she's currently reading his

"Grace and Courage,"

having read it five or six times and still finding it endlessly fascinating. He Ran has also read the book, though not with the same fervor. Yu Zhen loved watching He Ran paint. She made a promise that if she had the chance to be discharged from the hospital and her hair grew back, she would ask He Ran to paint a portrait of her. He Ran said no problem. As the sun was about to set, it was time for them to go back to their wards to rest. He Ran and Yu Zhen stepped out of the elevator in the inpatient department, and a gurney passed by them. A person was lying on the bed, their face covered by a white sheet, and only a vague outline could be seen. Having lived in the hospital for a long time, He Ran naturally saw this kind of scene quite often. He Ran usually pretended not to see it to avoid overthinking. Yu Zhen stood motionless, staring blankly at the person on the bed. Her eyes were unfocused, and she murmured as if in a nightmare,

"I saw the bracelet on her wrist. It's from Aman in the next ward. I was chatting with her yesterday..."

As she spoke, she turned to look at He Ran, her eyes vacant.

"Do you think that one day, I might also be lying on that cold, lifeless bed?"

He Ran didn't say anything. She silently walked behind Yu Zhen and pushed her to continue walking forward. She didn't know how to answer Yu Zhen's question, so she just said the same thing:

"Focus on getting better and don't overthink things."

Unexpectedly, Yu Zhen suddenly developed a high fever that night, and even suffered a cardiac arrest. He Ran was awakened by a series of beeping alarms and quickly got out of bed to call the nurse. The situation was extremely urgent; doctors used defibrillation several times to save her from the brink of death. Excessive chemotherapy caused hyperkalemia, leading to arrhythmia, but fortunately, she is now out of danger. He Ran breathed a sigh of relief as she watched the electrocardiogram on the instrument gradually stabilize. By the next morning, Yu Zhen was still in a coma with a high fever. Whether she could pull through depended on her own willpower. At eight o'clock, He Ran got out of bed to wash up as usual and stood in front of the mirror to comb her hair. Looking at the small tuft of black hair in her hand, she suddenly realized that she had been hospitalized for half a month. He Ran's stomach was still upset after breakfast, and she vomited again after eating. After resting for a while, He Ran called her friend in Beijing. Her voice was filled with worry:

"Xiao Han, I'm starting to lose my hair."

Before the other person could speak, she continued,

"Come see me soon, or you'll only see a nun."

Xiao Han remained silent for a while, then said in a deep voice,

"Okay."

He Ran waited in vain for Xiao Han, but instead received an uninvited guest. Qin Zao walked at the same pace as Yang Wenping in high heels. When the series of crisp footsteps rang out at the ward door, He Ran thought it was Yang Wenping who had arrived. She remained unmoved and continued reading with her head down. It wasn't until a bouquet of blue hyacinths appeared before He Ran's eyes that she realized the person standing opposite her couldn't be Yang Wenping. Yang Wenping was allergic to hyacinth pollen.

He Ran looked up, stunned for a moment:

"It's you."

Qin Zao smiled at her.

"It's me."

He Ran put the book aside and asked,

"How did you find your way here?"

Qin Zao said,

"I asked a friend about it."

He Ran wondered if the friend she was referring to was Xiao Han. She took the bouquet from Qin Zao and sniffed it. Blue hyacinths symbolize life; Qin Zao was thoughtful after all.

"What do you need me for?"

He Ran asked. Qin Zao opened her mouth but hadn't said anything yet when He Ran spoke first:

"My legs are a little numb from sitting for so long. Let's go downstairs for a walk and talk as we go."

They came to the lawn in the center of the garden, where many children were playing and laughing. Their faces showed no pain or illness, only joy and laughter. This is why children often have a higher survival rate than adults for many intractable diseases. Adults are more prone to pessimism; many people who were not meant to die end up dying from fear they themselves have. After walking for a while, He Ran glanced at Qin Zao and asked,

"Are you still living in Guangzhou?"

"No, I've gone back to Chongqing."

Qin Zao shook his head and said,

"I'm just back to take care of some business this time, I won't be staying long."

"Is your ex-wife still causing you trouble?"

"No, thank you."

Qin Zao gave a wry smile.

"Her reach isn't that far."

"Then I can't figure out why you came to see me."

He Ran pondered for a moment.

"I already told you about that million. You didn't want it then, so now you don't have the chance."

Qin Zao's expression darkened, and he quickly explained,

"I'm not doing this for the money."

She paused, remaining silent for a long time. He Ran also stopped and waited quietly.

"He Ran, you might not be happy what I'm saying..."

After a long wait, Qin Zao finally spoke:

"I've done a lot of research and I know your illness is very dangerous. I also hope you can be discharged from the hospital in good health, but have you thought about..."

She paused,

"...what if something happens to you, what will happen to Xiao Han?"

"That's a funny question."

He Ran smiled and answered easily,

"If I die, Xiao Han will just continue living, what else can happen?"

"But he's almost thirty-five! He has elderly parents to care for and young children to raise; he can't afford to delay."

He Ran turned around and continued walking forward.

"Whether I'm willing or not, it's none of your business."

Qin Zao followed, anxiously explaining,

"I'm just advising you as a friend; I mean no harm."

"I know,"

He Ran smiled.

"But you're quite an interesting person. How many years have you wasted on Xiao Han? Do you even have the right to advise me?"

Qin Zao was speechless. She stood there, looking slightly lost, and after a long while said,

"Have you made up your mind to be with Xiao Han?"

He Ran casually picked up a withered leaf from the ground.

"Yes."

Qin Zao said thoughtfully,

"But you are ultimately from different worlds, your family..."

With a gentle push, the withered leaf was crushed in her hand, and He Ran's voice was as light as a breeze:

"I can't worry about them."

Qin Zao stared blankly at her retreating figure, then slowed her speech,

"This isn't love, it's selfishness."

"Then what do you mean by love?"

He Ran said with a half-smile,

"If I can let him go and we go our separate ways, that's not love."

She stepped forward, tossed the crumbs of fallen leaves into the trash can, clapped her hands, and said,

"I am indeed quite selfish."

But what's wrong with that? Love is inherently selfish. The moment she decided to with Xiao Han, the collapse of the heavens and the earth became irrelevant to her; all she needed was for him to be by her side. After seeing Qin Zao off, He Ran returned to the inpatient department. As she stepped out of the elevator, a nurse pushed a bed past her. The thin white sheet outlined a slender figure, revealing that a young girl was sleeping on it. He Ran's heart skipped a beat, and her pupils dilated. She quickened her pace toward the ward, the wind she stirred up whistling past the sides of her trouser legs. He Ran pushed open the door forcefully, her breathing slightly erratic. Seeing Yu Zhen still lying peacefully on the bed with her eyes closed, a huge weight lifted from He Ran's heart. My gaze inadvertently drifted to the side, landing on the hyacinths in the vase by my bedside. A gust of wind blew in from outside the window, and a few petals had already fallen to the ground. Life is truly fragile. A week later, Xiao Han still couldn't return to Guangzhou to see her. He Ran's hair was falling out faster and faster, and at the doctor's request, she had to go to the hospital to shave her head. Before the hairdresser could even touch the scissors, He Ran took out her phone and snapped a selfie. She felt she wasn't as pretty as before; chemotherapy had left her weak and pale, making her look sickly. It was hard to imagine what a strange look it would have with a shaved head. He Ran sighed inwardly, then texted Xiao Han,

"Just how busy are you?"

Xiao Han never replied. He Ran sent him the selfie she had just taken, saying,

"Remember what I look like now."

A few minutes later, Xiao Han replied with only three words:

"I'm sorry."

These three inexplicable words made He Ran restless throughout the entire haircut process. After getting her hair cut, she immediately called Xiao Han. Just like the long time it took him to reply to the text message, the phone rang for a long time before he finally answered it. He Ran pressed her, asking aggressively,

"What do you mean?"

Xiao Han said,

"What do you mean?"

Why are you saying sorry? Xiao Han was silent for a moment, then said,

"I've been too busy to come see you."

He Ran kept her mouth shut for a while before asking,

"What are you doing right now?"

Xiao Han said,

"I work in the shop."

It's ten o'clock in the morning; the flower and bird market should be bustling with business. But Xiao Han's side is unusually quiet. He Ran had very keen hearing; she didn't make a sound, but simply listened quietly. After a long pause, she said,

"Xiao Han, are you at the hospital?"

Before Xiao Han could deny it, He Ran preemptively said,

"Don't say it's not true, I heard the sound of a handcart."

On the flight from Guangzhou to Beijing, He Ran sat in a window seat in first class, gazing at the city's night view from thousands of miles above with no interest whatsoever. She had barely managed to eat a little bread provided by the flight attendant when her body immediately reacted. Her stomach churned, and she nearly vomited several times, but she managed to hold it in. Given He Ran's current physical condition, she shouldn't be able to withstand such a long and arduous journey, but she still came. Without asking anyone's permission, she hailed a taxi without hesitation and sped off to the airport after leaving the hair salon. For no other reason than to see him once. Before landing, the plane was affected by turbulence, experiencing continuous shaking. He Ran was already dizzy and weak, and such a long period of shaking made her feel even worse. Looking at the lifeless face reflected in the glass, she was afraid that she would die on the plane. In Yang Wenping's words, that would be too shameful. Half an hour later, the plane gradually came to a stop on the runway. He Ran stepped out of the plane empty-handed, and shivered as her body came into contact with the intense cold air. She followed the flow of people into the arrival hall and stood in the center, looking around. There weren't many people, but it was bustling with activity. He Ran didn't see Xiao Han, but Xiao Han found her first. She was wearing a woolen hat and a thick mask that covered most of her face, with only her eyes showing. No one knew how Xiao Han recognized her. Feeling a warm coat draped over her shoulders, He Ran turned around. Xiao Han stood behind her, still tall and thin, his left arm wrapped in a thick bandage and slinged around his neck. He Ran's gaze lingered on his face for quite some time. He had a thin, wispy beard around his mouth, which he hadn't shaved in a long time, making him look disheveled and unkempt. He Ran was about to speak when Xiao Han said sternly before she could,

"You're being too reckless. How can you leave the hospital without permission at this crucial time?"

He Ran retorted even more fiercely:

"I'm still angry right now, so don't talk to me like that. I don't want to argue with you."

Xiao Han pursed his lips and remained silent. He Ran met his gaze head-on, neither giving an inch. After a moment, he stepped forward, took her hand, and said,

"We'll talk about it when we get home."

In February, winter in Beijing was not yet over. After leaving the airport, I felt the freezing cold and the biting north wind even more. He Ran is physically weak and cannot tolerate being cold. Xiao Han strode to the roadside, ignoring the queue, opened the back door of a taxi and shoved her inside. He Ran gave the driver an address; Xiao Han's house didn't have heating, so they would go to her rented apartment near the school. Sitting in the back seat, the two continued their previous conversation. He Ran looked at his arm and asked,

"What's wrong with your injury?"

Xiao Han said,

"It was a minor fracture. I had a follow-up check-up at the hospital this afternoon, and everything is fine now."

He Ran squinted and said,

"Was it Han Yu who did it?"

Xiao Han did not answer. He Ran stared intently at him and pressed,

"Is this why you haven't come to see me?"

Xiao Han remained silent. He Ran gave a heavy, cold snort:

"You were injured and didn't tell me, and you've been avoiding me. Do you know how I feel right now?"

She stared at him with wide eyes:

"If I get seriously ill in the future, I will hide from you until I die, and see how you feel."

"Alright, that's enough."

Xiao Han interrupted her in time, slowly pulling her into his arms. After a long while, he said,

"It was my fault."

This gesture signified admitting wrongdoing, but He Ran was still not satisfied and pinched his thigh hard. She only felt better after hearing him inhale. Upon returning home, the first thing He Ran did was turn on the heater. She didn't let go of Xiao Han's hand, pulling him straight to the bedside. Xiao Han sat down against the bed, and He Ran straddled him and sat on his lap. They faced each other, foreheads touching, their breaths mingling. Xiao Han removed her mask. The face was familiar to him, but she looked even thinner than before. He Ran's features weren't particularly striking; she had a pointed chin, a small nose, and thin earlobes, yet she still appeared endearing. He Ran asked softly,

"Did you miss me?"

"think."

He Ran smiled and asked,

"What are you thinking about?"

"I want both."

How much do you want to? Xiao Han didn't answer; his dry lips pressed against mine. His stubble was rough and itchy against her face, and He Ran dodged away in disgust, saying,

"Don't kiss me."

Xiao Han stopped what he was doing and looked at her somewhat helplessly. He Ran placed her hands on his shoulders, staring intently at him.

"Xiao Han, did you see that?"

"Even though I'm still getting injections, and I can't walk or eat, I still came to see you."

"So from now on, whenever I miss you, even if you're blind and have broken legs, you have to come to me without stopping."

She stated with absolute certainty:

"What I can do, you can do too."

After a moment of silence, Xiao Han nodded.

"Okay, I promise you."

He Ran stared intently into his eyes and made a promise:

"I will not hide my illness from you, and in return, you are not allowed to keep any difficulties from me."

Xiao Han reached behind her, covering the smooth back of her head, gently stroking the scalp where no hair obscured it. He nodded again,

"Okay."

He Ran buried half her face in his chest, her body gradually warming up. Suddenly remembering something, she turned around, took her bag from the bedside, and then took out a brocade pouch and a pair of scissors from the bag. Xiao Han stared at her, puzzled, and asked,

"What is this?"

"Didn't you say you liked my hair?"

She handed him all these things, and Xiao Han opened them with a puzzled look. Inside was indeed a lock of short, jet-black hair, tied in a small knot with a red string. He Ran said,

" I deliberately left a small tuft when I shaved my head today, as a souvenir."

Xiao Han held the tuft of fine hair in his hands, his eyes suddenly dimming. He wanted to say something, but in the end, he simply put it aside silently and gently pulled her into his arms. Back in Guangzhou, He Ran finally saw Han Yu. Everything was back to how it was two years ago. He Ran treated him with icy indifference, and he scoffed at her. This time, no matter how Yang Wenping and Mrs. Han tried to mediate, it was to no avail. Mrs. Han frequently visited He Ran at the hospital, bringing various expensive medicines and having heart-to-heart talks with her. Although both were from prominent families, her personality was completely different from Yang Wenping's. Yang Wenping was a consistently strong career woman, decisive and efficient. Mrs. Han, on the other hand, was devoted to being a wife and mother, gentle and kind, and lacked assertiveness. In that family, Han Yu was the little sun surrounded by stars, and he made all the decisions for himself. The reason he has such a bad temper today is because he was spoiled by his family. He Ran had thought more than once that if Mrs. Han and Yang Wenping's tempers could be switched, Mrs. Han would not allow Han Yu to marry a woman with a tragic fate, and Yang Wenping would not keep forcing her to marry someone she didn't love. Her ridiculous engagement with Han Yu would have been dropped long ago. But fate is cruel; in such an environment, Han Yu became like a stubborn leech that he could never shake off. During the Spring Festival, Dr. Xue granted He Ran permission to be discharged for a few days to relax. Yu Zhen, however, was not so lucky; she was still unable to get out of bed and walk. On the day He Ran was discharged from the hospital, Yu Zhen's envious gaze made her steps heavy. Looking at Yu Zhen's increasingly gaunt face, He Ran didn't know how to comfort her. Xiao Han was still staying in Xiaozhou Village, and He Ran would visit him whenever she had time. When Yang Wenping asked, He Ran only said she was meeting with a classmate. Yang Wenping had a slight suspicion, but she couldn't stop her. He Ran still loves to look good; she always wears a wig and light makeup before going out. Perhaps because she has stopped chemotherapy, her appetite has improved these past few days, and she has more energy. If it weren't for her frequent fainting spells, she would look no different from a normal person. On Valentine's Day, He Ran unexpectedly received roses from Xiao Han. In her memory, he wasn't the type of person who knew how to create romance. Xiao Han explained that he naturally learned some things after opening the flower shop. He Ran tiptoed and returned his kiss with a light, fleeting peck.

"Thank you."

After that, they walked side by side down the street like any ordinary couple, their fingers always intertwined, and they shared the same scarf around their necks for warmth. After watching the movie at noon, they ate at a tea restaurant on the fifth floor of the mall. By sheer bad luck, they ran into Han Yu and his new girlfriend as soon as they entered. Xiao Han looked at He Ran, asking for her opinion with his eyes. He Ran chose to ignore it and pulled Xiao Han straight inside. Han Yu had obviously seen them too. He snorted coldly and followed behind them without hesitation. After He Ran took her seat, Han Yu sat down not far from them. The waiter came over to pour tea for the two of them. Taking advantage of the gap, He Ran turned her head and glanced at Han Yu. She couldn't help but tease him,

"He's really persistent. When you're in Beijing, he stays in Beijing; when you go back to Guangzhou, he goes back to Guangzhou too. I'm starting to wonder if he's fallen in love with you."

Xiao Han still couldn't grasp her sense of humor and just silently drank his tea. Just as He Ran had expected, the meal was far from peaceful. Even though she deliberately ignored Han Yu's frequent provocative glances, Han Yu eventually couldn't hold back and took the initiative to come to her door. At that moment, He Ran was drinking soup when Han Yu swaggered up to their table and said

"Yo"

in a pretentious manner.

"I never expected you two to have a date at such a fancy restaurant,"

Han Yu said with a forced smile, looking at He Ran.

"Let me guess, you're definitely paying for this meal, right?"

He Ran and Xiao Han both ignored his malicious sarcasm. He Ran looked at Xiao Han, placed a slice of steak on his plate, and said softly,

"Try this."

Han Yu's gaze shifted to a bouquet of roses placed on the table, and he gave a cryptic twitch at the corner of his mouth. He reached out and picked up the bouquet, toying with it between his fingertips before ruthlessly plucking a petal.

"Aren't you the florist? How can you be so stingy, only sending a bouquet for a holiday? If you ask me, you should send a whole truckload."

The two people opposite remained unmoved, continuing to have lunch as if no one else was around. Seeing He Ran sitting next to Xiao Han like a little bird, Han Yu felt more irritable than ever before. His patience finally ran out, and the smile on his face could no longer be maintained. He slammed his hand on the table and shouted,

"I'm talking to you! Can't you hear me?!"

The sudden increase in decibels startled He Ran, causing her to lose her grip and drop the spoon to the ground with a clatter.

Han Yu went even further, sweeping his hands across the table, knocking everything over instantly. The floor was littered with cups and plates, and scalding soup splashed onto He Ran's cheeks and dress. She calmly picked up a tissue and wiped everything clean. Under the tablecloth, Xiao Han clenched his fists secretly. Just as he was about to stand up, He Ran pressed his hand down and shook her head slightly. It takes two to tango, He Ran knew this all too well. Han Yu's madness was enough; they didn't need to play along. Han Yu's attention shifted to Xiao Han. He strolled over to him leisurely, a smirk playing on his lips.

"I'm really sorry I accidentally hurt you that time."

As he spoke, he reached out and grabbed Xiao Han's left arm, his face contorted with rage as he squeezed hard.

"I wonder if it still hurts?"

Xiao Han frowned imperceptibly, remaining silent. He Ran stood up and beckoned a waiter. She briefly explained the situation, and several security guards immediately dragged the emotionally distraught Han Yu out. Then, He Ran glared at the waitress standing in front of her and reprimanded her:

"What kind of standard is your restaurant? How can you let in a mentally ill person who disturbs other people's meals?"

The waiter looked apologetic and said with his head down,

"I'm very sorry, it was our oversight. I assure you that this will never happen again."

He Ran wasn't buying it.

"What's the use of an apology? All the food we ordered has been spilled. Who's going to pay for this meal?"

"I'm so sorry, I'm so sorry,"

the waiter said, bowing repeatedly.

"I'll place an order right away and have the chef make you a new one."

"When will we be able to make another one?"

He Ran pressed on, still glaring at him.

"We're in a hurry to get to the next place right now."

The waitress looked embarrassed by what she said and stood there helplessly. Finally, they called the lobby manager over to resolve the issue. The manager personally apologized to He Ran, instructed the chef to immediately prepare a new meal for two for them, and waived the cost of the meal. After eating and drinking to their hearts' content, He Ran left the restaurant arm in arm with Xiao Han, completely satisfied. The two entered the elevator one after the other. He Ran raised an eyebrow at Xiao Han and said with a hint of smugness,

"I saved you the cost of a meal. Aren't you going to praise me?"

Xiao Han pursed his lips without smiling, his eyes thoughtful. Perhaps influenced by Han Yu, He Ran noticed that the people around her had become much quieter throughout the afternoon. She glanced at him several times, but he remained with his brows slightly furrowed, looking absent-minded. He Ran didn't say anything more, but simply held his hand tightly in her own. Yang Wenping has been keeping a close eye on He Ran these past few days, so He Ran can't stay out all night. After dinner, Xiao Han takes her home. They walked into the villa area without taking an electric cart, holding hands and strolling aimlessly until they unknowingly arrived near He Ran's house. The tree-lined path under the night sky possessed a mysterious beauty, with a bright moon hanging among the intertwined branches. A faint light could be seen in the darkness—the cigarette dangling from Xiao Han's lips. Although the moon has its phases of waxing and waning, and people experience joys and sorrows, at this moment, it seems that there are more people together than apart. Unconsciously, they walked to a stone bench, and He Ran said to stop and rest for a while. Having no tissues on him, Xiao Han hastily wiped the dust off the stone bench with his hands before letting her sit down. A cold wind was blowing, and He Ran exhaled a breath of warm air as she vigorously rubbed her hands together. Xiao Han said,

"It's too cold outside, you should go home early."

He Ran leaned against him without moving and said,

"It's okay, let's sit for a while longer."

Xiao Han's clothes were big enough, so he zipped them up, completely enveloping her. He Ran buried her face in his collar, close to the artery in his neck. From this spot, she could deeply feel his scent and his every heartbeat. He Ran said softly,

"Xiao Han, I have to go back to the hospital tomorrow."

"Um."

When are you going back to Beijing?

"By the end of the month."

After thinking for a few seconds, she demanded,

"Then you must come to see me at least once before you go back."

Xiao Han nodded:

"Okay."

He Ran suddenly felt a tickling sensation on her face. It turned out that Xiao Han was rubbing his chin against her cheek. In just a few days, his beard had grown back, fine and wispy, covering his jawline and sideburns, with a slightly stiff texture. He Ran raised her head and carefully observed him in the dim moonlight. Just like the first impression I had of him, Xiao Han had deep, mature features and a bronze complexion. His features, combined with his beard, easily reminded me of a man from the UAE leading a camel through the vast desert. He Ran couldn't help but smile.

"You look quite good with a beard."

Xiao Han didn't reply. He was always calm and composed, and didn't think he was good-looking. After sitting for a while, He Ran softly called out,

"Kiss me."

Xiao Han stroked his beard, hesitated slightly, and said,

"It's a bit prickly."

He Ran smiled and said,

"It's nothing."

She closed her eyes, waiting for him to slowly lower his face. Their lips met, and the warmth around them increased with each embrace and kiss. Being pampered and surrounded by others, Xiao Han could give her immense satisfaction even without using any techniques. The reason why the soul is so attached to and lingers in the human world seems to be just for this moment of tenderness. Even if she eventually leaves, at least her journey will not have been in vain. Xiao Han entwined his tongue with hers while tightly warming her two small hands, but they remained icy cold. He Ran showed no sign of ending the conversation, so Xiao Han had to leave prematurely. He straightened up and said seriously,

"You mustn't catch a cold right now. Go home quickly."

He Ran stared at him, her gaze lingering, but Xiao Han remained expressionless. She gently brushed his lips against hers again, but he didn't react.

He Ran sighed and had no choice but to stand up.

"Alright, let's go."

The comfortable hot bath dispelled the chill from her body. He Ran came out of the bathroom and subconsciously glanced at the clock on the wall. 11 o'clock. An hour has passed since Xiao Han left. At this time of day, Xiao Han would usually send a text message every now and then to let him know where he was, but today He Ran's phone remained completely silent. Unable to rest easy, she called Xiao Han again. The first few times it rang for a minute without being answered, but this time his phone was simply switched off. Without further hesitation, He Ran grabbed a coat and a backpack and headed outside. Yang Wenping stopped her at the top of the stairs, frowning as she asked,

"Where are you going so late?"

He Ran had no intention of explaining, so she walked straight out the door, bypassing her. As the night deepened, with no houses to block the view, the outside was freezing cold and the wind howled. He Ran walked along, her face pale from the headwinds. She kept retracing her steps, trying to find some clues, but the night was too dark for her to see through. At the end of the grove where she had just been walking with Xiao Han, He Ran suddenly heard the sounds of a fierce fight, and there seemed to be quite a few people involved. Her heart tightened, and she instinctively looked into the distance. From the depths of the jungle came the occasional angry roar of a man; He Ran recognized it as Han Yu's voice.

"How many times have I told you to get lost!"

"How can you be so shameless! You won't learn your lesson until you see the coffin!"

"Remember this lesson well! If I see you again, I'll have you crippled!"

That angry voice was full of ferocity, sounding particularly shrill in the silent, dark night. But what was most heartbreaking was that He Ran hadn't heard Xiao Han's voice at all. Her unease was amplified to the extreme, and she quickened her pace and ran in that direction. The scene she least wanted to see finally appeared before her eyes. He Ran couldn't tell if the person surrounded in the middle was Xiao Han, but she recognized that the phone that was smashed on the ground was the one she had gone to buy with Xiao Han, and now it was just a few pieces of broken wreckage. Fists rained down on Xiao Han, but he remained silent, whether from exhaustion or unconsciousness was unknown. Her gaze shifted to Han Yu, who was standing on the outer edge, and He Ran's voice was colder than the night wind:

"Tell them to stop."

Han Yu gave a cruel smile and ignored him.

"Didn't you hear me?"

He Ran was almost hysterical.

"Tell them to stop!"

Han Yu calmly replied,

"Do you think that's possible, my young lady?"

"Han Yu, you disgust me to the core!"

After shouting this, He Ran didn't bother to look at him again. He Ran walked into the crowd, trying to break up the fight, but her frail body was no match for anyone. In the chaos, someone's fist swung at her with great force. He Ran stumbled backward and fell to the ground, her palm scraped raw by a protruding stone.

"Stop it, all of you!"

Han Yu suddenly shouted. The group finally stopped beating her. Han Yu strode over to He Ran and reached out to help her up. He Ran ignored him, braced herself on the ground, and struggled to her feet on her own. After managing to stand up, He Ran rushed over to the person who had collapsed on the ground without hesitation, asking anxiously,

"How are you?"

Xiao Han's face was covered in blood and dirt, with a long gash on his brow bone, dripping with blood. His body was also covered in wounds, his left arm hanging limply from his shoulder. He Ran rolled up his sleeve to look, and the purplish-blue bruises were a shocking sight. She couldn't imagine what he would have looked like if she had arrived a few minutes later. Fortunately, Xiao Han was still conscious. He drooped his head and tried to force a smile.

"I'm fine."

He Ran put one of his arms on her shoulder and tried to help him up.

"Let's go to the hospital."

Xiao Han put half of his weight on her and barely managed to stand up. Han Yu stood in their way, asking,

"Did I say I'd let you go?"

He Ran frowned and glared at him coldly,

"Get out of my way."

Han Yu pursed his lips into a straight line,

"I won't allow it."

He Ran gritted her teeth; she had never shown her disgust so directly before. This will have endless consequences; it must be put to rest today. She helped Xiao Han to the side and made him stand against a tree.

"Wait for me, I'll take care of it soon."

Turning around, He Ran strode up to Han Yu and unhesitatingly took out the scissors she used for shaving from her bag. Caught off guard, the tip of the scissors pierced Han Yu's chest, barely retaining any force. He Ran clenched his fists tightly, his eyes blazing, but his tone remained flat.

"You say you're going to cripple him? I'll kill you first before that happens."

Han Yu was stunned for a moment, then burst into laughter.

"Kill me?"

As if he had heard something amusing, his shoulders trembled uncontrollably.

"He Ran, aren't you exaggerating a bit?"

"It's not an exaggeration at all. You should know me very well."

He Ran's voice was icy cold, as was her gaze.

"If you bother Xiao Han again, this pair of scissors will decisively pierce your heart next time. I mean what I say."

He Ran's eyes flashed with a cold light, like a sharp blade piercing straight into Han Yu's face. Every word she spoke was forceful and solemn, as if she were making a vow. This is not a joke, and it is definitely not meant to scare anyone.

"That sounds scary, but do you dare?"

Han Yu still smiled mischievously, seemingly unconcerned.
---

He grasped He Ran's hand and pulled it closer to his chest, saying,

"You can try stabbing it in now."

"Why wouldn't I dare?"

He Ran met his gaze without fear.

"The worst that can happen is jail. But jail isn't that scary. Being confined by you and my mother isn't much freer than being in jail."

Their gazes locked in a silent battle for a long time. He Ran's presence became increasingly formidable, while Han Yu suffered a series of defeats. It's hard to imagine that a woman who looks petite and delicate could possess such a chilling gaze. And it's that same sharp look that makes her slender frame seem more invincible than a giant. Han Yu's face gradually froze, filled with shock and disbelief. He gritted his teeth and said,

"He Ran, are you crazy?!"

"I've known you since we were kids. How long have you been with this man? And you're willing to fight me for him?!"

Han Yu pointed at Xiao Han, becoming more and more agitated as she spoke:

"What do I lack compared to him? I can give you everything he can! Why won't you be with me?!"

"I told you long ago, a single glance can determine life or death."

He Ran remained calm and composed, her face expressionless. She turned to look at Xiao Han, her eyes filled with unspoken words. Xiao Han also frowned as he looked at her, panting heavily, as if he wanted to open his mouth but couldn't utter a word. He Ran turned her head and continued to say to Han Yu,

"Even if you beat him to death today, it's impossible for me to be with you, it's never going to happen."

Once a life-or-death decision is made, there's no turning back. Han Yu slowly took several steps back, as if all his strength had been drained, and stood with his back against a tree stump. Someone came to help him, but he angrily pushed them away. Han Yu stood rooted to the spot, a deep sorrow spreading through his entire body. He stared stubbornly at He Ran, this seemingly harmless woman who always managed to deliver a fatal blow when she least expected it. This was the first time in his life he had shown such a distraught expression; he knew without a doubt that he must look utterly pathetic.

"He Ran, you're ruthless."

Han Yu's voice was almost squeezed out between clenched teeth.

"Before I regret it, get out of here with this man!"

He Ran took a step back, released the scissors from her hand, and let them fall into the pile of dead leaves on the ground. She turned and walked to Xiao Han's side, gave him a very faint smile, then put her arm around his and limped out. The biting wind whipped up withered grass and fallen leaves, and the gale swept past like a chaotic dance of demons.

"Even if I let you go, your mother won't."

Han Yu's cold voice came from behind, carried by the biting wind.

"This is your fate; you can't escape it!"

He Ran paused for a moment, but did not stop, and continued walking forward. He Ran took Xiao Han to the hospital for a full check-up. He suffered a mild concussion after being hit on the head with a heavy object. His left arm injury, which had not yet healed, was further aggravated by this incident. No major problems have been found in the rest of his body for the time being, but he will need to be hospitalized for observation for a period of time. He Ran's hand, which was rubbed raw by the stone, was still bleeding profusely, and the doctor had to help treat it as well. After the medicine was applied and the wound was bandaged, Xiao Han was transferred to a regular ward, while He Ran stayed behind to take care of him. They moved into a multi-person ward, with five or six beds placed against the wall, but no one was sleeping in them; the large room was empty and silent. Xiao Han lay on the hospital bed, his gaze fixed on He Ran's face. He Ran sat on the edge of the bed, gazing at him without saying a word. Xiao Han had several stitches on his forehead and near his brow bone; the thin black threads crisscrossed his flesh, and just looking at them was painful. Knowing that those injuries were for her, He Ran couldn't say a word. She knew that as long as they were together one day, the wounds on his face and body would only increase, not decrease. The words Han Yu said when he left still echoed in He Ran's ears. Is this her fate, something she can't escape? She didn't believe it. My second cousin chose to end her life, but she will rebel and change things in another way. A crazy idea suddenly popped into her mind, and He Ran grabbed Xiao Han's hand urgently,

"Xiao Han, take me away."

Xiao Han looked at her, his eyes revealing confusion. He Ran emphasized, her voice firm:

"Take me away from here, anywhere is fine."

--elopement. This bold idea came on so suddenly and violently that it could not be suppressed. He Ran had never had such a thought before, but when it suddenly popped into her head, it was like finding the only way out of a predicament, driving her crazy. Xiao Han understood what she meant, but remained silent. He pursed his lips, which were chapped from the wind, and the blood at the corner of his mouth had formed a hard scab. After an unknown amount of time, Xiao Han finally spoke:

"No."

He Ran was speechless, staring at him with wide eyes. Xiao Han continued,

"Your most important thing right now is to get your health back."

A thousand words stuck in her throat, and He Ran felt an intense urge to speak out. Many complex thoughts swirled within her, but ultimately she restrained herself from arguing with Xiao Han. He Ran kept her mouth tightly shut and didn't speak, and the atmosphere in the ward gradually became dull and stiff.

---

After a while, she stood up and walked quickly out of the ward, saying,

"I need to calm down by myself."

Xiao Han tried to reach out to stop her, but He Ran's figure had already disappeared outside the door in a flash, and his hand only managed to grab a wisp of air. In an instant, he was left alone in the empty ward. His gaze wandered aimlessly around him. The pale white walls seemed too cold and desolate, and even the spotless white sheets beneath him seemed lifeless, which made him feel terrible. Ten minutes passed, and He Ran still hadn't returned. Xiao Han began to worry and prepared to get out of bed to look for her. Just as he was about to sit up, the sound of high heels came from the distance down the corridor. Xiao Han listened carefully for a few seconds and realized that it was not He Ran's footsteps. Of course, it's not that nurses, who work in quiet places like hospitals, can't wear shoes that make noise. The footsteps grew closer and closer, eventually stopping in front of Xiao Han's hospital room. A woman dressed like a wealthy lady pushed open the door and walked in. She had a cold and beautiful face and looked somewhat familiar. The woman walked straight towards Xiao Han, stood in front of the hospital bed, and looked down at him. The arrogant aura emanating from her was exactly the same as Han Yu's. Xiao Han had already guessed what was going on when he heard the woman say coldly,

"I am He Ran's mother."
---

## Chapter Nine: My Heart Aspires To It

What might be someone else's twenties is my entire life. My life is too short; it's only enough to love one person. It has been five days since Xiao Han disappeared. He Ran finally stopped making countless phone calls every day, only to receive a message that his phone was switched off without exception. She could vaguely guess why Xiao Han had left.

---

That night, He Ran was carrying a kettle of hot water back to the ward when she heard Yang Wenping's aggressive voice coming from inside as she approached the door. He Ran didn't know how or when she found this place. She also only overheard a small part of Yang Wenping and Xiao Han's conversation.

"Let's put aside love and romance for now. I just want to ask you one question: if I hand my daughter over to you, how will you cover her medical expenses? Don't blame me for being blunt, but even if you go bankrupt, you still won't be able to cure her illness."

He Ran pushed open the door and went in, her footsteps very light, silently walking behind the two of them. Yang Wenping turned to look at her. He Ran placed the thermos on the table and gestured for her to leave:

"He needs to rest now. Please leave first."

Yang Wenping didn't say much. She gave He Ran a meaningful look, then turned and strode away. The echoes of her high heels reverberated endlessly down the corridor. After Yang Wenping left, He Ran sat down next to Xiao Han as if nothing had happened and poured him some water. Xiao Han lay on the bed, his face expressionless, remaining silent.

"I don't care about what she said at all."

He Ran handed him a glass of water, her expression indifferent.

"So you shouldn't care either."

Xiao Han simply took the water quietly without saying a word. When He Ran woke up the next day, she found herself sleeping in Xiao Han's hospital bed. He had left without saying goodbye, leaving nothing behind. He Ran's treatment after returning to the hospital was not smooth, and even hit a bottleneck at one point. The drug allergy was the most painful experience during this period.

---

That afternoon, she was receiving an IV drip in her hospital room as usual. After half a bottle of medicine was finished, she suddenly felt a strong sense of rejection and discomfort. It was a real feeling of suffocation, as if someone was forcefully choking her neck in her confusion, but she had no strength to struggle at all. Breathing became increasingly difficult, her chest felt so swollen it was about to burst, and she could almost see her soul slowly leaving her body. Within minutes, the bed sheet beneath He Ran was soaked with sweat. Finally, a nurse noticed her abnormality. Disordered footsteps and shouts rushed in from all directions. The noise was so loud that it trampled on every nerve in her body. In a daze, He Ran felt someone put an oxygen mask on her face. Her body felt like it was being pulled from underwater, and she finally had the strength to breathe deeply. A white figure kept swaying back and forth in front of her half-open eyes, with a strong hypnotic effect. He Ran thought she must be hallucinating, otherwise how could she see Xiao Han standing outside the frosted window, anxiously looking at her? Her vision was blurry, and she saw several double images. With the last ounce of strength she had left, she shakily reached out to the men of Xiao Han, but her hand slumped down a few seconds later, and she fainted. Not long after, He Ran regained consciousness. When she opened her eyes, the oxygen machine had been removed, the needle in her hand had been replaced with a different medication, and the discomfort in her body was slowly dissipating. Like someone who has endured countless hardships, He Ran had never looked so haggard before, her face pale and bluish. The nurse standing by told her that this was a normal reaction to the medication and that she didn't need to worry too much. He Ran learned from the nurse that Xiao Han had indeed come by, but left after her condition stabilized. He Ran asked the nurse to hand her the phone on the table. Perhaps because she felt weak all over, she found the small piece of metal in her hand to be very heavy. Unsurprisingly, Xiao Han's phone was still switched off. He Ran sent him a text message, even though she knew he probably wouldn't receive it.

"I have something to tell you."

Dr. Xue reported the sudden situation to Yang Wenping. After dinner, Yang Wenping came to the hospital to see He Ran. Knowing that Xiao Han's sudden departure was related to her, He Ran didn't say much, but her attitude towards her became even colder. Yang Wenping sat patiently by He Ran's bedside for a long time. Perhaps out of guilt, she remained silent, only pouring water for He Ran. Yang Wenping only got up to leave when He Ran was ready to rest. Reaching the ward door, she lingered for a long time, hesitated several times, and then turned back. He Ran covered herself tightly with a blanket and turned her back to her. Yang Wenping stared at the back of her head and said in a low voice,

"You are my daughter after all, I can't just leave you like this."

He Ran remained motionless, as if he hadn't heard a thing. Yang Wenping continued,

"Now you should just cooperate with the treatment and not think about anything else. Take this opportunity to cut it off completely."

After speaking, she received no response for a long time. Yang Wenping sighed silently, turned around and walked out. Two days later. While He Ran was taking a walk on the lawn, she finally saw Xiao Han, who was late. The afternoon sun was shining lazily at three or four o'clock. He Ran sat on a stone bench, sketching a sprig of winter plum blossom opposite him. She was wearing a mask and a hat, completely covered from head to toe, with only her hand holding the charcoal pencil exposed to the air. Xiao Han sat silently beside He Ran, watching quietly, not wanting to disturb her. If he also had hands that could draw, the image he would most want to capture of her while she was drawing. He Ran didn't close her sketchbook until the sun was almost setting. She turned to look at Xiao Han and sighed slowly,

"Why didn't you tell me you weren't wearing a wig before you came?"

Xiao Han reached out and straightened her hat.

" No need, it's fine like this."

He Ran touched her face, looking puzzled:

"Have I become ugly? Sigh, when people are sick, all the pigmentation on their faces comes out."

Xiao Han said in an unchanged tone,

"No, don't overthink it."

He Ran raised her head, her eyes fixed on his face, lost in thought.

---

After a while, she asked without warning, "Xiao Han, are you going to give up on me?"
---

Xiao Han was slightly taken aback, then subtly shifted his gaze away from her face. He Ran then asked,

"To put it bluntly, are you going to dump me?"

Xiao Han frowned, and after a few seconds said,

"No."

He Ran chuckled:

"But you don't look like you don't exist right now."

Xiao Han pursed his lips, his gaze dimming. He always acted this way when he couldn't speak. He Ran asked, seemingly casually,

"When are you going back to Beijing?"

Xiao Han replied,

"One week later."

She circled back to the original topic:

"Will this be the last time we see each other?"

"..."

After waiting for a long time without receiving his answer, He Ran curled the corners of her lips, but there was no smile in her eyes.

"So, you were convinced by my mother?"

"Child..."

Xiao Han began with difficulty, his voice low and hoarse,

"We all hope you are healthy."

"Including marrying another man after I get healthy?"

He Ran smiled disdainfully.

"Xiao Han, you don't naively think that we'll still have a chance to be together after I'm discharged from the hospital, do you?"

Xiao Han fell silent again, only the furrows between his brows deepened.

"Are you really prepared for this?"

He Ran leaned close to his face, her voice very soft.

"The man sitting next to me in the future will be another man, and he can hold me like you do. Are you really willing to do that?"

"Child..."

Xiao Han's voice seemed to be stuck in a quagmire, each word heavy with constraint.

"My goal used to be to give you a better life, but now... my only wish is for you to be healthy and safe."

He paused there, using all his strength to finish the sentence:

"No matter who you are with."

"You're truly selfless and noble."

He Ran sneered a few times and sat back down. She sighed and said softly,

"But what if I can't do that?"

Xiao Han hesitated for a long time before saying,

"You're still young, you'll meet someone better than me in the future."

He Ran smiled faintly,

"I'm afraid I won't be able to meet him."

She looked up at the sky, which was unusually pale today, without a single cloud or a breath of wind. With a long sigh, He Ran's voice seemed to drift over from a great distance,

"Do you know why I matured so early?"

"What is someone else's twenties may be my whole life."

Recalling the past, He Ran couldn't help but smile.

"So I should take advantage of my life to travel to more places, try more things, and... indulge myself in loving someone who may not have a future."

"Xiao Han."

She turned to look at him, a faint smile on her face.

"Even if I know there are people better than you, I can't do it."

"My life is too short; it's only enough to love one person."

"That person is you, only you. Should we say you're lucky or unlucky?"

As she finished speaking, He Ran was pulled into a warm embrace, Xiao Han's scent filling her nostrils. His hands were tightly bound around her waist, his injured arms trembling uncontrollably from the force. Xiao Han's clothes were made of poor quality, and the parts He Ran touched were hard and worn, but she cherished this embrace more than ever before. She could sense many things she wanted to say but hadn't yet uttered from that slightly trembling hug. After an unknown amount of time, Xiao Han lowered his voice and said,

"Listen to the doctor, you will get better."

He Ran rested her chin on his shoulder.

"Xiao Han, that's not what I want to hear."

She spoke slowly and deliberately, as if in a daze:

"Take me away."

Xiao Han placed his hand on the back of her head and stroked it again and again, saying,

"I'm sorry."

"It's not this sentence either."

"sorry."

"Xiao Han, take me away."

"sorry."

He Ran gave a silent, cold laugh, pulling away from his embrace.

"I poured my heart out to you, and all you say is these three words?"

Xiao Han's Adam's apple bobbed, and his face turned ashen. A gust of wind swept away the withered leaves on the ground, rustling and desolate, even the proud plum blossom on the branch trembled. The smile on He Ran's face vanished. She stood up and used all the strength she could muster to smash the sketchbook in her hand onto Xiao Han.

"Xiao Han, you're a coward!"

Without further hesitation, she left resolutely after uttering those words. The afterglow of dusk stretched Xiao Han's shadow long. He stood there for a long time, his figure sculpted into a statue by the biting wind. He Ran walked back to the inpatient department with lightning speed; she hadn't walked this fast since her illness relapsed. Pushing open the ward door, Yu Zhen sat on the bed, posing in front of the mirror. The wig she was wearing belonged to He Ran, and seeing that the owner had returned, she quickly took it off and returned it to He Ran. Yu Zhen stuck out her tongue sheepishly,

"Sorry, I just wanted to test the effect."

He Ran said nonchalantly,

"It's okay, take it if you like."

After declining several times, Yu Zhen accepted the wigs and then asked He Ran,

"Where did you buy your wigs? Every single one of them is so beautiful. Tell me about them."

He Ran took out her phone and shared a website address with her. This conversation might seem comical to outsiders, but it's perfectly normal for the female patients on their floor. Yu Zhen loved the wig He Ran gave her and wore it to bed. As He Ran was about to rest, Yu Zhen called out to her,

"He Ran, can you paint a portrait of me?"

She sat up straight and smoothed her hair with her hands.

"Just draw me looking like this."

He Ran smiled and said,

"Didn't we say we'd draw you after you were discharged from the hospital and your hair grew back?"

Yu Zhen seemed to remember something, a cloud of sorrow clouding her brows. Her voice lowered,

"I'm afraid I won't live to see that day..."

He Ran was speechless for a moment, not knowing what to say.

She picked up a piece of white paper and a charcoal pencil from the table, walked to Yu Zhen's bedside, and asked,

"I lost my sketchbook. Can I draw for you on regular paper?"

Yu Zhen smiled and said,

"I'll listen to you."

He Ran sat down, sharpening a pencil while carefully observing Yu Zhen's facial features, mentally sketching out her features. After gesturing for a moment, she suddenly realized:

"You look a lot like one of my former patients."

Yu Zhen laughed,

"Really?"

"Yes."

He Ran nodded gently.

"And she also likes to read Wilbur's books."

"What a coincidence!"

Yu Zhen immediately became interested and asked,

"So how is she now?"

"She..."

He Ran was speechless. In the few seconds that He Ran hesitated, Yu Zhen quickly understood her meaning, and her face gradually turned pale. The awkward atmosphere happened in an instant, and the only sound left in the room was the monotonous sound of sharp blades moving over pen tips. After that, they didn't communicate much; one sat quietly, and the other painted quietly. Since He Ran's allergic reaction to medication, she has been receiving lumbar puncture treatment. Normally, Dr. Xue performs the procedure himself. Dr. Xue is very skilled and finishes quickly without any pain. However, if Dr. Xue is not available and another doctor performs the procedure, it can be quite painful. He Ran lay curled up on the hospital bed, her back arched unnaturally. She could feel the icy steel needles piercing her flesh and slowly penetrating her bones; it was an indescribable pain and fear that tormented both her body and mind. Even with anesthesia in his lower back, his mind remained perfectly clear, and every second became unforgettable amidst He Ran's groans. The longest session lasted nearly an hour, and it took three or four doctors to complete the procedure. Afterwards, He Ran collapsed onto the hospital bed, utterly exhausted. She couldn't stop herself from breaking out in a cold sweat; her clothes were soaked through as if she'd just been pulled from ice water. If there is ever a moment when I want to end it all, this is the only time. For six hours after the lumbar puncture, He Ran had to lie flat on the bed and couldn't move. With no one to talk to, He Ran had to force herself to sleep. The sound of night rain is disturbing. He Ran was woken up at three in the morning and did not have a moment's peace for the rest of the night. That deep, bone-chilling pain began to resurface, spreading upwards from her legs. Perhaps due to the numerous lumbar punctures she'd had recently undergone, her senses in her limbs had gradually dulled; initially, it was just a slight numbness. Now, even walking was unsteady and unsteady. She tossed and turned in bed for an hour or so, unable to fall asleep. Suddenly, she heard a soft sob beside her. He Ran listened carefully and confirmed that it wasn't her imagination. The intermittent sobs blended with the pattering rain, making them difficult to detect. He Ran hesitated for a moment, then softly called out,

"Yu Zhen?"

The crying stopped abruptly, and a few seconds later a response came from the other side of the bed curtains:

"Mmm."

"What's wrong with you?"

"..."

There was a long silence from the other side of the bed:

"I'm fine."

"Are you really alright?"

He Ran asked, still worried.

"..."

He Ran struggled to move her numb legs, threw off the covers, and got out of bed. She turned on the light first, then slowly walked to Yu Zhen's bedside. What met my gaze was a pair of red-rimmed eyes, glistening with tears. The continuous high fever had ravaged this young girl, leaving her pale and emaciated, her eyes deeply sunken, and she was barely recognizable as a human. He Ran sat down and asked,

"Are you feeling unwell? Should I call a nurse for you?"

"No need."

Yu Zhen shook her head, her voice barely a whisper.

"I'm just scared..."

"What are you afraid of?"

She covered her face with her hands, her shoulders trembling, and said frankly,

"I'm afraid of dying."

He Ran paused for a moment, her composure seemingly feigned. The word

"death"

was a taboo subject on their floor; no one had ever mentioned it so directly before. Yu Zhen said with a sob in her voice,

"I've looked it up online, and many people who get this disease die because of a relapse. I feel like I'm not going to make it either..."

He Ran didn't know what to say, so she could only comfort her by saying,

"Don't think too much about it. Most people scare themselves to death."

Yu Zhen sniffled and said,

"I know, but I can't control myself. Every time I hear footsteps in the corridor, I start having all sorts of wild thoughts. I wonder if the Black and White Impermanence have come by? Just now I even dreamed that they were standing on the window, grinning sinisterly, trying to catch me..."

He Ran pouted and said,

"Maybe they're here to arrest me. You're just being presumptuous."

Yu Zhen burst into laughter through her tears, looking at her with teary eyes,

"You're quite humorous."

"Really?"

He Ran smiled faintly,

"But my boyfriend has never been made to laugh."

When this topic was brought up, Yu Zhen fell silent again. After a long while, she slowly spoke:

"Actually, I also have a boy I like."

"He confessed his feelings to me after the college entrance exam. A week before that, I suddenly fainted at home and was taken to the hospital where it was found that I had relapsed..."

"And then?"

He Ran asked. Yu Zhen said,

"I'm not with him anymore, he has a girlfriend now."

Does he know about your illness? Yu Zhen shook her head:

"I don't know."

He Ran was speechless for a moment. We didn't see eye to eye.

---

After a while, Yu Zhen continued,

"I really want to see him before I leave and tell him how I feel... But I look like a ghost now, it's better to remember him than to see him again."

She almost burst into tears again as she spoke, burying her face between her legs, leaving only her thin, frail shoulders trembling. He Ran gently hugged her. After what seemed like an eternity, Yu Zhen finally raised her head again, took a piece of drawing paper from under her pillow, and handed it to He Ran, asking,

"If I'm gone one day, could you please give these two things to him for me?"
