He Ran didn't immediately agree, but instead reached out and took it, then opened it to take a look. That was the portrait she painted for Yu Zhen. A tuft of hair tied with a red string was tucked in the fold of the paper. The smile that appeared on He Ran's lips carried a strong bitter taste; it turned out that every girl harbored the same thought in her heart— Centuries later, even though their ashes have flowed eastward with the great river and vanished into the dust, these fine strands of hair remain resilient and enduring, telling a story of untold affection. Perhaps the Black and White Impermanence that Yu Zhen dreamed of that day were not just an illusion. Two nights later, she passed away permanently in her sleep. Due to organ failure caused, the doctors were powerless to save her.

---

The following day, Yu Zhen's mother came to the ward to collect her belongings. To He Ran's surprise, Yu Zhen actually left her a book, her favorite

"Grace and Courage"

. He Ran hesitated for a long time before opening the book. Tucked between the pages was a homemade bookmark, emitting a faint, lingering fragrance. The bookmark bore delicate, elegant handwriting, recording a very famous passage from the book. Do not weep at my grave. I was not there, nor was I asleep. I am the howling wind, I am a diamond shining on the snow. I am the sunshine in the wheat field. I am the gentle autumn rain. You wake up in the stillness of dawn. I have transformed into a silent bird, flapping my wings and soaring away... I am a gentle constellation, twinkling faintly in the dark night. Do not weep at my grave. I wasn't there... He Ran slowly closed the book, a wave of sadness washing over her as she thought of the girl who hid in the night and cried alone. After that, He Ran moved back to a private room. Without gathering, there is no parting; she didn't want to experience such a brief separation again. Yang Wenping visits her once a day, and He Jin also shows up occasionally. He Ran has difficulty moving around, so they have hired a caregiver to take care of her daily needs. The nanny was a forty-year-old woman who was used to doing rough work. Every time she helped He Ran take a bath, the discomfort caused by her rough fingertips would remind He Ran of Xiao Han's severed finger. Once my bedmate, now we're worlds apart, and all I can do is sigh. The lumbar puncture treatment was still underway, and He Ran's numbness in her lower limbs was becoming increasingly severe. She worried that if this continued, her legs would gradually become paralyzed. The side effects of chemotherapy vary from person to person, and doctors could not give an accurate diagnosis. He Ran was unwilling to take the risk, especially not to gamble with her legs, so she had to stop treatment midway. However, the numbness in her legs did not improve after the lumbar puncture was stopped. Her days and nights became reversed; she was drowsy during the day due to the medication, but at night, she was unusually awake due to the throbbing pain in her bones. As Yu Zhen said, the hospital at night is a place filled with the aura of death. Unable to sleep, He Ran stared wide-eyed at the dark ceiling, hearing the sound of a gurney being pushed away outside the door, the echo lingering all the way to the end of the long corridor. That mournful sound was like the gates of hell opening, a hundred ghosts summoning souls, an incessant cacophony. At times like these, He Ran always felt particularly desolate. First it was Yuanyuan, then Yuzhen, and who knows if she'll be the next one lying on top of it? Even if she doesn't want to admit it, she's in a state of waiting to die. This is a place that can break a person's will; no one wants to stay here long. The next day, He Ran asked to go home and rest for a few days, and the doctor agreed. The day I was discharged from the hospital was at the end of February, and the weather was gradually warming up. The fine drizzle in the air and the green buds sprouting on the branches all herald the arrival of early spring. This vibrant scene also dispelled much of the gloom in people's hearts. Yang Wenping and He Jin have not been in Guangzhou for the past few days. According to Yang Wenping, she instructed Han Yu to pick up He Ran from the hospital. He Ran waited at the hospital entrance for a full half hour, but he never appeared. In the end, she had no choice but to use her crutches to hail a taxi and go home. After several days of insomnia, He Ran finally found relief when she touched the soft and comfortable bed at home. She buried her face in the blankets and slept soundly from the afternoon until dusk. She vaguely heard the door being pushed open, wondering who had returned. She kept her eyes closed, not wanting to move. Footsteps approached from afar, sometimes unsteady, sometimes heavy, like those of a drunkard. The man finally stopped in front of her bed, and He Ran had no choice but to open her eyes a crack and turn over. Upon seeing who it was, she immediately frowned.

"How did you get in?"

Han Yu leaned unsteadily against her bedside, a roguish smile on his face. His face was flushed from drinking, his eyes unfocused, and his cheeks glowing red. Han Yu flicked a bunch of keys in his hand and said,

"Your mom gave them to me."

He Ran sat up, reached out her hand to him, and said in a distant tone,

"I'm home safely. You can leave once you give me back the key."

Han Yu lowered his eyes, remaining motionless, his gaze fixed on her chest. He Ran looked down and realized she had exposed herself. She calmly pulled her pajamas up a little and smoothed out the wrinkles. Han Yu curled the corners of his lips into a flippant tone,

"No need to cover it up, there's nothing to see anyway."

He Ran kept her mouth shut and didn't say anything, having no intention of arguing with him. Han Yu sat down leisurely, tilting his head to look her over:

"I heard you broke up with that guy?"

He Ran remained expressionless and did not respond. Han Yu couldn't help but kick him while he was down,

"You were so sure he wasn't Lu Jingbai back then, but now he's still a deserter, hasn't he?"

He snorted dismissively and then reached out to pinch her cheek:

"I told you long ago that he wouldn't last long, but you didn't believe me. It's much better to be with me."

"What happens between him and me is none of your business."

He Ran dodged his hand, her face as cold as ice.

"You just need to remember, there's no possibility between us."

Han Yu's face immediately darkened; the alcohol made him look even more menacing.

"He Ran, you're so ungrateful!"

He Ran turned her head away without changing her expression.

"Please leave."

Han Yu, enraged, laughed coldly. He kicked his leather shoes onto her bedsheets and swaggered at her, saying defiantly,

"I'm not leaving today, what can you do about it?!"
---

He Ran looked at him for a while, then calmly stood up and said indifferently,

"Then I'll leave."

She had just taken a step when she was pulled back by a brute force. Her weak body couldn't withstand such a strong force, and she fell heavily onto the bed, where Han Yu pressed down on her.

"He Ran, are you deliberately trying to drive me crazy?"

His face was hovering above, contorted like a wild beast with bloodshot eyes.

"You said you wanted to be with that guy before, fine, I let you off the hook! Now that he's gone, you still look down on me! Tell me! What is it about me that you don't like?!"

He Ran's expression remained indifferent, completely ignoring Han Yu's madness:

"Have you shouted enough? If you have, then leave."

Han Yu was completely enraged, his teeth grinding together.

"I refuse to believe I can't handle you!"

He suddenly lowered his head and bit her shoulder hard, causing He Ran to wince in pain. The assault didn't stop there; the elastic band of her pajama bottoms was pulled and tugged back and forth between their hands. He Ran said something, but the person on top of her had completely lost their senses and didn't care about anything else. She let go, and Han Yu successfully pulled down half of her pajama pants. He Ran reached her arm towards the cabinet next to her, fumbling around for something. There was a fruit plate on the bedside table; she had peeled an apple and eaten it at noon. Her arms weren't long enough, so she gritted her teeth and stretched them forward, trying a little harder, a little harder still. I kept stretching forward until I finally reached it. Clutching the sharp object tightly in her palm, she raised it high and plunged it into Han Yu's back without hesitation. The blade sliced ​​through flesh and plunged deep inside, and the instant pleasure released all the pent-up frustration she had accumulated from the multiple. Han Yu let out a short, muffled groan, his facial muscles suddenly tightened, and his body stiffened so much that he couldn't move. After an unknown amount of time, his hand slowly moved and touched his side, where there was a bloody mess. He looked up at He Ran in disbelief:

"You really dare..."

He Ran pushed him away, panting heavily, and stood up. The blood-stained knife fell to the ground. She said,

"If you hadn't gone this far, I wouldn't have treated you like this."

Han Yu's drunkenness seemed to dissipate completely at this moment; his wide eyes were bloodshot, and the shock and pain in his eyes were about to burst forth. He Ran slumped down the bed, her body sliding slowly against the edge, and sat slumped on the floor, her eyes devoid of warmth:

"Han Yu, you're really out of your mind."

"You already have so many young and beautiful girlfriends, why do you keep bothering me, this half-dead, sickly guy?"

The excruciating pain prevented Han Yu from speaking loudly, and his anger gradually subsided, leaving him curled up and unable to move. After a long pause, he frowned and said,

"You ask me why, I don't know either."

"It's like when I ask you why you're so stubbornly insisting on being with that man, you can't answer me."

I'm still pondering Han Yu's words. He Ran slowly curled the corners of her mouth into a silent smile. Yes, many things in this world have no reason; they are just pitiful people driven by their hearts and unable to resist. He Ran calmly dialed 120 and then tossed her phone aside. She straightened her disheveled pajamas, put on a coat, and walked towards the door. Even with her unsteady steps, her back view carried a resolute and determined air, making her seem invulnerable. Han Yu stared intently at her, forcing out a few strained words through clenched teeth:

"Where are you going!"

Without turning her head, He Ran said firmly,

"Go find him."

Go find that person. My heart is set on it. He Ran had forgotten to take her crutches in her haste to leave home. The car stopped at the intersection of Xiaozhou Village, but construction was underway and vehicles couldn't drive in. He Ran reached into her coat pocket, counted out some cash, handed it to the driver, and got out of the car. A cold wind blew in her face, so she tightened her clothes and moved her legs to climb the arched bridge in front of her. With nothing to hold onto along the roadside, He Ran struggled to maintain her balance, making each step incredibly difficult. Her body swayed precariously, more unstable than a fragile leaf, whether drunk or insane was unclear. People on the street stared at her with strange or panicked looks, fearing she might suddenly have a mental breakdown, and kept their distance. I never realized this road was so long; looking up, I couldn't see the end. He Ran's legs gradually lost feeling and became unresponsive. She had to stop and rest every few meters. She had only covered less than a third of the distance when she was already exhausted, with only her willpower driving her forward. While crossing an uneven rocky pit, He Ran accidentally fell. In that instant, only two words came to her mind: It's over. After falling, she couldn't get up. She tried to support herself with her arms, her knuckles white from clenching her fists, but no matter how hard she tried, she couldn't lift her body. Looking back at her two motionless legs, which didn't respond to her punches or slaps, He Ran kept cursing them for being useless in her mind. Gradually, several passersby gathered around her, pointing and whispering, but no one dared to step forward and help her up. He Ran was covered in mud, cold sweat dripped from his forehead, and he tasted a salty flavor on his lips. She gritted her teeth and tried again with all her might. She stood up with trembling legs, but fell hard to the ground again halfway there. Along with the body, something indescribable also collapsed. She was dressed warmly enough to avoid injury, but the dull pain from her flesh hitting the ground still lingered in her heart. He Ran stopped resisting and collapsed helplessly to the ground, leaving her fate to fate. At that moment, she felt like a bird with broken wings, utterly devoid of dignity. Desperately struggling in her death throes, she ultimately couldn't escape being left to rot in the street, subjected to the strange looks of passersby. The sun sets in the west, casting its last rays. He Ran looked up at the blood-red light that filled the sky, feeling a sense of desolation in her heart.

"Xiao He?"

Someone in the crowd called out to her. He Ran snapped out of her daze, shifted her gaze to the side, and saw the fat man squeeze out of the crowd and walk towards her.

"What's wrong?"

the fat man asked as he walked up to her.

"I'm fine,"

He Ran said calmly, lowering her eyes. The fat man bent down, and with his help, He Ran finally stood up slowly. She asked,

"Have you seen Xiao Han?"

"He's gone back to Beijing."

The fat man paused for a moment.

"I just took him to the train station. His train is at 7:30."

He Ran paused for a moment:

"What time is it now?"

"It's almost 6:30."

After a few seconds of silence, He Ran said,

"Can you take me to see him?"

It was just past seven o'clock. The fat man raced against time, skipping countless red lights before finally getting He Ran to the train station. Since parking was not allowed nearby, the fat man drove away, leaving He Ran to walk in alone. The only impression the train station left on He Ran was chaos—a sea of ​​people of all kinds, even at night. Most of the people in the square were dragging suitcases, hurrying along; there were students, migrant workers, and many white-collar workers. A few people in tattered clothes sat on their bags, leaning against the railing, either holding a hot bun in their hands or fast asleep with their heads tilted back. He Ran followed the crowd into the ticket hall, where the crowd was even denser. She looked up at the timetable on the wall; there were only ten minutes left before Xiao Han's bus scheduled to depart, and time was running out. She stood still, looking around, her gaze scanning through the crowd like a scanner. Unfamiliar faces flashed before her eyes, some laughing and talking, others looking serious and tired, but none of them were the person she was looking for. He Ran searched anxiously, but the more anxious she became, the more confused she became. She felt like she had fallen into a maze with a thousand faces, and after going around in circles, she returned to the same place, losing her way. The surrounding scenery was like a concave-convex mirror, constantly magnifying and, causing blemishes in her vision, dimming the light, and the incessant noise in her ears constantly assaulting her nerves. Years of experience taught He Ran that this was a sign she was about to faint. She blinked hard, forcing herself to stay awake. The crowd surged and subsided, and she stood in the very center, clenching her fists and straining with all her might. The name she had been suppressing in her heart finally burst forth:

"Xiao Han—!"

This long howl reached a volume she had never heard before, drowning out the whispers of the crowd and the announcements over the loudspeaker. Those two words were powerful and resonant, like a great bell tolling in the mountains, their echoes reaching far and wide, and the air in the entire hall was stunned by her. Time seemed to stand still; everyone stopped what they were doing and looked at her. That's not enough. He Ran took a deep breath and shouted his name again:

"Xiao Han—!"

This shout was even louder than before, like an eagle soaring through the sky, piercing through the clouds, exhausting all her lung capacity. Heart-wrenching cries and desperate screams are the explosive power of life. In the complete silence, He Ran sensed someone walking quickly towards her from behind. Her scalp tingled, her fingers trembled, and every cell in her body seemed to scream and resonate with the arrival of that person. He Ran turned around, almost in tears. Xiao Han stood in front of her, his gaze deep, his eyes filled with a complex mix of emotions. Under the bright lights, his eyes were tinged with a light gray halo, and when he was silent, he carried a faint melancholy. He Ran was not doing well, and neither was he. A tuft of stubble has sprouted on his chin, the cast on his arm hasn't been removed yet, and although the stitches on his face have been taken off, they've left permanent scars—if he were a woman, he'd be disfigured. They had made a promise long ago that when she needed him, even if he went blind or broke his leg, he would rush to her side without stopping. Now, he stands before her, covered in wounds.

"Xiao Han."

He Ran looked up at him and gave him a faint smile. Calling out his name, she finally couldn't hold back anymore and collapsed into his arms like a puddle of mud.

"Thank goodness, you haven't left yet."

He Ran fainted. Xiao Han took her back to Xiaozhou Village, carried her to bed, and covered her with a blanket. He Ran showed no signs of waking up. Xiao Han took her temperature and breathed a sigh of relief when he saw that it was normal. But I still can't help worrying. She's been getting thinner and thinner lately. The body I'm holding is so thin and frail, it looks like it would break if I pinched it. Xiao Han removed her hat, which was askew to one side, revealing her bald head, which was tinged with a faint bluish-black tinge. He calmly examined her face, his hand gently brushing against her cheek, and he sighed almost imperceptibly. He Ran was sleeping soundly, her pale lips tightly pressed together, and her furrowed brows looked like a knot in the sand. Perhaps she was having another bad dream. He watched her suffer, yet he was powerless to help. There is nothing more painful than this.

---

The next morning, Xiao Han was awakened by an unusual reaction in his body. He mumbled a few words, slowly opened his eyelids, and felt a warm, wet sensation at the corner of his mouth.

"Did you dream?"

He Ran looked at his sleepy eyes and playfully curled the corners of her lips:

"Did you dream about me?"

Xiao Han looked back at him, his voice hoarse and husky, half-asleep,

"No."

He Ran chuckled,

"You still say you didn't? I heard you talking in your sleep."

What did I say?

"you guess."

"..."

Xiao Han stared at the smiling face in front of him, lost in thought. He never knew what to do with her. For example, right now, he should be condemning her, but he didn't even have the courage to raise his voice at her.

"Why did you run away from the hospital again?"

Xiao Han finally asked in a rather indifferent tone. He Ran was a little displeased:

"When men and women are making love, can we not mention such disgusting things?"

Okay, I won't say.

---

After a while, Xiao Han changed the subject:

"I heard from Fatty that you collapsed on the street today?"

He Ran gave a soft

"hmm"

and said casually,

"It's all because I wanted to find you."

Xiao Han then asked,

"How does a doctor diagnose your condition?"

He Ran yawned casually,

"Saying I won't live past tomorrow."

Xiao Han didn't say anything, but He Ran felt that he was glaring at her with a rather fierce expression. He Ran ignored him and asked,

"Did you change your train ticket?"

"Um."

"Which number was changed to?"

"This afternoon."

"So fast."

He Ran frowned almost imperceptibly.

"Are you in a hurry to go back?"

Xiao Han said,

"The flower shop has been very busy lately, and Lao Chen can't manage it all by himself."

He Ran stopped asking questions and slowly closed her eyes:

"Then let's continue sleeping for a while."

It was almost eight o'clock, and Xiao Han was no longer sleepy. He got out of bed, washed up, got dressed, made breakfast, and then woke He Ran up. He Ran sat up and stared blankly for a while before gradually regaining control of her body and slowly getting out of bed. Entering the bathroom, she noticed her used toothbrush was still stuck in Xiao Han's cup, and couldn't help but smile. He Ran lingered for more than ten minutes without coming downstairs. Xiao Han was worried that she might faint again, so he came up to check on her. Seeing that she was alright, Xiao Han urged,

"Come down and eat something quickly. After you finish eating, I'll take you back to the hospital first, and then we'll go to the station."

He Ran stood still and beckoned to him mysteriously with her finger. Xiao Han asked,

"What?"

He Ran said,

"I have something to tell you."

Xiao Han walked towards her with some skepticism. When he got close, He Ran hooked her arm around his neck and leaned her upper body against him. She was breathing heavily with her mouth open, her complexion looking unwell, but her eyes sparkled as she smiled at him. Xiao Han chose not to look. He turned and walked out indifferently, saying,

"Time to eat."

He Ran was caught off guard and stumbled forward as he pulled her along. Xiao Han quickly reached out to support her. He was still shaken, and his tone became a little harsher:

"You better behave yourself."

He Ran ignored him and, taking advantage of her tilted body position, lifted her leg and gently rubbed it against his body. Xiao Han's face was taut, his eyes were dark, but his gaze was intense and bright. He Ran's eyes curved upwards, and all her body language conveyed two words: seduction. Xiao Han's voice was deep and somber:

"My train is at ten o'clock."

"I know."

"Knowing you still..."

He Ran interrupted him,

"Xiao Han, if we're going to leave, we'll leave together. Don't even think about getting rid of me."

Neither of them would give in. She grabbed his collar tightly, forcing him to bend over.

"Do you miss me or not?"

She blinked and whispered in his ear,

"Stay if you want."

A lover's gaze is perhaps the most vulnerable and irresistible force in this vast world, amidst its illusions and ups and downs. In the end, Xiao Han was defeated, utterly and completely. At six o'clock in the evening, it was getting dark. The train bound for Beijing has already departed from the station. The man lay sprawled in the middle of the messy bed, his strong arms and thighs covered in a fine layer of sweat, while the woman, equally drenched in sweat, lay on top of him. The sky outside had turned overcast at some point, and the small space was so quiet that only the sound of raindrops hitting the window could be heard. Xiao Han stared at the ceiling, trying to catch his breath, and said,

"You're deliberately trying to keep me from leaving."

He Ran gave a half-smile,

"I said, if we're going to leave, we'll leave together."

"I can't take you with me."

He Ran stubbornly said,

"I will not go back to the hospital."

Xiao Han frowned at her,

"Why can't you just listen to me?"

"Xiao Han."

He Ran didn't answer directly, but simply called his name calmly. She raised her chin, looking directly into his eyes,

"Do you know how I've been getting by in the hospital these past few days?"

"Living a miserable existence is worse than death..."

she said calmly and earnestly,

"If I stay there any longer, I won't live past this month. Even if my body doesn't die, my heart will."

Xiao Han frowned, his tone stern:

"What nonsense are you talking about? As long as you cooperate with the doctor's treatment, there won't be so many problems."

"Doctors are not gods. There are too many uncertainties, and they are powerless to do anything about it."

"You must believe in science."

"The price is losing you, and I don't want that."

"..."

The two remained deadlocked, and the small house fell silent once again.

"Before I came to find you yesterday, I had already cut off my own escape route."

He Ran looked through her hands, trying to make out something, but the bloodstains clinging to her fingers had already been washed away.

"Han Yu tried to rape me, so I stabbed him. I don't know if he's alive or dead now."

Her voice was very soft and indifferent, as if what happened yesterday had nothing to do with her.

"If you send me back now, I will face a situation a hundred times, a thousand times worse."

Xiao Han frowned even more deeply at her words and remained silent for a long time.

"This is the last time I'll ask you this."

His playful attitude vanished, replaced by an unprecedented sincerity in He Ran's eyes.

"Will you take me with you?"

"There's no need to worry too much. In my understanding, love is such an extreme thing."

She said patiently and calmly,

"You can either take me away or kill me here and end it all. I'd rather die in your arms than die on that cold bed."

"Xiao Han, this is the last chance for us to be together."

She bit her lower lip hard, but still couldn't stop her voice from trembling.

"If you walk out of this house today, I will never come back to find you, and we will never see each other again until we die."

"Take me away."

He Ran stretched out her hands to him, hoping for a tight hug.

"Xiao Han, take me away."

After a long silence, Xiao Han's expression gradually softened. He finally pulled her into his arms, his lips moving with difficulty, uttering words instead of uttering anything.

## Chapter Ten: Summer Flowers in Full Bloom

Chapter Preface: That year, she was still beautiful, and he was still young. Their love may have been crazy, perhaps absurd. But because it was fleeting, it deserves all the more to be known by the world. In the end, they didn't go to Beijing, but returned to Xiao Han's hometown. That secluded place allowed He Ran to find a long-lost sense of belonging. Without informing the family beforehand, Quan Quan was overjoyed by this unexpected surprise, jumping up and down and circling around He Ran. Xiao Han's mother remained lukewarm towards He Ran, especially after learning of her serious illness. No one likes a sickly daughter-in-law, as it means countless expenses and burdens for the family. Every day after meals, the mother and son would argue about He Ran. The arguments would often escalate to a point where they couldn't hold back their anger. The old lady's face turned bright red, and she was struggling to breathe. Quan Quan helped her back into the house to rest. Xiao Han, who was not good with words, was also exhausted. At this time, He Ran remained silently in the room, not showing herself. After cleaning up the mess, Xiao Han went back to the room to find her. His expression had returned to calm:

"My mother is old and likes to nag. Don't take it too seriously."

"It's nothing, I couldn't understand what she was saying anyway."

He Ran didn't mind, she beckoned him to come and sit down,

"But you, there's no need to argue so fiercely with an old person."

Xiao Han said solemnly,

"If I want to marry you, of course I have to keep talking until she agrees."

"Marry me?"

He Ran was stunned.

"Okay."

Xiao Han nodded, and then he talked about his plans:

"Once you're feeling better, we'll have a banquet in the village."

He Ran couldn't help but ask curiously,

"What do brides here dress up like?"

Xiao Han told her,

"There's nothing particularly special about it. In the past, it was a red overcoat, but now we wear wedding dresses too."

"Then I'll wear the red overcoat,"

He Ran said, rubbing her hands together.

"It's so cold, I can't handle the wedding dress."

Xiao Han nodded in agreement:

"Okay."

He Ran smiled again,

"Whether we get married or not is just a formality. It's enough that we're together forever."

Xiao Han reached out and embraced her, placing his warm palm on her lower abdomen.

"That's not enough. We need another child."

He Ran didn't say anything, she just smiled at him and said,

"Okay."

She has never been a fan of children, but she was willing to seriously consider this matter for him, which is perhaps the best proof of her love for him. Unfortunately, things didn't go as planned. Although He Ran's mental state was much better than when she was hospitalized, her physical functions were deteriorating day by day. After staying in her hometown for more than half a month, she had completely lost feeling in her legs and was unable to walk. She needed Xiao Han's help with all her daily life, even bathing and using the toilet. Every afternoon, when the sun was warm but not too hot, Xiao Han would take He Ran for a walk outside in the yard to stretch her muscles. She walked with great difficulty, one hand on her cane and the other on Xiao Han's shoulder. Unaware villagers passing by would often tease Xiao Han, saying he was supporting two elderly mothers. He Ran thought about the statement carefully and realized that it was true. The old lady could still take care of herself, while she was even more useless than Xiao Han's mother. In the dead of night, He Ran was awakened by a bloating sensation in her lower abdomen. She looked at Xiao Han, who was sleeping soundly beside her, hesitated for a long time, but finally couldn't bear to wake him. Finally, He Ran gritted her teeth, moved her heavy legs, and with considerable effort, stepped over Xiao Han's body and got off the bed. She retrieved the chamber pot from under the bed, then leaned over the edge, trembling as she crouched down. Her legs shook uncontrollably, more violently than a leaf, as she desperately wanted to finish quickly, unsure if she had spilled anything. In the end, He Ran couldn't hold on any longer and collapsed to the ground. The chamber pot was knocked over, making a loud noise. Startled awake by the noise, Xiao Han sat up, squinting.

"What's wrong?"

He Ran lay half-prone on the ground and whispered,

"It's nothing."

Xiao Han got out of bed, turned on the light, and was stunned when he saw the situation in front of him. He Ran turned her head away, her voice low and muffled:

"Don't look at me."

He Ran hadn't even had time to put her pants on yet, and she could even feel that her trouser legs were wet. This made her feel more embarrassed and ashamed than ever since she got sick. Xiao Han walked a few steps to her side, intending to reach out and help her up. He Ran pushed his hand away, her voice cold:

"Don't help me up, I can get up by myself."

Xiao Han ignored her and reached under her arms again to scoop her up. He Ran roared,

"I said I'll do it myself!"

Xiao Han paused, looking down at her stubborn face, and softly called her name:

"Child..."

He Ran looked at him calmly and said firmly,

"Xiao Han, I must stand up on my own."

"...... "

Get out of my way, let me do it myself.

" In the end, Xiao Han let go of her hand and stood far away. "

Could you get me a rag?" He Ran said. Xiao Han walked out of the house and returned shortly afterward with a rag. He Ran took the rag, wiped the floor clean, and then tried to stand up. It was to no avail. Her legs seemed to not exist at all; she struggled, fell, struggled again, and fell again. She repeated this for an unknown amount of time, her body covered in dust, but she still refused to give up. Xiao Han couldn't bear to look any longer, so he turned around and looked up at the roof. He Ran was tired, so she sat down and rested for a while.

After regaining her strength, she dragged her legs to the edge of the bed, braced herself with her hands on the bed board, and finally slowly stood up with the help of the support. People are easily satisfied in adversity. She sat on the bed, a slight smugness on her lips, and her eyes subconsciously looked for Xiao Han, but she only saw his back. He Ran called out to him,

"Xiao Han, I'm all better now."

Xiao Han's movements were like slow motion; it took him several seconds to turn around. He kept his head down, not looking at He Ran, but he couldn't hide the redness in his eyes. It took He Ran a few seconds to make sure she wasn't seeing things. She frowned almost imperceptibly.

"Why are you crying? I'm not crying."

Xiao Han neither argued nor spoke, standing still like a stiff stone statue. The rest of the night passed in silence. Recently, Xiao Han's smell of cigarettes has become increasingly strong, and it can be smelled even if you try to ignore it. Every night, whenever He Ran woke up in pain, he would also feel it and wake up as well. He would lie awake in the darkness, staring at something that was not there, without uttering a sound. He waited for the long night to pass quietly until the person next to him stopped tossing and turning. Only then did he quietly get out of bed and go outside to smoke a cigarette. It's not just one cigarette; lately he needs to smoke two or three in a row to feel satisfied. What can he do with that much? He didn't know. After the cigarette butt burned out, he would stay outside for another ten minutes so until the smell of smoke on his body dissipated before going back inside. The person on the bed lay peacefully with their eyes closed, breathing steadily. He Ran used to always kick off the covers, but now that her legs couldn't move, she behaved herself, staying in the same sleeping position all night. Xiao Han lay down beside her and habitually reached out to touch her forehead, but he was suddenly startled when he touched it. He Ran was unaware that she had developed a high fever again. In her dazed state, she only felt someone carry her on their back. The person's back was strong and broad, making her feel very safe. She quickly fell asleep again on their back. Xiao Han took He Ran to the hospital in the county town overnight, and she finally regained some consciousness when she lay down on the hospital bed. An intern nurse was giving her an injection. He Ran's veins were already hard to find, and after long-term chemotherapy, they were so thin that they could not be seen with the naked eye. The nurse tried to insert the needle four or five times, but all attempts failed, leaving several bloody holes on the back of her hand unnecessarily. He Ran remained expressionless, while the nurse became so nervous that she started sweating. The more nervous she became, the more likely she was to make a mistake, and her next two injections were even more off-target. Xiao Han finally lost his temper and called the head nurse over. Even the experienced head nurse was stumped by He Ran's condition, and her attempts to insert the needle repeatedly failed to find a vein. He Ran's hands were already covered in sores, but she couldn't feel the pain anymore. She acted as if nothing was wrong, comforting Xiao Han with her eyes. In the end, the head nurse had no choice but to stick the needle into the back of her foot, leaving He Ran both amused and exasperated. For the next eight days, she kept going back and forth between having a fever and the fever subsiding, and she was in a drowsy state for almost twenty hours every day. He Ran had received countless injections of fever reducers and antibiotics. Every time she opened her eyes, she couldn't distinguish between day and night. The only constant was the figure that stayed by her bedside without ever leaving her side. Due to inflammation, half of her mouth was ulcerated, and she had no appetite, relying solely on intravenous nutrition. She couldn't eat anything. In just a few days, she lost even more weight, with only her face remaining swollen. He Ran woke up during lunchtime, which was unusual for her. Xiao Han was slurping a bowl of noodles when he looked up and saw her lying on the bed looking at him. He quickly put the bowl down and asked,

"Are you hungry? Is there anything you want to eat?"

For the first time ever, He Ran had an appetite. After thinking for a while, she said,

"I want to eat rouge radishes, the kind you brought me when I first went to your house."

Xiao Han was overjoyed that she was willing to eat, and rushed out to buy her some before he had even finished his noodles. He returned in less than twenty minutes, running into the ward. Despite the cold weather, sweat beaded on his forehead, and he was panting heavily. Seeing the sweat on Xiao Han's head, He Ran couldn't help but reach out and wipe it away for him. Xiao Han handed her a lunchbox full of radishes and a bowl of white porridge. Looking at the astonishing amount, He Ran said with a helpless tone,

"How could I possibly eat that much?"

Xiao Han said,

"It's okay, I'll eat too."

He Ran casually picked up a piece of radish with a toothpick, looking somewhat troubled. The radish was cut into very large chunks, and she couldn't open her mouth wide enough to bite into it. Xiao Han helped her chew the food and then fed it to her bite by bite. The food traveled on her tongue and slowly melted in her mouth. He Ran couldn't tell whether the taste was salty, sour, or bitter. Swallows carry food, showing mutual respect and affection. This feeling was far deeper and heavier than she had imagined. Over the weekend, Quan Quan also came to the hospital to visit He Ran. He stayed overnight, and Xiao Han gave her. He Ran's temperature started to rise again in the afternoon, and it didn't show any signs of improvement until the evening. She woke up once in the middle of the night. Although her fever had subsided, she was still somewhat confused. Seeing Quan Quan sleeping on the bed next to her, He Ran was in a daze for a while before she came to her senses. She looked around the ward but couldn't find Xiao Han. He Ran slowly sat up, knocking something to the ground. Quan Quan was woken up. He opened his eyes, saw He Ran, his eyes lit up, and he immediately got out of bed and walked towards her. The little boy very thoughtfully tucked her in and said earnestly,

"Auntie, you're sick, you need to rest more."

He Ran couldn't help but laugh and reached out to pinch his cheek. Quan Quan asked again,

"Do you want some water?"

"I don't want to drink."

He Ran waved her hand, covering her cheek, and said,

"My mouth hurts."

Quan Quan frowned her delicate eyebrows and asked,

"Does it hurt a lot?"
---
title: 'Chapter Ten: Summer Flowers in Full Bloom'
chapter: 104
wordCount: 1112
date: 2026-09-28
time: 09:00:31
---

He Ran nodded, making a wronged expression,

"It hurts so much."

The little one took her words seriously and immediately became nervous, anxiously pacing around in circles,

"What should I do? Will you die?"

He Ran couldn't help but chuckle, shrugged, and said,

"Everyone will die."

Quan Quan was silent for a while, then asked, puzzled,

"What happens after you die?"

He Ran was speechless at the question. She had to borrow what adults often said:

"After we die, we sleep for a very long time, and then we go to heaven."

After hearing He Ran's explanation, Quan Quan finally smiled and said innocently,

"Then go to heaven, you won't feel the pain when you're asleep."

He Ran stroked his head, smiling without saying a word.

"Where's your uncle?"

He Ran asked after a while. Quan Quan said,

"He's outside. I'll go call him."

He Ran nodded,

"Okay."

Quan Quan stood at the door of the ward, peeking out. The long corridor seemed to stretch on forever, dimly lit and eerily quiet, save for the faint green glow of the emergency exit sign. He took a deep breath, mustered his courage, and walked out, continuing forward until he finally found Xiao Han at the end of the corridor. These past few days, Xiao Han has barely slept a wink. He spends his nights either sitting by his bed or in the corridor. Smoking is prohibited in the hospital, so when he can't resist, he can only sneak a few puffs in this secluded corner. The moonlight was cold, and the night air was chilly, yet he managed to withstand it despite wearing only a thin long-sleeved shirt. The wind was particularly strong tonight, blowing wildly and erratically, causing the cigarette butt between Xiao Han's fingers to flicker. Whether it was her imagination or not, Quan Quan vaguely saw a few tears welling up in the corners of his eyes. When I blinked again, that transparent color had disappeared. This question troubled him for a long time, until he grew up and realized that tears could flow back into the heart. The figure seemed unfamiliar, and Quan Quan hesitated to call out to him. After standing there for a long time, he timidly called out,

"Uncle..."

Xiao Han snapped out of her daze, turned to look at him, and asked,

"What's wrong?"

Quan Quan said,

"Auntie is awake, she's calling you."

Xiao Han nodded, put out his cigarette, and walked towards him.

"Let's go."

Back in the ward, Quan Quan, being the precocious child she is, first handed Xiao Han over to He Ran, then pulled the curtain closed and climbed back into her own bed to sleep. He Ran beckoned to Xiao Han, who slowly walked to her bedside and looked down at her. The bed sheet was soaked with He Ran's sweat; she looked completely drained. She was wearing the smallest size patient gown, but it was still too loose for her. She never complained, but all her discomfort was written on her haggard face. He Ran moved Xiao Han to a different spot, patted the bed, and said,

"Come here."

Xiao Han hesitated for a moment, then climbed onto the bed and lay down beside her. He Ran quietly looked him over, and then, just like she did with Quan Quan, she reached out and pinched his cheek. She teased, laughing,

"You should get some sleep. If your dark circles get any worse, I won't recognize you anymore."

Xiao Han forced a smile, barely managing to call it a smile. He Ran cupped his face in her hands and kissed his lips. After the kiss, she buried her face in his chest and lay there quietly, without saying anything more. He Ran sensed his need, but unfortunately, she was powerless to help. She sighed,

" Xiao Han, I think this is a really bad thing."

Xiao Han put his arm around her.

"What's wrong?"

He Ran said in a low voice,

"No matter what, I used to want to sleep with you whenever I saw you, but now... I don't have any of those thoughts anymore."

Xiao Han kept his mouth tightly shut and remained silent for a long time. After what seemed like an eternity, He Ran continued,

"If I can pull through this, let's go on a trip."

She looked into his dark eyes, smiled, and said,

"No matter how much longer I have to live, I want to go with you to see the spring flowers bloom and listen to the ebb and flow of the tide."

This experience can be considered a narrow escape from death for He Ran. After eight days of high fever, her temperature finally stabilized. The doctor said that if she had continued to have a fever for another two days, her condition would have been very dangerous. She was then hospitalized for three more days for observation, and was only allowed to go home after confirming that she had no fever again. Before being discharged from the hospital, He Ran had another blood test. All the blood counts were pitifully low, and she knew what was going on, so she didn't say anything. After being discharged from the hospital, they did not go home immediately, but stayed in a hotel in the county town. Xiao Han's eldest sister's father-in-law is a senior traditional Chinese medicine doctor who opened a small clinic in Fuling after retiring. Every year, countless people travel from other places to see him. After discussing it with He Ran, Xiao Han decided to pay him a visit. Throughout history, there have been many cases of people being brought back from the brink of death by traditional Chinese medicine. While some of these cases may have been exaggerated, their efficacy is not entirely fabricated. After multiple treatments with traditional Chinese medicine and acupuncture, He Ran's legs gradually improved, and a month later she was even able to stand up and walk slowly without relying on anything else. The happiest person was naturally Xiao Han. He's currently unemployed and spends all his time taking care of He Ran. Recently, He Ran's appetite has improved, so Xiao Han has started preparing various delicious meals for her, hoping to fatten her up a bit. The hotel they stayed in was in poor condition; the bathroom and kitchen were shared, and it was damp and dirty. Whenever it rained heavily, the roof leaked, dripping constantly. He Ran had been falling asleep to the intermittent sound of dripping water for the past few nights, with Xiao Han holding her until she closed her eyes.

title: 'Chapter Ten: Summer Flowers in Full Bloom'
chapter: 105

---

A week ago, she had another low-grade fever and spent most of the night at the hospital before the fever subsided. The hospital equipment here is not good enough, and the antibiotics and anti-inflammatory injections are not as effective as those she used in Guangzhou. Half-asleep, He Ran heard Xiao Han whisper in her ear:

"Kid, you should go back to Guangzhou. You can get better treatment there."

Even though she was very sleepy, He Ran forced herself to open her eyes. She said softly but firmly,

"That's not necessarily true. Look at me now, I can eat, sleep, walk, and talk to you. I'm already very satisfied."

As drowsiness crept in, she yawned before continuing,

"In short, Xiao Han, this is the path I chose myself, and no matter how it turns out, I will have no regrets."

Xiao Han gently put his arm around her back and didn't say anything more. When the weather gets warmer, Xiao Han will fulfill his promise and take her on a trip. They went to Yongning Township in northern Yunnan, which, as its name suggests, is a place far from the hustle and bustle of the world, a place where one can find peace of mind. In May, the temperature is moderate, and the water of Lugu Lake is bluer than the sky, as still as a mirror. The distant scenery is reflected in the water completely and clearly, creating a scene that is both real and unreal. For people who have lived in modern metropolises for a long time, this is undoubtedly a spectacle. Xiao Han and He Ran checked into a quaint inn in a large village. The landlady was a local resident, a Mosuo girl named Ami'er. She had delicate eyebrows and big eyes, light brown skin, and her cheeks were flushed with a unique charm. Many families here still practice the ancient custom of walking marriage. It is a matriarchal society where women are in charge and men come and go at night. Ami'er doesn't look much older than He Ran, but she is already the main breadwinner of the family. Xiao Han and He Ran stayed here for a week. They originally planned to go to Dali to see Cangshan Mountain and Erhai Lake next, but He Ran changed her mind and didn't want to leave. The serene scenery here had a power to keep people here. In her carefree days, He Ran no longer had to worry about whether her white blood cells and bone marrow levels were undergoing adverse changes. She even forgot that she was a patient. As long as she lived happily every day, that was the best way to give herself peace of mind. The afternoon sun was shining lazily as Xiao Han led He Ran for a stroll on the long Caohai Bridge. Wildflowers bloom all around, and the stream is as blue as the sky. He Ran stopped and leaned against the railing, looking down into the clear water. Her face was reflected in the water. Her face was no longer sallow and thin; it was finally showing signs of regaining its color. She wasn't wearing a hat, and three months after stopping chemotherapy, her hair started growing again. Although it's only grown a short bit now, she looks much more energetic. Turning to look at the man standing beside her, He Ran knew she owed him a thank you. If Xiao Han hadn't agreed to take her away from Guangzhou three months ago, she would still be facing the same unchanging gray walls, instead of this beautiful scenery before her. Looking into the distance at the azaleas blooming all over the mountains, He Ran sighed softly,

"Summer is almost here."

"Hmm,"

Xiao Han replied in a neutral tone. He Ran reached out and gently wrapped her arms around his waist.

"If we were still in Guangzhou, these past few months would have been the hardest time for you to work."

Xiao Han nodded and said,

"Yes."

"Do you remember the first time I went to the central lake to find you?"

He Ran gently rested her head on his arm and smiled.

"At that time, I was wondering if you thought I was a plant when you cut my hair?"

Xiao Han didn't reply. He touched the short hair that had just sprouted on her head, which was prickly and prickly. After a while, he said,

"I'll cut it better for you next time."

"Okay."

He Ran pursed her lips and said with a smile,

"I hope we'll still be together next summer, the summer after next, and all the summers to come."

When the bright month of June arrived, Xiao Han and He Ran held a cohabitation wedding ceremony in accordance with the local Mosuo custom. Naxi men and women typically form free and unrestrained marriages. They are loyal to their love, and even without the protection of marriage laws, they often stay together for life. If given a choice, He Ran would be willing to spend the rest of her life here. After the banquet, a lively bonfire party was held in the courtyard. He Ran, who had difficulty moving around, was unable to participate and could only watch from the outside. Xiao Han was enthusiastically invited by a group of Mosuo girls to dance around the bonfire. Watching from afar the rare smile on her face, a smile rarely seen on her face, He Ran felt a pang of sadness. Back at the inn that evening, He Ran, having worked up a sweat, went to take a shower. When she came out of the bathroom, Xiao Han was smoking on the balcony. With the large temperature difference between day and night, He Ran put on a coat and slowly walked to his side. Lugu Lake was completely still and silent under the cover of night. The distant peaks lay dormant in the darkness, and the sky was woven from scattered stars into a beautiful yet fragile dream. Xiao Han was lost in thought and hadn't noticed He Ran's presence until she coughed softly. He then came to his senses and urged her to go back inside. Just as Xiao Han was about to extinguish the cigarette, He Ran snatched it away and tried to put it in her mouth. He stared at her and asked,

"What are you doing?"

He Ran said,

"I'll take a puff."

Xiao Han frowned, about to reach out to stop him. Earlier that day at the banquet, he had also stopped He Ran from drinking. He Ran tried her best to persuade him, her tone indifferent:

"Xiao Han, you have to let me have a taste at least once."

Xiao Han said,

"No, you're not in good health."

He Ran argued,

"One bite won't hurt. I just want to know what it tastes like."

After some haggling, Xiao Han finally conceded.

"Just one bite,"

he said. He Ran nodded:

"Okay."

It was He Ran's first time smoking, yet she seemed strangely familiar with the feeling. Holding the cigarette between her fingers, she took a deep drag, not inhaling it, but simply swirling it in her mouth before slowly exhaling. The smoke rings dispersed, lingering in front of the two of them, appearing and disappearing intermittently. After trying it, Xiao Han looked at her and asked,

"What does it taste like?"

He Ran didn't reply, pondering for a long time. The lingering taste in her mouth was a little bitter, a little pungent, and even had a numbing effect. It didn't smell good in my mouth, but left a faint fragrance on my fingertips. She concluded,

"It's the smell of you."

The longer I stay around you, the more my cigarette smells like you.

Xiao Han, whether he understood or not, took the cigarette back from her hand, tossed it aside, and said,

"Alright, go and rest."

The two returned to the house, and He Ran suddenly said,

"Xiao Han, I want to paint."

As Xiao Han spread out the bed sheets, he said,

"It's pitch black, what are you drawing?"

He Ran said,

"I'll draw you."

He paused for a moment, turned to look at her, and said,

"Didn't you draw me before?"

He Ran said,

"That was before, and the feeling is different now."

She loved his smile the most, but sadly, it was never captured on camera, perhaps because he hadn't smiled much lately. Xiao Han considered for a moment and said,

" Then let's do it tomorrow. I'll go ask Ami'er if she sells any art supplies here."

He Ran nodded and said,

"Okay."

---

The next morning, when He Ran woke up, Xiao Han had already prepared everything. After washing up and coming out for breakfast, she was surprised to find easels, drawing boards, folding stools, and various colors and sizes of paints and brushes set up in the yard. It was truly remarkable to find such a complete set of art supplies in such a remote mountain area. When He Ran asked, Xiao Han explained,

"Ami'er said that a customer who was also an artist didn't want these things anymore, so Ami'er helped us borrow some."

He Ran nodded, seemingly lost in thought. Someone who brings so much art supplies on vacation must be no ordinary person. After breakfast, He Ran went to the courtyard and began to paint. Xiao Han asked,

"Do you want me to be your model?"

He Ran shook her head and smiled,

"No need, I know what I'm doing."

Xiao Han then left temporarily to go back inside and make a phone call. He Ran's hand moved across the drawing paper as she drew Xiao Han's face. When she paused to think, her gaze drifted into the distance. A few wisps of light clouds on the horizon formed the arc of his smile. She used to enjoy depicting Xiao Han's eyes, but today, when she got to this part, she hesitated with her pen in her hand. Those eyes told a story, and without understanding them, one could not truly depict him. She hadn't grasped the complexities within them before, but now she was certain the story was entirely about her. I wonder if one day her departure will add another layer of sadness to it. The images flashed through her mind: in the dead of night in the mountains, the emotions in those red-rimmed eyes were too intense and too heavy for He Ran to bear. Subjective color can infuse a painting with a powerful soul and awe-inspiring presence, but it can also disrupt an artist's control over technique. Ultimately, the painting was left unfinished. He Ran only drew Xiao Han's general outline and eyes, leaving the rest blank. Her original intention was to depict his smiling posture, but now, in her opinion, the eyes in the painting could never truly smile. He Ran put away the half-finished painting, intending to continue painting some landscape sketches, but unexpectedly it suddenly started to rain. Her mood for painting was ruined by the weather, so she had to move all her art supplies under the eaves and return to her room on the second floor, feeling rather disheartened. Xiao Han sat on the bed, holding his phone and thinking about something. He didn't ask her anything when she came into the room. He Ran sat down next to him, noticing his serious expression, and asked with some concern,

"What's wrong?"

Xiao Han remained silent for a long time before saying,

"Quan Quan is sick. My mother took him to the hospital, but he fell on the way and twisted his back."

He Ran was taken aback and asked,

"How are they now?"

Xiao Han said,

"The neighbors helped take it to the hospital; they just called to tell me."

He Ran breathed a sigh of relief, then asked again,

"Is it serious?"

"Quanquan is fine, just a minor cold."

Xiao Han's voice deepened, his brows furrowing.

"My mother is getting old, it's hard to say..."

He Ran pursed her lips and thought for a while before making a decision:

"Then let's not go to Dali, let's go back early."

Xiao Han glanced at her thoughtfully, then nodded after a moment:

"Okay."

He Ran took out her phone to check the weather forecast, and said while browsing,

"It's going to rain for the next two days, and we might encounter landslides and mudslides. Let's wait until the weather is better before we leave."

Xiao Han had no objection and simply hummed in agreement. He Ran started booking a plane ticket online, but the internet speed was poor and the progress bar moved very slowly. Xiao Han waited for a while, then stood up and said,

"I'll go out and buy some groceries first. What would you like to eat for lunch?"

He Ran covered her cheek. Her gums were swollen and painful again after she woke up this morning. She wondered if it was because she ate some spicy food at the banquet yesterday. After thinking for a while, He Ran said,

"Let's just buy something light."

Xiao Han nodded and said,

"Okay."

He Ran's appetite has been fluctuating lately, but under Xiao Han's supervision, her three meals a day are still very regular. Today is a bit strange; Xiao Han went out and hasn't returned by almost 1:30. Usually, they would have already eaten lunch and be enjoying a lazy afternoon nap by this time. He Ran was worried and wanted to call Xiao Han, but found that he had forgotten to bring his phone. As the clock struck two, He Ran finally couldn't stand the hunger any longer and decided to go out and find something to eat. She had just stepped out of her room when she saw the landlady, Ami'er, rushing upstairs with a look of utter dismay. She stopped in front of He Ran, panting, and said,

"Your man is fighting with someone and you can't stop him. You have to go and see!"

The rain was getting heavier and heavier, relentlessly pounding against the top of the umbrella, as if it wouldn't stop until it smashed a hole in the protective shield. He Ran struggled forward against the wind and rain, most of her body soaked by the rain that had blown in. It took nearly fifteen minutes to walk to the scene of the incident. At the bottom of a small slope, where there was nowhere to step, He Ran waded through the water, her shoes and trouser legs completely submerged.

Two men not far away were locked in a fierce fight on the ground, completely disregarding the terrible weather, while several people trying to break them up were completely ineffective. He Ran quickened her pace and shouted into the distance,

"Stop fighting!"

Her voice was naturally high-pitched, and in the torrential rain, it couldn't be heard at all. The two on the ground continued to wrestle, exchanging punches and kicks, determined to fight until one of them was stronger. At that moment, He Ran didn't care about catching a fever. She threw her umbrella aside, instantly soaked and with nowhere to escape. She yelled at the two of them,

"Stop it, both of you!"

The two men in the rain stopped what they were doing at the same time and looked at her. Both of them were bruised and swollen, their faces unrecognizable. He Ran picked up the umbrella and walked quickly over. She stopped next to Xiao Han and held the umbrella over his head. He Ran stared at him, her lips twitching a few times, but she ultimately decided not to say anything for the time being. Looking at Han Yu, who was also soaking wet, He Ran scrutinized him for a few seconds before asking,

"How did you find your way here?"

Xiao Han sat on the bed, holding a bag of ice in his left hand and applying it to his swollen face. He Ran sat opposite him, applying medicine to his wounds with a cotton swab, her touch not gentle. Occasionally, when Xiao Han's sore spot was touched, his facial muscles would twitch, but he would remain silent. He Ran glared at him, her eyes sharp as she interrogated,

"Who struck first?"

Xiao Han answered in a muffled voice,

"Me."

He Ran then asked,

"Why did you take action?"

Xiao Han, usually so reserved, finally managed to squeeze out two words after a long pause:

"He's annoying."

He Ran frowned slightly and said unhappily,

"Then you shouldn't have fought with him. He was impulsive, but were you going to be impulsive too?"

Xiao Han stared at her with wide eyes, but didn't reply. Having been with him for so long, He Ran can now read his eyes. She said,

"Don't worry, he didn't do anything to me that time. I didn't stab him for nothing."

After a long pause, Xiao Han finally gave a soft

"hmm"

. He Ran instructed,

"Next time you encounter this situation, just ignore whatever he says."

Xiao Han nodded reluctantly:

"I understand."

After applying the medicine to Xiao Han, He Ran went into the bathroom to take a hot shower. Xiao Han waited at the door, and as soon as she came out, he quickly wrapped a towel around her head and vigorously dried her. He Ran felt a little dizzy. She swayed and reached out to support herself on the door frame. Xiao Han quickly helped her up. Giving him a disgruntled look, He Ran said,

"If I get a fever tonight, it's all your fault."

Xiao Han pursed his lips tightly and remained silent. Her short hair was half-dry quickly, but Xiao Han still insisted on using a hairdryer to dry it for her. He Ran had just sat down on the edge of the bed when she heard a loud knocking on the door. The person knocking on the door was very strong and kept knocking repeatedly, as if they were about to shatter the door. He Ran replied slowly,

"Who is it?"

Outside the door, Han Yu answered in a deep voice,

"Me."

After hesitating for a moment, He Ran stood up and walked towards the door. Xiao Han tried to pull her hand away, but she broke free. He Ran walked to the door and opened it. Han Yu stood there, motionless. He had just checked into the inn and had taken a shower and changed into clean clothes. He Ran looked him up and down and asked,

"Is the knife wound healed?"

Han Yu pursed his lips into a straight line and said,

"Don't worry, I didn't come to you to pursue this matter."

He Ran asked casually,

"What is it?"

Han Yu cut to the chase and said,

"Come back with me."

He Ran asked expressionlessly,

"Where to?"

"Guangzhou."

He Ran didn't answer, so Han Yu continued,

"My dad contacted an expert in the United States, and he said he's confident he can cure you. Pack your things and come with me right now."

Without even thinking, He Ran immediately refused:

"I'm not going."

"Why aren't you going?"

Han Yu almost choked on his breath, his face hard as a board.

"Instead of getting proper treatment, you're running around everywhere! Do you even know what's going on at home? Your mother is about to be driven crazy by you!"

"I don't know."

He Ran didn't want to say more. She slammed the door shut and said through the door,

"You'd better leave now. I won't open the door for you next time."

Han Yu outside the door was furious and kept banging on the door, but He Ran ignored him. She caught a chill from the rain, and sure enough, He Ran developed a fever in the middle of the night. She was awakened from her sleep by her rapidly rising body temperature; her head was spinning, and she instinctively reached out to pat the person beside her. Xiao Han woke up shortly after and asked in a low voice,

"What's wrong?"

"I think I have a fever,"

He Ran mumbled,

"Get me some medicine."

Xiao Han quickly got out of bed, turned on the light, and fed her some pills. Even without speaking, He Ran could feel that her cheeks were swollen badly, perhaps due to a respiratory infection. She even had great difficulty drinking water and swallowing medicine. After taking two anti-inflammatory pills, He Ran lay down again. Xiao Han took her temperature; it was 39 degrees Celsius, which was not a good sign. He Ran remained in a dazed state throughout the rest of the night. Xiao Han wrapped a thick blanket tightly around her, but she couldn't fall asleep despite her drooping eyelids. Xiao Han lay down beside her, and didn't close his eyes all night either. He hadn't forgotten the eight days He Ran had spent in a daze with a high fever a few months ago, and he prayed that this time it was just a common cold causing a fever.

---

The next morning, just as dawn was breaking, Xiao Han called a car to take them to the health center. At this time, most chartered drivers hadn't started work yet, and it was raining, making travel inconvenient, so the fare was twice as high as usual. Xiao Han didn't have time to haggle and agreed immediately.

The local clinic was very poorly equipped, and beds were scarce. He Ran had to sit in a chair in the corridor to finish receiving two bottles of IV fluids. She was given very poor-quality anti-inflammatory drugs and fever-reducing injections, which had no effect for a long time, and her forehead was still frighteningly hot. Xiao Han was anxious to find a doctor to consult about his condition, but He Ran stopped him and said,

"It's no use asking. I think it's a relapse. They can't detect it here."

Xiao Han looked down at her, his eyes filled with worry.

"What should we do then?"

He Ran said decisively,

"Let's hurry to Lijiang and find a bigger hospital."

Xiao Han quickly took out his phone to contact the driver he had just hired. The driver could tell he was in a hurry, so he took the opportunity to overcharge him again. Xiao Han helped He Ran out of the hospital. They hadn't gone far when a gleaming black luxury car stopped in front of them. The car window slowly rolled down, revealing a dark, menacing face inside. Han Yu said to the two of them,

"Get in."

Xiao Han looked at He Ran, then at the van parked not far away, and hesitated slightly.

"What are you dawdling for? Hurry up!"

Han Yu frowned and gave the order,

"If you make her take one of those minivans, she'll be jostled to death before she even gets to the hospital!"

He Ran was delirious with fever and didn't say anything. Xiao Han quickly opened the car door, picked her up, and put her in the back seat. The roads were slippery due to the rain, and the fog in the mountains was thick. The journey that usually takes five hours took a full seven hours to complete today. The group arrived in Lijiang in the evening. Han Yu had already arranged everything, and He Ran was directly admitted to the intensive care unit of a hospital. She could barely stay awake on the long journey, but when Xiao Han hurriedly carried her into the ward, she fell into a deep sleep. When He Ran woke up again, the light outside was gradually brightening, and the sky was already showing a pale, fish-white glow. He Ran didn't know how long she had slept. Her gaze shifted to the side, where Xiao Han was lying motionless beside her bed. Just looking at his hunched back, she knew how tired he was. Last night must have been a chaotic one, with her being the only one uninvolved. He Ran slowly withdrew her hand from Xiao Han's palm and glanced down. Several more needle marks appeared on the back of her pale, slender hand. She had absolutely no recollection of which skilled nurse had helped her insert the needle into her vein last night. He Ran lay on her back on the bed, quietly feeling her body for a while. The fever had subsided, and her forehead was no longer so hot, but she felt uncomfortable all over, weak and listless, perhaps the inflammation had flared up again. The quiet corridor was suddenly filled with Han Yu's voice; he seemed to be on the phone. Han Yu's voice was loud, and he never considered other people's feelings when he spoke. He Ran listened for a while, and the gist of it was that Han Yu had found her and asked Yang Wenping to come and take a look when she had time. At that moment, she felt like a fugitive had been caught, and couldn't help but sigh. She was simply too powerless and too lazy to bother anymore. A short while later, Xiao Han woke up, perhaps because Han Yu had woken him up. He looked up at her, his eyes bloodshot, and asked in a hoarse voice,

"Are you feeling better?"

He Ran nodded, letting out a soft

"Mmm"

. Xiao Han held her battered hands, stroking them slowly, and finally lowered his head to give her a gentle yet profound kiss. It was getting late, and the nurse came to check on He Ran, asking her some basic questions about her health. Before breakfast, He Ran had a blood test and a bone marrow examination, the results of which didn't come out until the evening. Han Yu was the first to receive the test results, and after looking at them two or three times, he blankly handed them to Xiao Han. Xiao Han reached out and took it. After reading it, he had the same expression as Han Yu, his face ashen. He Ran's bone marrow smear showed an unprecedentedly high proportion of immature lymphocytes, while her white blood cell count was abnormally low. The doctor said that her body was currently very weak, and chemotherapy was too late and would have the opposite effect. Having survived countless major disasters, He Ran remained remarkably calm upon receiving such devastating news. Just like the persistent high fever she had experienced a few months prior, she ate and slept as usual, leaving everything to fate. While her temperature was still normal, He Ran received a whitening injection. That night, she slept unusually soundly, unlike the restless sleep she experienced during a high fever. However, her good condition didn't last long. The next day at noon, He Ran's temperature started to rise again, quickly exceeding 40 degrees Celsius. Without her knowledge, the doctor used many medications to suppress it.

---

When she woke up again, she had no idea what year it was. He Ran stared at the ceiling above her head, her gaze gradually focusing. She hadn't expected this relapse to be so severe, like a blow to the head, giving her no time to react. Life is always so unpredictable, always ready to deliver a heavy blow when you're at your most triumphant. Just a week ago, she was enjoying herself with Xiao Han, how could she have imagined that a week later she would be bedridden again? He Ran slowly turned her head to look at the person who had been watching over her bedside, worrying about her. She spoke with difficulty, her voice hoarse and dry,

"Xiao Han, I'm hungry."

Xiao Han held her hand and asked softly,

"What would you like to eat?"

He Ran gave a weak, almost forced, laugh.

"Did I even have a choice?"

Her throat was sore and her tongue was swollen, making it difficult for her to speak clearly. She could only eat liquid food. Xiao Han made a bowl of corn porridge and fed her spoonful by spoonful. He Ran swallowed with difficulty, her mind blank, and after a long while she asked,

"How many days have I been in the hospital?"

Xiao Han was silent for a moment before replying,

"It's been four days."

He Ran frowned imperceptibly, as if this action required a lot of strength from her. She said to Xiao Han,

"You should go back to Fuling."

Xiao Han seemed not to hear her, and lowered his head to scoop up another spoonful of corn paste to continue feeding her. He Ran slightly turned away and said,

"Quan Quan and your mother are waiting for you."

Xiao Han said indifferently,

"It's nothing."

"Who said it's okay?"

He Ran stopped his hand, her tone slightly firm:

"Your mother is so old, she can't go on without someone taking care of her."

Xiao Han lowered his eyes and said after a while,

"I'll leave when you're feeling better."

"I'm fine. Han Yu is here, and my mom will be here soon."

He Ran looked at him calmly and said,

"Xiao Han, I can disregard my family for you, but I don't want you to become like me."

Xiao Han was speechless for a moment, staring at her silently. They looked at each other for a long time before he finally nodded and said,

"Okay, I'll leave tomorrow morning."

---

That evening, Xiao Han packed his things and went to He Ran's bedside to say goodbye. He Ran had just finished her injection and taken a nap. Hearing the light footsteps, she seemed to sense something and slowly opened her eyes, quietly looking at him.

Xiao Han sat down, a thousand words stuck in his throat. After a long pause, he finally managed to say,

"You need to get better soon and wait for me to come back."

He Ran blinked, nodding instead, and whispered,

"Come closer, I have something for you."

Her voice was very low, and Xiao Han had to put his face close to her ear to hear her clearly. He Ran took out a bank card and handed it to him, saying,

"There's a million in here. I've been keeping it for you."

Xiao Han frowned slightly.

"Why are you giving me this?"

He Ran said casually,

"You'll need it later."

"I don't need it."

Xiao Han resisted and pushed the card back.

"No, no."

He Ran pushed the card back and said,

"I know you can take care of yourself, but this money is a gift for Quan Quan. He will need it in the future."

Xiao Han remained silent. He Ran coughed violently several times before continuing,

"It's rare that he's so interested in painting. You should nurture him. Consider this money as his future tuition. You have no right to refuse on his behalf."

Xiao Han slowly lowered his head, staring at the card. Finally, he picked up the card and placed it in He Ran's palm, saying,

"I will accept it, but let's make a promise. You can give it back to me when I come back."

He Ran pursed her lips and smiled,

"Okay, I'll wait for you to come back."

As Xiao Han turned to leave, He Ran quietly slipped the card into his backpack. He never imagined that her last words,

"I'll wait for you to come back,"

would be her last. The high fever persisted, and the inflammation gradually spread throughout the body. It started in the mouth, then the respiratory tract, and finally the lungs. He Ran often experienced short, sharp pains in her chest, as if pricked by needles, and was constantly sweating. She had to change the sheets under her bed again and again. She spends more time unconscious than conscious. She would rather sleep longer, because once she wakes up, she has to endure excruciating pain all over her body and has to be injected with painkillers to get some relief. He Ran could feel her vital energy slowly dissipating under the intense heat, and all signs indicated that she might not escape this calamity. Han Yu was furious and helpless, and asked to transfer to another hospital several times, but the doctor stopped him each time. He Ran's body was too weak, and transferring her to another hospital at this time would only shorten her life in vain. Knowing her days were numbered, He Ran wasn't panicked. These three months were the result of her best efforts. If Xiao Han hadn't taken her away three months ago, she would have died long ago. After Xiao Han left, Han Yu replaced him as the person sitting by her bedside. He didn't offer any words of comfort or concern; every time He Ran woke up, he would sit there silently, staring at her. Not long ago, he would constantly curse the doctors and nurses, complaining that the medical equipment here wasn't advanced enough; it seemed that everyone and everything could make him extremely unhappy. But as He Ran's face grew more and more haggard each day, even he became silent. It's better that they don't talk. They used to always start arguing after just a few words, and it was rare for them to be this peaceful. At noon, the nurse fed He Ran some liquid food. She lay in bed and waved to Han Yu. Han Yu was staring at her blankly when he heard this. He paused for a moment, then moved closer to her and asked in a low voice,

"What?"

He Ran said softly,

"First, cremation, keep everything simple."

"Secondly, scatter my ashes into the sea. Even as ashes, I am still me. I don't want to be sealed in a box."

"Third, I will donate my corneas to Xu Yafei."

It wasn't until He Ran finished speaking that Han Yu realized she was giving her last words. He bit his lip hard, trying to suppress a strong emotion welling up inside him. After a long while, he finally released his lips and shouted loudly, feigning strength,

"Don't tell me this, I can't remember it. Tell that man when he gets back!"

"Actually, I never thought that one day I would be saying these words to you."

He Ran gave a bitter smile, with a hint of helplessness.

"But I probably won't be able to wait for him to come back."

"Why aren't we waiting any longer!"

Han Yu shouted even louder,

"He'll be back soon!"

He Ran was a little tired, her eyes slightly closed, her voice softer than the wind:

"It's better that he's not here. I would miss him if I saw him."

Han Yu stared at her intently, his lips almost bleeding from being bitten, unable to utter a single word for a long time. He really wanted to say something, he really wanted to tell her:

"You have to hold on! I've never properly told you that I like you before, you have to wait until that day!"

But he didn't dare. Recalling his relationship with He Ran from childhood to adulthood, he was always the one who relentlessly pursued her, while she always ran away, never staying for him. He didn't know why a perfectly healthy person would suddenly fall ill, so much so that for the past few days he had a persistent feeling that it was because he had been pushing her too hard that she kept running away, running to wherever it was far away, and this time she was going to run away to a place he could never catch up with again. So he dared not, absolutely dared not, pressure her again; he was afraid that this time she would really run away without looking back. After waking up from her coma for what felt like the umpteenth time, He Ran found herself with an oxygen mask over her face. Her body had completely lost its ability to produce blood, and she had to rely on blood transfusions to stay alive for the past few days. Another discovery was that Yang Wenping and He Jin had arrived. They arrived at some unknown time. Yang Wenping sat on the edge of the bed, and He Jin stood beside her. Both of them were looking at He Ran.

"Ranran."

Yang Wenping called her name softly, her expression blank, with many unspoken words stuck in her throat. He Ran was momentarily dazed. How long had it been since Yang Wenping had called her that? They used to be able to chat calmly and peacefully, but later...

---

Later, it's best not to mention it. He Ran wanted to respond, but when she opened her mouth, not a single sound came out. Most of her throat burned like it was on fire, as if it had been branded with a hot iron. Yang Wenping gently wiped her moist eyes, turned around and leaned her head against He Jin. He Jin sighed deeply, "What a tragedy." Now that things have come to this, scolding her for being disobedient is no longer of any use. Who can I curse? I can only curse the heavens. Xiao Han returned to Fuling County early in the morning and went straight to the hospital after getting off the bus. The old lady had just woken up and was sitting on the bed, being fed porridge by Quan Quan. Even a broken bone takes a hundred days to heal, let alone someone of her age; this fall could cause her considerable suffering.
