Upon seeing Xiao Han enter the ward, the old lady gave him a cold look and uttered two sinful words:

"Unfilial."

Xiao Han's expression remained indifferent, and he didn't offer any explanation. He put down his luggage, walked to the bedside, and gently patted Quan Quan's back. Quan Quan thoughtfully stood up, offered her seat to Xiao Han, and handed him her lunchbox. Xiao Han sat down, slowly scooped up a spoonful of porridge, blew on it to cool it down, and handed it to the old lady. The old lady stubbornly refused to eat; if Xiao Han went left, she went right, and if he went right, she went left. Xiao Han put down his bowl and said helplessly,

"Mom..."

The old lady kept her mouth shut, her face tense, ignoring him. Xiao Han tried everything to persuade her, but to no avail, and finally had to return the bowl to Quan Quan. After helping the old lady to sleep at noon, Xiao Han went outside the ward to send a text message to He Ran. After sending the text message and receiving no reply for a long time, Xiao Han guessed that she was probably in a high fever and unconscious state again. Soon after, Quan Quan came out and tugged at Xiao Han's sleeve, asking,

"Why didn't Aunt He come?"

Xiao Han squatted down and patted his head.

"She's sick and resting in the hospital."

Quan Quan said, puzzled,

"This is a hospital."

Xiao Han said,

"It's not a hospital here; it's in Lijiang."

Quan Quan asked, seemingly understanding but not quite,

"Will she come to see me when she gets better?"

Xiao Han nodded and smiled,

"Yes."

Hospital beds were in short supply, so after the old lady lay down to rest that night, Xiao Han took Quan Quan away from the hospital to find a hotel nearby. Before going to sleep, he finally received a text message from He Ran. She briefly explained what she had eaten that day, what injections she had received, and her temperature. Unable to offer her the closest care she could, Xiao Han could only offer comfort and prayers:

"You'll get better, hang in there."

The old lady was worried about the money, so after staying in the hospital for a few days, she insisted on going home. Neither the doctor nor Xiao Han could persuade her otherwise, so they had no choice but to sign the consent form. Although the old lady was old, she was still quite healthy. On the way home, Xiao Han helped her, and she was able to walk steadily. July is the rainy season, and this week the rain has been falling intermittently, sometimes heavy and sometimes light, without stopping. The sky is always shrouded in gloom and oppression, which affects people's moods as well. After arriving home, the old lady was unable to do heavy work and spent all her time resting in bed. Xiao Han personally took care of her daily needs. After being served for a few days, the old lady's complexion finally improved, and she began to talk to him again. A peaceful week has passed. After not receiving any text messages from He Ran for several days, Xiao Han could no longer convince himself to stay here calmly.

---

After lunch, he made up his mind and went to the old lady's bedside, saying,

"Mom, I've booked a plane ticket tonight to go to Lijiang to see her."

The old lady was displeased upon hearing this, her eyes widening:

"You've only been back a short time and you're leaving again?!"

Xiao Han kept his mouth shut and didn't say a word, his intention was very clear. The old lady was furious. She pointed at him and said,

"I raised you with so much care and hardship, and now that I'm old and useless, all you do is think about those young girls outside!"

Xiao Han was silent for a while before speaking:

"Mom, I'm sorry... She's very weak right now, and I have to stay by her side."

The old lady wouldn't listen to any of this; she continued to grumble incessantly about Xiao Han's ingratitude. Xiao Han didn't argue back, sticking to his own opinion. Only after the old lady finally stopped talking because she was thirsty did he go back to his room to pack his luggage. Xiao Han didn't need to bring much; he only needed two sets of clothes to change into, and he packed them up quickly. Quan Quan quietly entered his room and whispered,

"Uncle, are you going to see Aunt He?"

Xiao Han turned around and nodded.

"Mm."

Quan Quan was holding a stack of drawing paper. He hesitated for a while before stepping forward and saying,

"These are my recent drawings. Could you please give them to my aunt for me?"

Xiao Han lowered his head, reached out and took the papers, looking at them one by one. After repeated practice, Quan Quan's paintings are no longer as childish as they used to be, and have begun to take shape. One of the paintings depicts the three of them sitting in a tall Ferris wheel. Quan Quan and He Ran are chatting and laughing happily, while he alone has a stern face because of his fear of heights. Xiao Han's gaze lingered on the screen for a long time, his eyes filled with an indescribable mix of longing and something else. If they could ride a Ferris wheel like this again, he would definitely try his best to smile. Xiao Han carefully arranged the sheets of drawing paper one by one and put them into his backpack, promising Quan Quan,

"Don't worry, I will definitely show them to her."

He slung his bag over his shoulder and prepared to leave, with Quan Quan following behind him to the gate. Watching Xiao Han gradually disappear into the distance, Quan Quan waved repeatedly at his retreating figure,

"Go ahead without worry, I'll take good care of Grandma—"

The trip started badly. Xiao Han walked several miles to the place where the bus passed by, but waited for more than two hours without seeing a single bus. I later asked some villagers who were passing by and learned that due to the continuous rainfall in the past few days, several sections of the road in the mountains had collapsed, and the road to the city had been blocked and vehicles were not allowed to pass for the time being. Xiao Han was in a hurry, so he had to go back to the village and visit several households with vans, asking if he could hire one for double the price. The families all gave the same answer:

"It's too dangerous in the rain. There might be landslides on the road. No matter how much money you offer, we wouldn't dare go."

Xiao Han didn't give up and pleaded relentlessly for a long time, but the other party remained unmoved. With nowhere else to turn, Xiao Han was trapped in the mountains.

In the evening, he returned along the same route, completely soaked. As darkness fell, the rain showed no sign of stopping. The mountain path was full of potholes, and the cold, muddy ground made each step a heavy, arduous journey. He wasn't usually an emotional person, but at that moment he couldn't help but clench his fists and slam them heavily against the door. Quan Quan heard the noise and ran out. She was startled when she saw him:

"Uncle, why are you back again?"

Xiao Han lowered his head, his expression unreadable. Raindrops streamed down his hair and fell to the ground one after another. After a long pause, he finally said,

"There's no car, we can't leave."

Quan Quan was speechless.

"...Then what should we do?"

Xiao Han sighed silently,

"Let's wait a day or two."

He lifted his leg and walked into the house, took out his phone and sent a text message, even though he knew he might not receive a reply. After sending the text message, he sat on the bed, staring at the rain outside the window, his gaze lost in a daze. A few minutes later, the phone suddenly rang. Xiao Han rushed over with great joy and answered the phone as quickly as possible. Han Yu's voice came through the phone,

"He Ran is awake. Talk to her something; she can hear you."

In that instant, too many words surged to Xiao Han's throat, vying to be spoken. He suppressed them for a long time before asking,

"How is she now?"

Han Yu held the phone very close to He Ran's mouth. He Ran is unable to speak and can only utter some simple syllables. She mumbled incoherently for a long time, as if trying to prove to him that she was still there. "You'd better come back soon, she..."

He didn't continue speaking. Xiao Han's hands were trembling as he hung up the phone. He couldn't help but wonder what He Ran wanted to say to him, but how could he possibly guess? "冉"

on the window, stroke by stroke. After eight o'clock, Quan Quan and the old lady turned off the lights and went to bed. He didn't know how long he had slept, but in the dead of night, he vaguely felt a pair of hands gently stroking his face. The sensation was surreal, yet strangely familiar. He couldn't hear clearly who was whispering in his ear, as if through a veil. Xiao Han frowned, trying hard to hear something, but the hands began to slowly move away from his face. He instinctively reached out to grab something, but he couldn't stop what was destined to happen. Those hands were slowly slipping from his grasp, taking with them something more important than life itself. The more anxious he became, the stronger that feeling grew. His hands were icy cold, almost imperceptible; he couldn't grasp anything, and in the end, he could only grab at things haphazardly. The tenderness of the past gradually faded and left, eventually disappearing into the silent night. Desperate to find something to dispel his unease, he hurriedly picked up his phone and dialed the number with trembling fingers. The monotonous beeping sound looped endlessly in the silent room, leaving my heart hanging in suspense. Countless forty seconds passed, but no one answered, and the call automatically ended. This seems to be an answer. Xiao Han got out of bed, staggered a few steps, and knelt on the ground. He looked up blankly at the dark window; the character

"冉"

was no longer there. Three in the morning is known as the witching hour. This is the time when the chances of dying in a hospital are at their highest. He Ran's passing was anything but peaceful; the doctors and nurses in the entire ward were all terrified by her death. Outside in the corridor, Han Yu flew into a rage, uttering a harsh threat,

"If you can't save her, none of you will be able to stay here!"

Yang Wenping pressed down on his shoulder and gently comforted him,

"Don't be nervous, don't put too much pressure on them."

Han Yu wouldn't listen to reason, and angrily kicked the wall, shaking the entire building. He sat down forcefully, his fingers interlaced tightly, his eyes glaring fiercely, and no one dared to look at him. He knew perfectly well that he shouldn't blame the medical staff; a life hanging in the balance was in their hands, and he should be grateful and plead with them. But even understanding this, he couldn't control his rage, as if only through such extreme actions could he slightly alleviate his psychological burden. The ward door was tightly closed, separating two worlds; those on this side were filled with anxiety, while those on the other side were in a state of life and death uncertainty. The corridor fell silent; everyone held their breath, their hearts clenching. The sounds of resuscitation efforts could be faintly heard inside the ward, and the conversations between the doctors and nurses never stopped.

"One milligram of adrenaline was administered intravenously."

"Prepare for defibrillation, 200 joules."

"Xiao Tang."

Tang Ying smiled and congratulated Xiao warmly, saying,

"Congratulations, Teacher Xiao, on winning another big prize!"

"Just lucky,"

Xiao Quan smiled modestly, then asked,

"How's the art exhibition going?"

"Don't worry, don't worry, everything is in the process of preparation!"

Tang Ying said confidently, but then her tone became hesitant as if she remembered something.

"Teacher Xiao... are you sure you want to exhibit that painting?"

Tang Ying asked uncertainly. Xiao Quan succinctly replied:

"Exhibition."

Tang Ying was silent for a moment, still finding it hard to understand.

"Does it really have to be placed in the main exhibition area? After all, it's not a famous work by a renowned artist. Wouldn't that be a waste of resources?"

"She deserves that position."

Xiao Quan put his sunglasses back on, shutting out the outside world.

"Without her, I wouldn't be who I am today."

Tang Ying understood what he meant, so she didn't say anything more, nodded and said,

"Okay, I'll take care of it."

"Thank you for your help, Xiao Tang."

Xiao Quan smiled at her and then said,

"I'll go back to the hotel to rest for a while. I'll go with you to do the interview this afternoon."

"No need, no need,"

Tang Ying hurriedly waved her hand and said,

"You're having a hard time adjusting to the time difference, so please get some rest. I can go by myself; it's not a difficult task."

Xiao Quan thought for a moment, nodded, and said,

"Okay, then I'll leave it to you."

Before setting off in the afternoon, Tang Ying went back to the gallery to check the progress of the setup one last time. The upcoming art exhibition will be the gallery's first public appearance, which is of great significance. Tang Ying, as one of the planners, bears a heavy responsibility. The gallery isn't very large, but it's uniquely decorated. Each exhibition wall is crisscrossed, and warm orange light shines down from above, creating an artistic atmosphere. The paintings, some vibrant and others somber, are arranged in an orderly fashion. Among them are not only Xiao Quan's recent works, but also several other classic pieces by masterful artists. From any perspective, this will be an exhibition of extremely high quality. However, in the most prominent booth in the entire gallery, there was a framed, unremarkable painting. Tang Ying, a recent graduate, is considered a semi-professional with a certain level of connoisseurship. The painting in front of her, nearly twenty years old, is still remarkably well-preserved. Although the painting style is somewhat outdated and the brushstrokes are not particularly mature, it inexplicably conveys a captivating power. Tang Ying couldn't quite put her finger on what exactly had moved her. More information about this painting is yet to be discovered by her; currently, all that is known is that the painting is titled

"He Stands Amidst the Splendor of Summer Flowers"

and that the artist is He Ran. Tang Ying wasn't entirely unfamiliar with the name. Years ago, there was a once-famous but short-lived female painter named He Yihua, and it was rumored that He Ran was her niece. However, this weak connection alone wasn't enough to justify exhibiting her paintings on such an important occasion. Tang Ying expressed her doubts to Xiao Quan more than once, and always received the same answer - don't ask too many questions, just do as he says. Today, she is finally going to solve this mystery. Tang Ying's heart was filled with anticipation. Following the address given by Xiao Quan, Tang Ying finally found the flower shop. Rows of tall flower stands lined the entrance of the flower shop, adorned with clusters of flowers, creating a warm and inviting atmosphere. Sunlight streamed through the two transparent glass doors, dappling the exotic flowers and plants inside, causing dewdrops to glisten and filling the air with their fragrance. The shop owner wasn't there; a young man was minding the shop. Tang Ying pushed open the door, and a lucky cat beside her smiled and said,

"Welcome."

The moment she stepped into the store, a little girl suddenly rushed up to her and sweetly called out,

"Daddy."

Seemingly realizing that the thigh she was holding was the wrong size, the little girl immediately let go, staring blankly into space. Tang Ying was also stunned, standing there at a loss for what to do. The man minding the shop quickly came over, took the girl away, and bowed to apologize to Tang Ying:

"I'm sorry, she's blind and thought our boss was back when she heard the door open."

Tang Ying smiled with relief, indicating that it was okay. After standing there for a while, the man asked again,

"Is there anything you need to buy?"

"Oh, I'm not here to buy flowers."

Tang Ying took out a business card from her bag and introduced herself:

"My name is Tang Ying. I have an appointment with your boss to interview him."

"Oh."

The man quickly remembered,

"The boss just went out to run some errands, he'll be back soon, please wait a moment."

Tang Ying nodded and said,

"Okay, no problem."

The man led her inside and served her tea with great hospitality. The little girl who had bumped into Tang Ying earlier clung to the man's trouser leg, following him closely. Tang Ying couldn't help but take a few more glances at her. The little girl was very pretty, with fair skin, small ears, and round, bright eyes. It was just a pity that her eyes were lifeless, like a puppet doll that had lost its spirit.

"What's her name?"

Tang Ying asked.

"Xiao Sisi"

. How old are you?

"Six and a half years old."

Tang Ying's gaze swept over the little girl a few times, and unable to contain her curiosity, she asked again,

"Why is she blind?"

"It's a condition you were born with, it can't be cured."

The man said calmly, as if he had answered this question many times before. Tang Ying couldn't help but sigh,

"How pitiful."

"You can't say that,"

the man smiled.

"The boss treats her like a precious gem, and does everything she wants. In my opinion, she's happier than many children."

Tang Ying couldn't help but laugh, but after laughing, she realized something was wrong. Tang Ying wondered to herself: According to Xiao Quan, his uncle had never been married, so how did a daughter suddenly appear out of nowhere? She raised her question, and the man explained,

"Sisi is not the boss's biological daughter; she was adopted from an orphanage."

"Oh—I see."

Tang Ying drawled out her words, seemingly lost in thought. After chatting idly for about ten minutes, the boss finally returned. The lucky cat at the door chimed a welcome message again, and Xiao Sisi was the first to react. She turned around nimbly, and even though she couldn't see, she jumped into someone's arms with perfect accuracy, calling out softly,

"Daddy."

Tang Ying turned around at the sound and looked at the man standing at the door, squatting down to talk to Xiao Sisi. Even though he was middle-aged, the man still maintained a good figure. He was slightly hunched over, but overall he was still thin and upright. His face didn't show many signs of age, except for two deep nasolabial folds on either side of his nose. Perhaps because his features resembled Xiao Quan's, Tang Ying didn't find him unfamiliar. After staring at him for a while, Tang Ying realized her rudeness and quickly stood up to greet him with a smile: "Hello, Mr. Xiao, I am Tang Ying." The man looked up at her and said succinctly, "Hello, Xiao Han." "Um..." The man opposite her just looked at her without making a sound, but for some reason, Tang Ying felt the same nervousness she had when she first interviewed at Xiao Quan's studio. She became incoherent: "I am Xiao Quan's assistant. I am here to interview you about that painting. I hope you can cooperate... Xiao Quan should have notified you in advance, right? I have heard him talk about some stories about you, but I still think it is better to talk to you in person." Xiao Han nodded slightly. "Yes, I know." Tang Ying took out her notebook from her backpack and asked cautiously, "So... can we begin now?" "Okay." Xiao Han nodded sparingly. He slowly and deliberately put Xiao Sisi down on the ground, then led Tang Ying into the inner room. The place was rather small, and the two sat facing each other somewhat awkwardly. Tang Ying gradually regained her professional attitude, opened her notebook in an orderly manner, took out her voice recorder, and nodded to Xiao Han, indicating that she could start speaking now and that she would take careful notes. Late October, late autumn in Beijing. According to Xiao Han, this is the woman's favorite season. Just before the golden maple forest was about to be covered by a vast expanse of white, the first exhibition of Qingbiao Gallery finally officially opened. On that day, many renowned artists and connoisseurs from the industry attended the event, and numerous media reporters also rushed to report on it, all in order to catch a glimpse of the legendary master Fu's prized student. The gallery was packed with visitors, and long queues formed outside, making it completely congested. Tang Ying had been so nervous about this matter for the past few nights that she couldn't sleep. Only now, having witnessed the successful holding of the entire art exhibition, did a huge weight lift from her heart, replaced by an overwhelming joy. Wearing her work badge, Tang Ying walked back and forth in the venue, patiently explaining each painting on display to the guests. On this day, unsurprisingly, the painting titled "He Stands Amidst the Splendor of Summer Flowers," which had never been shown before, on the main exhibition booth, sparked heated discussion. On this same day, Tang Ying recounted the story of Xiao Han and He Ran to the guests in great detail, repeating it countless times until her throat was dry. That long-buried past, unearthed twenty years later, is still filled with endless regret and helplessness. It is said that Xiao Han was unable to see He Ran one last time until her body was taken into the morgue. After her death, he was not in a suitable capacity to attend her funeral. He didn't even get to touch her ashes before they were scattered into the sea. The tragedy of life and death, separation and parting, often brings tears to one's eyes, but Tang Ying couldn't help but recall the expression on Xiao Han's face when he reminisced about this past event. The sadness on his face was very faint, so faint that it was almost impossible to find, as if these painful experiences had never happened to him. He said, "She's always been there." Tang Ying still couldn't understand what that sentence meant. Perhaps he was still living in his own memories, or perhaps he was just using this assumption to comfort himself. As dusk approached, the number of people in the gallery finally began to decrease. It had been a busy but fruitful day for Tang Ying. Xiao Quan hosted a banquet at the hotel to invite guests, while she stayed at the gallery to finish up the last bit of work. During the clearing process, Tang Ying noticed a man holding a little girl standing in front of the main booth, lingering there. She had to step forward and remind him, "Sir, I'm sorry, the gallery is about to close..." Before she could finish speaking, she paused for a moment before realizing that the person in front of her was an acquaintance. Tang Ying's voice was very soft, and Xiao Han didn't notice her arrival. His gaze remained fixed on the painting in front of him, motionless, completely oblivious to the outside world. An indescribable emotion lingered in his eyes, heavy and profound, his longing painting a picture that no one else could penetrate. Tang Ying couldn't help but look at the man standing amidst the vibrant summer flowers in the picture. A few seconds later, she turned her head and glanced at Xiao Han a few more times. From the side, he seemed to have changed exactly, just like he had back then. The man's profile, showing him intently working, leaves a lingering question. The next second, he might turn his head to look at her. Perhaps he would raise his hand at her, and she would smile back at him. Perhaps after work, he and she would go to the archway in Xiaozhou Village and share a bowl of noodles. But who knows? Holding Xiao Sisi in his arms, she was very excited and babbled, "When I grow up, I want to be a painter!" This time, Xiao Han came to his senses, and he smiled somewhat bitterly: "Silly girl, how can you draw if you can't see it?" Xiao Sisi waved her little fist in dissatisfaction, "I can draw, I can draw!" The father and daughter were enjoying themselves, and Tang Ying couldn't bear to disturb them. She quietly returned to her office and waited until Xiao Han led Xiao Sisi away before she came out. Standing at the doorway, I watched the man and the little girl's figures fade into the distance as they walked towards the road. As dusk settled, a gust of autumn wind swept by. The two walked hand in hand into the distance, their figures blurring as if they had stepped into a yellowed piece of parchment and been permanently frozen in the image. Tang Ying suddenly understood something. Perhaps, it really is as he said. Time may pass, but some things will never change, just like she has always been there. As night quietly fell, Tang Ying was the last to leave the gallery. She slowly pulled down the roller shutter, and with her last glance before turning around, she looked at the silent, unchanging painting in the distance. A painting tells an eternal story, and those who view the painting are witnesses to that story. That year, she was still beautiful, and he was still young. Their love may have been crazy, or absurd. But because it was fleeting, it deserves all the more to be known by the world.

When the morning sun shines on your body
That's me touching you.
When the breeze caresses your face
That's me gently kissing you.
When the sky is filled with white snow
That's me singing for you.
When you miss me
I am in your heart
I've always been by your side.
